**Not a relaxation — three cases TIGHTEN:** an unlinked Doctor-role user previously got a branch and now gets `[]`; an inactive doctor master now resolves to `[]`; a stale `trx_user_online_contexts` row on a revoked practice branch grants nothing (the pivot is the authority, so revoking a practice branch revokes the reads with it). Every other gate is untouched — authentication, named read permission, server-resolved branch scope, treating relationship, PUBLISHED status, private-disk policy. **A doctor's legacy reach stays strictly narrower than their native reach**: native access is the clinical relationship across the RME branch set, while the archive additionally requires the origin branch to be one they practise in.

**Authorization matrix (all pinned by tests):** treating + in practice set → **ALLOW** · treating + no practice branch → DENY 404 · treating + branch not in set → DENY 404 · **same branch + not treating → DENY 403** · no doctor master link → DENY · inactive master → DENY · branch deactivated or RME switched off → DENY · VOID/non-PUBLISHED → hidden · other patient's record id → DENY · read permission removed → DENY. **404 = outside branch scope (anti-enumeration); 403 = inside scope, not authorized** (1C/1D/ROLL-2 convention preserved).

**Fixture correction (not a weakening).** `DoctorFactory::configure()` seeds the practice pivot from the master's own `branch_id`; the existing legacy doctor fixtures created the master **without** a branch, so the doctor's practice branch was a random unrelated branch and those tests passed only because `users.branch_id` was the authority. The fixtures now state the branch on the **doctor master** — what they always meant — and every assertion, including the same-branch-non-treating **403**, is preserved verbatim. Suite returned to exactly baseline: **483 passed / 5 skipped / 1308 assertions**. **Durable test rule: state a doctor's practice branch on the doctor MASTER (`withAllowedBranches([...])` or `branch_id`), never on the user row alone.**

**Stale comments corrected as part of rules sync:** `RoleSeeder` Doctor block ("never widens the doctor beyond their own BranchContext branch") and `LegacyRmeRecordPolicy` ("a clinical reader stays pinned to their own branch") both described the superseded model.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeDoctorClinicalBranchContextTest.php` (20) — every case run with migration capability OFF and admission EMPTY, including a case asserting the read works with `users.branch_id` NULL and zero online-context rows, and a side-effect case asserting **zero delta** across `trx_clinic_visits` / `trx_medical_records` / `trx_rme_invoices` / `trx_rme_payments` / `trx_lab_orders` / `trx_lab_case_candidates` / `trx_satusehat_candidates` / `trx_rme_legacy_records` / `stg_rme_legacy_imports`.

**Durable rules (mirror rule 99):** a doctor needs BOTH the treating relationship AND a valid clinical branch context · same branch alone never authorizes · a treating relationship never bypasses branch isolation · the doctor branch context is server-derived from `mst_doctor_branches` ∩ active RME-enabled branches · NULL/empty/unknown/inactive fails closed with no fallback · the online context never widens the practice set · client `branch_id`/`patient_id`/`legacy_record_id` are never authority · `origin_branch_id` stays a visibility scope, never a bypass · legacy read stays read-only and creates no ClinicVisit/MedicalRecord/billing/Lab/SATUSEHAT · migration OFF keeps every mutation path blocked · branch admission stays separate from a doctor's clinical branch context · doctor practice assignment is operational configuration (doctor master), never hardcoded ids · **HISTORY-1B authorizes NO rollout — production stays `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE=false` with an EMPTY admitted set** · real production Doctor UAT (positive + same-branch non-treating negative) is required before GO.

## LEGACY-RME-PDF-ROLL-4 — Production Migration Operations & Scale-Up (2026-08-14)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-4-production-migration-operations-scale-up` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline HISTORY-1B GO `c947b94dc819bc9ec535b1288420ea06542026b3`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-4-production-migration-operations-scale-up.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-migration-operations-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/97-legacy-rme-production-migration-operations.mdc`. ROLL-3 / HISTORY-1 / HISTORY-1A / HISTORY-1B GO tags immutable and untouched.

**What it adds.** ROLL-3 answered *"may this BRANCH migrate?"*. ROLL-4 answers the four questions a real multi-branch migration then raises — **WHO** (per-branch operator assignment), **HOW MUCH** (server-enforced daily quota), **can we stop safely** (wave/branch pause + drain), and **did anything go missing** (reconciliation + completion sign-off).

**THE COMPOSITION RULE — the whole safety argument: ROLL-4 CAN ONLY NARROW, NEVER WIDEN.** Ingestion order is `capability → date rules → RM-derived branch → ROLL-3 admission → ROLL-3 capacity → ROLL-4 operations gate → storage → transaction(quota reserve + staging row) → after-commit dispatch`. The operations gate is consulted ONLY after admission has admitted and can only refuse; no code path turns a ROLL-3 denial into an acceptance (pinned by `it cannot rescue a branch that ROLL-3 refuses`). **Evidence it is genuinely additive: all 503 pre-existing LegacyRme tests pass with ROLL-4 enforced and ZERO existing test files edited.**

**The layer is REQUIRED, not opt-in.** Capability ON + a non-empty admitted set with no registered ACTIVE wave ⇒ `WAVE_NOT_REGISTERED`. An opt-in control repeats the exact defect ROLL-3 removed.

**Two records of one decision.** `legacy_rme_rollout.admission` (config) stays the **AUTHORITY** — deploy-time, outside the app's write path. The new `ops_rme_legacy_migration_waves` row is an **operational MIRROR** of the wave label + approval reference + exact approved branch set. When they disagree, **neither is assumed correct** and ingestion stops with `WAVE_BINDING_MISMATCH` (ROLL-3's scope-binding fix, one level up). The approved set is compared as a SET, so order is irrelevant but membership is not.

**Migrations (5, additive only — `migrate`, never `migrate:fresh`/`db:wipe`):** `2026_08_14_100001..4` create `ops_rme_legacy_migration_waves`, `ops_rme_legacy_wave_branches` (UNIQUE wave+branch), `ops_rme_legacy_wave_operators` (UNIQUE wave+user+branch, soft revoke), `ops_rme_legacy_migration_quotas` (UNIQUE wave+branch+date — every component NOT NULL, because PostgreSQL treats NULLs as distinct and a nullable component would silently permit duplicate buckets = double quota); `100005` adds nullable indexed `stg_rme_legacy_imports.migration_wave_id` (operational provenance, **NOT** an authorization field — no policy or repository scope reads it; legacy rows keep NULL and NULL never means "the current wave").

**Operator assignment + its ONE documented exemption.** An intake operator (holding `create_legacy_rme_imports` only) needs an explicit unrevoked assignment for that exact branch. A `manage_legacy_rme_migration_operations` holder is treated as assigned — **not a bypass**: they can `assignOperator()` themselves onto any enrolled branch unilaterally, so the reachable branch set is *identical* with or without the check; the exemption removes a step, not a capability, and every other gate still applies (pinned by `it still refuses a wave governor when the wave itself is paused`).

**Quota.** Counting point = **accepted into staging**, reserved inside the same transaction as the staging row, so a rolled-back import releases its quota with no compensating write. **Retries are NOT charged again** (the document was already accepted; render load is ROLL-3's capacity gate). Concurrency safety comes from locking a *pre-existing* counter row (`insertOrIgnore` then ONE `ORDER BY branch_id … FOR UPDATE`, which also makes it deadlock-safe) — counting staging rows is derivable but unlockable, because the row that should block the second uploader is the one neither has written yet. **`NULL` ≠ `0`**: NULL declines to limit, 0 admits nothing.

**Reconciliation.** `accepted = published + cancelled + failed_unresolved + in_flight`; `unexplained` is the remainder and is zero *by construction*, which is exactly why it is asserted — a tenth status added without updating the report makes it non-zero and blocks sign-off instead of losing a document. `quota_drift` is a second independent check from a different table. Branch completion requires zero in-flight, zero unresolved failures, zero unexplained, zero drift, status `DRAINING`, and a sign-off note; the reconciliation is **recomputed under the row lock** and frozen onto the row. Wave completion requires every enrolled branch `COMPLETED` or explicitly `CANCELLED`. **An empty queue is never completion, and `failed_jobs = 0` is never queue-health evidence** (the ROLL-2 lesson).

**Controls.** `PAUSED` and `DRAINING` behave identically at runtime and differ in what they permit *next* (drain is not resumable) — merging them would force "we are stopping for good" to be expressed by a state that invites a resume. **No ROLL-4 state ever removes authorized PUBLISHED clinical read** (HISTORY-1A/1B): pinned for paused, drained and closed waves, plus the negative case that a non-treating doctor stays denied throughout. Capability OFF remains the emergency control and still preserves published read.

**Monitoring honesty.** Unmeasurable probes return `null`/"not measurable", never 0 (a fabricated zero reads as "healthy, nothing pending" — the ROLL-2 failure). `planned_document_count` stays nullable and `completion_percent` is `null` without it — there is no way to derive the size of a paper archive. Stale `PROCESSING` is **surfaced, never auto-mutated**. Storage is measured; disk-free delegates to the ROLL-3 capacity probe so the dashboard cannot contradict the gate.

**RBAC (3 new permissions, granted to NO role by default — Super Admin passes via `Gate::before`):** `view_legacy_rme_migration_operations`, `manage_legacy_rme_migration_operations`, `approve_legacy_rme_migration_wave` (split so separation of duties has something to enforce; `require_separate_approver` adds approver≠creator server-side, **off for the pilot with the risk assessed and recorded in `config/legacy_rme_operations.php`** — accepted because a wave still cannot admit a branch the owner did not approve). Routes `settings.rme.migration-operations.*` (9). Surface 404s while the capability is off.

**Security.** All gates server-side; the branch is always RM-derived; IDOR re-checked in the **service** so the CLI is covered too; `legacy-rme:wave-admin` authorizes `--actor` through the same policy as HTTP and is **dry-run unless `--apply`**; wave codes validated to a canonical token. New PII-free audit events `LEGACY_RME_IMPORT_OPERATIONS_REJECTED` + 7 `LEGACY_RME_WAVE_*`; new allow-listed keys `operator_user_id`, `daily_quota`, `reason_length` (free-text reasons recorded by LENGTH only, as 1D does for `void_reason_length`).

**Two real defects found by the new tests and fixed (not worked around):** (1) `migration_wave_id` was missing from `LegacyRmeImport::$fillable`, so mass assignment silently dropped it and every reconciliation would have measured zero accepted documents; (2) the Pest wave fixture force-reset the wave to `ACTIVE` on every call, which would have made every pause/drain assertion vacuously pass.

**Commands:** `legacy-rme:migration-status [--wave=][--json][--strict]` (read-only; `--strict` fails on WAVE_NOT_REGISTERED / WAVE_BINDING_MISMATCH / ADMITTED_WITHOUT_APPROVAL / PIPELINE_SATURATED / UNEXPLAINED_RECORDS / QUOTA_LEDGER_DRIFT / STALE_PROCESSING / REVIEW_BACKLOG / OPERATIONS_NOT_ENFORCED) and `legacy-rme:wave-admin <action>` (register/approve/activate/pause/resume/drain/cancel/complete/assign/revoke/branch-*). `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` gains a `migration_operations_layer` check (empty admission = GO; a wave is only required when something is admitted).

**Tests:** `LegacyRmeMigrationOperationsGateTest` (32), `LegacyRmeMigrationReconciliationTest` (24), `LegacyRmeMigrationOperationsAccessTest` (11). Green: ROLL-4 67; `tests/Feature/LegacyRme` **570 passed / 5 skipped / 1501 assertions**; permission+access-control+doctor-scope regression 434 passed; repo-wide `pint --test` passed; `git diff --check` clean; `npm run build` + `view:cache` pass; `config:cache` round-trip holds the ROLL-1 capture rule; `foundation:security-compliance-check` GO (9/9), `foundation:ci-runtime-control-check --strict` GO, `architecture:ui-governance-check --strict` GO, `foundation:roadmap-check --strict` GO (next `MON-1`, not stale).

**ROLL-4 GO does NOT authorize** any branch, global/permanent enablement, an approved wave, bulk unattended import, automatic ingestion, auto-publish, OCR as clinical-date authority, or any widening of doctor read scope. Every production wave still needs a fresh owner approval naming the exact branch set; historical approvals never authorize a new wave. **Safe resting state: capability OFF, admitted branches EMPTY, published read AVAILABLE.**

## INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 — Production Secret File Permission Hardening (2026-08-15)

Branch `feature/infra-sec-env-1-production-secret-file-permission-hardening` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `2ffe00c`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/infra-sec-env-1-production-secret-file-permission-hardening.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/production-secret-file-permissions-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/100-production-secret-file-permission-hardening.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (SECURITY_FIX). **No migration, no permission, no route, no application runtime change, no clinical side effect.** **Finding:** the production environment file at the app root was `root root 0644` — **world-readable** on a host shared with a co-tenant application (`aish-pos`) and a `postgres` shell account. **Root cause: no deploy step had ever asserted a mode on it** — `deploy-vps.sh` hardened only `storage`/`bootstrap/cache`; the file was created once by hand under the default `umask 022` and kept `0644` forever. Existing gates all checked *writability for the runtime user*, never *unreadability for everyone else*, which is why none caught it (an absent invariant, not a regression).

**Approved state (derived from the real runtime, NOT assumed): owner `root`, group = PHP-FPM runtime group (`www-data`), mode `0640`.** **`0600` is wrong here and would cause a silent total outage:** `deploy-vps.sh` rebuilds caches **as the PHP-FPM runtime user** (`as_runtime php artisan config:cache`, from FIX-LOGIN-REDIRECT-RUNTIME-PERMISSIONS), and phpdotenv's `safeLoad()` **swallows a permission error and returns an empty environment** — so an unreadable file silently yields a config cache with an empty application key and empty DB credentials, with no error. Secret-bearing backups (`.env.bak*`/`.env.backup*`/`.env.save*`/`.env.old*`/`.env.orig*`) → `0600`; `.env.example` untouched.

**Shipped:** new `scripts/harden-secret-permissions.sh` (idempotent `apply`/`verify`; never prints contents; never creates/edits a secret file; never recursive-chmods the tree; fails closed on a symlinked secret file; detects named-user/group read ACLs when `getfacl` exists; `chown` only as root so it is testable unprivileged). `deploy-vps.sh` hardens **early — before it sources the file** and before the runtime user rebuilds the config cache — then re-asserts + `verify`s late, aborting with `NOT GO`. `rollback-vps.sh` re-asserts the same invariant. **Durable regression protection:** `harden-secret-permissions.sh` is a **required marker** in `config/deployment_rollback.php` for BOTH `deploy_expectations` and `rollback_expectations`, so `foundation:deployment-rollback-check` (runs in CI **and** inside every deploy) fails if the call is ever removed. `.gitignore` → `.env.*` + `!.env.example`.

**Known residual, deliberately NOT claimed as closed:** the co-tenant `aish-pos` FPM pool runs under the **same `www-data` account**, so a file mode cannot stop same-UID read. `0640` closes `postgres` and every other local account; closing the co-tenant needs a dedicated runtime account (own FPM pool user + queue worker `User=` + `storage`/`bootstrap/cache` ownership) — a separate sprint, out of this scope. **Gotcha for that future sprint:** `deploy-vps.sh` resolves `RUNTIME_USER` by grepping **all** FPM pool files and taking `head -1`; with two pools that only works while both declare `www-data` — make it pool-specific first.

**Gotchas:** (1) `release_evidence.forbidden_patterns` contains the literal `.env`, and ENT-15's `EnterpriseDocumentationScanner::sensitiveContentPosture()` applies it to **registered** runbooks + the policy doc — so the new runbook is deliberately **NOT** added to `config/enterprise_documentation.php` `mandatory_runbooks` (registering it without rewording would fail `foundation:enterprise-documentation-check` in CI and on deploy). (2) A fresh git worktree has no local env file, and phpdotenv's suppressed read makes **every** test report a PHP warning — including untouched pre-existing tests; create a local gitignored env file (or expect warnings). (3) The deploy runner `bash`-buffers `deploy-vps.sh`, so the deploy that *introduces* a script change runs the OLD body — the new hardening therefore takes effect on the **next** deploy, which is why enforcement was proven with a second deploy rather than assumed.

**SECOND FINDING (same deploy path, larger blast radius): `storage/app/backups/` held **175 database dumps at mode 0664 — world-readable**, each a full `pg_dump` of the clinical database (patients, KTP/NIK, records, invoices); `runuser -u postgres -- test -r` confirmed an unrelated account could read them. Same root cause (`pg_dump > file` under the deploy user's `umask 022`, then `normalize_runtime_ownership` sets every storage file to `0664`). Fixed **`other`-bits-only** (`0664`/`0644` → `0640`, owner/group untouched) so root (backup-verify/restore/rehearsal/evidence) and the runtime user (ENT-7 dev-console backup listing) keep the exact access they had and only unrelated accounts lose it — cannot break the ENT-11 storage contract or ENT-12 backup/DR. `deploy-vps.sh` and `rollback-vps.sh` also `chmod 0640 "$BACKUP"` immediately after `pg_dump` to close the creation window.

Tests: `tests/Feature/Deploy/SecretFilePermissionHardeningTest.php` (25 — functional runs of the real script against synthetic `APP_KEY=fake-test-value` fixtures in a private 0700 temp dir: 0640/0600 accepted; 0644/0666/0660/0664/0604/0646 rejected; apply converges + idempotent; backups forced 0600; example file untouched; wrong owner/group rejected; symlink fails closed; missing file creates no replacement; output never contains a fixture secret; plus deploy/rollback/ENT-11/gitignore contract). Green: Deploy dir 38 passed/109 assertions; `Ent11DeploymentRollbackAutomationTest` 12 passed/115; `Ent10|Ent12|Ent15|SafeCiRuntimeControl` 54 passed/519; pint + `git diff --check` clean; `bash -n` ×3 + `shellcheck -S warning` clean; CICD-CTRL-1 classifier → `unknown_high_risk` (all gates run). Deploy: **no migration, no seed**; post-deploy verify with `bash scripts/harden-secret-permissions.sh verify --owner root --group www-data`.

## INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 — Dedicated Runtime Identity & Co-Tenant Isolation (2026-08-15)

Branch `feature/infra-sec-runtime-1-dedicated-runtime-identity-co-tenant-isolation` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `19d18a41` = the INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 GO SHA, which was also the remote tip and the VPS HEAD; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/infra-sec-runtime-1-dedicated-runtime-identity-co-tenant-isolation.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/dedicated-runtime-identity-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-dedicated-runtime-identity-co-tenant-isolation.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (SECURITY_FIX). **No migration, no permission, no route, no product runtime change** — deploy/host automation only. **INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 remains CLOSED / IMMUTABLE / GO**; this sprint closes the residual it explicitly declined to claim, and re-asserts every ENV-1 invariant (including across a rollback).

**Production finding (srv1730088, Ubuntu 24.04.4, measured not assumed):** DaengtisiaMS ran on the **distribution default `[www]` pool** (PHP 8.3) as `www-data` uid 33; the co-tenant **aish-pos** ran its own pool (PHP 8.5, own socket) *also* as uid 33; the queue worker and `daengtisiams-pilot-snapshot.service` were `www-data` too. Same uid ⇒ a file mode cannot stop the co-tenant reading the secret or `storage/app/private` (lab evidence, patient documents, legacy imports, KTP scan staging). `daengtisiams` user/group did not exist; no ACLs anywhere (`acl` package not installed, no mode string carries `+`). Also found: **`aish-pos` currently has NO nginx server block** (`nginx -T` shows only the DaengtisiaMS site, `conf.d/` empty, no app dir, zero Docker containers) — its pool runs but nothing routes to it; left untouched, and still a live same-uid execution surface. Two old `backups/*.sql` at the app root were `0644 root:root` (world-readable clinical dumps) — hardened to `0640 root:root`.

**Second, independent defect (the one the prompt predicted):** `scripts/deploy-vps.sh` resolved its runtime user with `grep -r '^user =' /etc/php/*/fpm/pool.d/ | head -1`, then a process-table guess, then `[ -n "$RUNTIME_USER" ] || RUNTIME_USER="www-data"`. The glob spans **every PHP version incl. the co-tenant's 8.5 pool**, so `head -1` is a directory-walk race — once DaengtisiaMS got a dedicated pool this could resolve the **co-tenant's** identity and chown DaengtisiaMS's runtime to it. All three mechanisms guess; a guess that lands on a shared account is the vulnerability itself.

**Built:** (1) `deploy/runtime-identity.conf` — the single shell-sourceable authority (`DMS_RUNTIME_USER=daengtisiams`, `DMS_FORBIDDEN_RUNTIME_USERS="root www-data nobody daemon postgres"`, pool/socket/units/writable/immutable/private path sets, `DMS_SECRET_OWNER=root`); no secrets, committed. Resolution is **fail closed, no fallback** (`exit 2` on missing authority / undeclared / forbidden identity / missing account / primary-group mismatch). (2) `deploy/php-fpm/daengtisiams.conf` — dedicated pool on `/run/php/php8.3-fpm-daengtisiams.sock`; pm sizing at parity with the `[www]` pool it replaces and **no `php_admin_value` limit declared** so `php.ini` (`upload_max_filesize`/`post_max_size`, which the Legacy RME PDF path depends on) is inherited unchanged — identity changes, behaviour does not; `clear_env = yes`, `security.limit_extensions = .php`. (3) `scripts/provision-runtime-identity.sh` — idempotent, **dry-run by default**, `--apply`/`--rollback`; creates the system account (no password/home/login shell, refuses to co-opt a pre-existing interactive account), installs the pool, validates `php-fpm8.3 -t` **and** `nginx -t` before any reload, cuts over inside `php artisan down` with an EXIT trap that lifts maintenance even on failure, retires the default `[www]` pool, rewrites **only** `fastcgi_pass` in place (preserving the `listen 80 default_server` that keeps the app reachable on this shared host), moves ownership of the writable set, strips world bits from private storage, delegates the secret group to the **INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 helper** (never chmods a secret itself), installs the units, and rebuilds the cache **as** the runtime user. Never runs a DB command; never `chown -R` the checkout. (4) `scripts/verify-runtime-isolation.sh` — read-only verifier proving identity/least-privilege/pool/socket/nginx/units/secret/writable/source-immutability/private-storage plus a live `runuser` **co-tenant denial** proof; `--require-host` turns "could not inspect" into FAIL so production never reports GO on unseen evidence.

**Wiring:** `verify-runtime-isolation.sh` and `chown -R "${RUNTIME_USER}:${RUNTIME_GROUP}"` are **required markers** in `config/deployment_rollback.php` for BOTH deploy and rollback, so removing either fails `foundation:deployment-rollback-check` in CI and on the VPS — the durable protection, not a one-time chmod. **Contract evolution (deliberate, moved coherently):** the ENT-11 required literal `chown -R www-data:www-data` was **replaced** (it pinned the contract to the co-tenant-shared account); `config/enterprise_foundation_runtime_hardening.php` `queue_worker.service_user` www-data→`daengtisiams` + marker `User=daengtisiams`/`Group=daengtisiams`; `deploy/systemd/daengtisiams-queue-worker.service` User/Group→`daengtisiams`. **Sibling repins:** `PostEntFoundationRuntimeHardeningTest` (now asserts `User=daengtisiams` + `not User=www-data`) and `DeployRuntimePermissionsScriptTest` (its `toContain('php-fpm')` assertion encoded the pool-scan heuristic — repinned to explicit-authority + forbidden-identity). **Gotcha:** CLAUDE.md records that a prior sprint accidentally dropped that ENT-11 literal and failed CI; here it is changed on purpose with config + both scripts + tests together.

**Rollback:** `scripts/rollback-vps.sh` now resolves the identity **before** `git checkout` and stages the verifier + authority in a private 0700 temp dir, because the target ref may predate this sprint — rolling the *code* back can neither hand secret read back to `www-data` (its hardcoded `--group www-data` is gone) nor silently drop the isolation gate. `provision --rollback` restores the shared runtime for operational recovery only, **keeps the secret at 0640** (never 0644), and prints that the sprint is then **WATCH / NO-GO, never GO**.

**Accepted residuals (stated, not hidden):** nginx runs as `www-data` and must *connect* to the FastCGI socket — socket-connect ≠ secret read, but any uid-33 process can also reach the socket; closing that needs a per-application reverse-proxy identity (out of scope, does not re-open secret read). root can read everything (deploy authority unchanged).

**Verification is split and neither half is claimed as the other** — CI cannot reproduce `/etc/php`, systemd or a Unix account. Tests: new `tests/Feature/Deploy/RuntimeIdentityIsolationTest.php` (26 passed/140 assertions — contract assertions plus a functional negative matrix running the real verifier against synthetic fixtures in a private 0700 temp dir: pool user www-data, shared socket, wrong nginx binding, worker on the shared account, default pool still active, world-readable secret, world-readable private storage, forbidden/root declared identity, secret group mismatch, runtime-owned source — each must FAIL, not pass quietly). `tests/Feature/Deploy` 64 passed. Gates GO: `foundation:deployment-rollback-check`, `queue-worker-runtime-check`, `runtime-hardening-check`, `ent-1-4-audit-check`, `cicd-enterprise-gate-check`, `security-compliance-check`, `enterprise-closure-check`, `ci-runtime-control-check --strict`. `bash -n` all scripts + `shellcheck -S warning` clean.

**Deploy note:** no migration, no seed. Order matters — **pre-pull the merge SHA on the VPS first** (the runner executes `deploy-vps.sh` from disk while `git pull` rewrites it mid-run; known transition gotcha), then `bash scripts/provision-runtime-identity.sh` (dry run) → `--apply`, then `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start` **on the VPS** and wait for `exit=0` + `DEPLOY OK`. Post-deploy verify `bash scripts/verify-runtime-isolation.sh --require-host` and confirm `sudo -u www-data test -r .env` is **denied**.

**INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 SHIPPED + VPS PILOT DEPLOYED (2026-08-15):** PR #293 squash-merged as `acf1e224cbf54d4e201a20fcaae697dc55e01069`. Authoritative CI green on the exact candidate `f16ba9c` — all 6 required gates (CICD-CTRL Classifier, NSF-R012 Quality, NSF-R011 Critical 36m49s, CICD-CTRL Selective Module 39m16s, NSF-9 Release Safety+Smoke, NSF-10 Release Evidence); NSF-R011 Full Suite skipped per standing pattern. Annotated GO tag `infra-sec-runtime-1-dedicated-runtime-identity-co-tenant-isolation-go` (tag object `023dfa2b`) @ `acf1e224` — local/remote/VPS peeled SHA exact-match, `git describe --exact-match` confirmed on VPS HEAD. **Production sequence (order matters):** VPS pre-pulled the merge SHA FIRST (the runner executes `deploy-vps.sh` from disk while `git pull` rewrites it mid-run — the documented buffered-script gotcha), then `provision-runtime-identity.sh` dry-run → `--apply`, then `deploy-vps-runner.sh start` **on the VPS** → `exit=0` + `DEPLOY OK: 20260815-035524`. Deploy log proves the fix: `Runtime user: daengtisiams:daengtisiams (explicit, from deploy/runtime-identity.conf)`; `RUNTIME ISOLATION: GO 70 GO / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP`; `SECRET FILE PERMISSIONS: GO`; all 5 writable gates GO; `Nothing to migrate.`; smoke 7/7 GO; `secret-perm: applied 0640 to 198 database backup file(s)`. **Before/after on the live host, same command:** `sudo -u www-data test -r .env` CAN read → **cannot** read; same for `storage/app/private`, `legacy-rme-private`, `storage/app/backups`, `storage/logs`; `nobody`/`daemon`/`postgres` also denied; `daengtisiams` CAN read the secret + write storage/bootstrap, and CANNOT write artisan/composer.json/bootstrap/app.php/the 4 deploy-rollback-provision-verify scripts/the ENV-1 helper/the identity authority. Post-deploy: health `/login`+`/health/live`+`/health/ready`+`/health/lb` 200; guest `/dashboard`+`/rme/visits`+`/dev-console` 302 (no 500); env=pilot debug=off maintenance=OFF; queue worker active as `daengtisiams`, `queue:failed` empty; Legacy `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness --expect=off --strict` Decision GO (admission closed, no wave); 0 new Laravel log errors. Rollback proved by rehearsal (dry-run of the real `--rollback` path: restores `www.conf`, moves the dedicated pool aside, restores the REAL nginx backup provisioning created, validates `php-fpm -t`+`nginx -t`, writes www-data drop-ins, and re-hardens the secret to `root:www-data 0640` — **never 0644**, so INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 survives a rollback) — production was deliberately NOT taken back to the shared runtime, since that state is WATCH/NO-GO by definition. **`aish-pos` non-regression:** its PHP 8.5 pool is active as `www-data` on its own socket, untouched. `AISH_POS_HTTP_SMOKE = NOT_AVAILABLE` — this host has **no nginx server block for aish-pos** at all (`nginx -T` shows only the DaengtisiaMS site, `conf.d/` empty, no app directory, zero Docker containers), so no HTTP endpoint exists to probe; verification was at the FPM pool + socket level only. **Accepted residuals (stated, not hidden):** (1) nginx runs as `www-data` and must *connect* to the FastCGI socket — socket-connect is not secret-read (the request executes as `daengtisiams`), but any uid-33 process can reach the socket; closing it needs a per-application reverse-proxy identity, out of scope. (2) DB dumps end at `daengtisiams:daengtisiams 0640` — co-tenant **denied**, but the runtime *owner* can read them. The stricter "runtime → DB backup read DENIED" posture was **not** adopted because the ENT-7 developer console reads that backup inventory as the runtime and would degrade; revisit in a dedicated change. (3) root can read everything — deploy authority intentionally unchanged. **Test-infra gotcha (cost ~2h):** `pkill -f 'artisan test'` kills only the wrapper, leaving orphaned `vendor/pestphp/pest/bin/pest` workers running; four accumulated at 99.8% CPU and raced over `storage/release-evidence/latest/`, producing 3 bogus `release evidence capture` failures that pass cleanly in isolation (~210s each). Kill `-f 'pestphp/pest/bin/pest'`, and never diagnose a governance-evidence failure without first confirming only one Pest worker exists.

## DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 — Immutable Deployment Entrypoint & Self-Update Safety (2026-08-15)

Branch `feature/deploy-harden-1-immutable-deployment-entrypoint-self-update-safety` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 GO `acf1e224` / base tip `a3d1723`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/deploy-harden-1-immutable-deployment-entrypoint-self-update-safety.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/vps-deploy-rollback-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-immutable-deployment-entrypoint.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (INFRA_RELEASE). **No migration, no permission, no route, no application runtime change.**

**Root cause.** `scripts/deploy-vps.sh` was executed straight out of the working tree while, halfway through its own body, it ran `git checkout` + `git pull --ff-only`. bash reads a script **incrementally** and keeps a byte offset into the open file, so rewriting those bytes under a running interpreter is undefined behaviour. The same defect existed one indirection removed: after the pull the deploy re-read `bash scripts/harden-secret-permissions.sh` (INFRA-SEC-ENV-1) and `bash scripts/verify-runtime-isolation.sh` (INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1) out of the tree it had just rewritten — the two most security-critical gates in the chain reading post-mutation bytes. `rollback-vps.sh` was equally exposed; its `mktemp` staging of the verifier protected the verifier but left the rollback script itself mutable. Two releases were rescued with a manual pre-pull; that workaround is now removed as a prerequisite.

**Invariant locked: `RUNNING_DEPLOY_PROGRAM != MUTABLE_REPOSITORY_FILE`.**

**New execution model.** `deploy-vps-runner.sh start` (unchanged operator interface, VPS-only) → `deploy-vps.sh` hands over before mutating → **new `scripts/deploy-immutable-exec.sh`** (the trusted bootstrap): exclusive `flock` on `/run/daengtisiams-deploy/<role>.lock` → `git fetch` + **pin `TARGET_SHA`** → `git archive TARGET_SHA -- scripts deploy` into a per-run **0700 root-owned** dir under `/run` (fallback `/var/lock`) → overlay the **live** `deploy/runtime-identity.conf` → trust check (owner == deploy uid, restricted mode, no symlink) → run `<snapshot>/scripts/deploy-vps.sh` as a **child** (not `exec`, so the lock fd stays open and the EXIT trap survives) → trap purges the snapshot and **preserves the real exit code**. The deploy then fast-forwards with `git merge --ff-only "$TARGET_SHA"` and verifies `HEAD == TARGET_SHA` twice (after checkout, and as the last gate before `DEPLOY OK`). Post-mutation helpers are invoked as `bash "${DEPLOY_TOOLS_DIR}/scripts/..."`. **Pre-mutation bootstrap chain may still read the working tree** (nothing has mutated yet); everything at/after the first mutation runs from the snapshot.

**Rollback** gets the same lock/pin/snapshot treatment, resolves the operator tag to one exact `ROLLBACK_TARGET_SHA` before mutating, and takes its snapshot from **HEAD (current code)** — never from the older target — so INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 + INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 still run in their modern form. The old `RUNTIME_GUARD_DIR` mktemp staging is gone (superseded).

**Durable rules (mirror `.cursor/rules/92`):** running deploy program is never a mutable repo file · immutable trusted snapshot from a git object · full execution closure in the snapshot · exact-SHA pin before any mutation · moving remote never retargets a running deploy · `HEAD == TARGET_SHA` before `DEPLOY OK` · one deployment critical section at a time (second → exit **75** BUSY before backup/migration/checkout) · lock+snapshot on a root path, never `storage/`/`bootstrap/cache` · advisory lock is authoritative, a lock **file** on disk is not (never delete it to unstick a deploy) · trap cleanup guarded + exit code preserved · backup before migrate absolute · `migrate --force` only · dirty tracked checkout fails closed, never `git reset --hard` · runner runs **on the VPS only** · launcher start ≠ completion · **manual pre-pull is never a prerequisite (reintroducing one is NO-GO)** · production deploys merged authority only · scripts never print a secret.

**Governance.** `config/deployment_entrypoint.php` (all literals, config-not-code) + `App\Support\Deploy\DeploymentEntrypointScanner` + `App\Services\Foundation\DeploymentEntrypointGovernanceService` (**DH1-R001..R012**, informational `deployment_entrypoint_governance` section — NOT in the blocking combined decision; blocking enforcement is the deploy script's own fail-closed bootstrap + the pre-deploy gate) + `php artisan foundation:deployment-entrypoint-check` (`--json`/`--strict`). Registered in `release_safety.required_pre_deploy_gates`, `config/foundation_governance.php`, the CI workflow, `scripts/ci/foundation-evidence-gates.sh`, `deploy-vps.sh` and `rollback-vps.sh`; optional evidence artifact `deployment-entrypoint-check.json` (ci+vps).

**Tests → CI.** `tests/Feature/Deploy/DeployHardenImmutableEntrypointTest.php` (20) + `DeployHardenConcurrencyLockTest.php` (8) — **`DeployHarden` token added to BOTH critical-filter occurrences** (GitHub-hosted + self-hosted) so the suite can never exist only locally. Behavioural tests run the **real** bootstrap against a synthetic git repo with its own lock dir: mid-run repository rewrite cannot change the executing program; pinned SHA beats a newer commit; snapshot trust-verified + removed; failing run keeps exit 42; second deploy exit 75 with no snapshot/pin; lock survives success/failure/SIGKILL; stale lock file does not block. **No production deploy is ever raced to test concurrency.** Repinned 3 existing assertions (`RuntimeIdentityIsolationTest` `RUNTIME_GUARD_DIR`→`DEPLOY_TOOLS_DIR`; `SecretFilePermissionHardeningTest` ×2 → quoted `harden-secret-permissions.sh" apply|verify` form).

**Deploy gotcha (one-time, this release only).** A host on the pre-DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 launcher has no bootstrap in its checkout and the new deploy script **fails closed** rather than falling back to the unsafe path. Transition: `git fetch` → `git archive <MERGE_SHA> -- scripts | tar -x -C /root/dh1-bootstrap` (0700, outside the checkout) → run the runner from there with `DEPLOY_SCRIPT` pointing at it. Mutates nothing in the working tree. `TRANSITIONAL_BOOTSTRAP=YES` for this release, `NO` afterwards — the canonical in-tree `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start` is then permanently safe.

**DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 shipped + VPS pilot deployed (2026-08-15):** PR #294 squash-merged as `b11bbbcffe41599f4a3f9999224f4c7503106bbf`. Exact-SHA CI green on candidate `5a092b5096c215d6492afef7ee7ccf79588f6548` (run `31869500182`: Classifier / NSF-R012 Quality / NSF-R011 Critical / CICD-CTRL Selective / NSF-9 / NSF-10 all success; NSF-R011 Full Suite skipped — PR events never run it, it fires on push-to-base; the second Critical row is the mutually-exclusive self-hosted variant). A first candidate `068f53ea` was also fully green but **superseded and deliberately not reused** after a real fix landed. GO tag `deploy-harden-1-immutable-deployment-entrypoint-self-update-safety-go` @ `b11bbbc`.

**Deployed in TWO runs, both `exit=0` + `DEPLOY OK`, both on the VPS.** Run 1 = the one-time transition (`TRANSITIONAL_BOOTSTRAP=YES`): the VPS sat at `acf1e224`, **two commits behind** the target — a genuine stale checkout, not a manufactured one — so `git archive <MERGE_SHA> -- scripts | tar -x -C /root/dh1-bootstrap` (0700 root:root, outside the checkout, working tree untouched) then `DEPLOY_SCRIPT=… bash /root/dh1-bootstrap/scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start`. Run 2 = the canonical in-tree `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start`, no override, no transition, **no pre-pull** — this is the one that proves the permanent architecture. `TRANSITIONAL_BOOTSTRAP=NO` from here on.

**Hash evidence covers both cases and is worth understanding.** Run 1: `SOURCE=619761a4…` ≠ `SNAPSHOT=46f26b6e…` because the tree still held the OLD script while the snapshot came from the target commit object — direct proof the payload is never read from the working tree. Run 2: `SOURCE == SNAPSHOT == 46f26b6e…` — the capture-time equality proof, plus `Already up to date.` for the idempotent same-SHA deploy. Both runs: `DEPLOY_LOCK_ACQUIRED=YES`, `DEPLOY_TARGET_PINNED=b11bbbc…`, `DEPLOY_SNAPSHOT_TRUSTED=PASS`, `DEPLOY_EXECUTION_SOURCE=/run/daengtisiams-deploy/deploy-<RUN_ID>/scripts/deploy-vps.sh`, `DEPLOY_HEAD_TARGET_MATCH=YES`, `DEPLOY_SNAPSHOT_CLEANED=YES`.

**Concurrency proven on the LIVE host, never by racing two migrations:** while run 2 held the lock, a second attempt gave `lock state: HELD`, `SECOND_DEPLOY_EXIT=75`, *"refusing to start a second deploy — no backup, no migration, no checkout was performed"*, with the challenger creating 0 snapshots, pinning 0 targets and taking 0 backups. **Rollback proven without downgrading production:** contract present on the deployed code; an unresolvable ref fails closed (`exit=2`, HEAD unchanged); the rollback-role mechanism ran end-to-end with its own `rollback.lock`, exact pin, trust PASS, matching hashes, snapshot cleaned, HEAD unchanged, 0 residue.

**Post-deploy production state:** `VPS_HEAD=b11bbbc` (clean tracked tree); INFRA-SEC-ENV-1 `.env` 640 root:daengtisiams + secret verify PASS; INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 `--require-host` **70 GO / 0 FAIL / 0 SKIP twice** (identical to the immutable baseline); php8.3-fpm + nginx + queue worker active, FPM pool as `daengtisiams`; `/login` `/health/live` `/health/ready` `/health/lb` all 200; `foundation:deployment-entrypoint-check --strict` GO; 0 active snapshots, deploy lock free, `/run/daengtisiams-deploy` `drwx------ root root`, 0 deploy processes; Legacy archive flag false, env pilot, debug OFF, maintenance OFF; no new Laravel errors, 0 failed jobs.

**Gotcha — evidence artifacts need TWO registrations.** Declaring `deployment-entrypoint-check.json` in `config/release_evidence.php` `optional_artifacts` is not enough; it must ALSO get a capture entry in `App\Services\Foundation\ReleaseEvidenceService`, or it can never be produced and becomes a permanently-missing artifact that pushes the profile to WATCH (the same wart that keeps 3 POST-ENT artifacts absent). NSF-10 does **not** catch this because the artifact is optional — only the local `Cache1`/`Dbperf1`/`Rpt1` Architecture tests, which assert `decision === 'GO'` for the **ci** profile, do. Fixed in `5a092b5`; ci profile now 30/30, 0 warnings, GO.

**Honest CI semantics (pre-existing, NOT this sprint).** The critical gate reports `1085 warnings (4012 assertions)` with `critical_test_exit_status=0` — every test is classified *warning*, not *passed*. Base commit `19d18a41`, before this branch existed, shows the same shape (`1030 warnings, 3737 assertions`, exit 0, 131 `file_get_contents` occurrences), so it is CICD-FIX-1's backlog item, not a DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 regression. It is NOT the Vite-manifest theory: there is no `public/build/manifest.json` locally either and the same tests pass locally. What this sprint claims is narrower and verified: both `DeployHarden` suites are named in the CI log, ran real assertions, and produced 0 failures / 0 errors. **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-23 by CICD-CRITICAL-GATE-FILE-GET-CONTENTS-WARN-1:** the cause is now evidenced — every one of those warnings is the framework's optional application-environment-file read failing during `LoadEnvironmentVariables`, because no CI job provisioned one. "The same tests pass locally" holds precisely because a developer machine HAS that file. CI now writes an empty one and the count is zero.**]**

## LEGACY-RME-DATE-TZ-1 — Canonical Clinical Timezone & Date-Boundary Correctness (2026-08-15)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-date-tz-1-canonical-clinical-timezone-date-boundary-correctness` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, BASE_SHA `a258586`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-date-tz-1-canonical-clinical-timezone-date-boundary.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/clinical-timezone-date-boundary-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-canonical-clinical-timezone-date-boundary.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (RUNTIME_FIX). **No migration, no data backfill, no permission, no route, no schema change; ledger/RME/inventory workflows untouched; KTP/NIK never rendered; Legacy archive stays OFF.**

**Root cause (verified, not inferred).** `config/app.php` hard-codes `'timezone' => 'UTC'` and never reads `APP_TIMEZONE`; the legacy archive declared its clinical calendar as `env('LEGACY_RME_CLINICAL_TIMEZONE', env('APP_TIMEZONE', 'UTC'))`; neither variable is set in production and `.env.example` carried no timezone key at all — so the clinical calendar silently resolved to **UTC**. Two services repeated the same inline fallback (`LegacyRmeDateRuleService::timezone()`, `LegacyRmeMigrationQuotaService::today()`). Because `Asia/Makassar` is UTC+08:00, `2026-08-13T16:00:00Z == 2026-08-14 00:00:00` in the clinic: for the eight hours between 16:00Z and midnight Z the two calendars name different days, so the rule `latest_rme_date < today` gave the **same document two different answers depending only on the hour of submission**. A second, quieter defect: `config/satusehat.php` already declared `clinic_timezone` defaulting to `Asia/Makassar`, so the codebase carried **two clinical-timezone authorities that disagreed in production**.

**Two semantic domains — permanently separate.** TECHNICAL INSTANT (`2026-08-13T16:00:00Z`; `created_at`/`updated_at`/queue/`sys_audit_logs`/deploy logs/telemetry) keeps the existing **UTC** architecture — `config/app.php` stays `'UTC'` **on purpose** and **no timestamp was converted**. CLINICAL CALENDAR DATE (`2026-08-14`) is `Asia/Makassar` and is read **only** through `App\Support\Clinical\ClinicalClock`. A UTC process default beside a WITA clinical calendar is the CORRECT posture, not a finding.

**Canonical authority (one literal, one reader).** New `config/clinical.php` → `'timezone' => env('CLINICAL_TIMEZONE', ClinicalTimezone::DEFAULT)`; new `App\Support\Clinical\ClinicalTimezone` (`DEFAULT = 'Asia/Makassar'` — the ONLY literal; `isValid()` matches the platform's IANA identifier list so `WITA`/`UTC+8`/`GMT+8`/`+08:00` and the real typo `Asia/Makasar` are all **rejected**, not silently accepted as a fixed offset); new `App\Support\Clinical\ClinicalClock` (`timezone()`/`today()`/`now()`/`toClinicalDate()`/`todayString()`/`inspect()`); new `InvalidClinicalTimezoneException`. **`config/legacy_rme.php` `dates.clinical_timezone` was REMOVED** (comment records why it must never come back) and `config/satusehat.php` `clinic_timezone` now derives its default from the same constant (effective value unchanged) — two config files that can disagree about what day it is are the defect, not the fix. `LegacyRmeDateRuleService` + `LegacyRmeMigrationQuotaService` inject the clock and their inline UTC fallbacks are gone; `LegacyRmeMigrationQuotaFactory` uses it so fixtures land in the bucket the gate reads.

**Fail closed.** An invalid/blank clinical timezone throws `InvalidClinicalTimezoneException` — there is **no UTC fallback**, because the silent fallback WAS the defect. `LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessService` gains a `clinical_timezone` check: **FAIL** on invalid (→ NO_GO), **WATCH** on valid-but-non-canonical, GO on canonical; `ClinicalClock::inspect()` is the non-throwing posture reader so a gate can report instead of crashing.

**Read-only production diagnostic.** `php artisan clinical:date-diagnose [--instant=<ISO8601>]... [--json] [--strict]` computes the clinical date for supplied instants. It writes **no row**, mutates **no system clock**, installs **no global test-now** (the instant is a pure calculation input local to the process), so the WITA midnight boundary can be proven on production without waiting for 16:00 UTC or fabricating a clinical document.

**Durable rules (mirror rule 92).** (1) Canonical clinical calendar timezone is `Asia/Makassar`, IANA identifier only. (2) Technical/audit timestamps keep their instant-storage semantics (UTC) — never mass-convert them. (3) Clinical date and technical timestamp are different domains. (4) Historical DATE values (`selected_rme_date`, `latest_rme_date`, `rme_date`, `earliest_native_rme_date`, `visit_date`) are **never timezone-shifted** — timezone derives *today* from an instant, it never mutates a stored date. (5) `selected_rme_date` = earliest, `latest_rme_date` = latest human-confirmed date; OCR is never a date authority. (6) Clinical "today" comes only from `ClinicalClock` — never `Carbon::today()`, `now()`, `date_default_timezone_get()`, php.ini, OS/CI-runner zone, or `config('app.timezone')`. (7) Never from a browser/device/request/header/cookie/query value. (8) Invalid timezone fails closed. (9) Upload eligibility and publish revalidation share ONE clock (publish re-enters `evaluate()`); real time may advance the clinical day between them — one clock moving, never two disagreeing. (10) `latest_rme_date == clinicalToday` is **not yet historical** (strict `<`; never relax to `<=`). (11) Native cutoff stays DATE-vs-DATE and timezone-invariant. (12) `NO_NATIVE_REFERENCE` stays valid — never fabricate a ClinicVisit/native RME to create an anchor. (13) DB `CURRENT_DATE`/`NOW()` must not decide clinical eligibility. (14) No per-branch timezone architecture without a separate product decision. (15) Tests must restore the clock (`Carbon::setTestNow()`), never leak a frozen instant. (16) Production timezone verification is read-only — never change the VPS clock, never fabricate clinical data.

**Data-impact assessment: DECISION-TIME defect, NOT a persisted-data defect — no backfill, none escalated.** Every legacy/visit clinical date is a `date` cast and `LegacyRmeDateRuleService::normalize()` already reduced every input to `toDateString()` before comparing, so nothing was ever stored through a timezone conversion. The only clock-dependent rule is the `< today` age gate, evaluated at decision time and never written. The UTC clock is strictly *behind* the clinical clock, so the defect could only cause a **wrong refusal** of a genuinely historical document during the 16:00–24:00 UTC window — it could never admit a document that should have been refused, and never wrote a wrong date.

**Tests: 35 new.** `tests/Feature/Clinical/ClinicalClockGovernanceTest.php` (17) + `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeClinicalDateBoundaryTest.php` (18) — canonical timezone, IANA-only, fail-closed invalid/blank, `inspect()` non-throwing, process-default-UTC independence, 23:59:59/00:00:00/00:00:01 WITA, month/year/leap-day rollovers, `latest == clinicalToday` refused, `latest < clinicalToday` passes, upload/publish one clock, legitimate clock advance, native cutoff invariant, `NO_NATIVE_REFERENCE` preserved, stored DATE never shifts, single/multi-date semantics preserved, birth-date rule invariant, quota day == clinical day, request/header/cookie/query timezone cannot override, readiness GO+FAIL paths, evaluation persists nothing. **CI mapping is explicit:** `LegacyRme` was already in the NSF-R011 critical filter; **`ClinicalClock` was ADDED to both critical-gate variants** so the new foundational suite maps to a real authoritative path rather than repeating the earlier "suite exists locally, matches no filter" defect.

**Deploy:** no migration/seed. Run `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start` **ON the VPS** (DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 pins the SHA and runs from an immutable snapshot — **no manual pre-pull**); complete only at `exit=0` **and** `DEPLOY OK`. Post-deploy verify `clinical:date-diagnose --strict`, the two boundary instants, and `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness --expect=off --strict`.

## DEVFLOW-FIX-BASE-REF-1 — Canonical Remote Base Resolution (2026-08-15)

Branch `feature/devflow-fix-base-ref-1-canonical-remote-base-resolution` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, canonical base at start `b8038c567360b323bc56a185cd518ed1c4b41a28`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/devflow-fix-base-ref-1-canonical-remote-base-resolution.md`; runbook `docs/runbooks/devflow-canonical-base-resolution-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-devflow-canonical-base-resolution.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (RUNTIME_FIX). **Governance/DEVFLOW tooling only — no migration, no route, no permission, no seeder, no product behaviour change.** Clinical timezone stays `Asia/Makassar`; Legacy migration capability OFF / admission EMPTY / active wave NONE; INFRA-SEC-ENV-1, INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 and DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 untouched; all historical GO tags immutable.

**Root cause (reproduced live, not inferred).** `GitChangeInspector::changedFiles()` diffed against a bare base **branch name**, which git resolves to the LOCAL `refs/heads/<branch>`. At sprint start the local base was `a3d1723` while the canonical remote was `b8038c5` — three commits behind. A stale/ahead/diverged local ref silently changed the scope audit, the manifest-vs-diff contradiction checks and the diff fed to security review, which is how earlier workstreams got a ~14.7 MB unrelated-checkout diff, a Graphify pre-state from another branch, and false "candidate regression" / "pre-existing failure" conclusions. Three paths, one defect class: **stale local**, **wrong checkout**, **base moving mid-run**.

**Canonical authority model (permanent).** `1. explicit verified exact SHA → 2. origin/<canonical-base> after fetch → 3. FAIL CLOSED.` There is **no** step 4: never `refs/heads/<base>`, `main`, `master`, `HEAD`, `HEAD~1`, or the latest tag. **Branch name = discovery authority; exact SHA = comparison authority.** A fetch/network/auth failure is an error, never a governance PASS over stale data.

**Delivered.** New `App\Support\Devflow\CanonicalBaseRefResolver` (the ONLY place a base is resolved) + immutable `CanonicalBaseRef` value object (pinned per invocation — a remote advancing mid-run cannot change that run; a new invocation may resolve a newer base). New `php artisan devflow:base-ref-check` (`--json`/`--strict`/`--base-sha`/`--base-branch`). New `config/devflow.php` `base_resolution` block (explicit `remote`, fetch toggle/timeout, `explicit_sha_env`, exact-SHA + safe-branch patterns, declared `forbidden_fallbacks`). `GitChangeInspector` consumes the resolver and returns `base_ref` metadata; **an unresolvable base surfaces as UNRESOLVED, never as an empty change set** (an empty set passes every contradiction check and silently fakes a GO). `ResolvesSprintContext` resolves once per command and exposes `--base-sha`/`--base-branch` + `reportBaseAuthority()`; `sprint:manifest-check` and `sprint:scope-audit` **fail closed (NO-GO)** on an unresolved base and print `BASE_SOURCE`/`BASE_BRANCH`/`BASE_SHA`/`HEAD_SHA`(+`MERGE_BASE_SHA`/`LOCAL_BASE_STALE`). `scripts/ci/resolve-gates.sh` gains `--base-sha`, validates refs, pins an exact commit before diffing, and publishes `base_source`/`base_sha`/`head_sha` in kv + JSON + stderr summary. CI `classify` now uses the **immutable** `github.event.pull_request.base.sha` instead of the moving `origin/${{ github.base_ref }}`, publishes the authority as job outputs + step summary. `DevflowScanner::baseResolutionPosture()` + `DEVFLOW-BASE-AUTHORITY` governance check; rules **DEVFLOW-R011..R020**.

**Two pre-existing fail-opens found by RUNNING the new tooling (fixed here — same function).** (a) `lines()` trimmed each `git status --porcelain` line **before** `substr($line, 3)`, so every MODIFIED tracked path shifted left one char (`app/Policies/X.php` → `pp/Policies/X.php`, `database/migrations/…` → `atabase/migrations/…`) and stopped matching the `security_impact`/`schema_change` contradiction patterns. (b) Default porcelain collapses untracked files in a brand-new directory to `newdir/`, so a new module folder of `.js` or migration files could never match an extension/filename pattern. Now parsed with `-z -uall` + `core.quotePath=false`, sliced at the exact offset, both sides of a rename recorded.

**Gotcha (cost one debugging cycle).** `git rev-parse --verify -- <rev>` is **wrong**: for `rev-parse`, `--` marks the start of *paths*, so it asks about a file named `<rev>` and always fails — the first classifier implementation had this and every happy path fell through to `unknown_high_risk`. Option injection at that call site is blocked by **validation**, not by `--`. `git diff` *does* take a trailing `--`.

**CI mapping is explicit:** `Cicd` already reached `tests/Feature/Cicd/CiClassifierBaseAuthorityTest.php` (verified empirically, not assumed); **`Devflow` was ADDED to both critical-gate variants** so `DevflowCanonicalBaseRefTest` maps to a real authoritative path rather than repeating the earlier "suite exists locally, matches no filter" defect. Tests: `tests/Feature/Foundation/DevflowCanonicalBaseRefTest.php` (41) + `tests/Feature/Cicd/CiClassifierBaseAuthorityTest.php` (22) — stale/ahead/diverged local, explicit exact SHA, remote unavailable, 11 invalid-SHA variants, missing object, non-commit (tree), 8 unsafe-branch variants, absent/ambiguous remote, mid-run remote movement, wrong-primary-checkout isolation, non-repo path, stale-base-would-widen-diff, unresolved-not-empty, porcelain modified/staged/untracked/renamed/spaced paths, 7 hostile CI ref values, docs_only invariant preserved, deterministic pinned classification.

**Deploy:** no migration/seed. Run `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start` **ON the VPS** (DEPLOY-HARDEN-1 pins the SHA and runs from an immutable snapshot — **no manual pre-pull**); complete only at `exit=0` **and** `DEPLOY OK`. Post-deploy verify `devflow:base-ref-check --strict`, `foundation:devflow-check --strict`, `sprint:manifest-check`, `sprint:scope-audit`, and `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness --expect=off --strict`.


## LEGACY-RME-PDF-ROLL-4-WAVE-2 — Controlled Production Migration Wave-2 (2026-08-16)

**Status: COMPLETE — `WAVE-2R` EXECUTED, RECONCILED, CLOSED. 4 GENUINE DOCUMENTS PUBLISHED. GO.** Doc: `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-pdf-roll-4-wave-2-controlled-production-migration.md`. Four attempts: (1) WATCH no candidates/approval; (2) WATCH one human held both maker+checker; (3) `WAVE-2` **WATCH/NO-GO** — 5 accepted, 0 published, every import filed by `u1` instead of approved maker `u7`, wave `CANCELLED`, all imports withdrawn (evidence PR #299 → `dfbbe5b`); (4) **`WAVE-2R` GO** — 4 accepted, **4 published**, maker `u7` → checker `u11`. **Approval-UI defect fixed first (PR #298 `f28df89` → `25dc2b9`, deployed exit=0 + DEPLOY OK):** the approve form was nested inside the wave view's `@if ($canManage)` block, so the designated approver — who by design holds no manage permission — could never reach it, and separation of duties was satisfiable only by a CLI actor impersonating them; now its own card **outside** the manage block gated on the POLICY (`@can('approve', $wave)`), with regression test `LegacyRmeWaveApprovalUiReachabilityTest` **verified to fail without the fix**. Also fixed a pre-existing timezone bug (`LegacyRmePdfValidationTest > refuses a legacy date of today` derived "today" from `now()`/UTC not `ClinicalClock`; failed ~8h daily — proven pre-existing on the pristine base). **WAVE-2R:** approval `ROLL-4-WAVE-2-RETRY-OWNER-APPROVAL-2026-08-16` (LDK2, DESIGNATED=5/CEILING=4/VALID=4 — governance ties the ceiling to nothing but `max_declarable_daily`, so 4 was used); `created_by=1 approved_by=11` (Jene Monika, Supervisor RME) approved **through the UI**; imports 12–15 → records 7–10, all `uploaded_by=7` (Yuni FO) / `published_by=11` → **SEPARATED on every record**, branch **derived** from Nomor RM, `FROZEN_HASH_MATCH=5/5`. **Reconciliation recomputed by the app under lock:** `accepted 4 published 4 cancelled 0 failed_unresolved 0 in_flight 0 unexplained 0 quota_consumed 4 quota_drift 0 published_records 4 void_records 0 balanced true blockers []`; **zero native/billing/lab/SATUSEHAT delta** (visits 27, MRs 27, odontograms 27, invoices 19, payments 27, lab 13, satusehat 1 — all unchanged); legacy archive 6 → 10 records (9 PUBLISHED + 1 pre-existing VOID). **W2-003 (RM 27541) remains an explained `PATIENT_NOT_FOUND` rejection — not substituted**; the one-digit-away `DG-LDK2-2026-22541` (patient 43) was never matched and no patient was created; its hash appears in **zero** live imports. **History/doctor scope:** 1 published record per patient; all four patients have 0 visits + 0 MRs so no treating relationship exists → `POSITIVE_DOCTOR_READ=0` (correctly N/A, **not faked**), `NEGATIVE_SCOPE_PROOF=4`. **Honest limits:** `LIVE_QUOTA_EXHAUSTION` reached exactly 4/4 but refusal-on-overflow **not exercised live**; `PAUSE_RESUME_LIVE` exercised only in the aborted attempt 3. Storage measured 13,372,432 → 26,456,198 B (**13,083,766 B / 4 docs**, render-dominated). **Order of operations** (capability opened/closed 3× so a write capability never waited on an unscheduled human): old imports cancelled by u7 → reverified at rest → config binding → register → approval-only window → u11 approves → window closed → activate/assign/admit **with capability OFF** → capability ON as final gate → u7 files → **maker-identity hard gate before review** → u11 reviews+publishes → drain → branch complete → wave complete → OFF/EMPTY/NONE. Both stop-conditions tested not assumed: closing the capability window does **not** invalidate an approval (persisted DB state), and activate/assign/admit run correctly with capability OFF. **Final state:** capability OFF, admission EMPTY, active wave NONE, wave+branch `COMPLETED`, backlog zero, jobs/failed 0, staged sources removed, rollout-readiness `--expect=off --strict` GO, deployment-entrypoint GO, runtime isolation 70 GO/0 FAIL/0 SKIP, secret `0640 root:daengtisiams`, clinical `Asia/Makassar`, health 200×4, `VPS_HEAD=25dc2b9`. **Durable rules:** a permission no reachable UI exposes is not a control (assert reachability for the owning role); verify `uploaded_by` against the approval **before** review — assignment is not participation; a cancelled import still consumes quota (size waves with correction headroom); `complete` is refused while a branch is unfinished and `cancel` is the honest terminal state; ~~import lifecycle actions are UI-only (no CLI — report the gap, never SQL/Tinker)~~ **— SUPERSEDED by LEGACY-RME-OPS-CLI-1, which closed exactly this gap: `legacy-rme:import-admin` is now the canonical SSH path; SQL/Tinker remains forbidden**; "Cabang belum masuk gelombang migrasi" is the admission gate refusing, not a permission fault.

A Wave-3 would have repeated an already-proven workflow while consuming a fresh owner approval and reopening a write capability over live clinical data — and would have left untouched the one thing Wave-2 actually exposed: the **operational lifecycle gap** (an aborted wave could not be withdrawn over SSH).
