## LEGACY-RME-OPS-CLI-1 — Canonical Import Lifecycle CLI & Abort-Recovery Operations (2026-08-16)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-ops-cli-1-canonical-import-lifecycle-cli-abort-recovery` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `eb96dae`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-ops-cli-1-canonical-import-lifecycle-cli.md`; runbook §16 of `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-migration-operations-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/101-legacy-rme-import-lifecycle-cli.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **No migration, no new permission, no new route, no seeder/role change, no schema change; the observable HTTP contract is unchanged (404 out-of-scope, 403 refused, field errors on service refusal).**

**Why:** Wave-2 was opened against live clinical data and aborted, and the operator could not canonically withdraw or progress the staged imports over SSH — `cancel`/`review`/`publish`/`retry` existed **only** as controller methods. The alternatives an operator reaches for (direct `UPDATE`, Tinker `->update(['status'=>…])`, hand-edited `published_by`, manual quota adjustment) bypass the transition map, branch scope, policy, quota semantics **and** audit trail at once.

**Architecture — one business path, two adapters.** New `LegacyRmeImportLifecycleService` is the ONLY implementation of the gates; **both** `LegacyRmeImportController` and the new `legacy-rme:import-admin` command enter through it, and it delegates every state change to the unchanged canonical services (`LegacyRmeImportProcessingService` retry/cancel, `LegacyRmePublishService` review/publish). The controller no longer calls `$this->processing->`/`$this->publishing->` at all — **asserted structurally by a test**, so a future direct call fails CI. Gate order, each failing closed: **(1)** feature flag → `FEATURE_DISABLED` (a recovery tool that worked while the emergency stop was pressed would BE the bypass); **(2)** branch scope via `LegacyRmeWorkspaceScope` → `IMPORT_NOT_IN_SCOPE` (fires BEFORE permission on purpose — "not found" and "not yours" are deliberately the same answer so id probing cannot reveal another branch's staged documents); **(3)** the named permission the HTTP **route** declares as middleware → `PERMISSION_DENIED`; **(4)** `LegacyRmeImportPolicy` → `POLICY_DENIED`; **(5)** separation of duties → `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES`; **(6)** canonical service → `SERVICE_REFUSED`. New Support types: `LegacyRmeLifecycleAction` (closed vocabulary + the route-permission table both surfaces read), `LegacyRmeLifecycleRefusal` (stable machine-readable codes), `LegacyRmeLifecycleOutcome` (PII-minimized result), `LegacyRmeImportNotInScope` (**extends `NotFoundHttpException`** so the browser's long-standing 404 is byte-identical while the CLI reports a branchable code), `LegacyRmeLifecycleDenied` (extends `AuthorizationException`, still 403, but remembers WHICH gate refused), `LegacyRmeCommandRefusal`.

**CLI:** `php artisan legacy-rme:import-admin {cancel|review|publish|retry} --import=<id> --actor=<id|email> [--title= --description=] [--apply] [--json]`. **Safe by default three ways:** one import per invocation (**no `--all`/`--branch`/`--wave`/`--force`** — asserted by a test); **dry run unless `--apply`** (zero row/audit/job delta, asserted by counting); an **explicit, real, ACTIVE, non-soft-deleted** `--actor` (the Linux user, the SSH login and root are never an application identity). Exit `0` only when the requested outcome was reached or a dry run found the action eligible; **every refusal exits non-zero**, including an ineligible dry run.

**Gotchas found by running, not reading.** (1) **`LegacyRmeAuditService` had to become a container singleton** (`RepositoryServiceProvider::register`) — the channel tag is set by the lifecycle service but audit rows are written by the canonical services from their **own injected copy**, so without a shared instance every row silently lost the tag; state is scoped by `withChannel()` and restored in a `finally` so a long-lived queue worker cannot inherit a stale value. (2) **`Exception::$code` cannot be redeclared readonly** — the refusal types carry `refusalCode`. (3) **A dry run must consult the transition map DIRECTLY**, not just the policy: a Super Admin's global `Gate::before` makes every policy answer yes, so the policy predicts nothing for the account most likely to be running a 2am recovery; `TRANSITION_NOT_ALLOWED` exists solely so `preview()` cannot lie, and `perform()` deliberately does NOT duplicate it (the canonical services own that rule). (4) **A Kasir is refused by the SCOPE gate, not the permission gate** — and tests must pin `users.branch_id` explicitly, because `BranchContext` otherwise falls back to the first active RME-enabled branch and can coincidentally BE the import's branch, passing for the wrong reason. (5) Test fixtures needed **per-call-unique PDF bytes** (vary the page width) — the shared helper emits byte-identical PDFs and the archive correctly refuses an identical checksum staged against a different patient.

**Semantics preserved verbatim (each pinned by a test):** cancelling **does NOT refund quota** (the bucket is a reservation spent at intake; a refund would let an operator loop `upload → cancel → upload` past the approved ceiling); **review is a real gate** — an unreviewed import cannot be published, by the policy for a checker AND by the canonical service for a Super Admin whose `Gate::before` bypasses the policy (both paths tested); **publish is idempotent, never duplicating** (a repeat returns the existing record with `changed:false`; `UNIQUE(source_import_id)` is the DB backstop); **retry means requeue, not reset** (`PUBLISHED`/`CANCELLED` terminal and refused; a retry whose source PDF is gone is refused with a reason); **no downstream state ever** — no `ClinicVisit`/`MedicalRecord`/odontogram/invoice/payment/`LabOrder`/SATUSEHAT candidate from any action on either surface, asserted per action including refusal paths.

**Separation of duties — recorded honestly.** `imported_by != published_by` was **NOT** enforced in code before this sprint. What actually enforces maker/checker is the **ROLE SPLIT** (maker holds `create_legacy_rme_imports`, denied `review`/`publish`; checker holds `review`+`publish`, denied `create`) — unchanged and still load-bearing. New `legacy_rme_operations.require_separate_publisher` (env `LEGACY_RME_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_PUBLISHER`) adds the account-level check, **default OFF**, mirroring `require_separate_approver` including its "turn on once two staffed accounts exist" posture; it lives in the **shared** lifecycle service so browser and CLI tighten together — deliberately no way to have it on one surface only (tested on both). It closes the one hole the role split cannot: a Super Admin holding both duties. **Recommended owner follow-up: switch it on.** Human attestation is never synthesized — the CLI lets an authorized person review/publish over SSH, never on their behalf.

**Audit:** reuses the existing `sys_audit_logs` trail (no new table, no parallel system, no second event invented for the CLI); every lifecycle row gains structure-only `channel = HTTP|CLI` (added to `ALLOWED_METADATA_KEYS`). Audit payloads and every **structured** `--json` field stay free of patient name/KTP/NIK/medical-record-number/filename/absolute path/clinical content — asserted; the source document appears only as a 12-char checksum prefix. **One stated exception:** `refusal_message` is the canonical service's operator-facing sentence verbatim and may quote the clinical dates a refusal is about (one date rule names the patient's date of birth) — it reaches only a caller who already passed every gate for that import, i.e. the same audience the browser shows the same sentence to; name/RM/KTP still never appear (asserted).

**Security-review findings, both fixed in-sprint.** (1) **A FormRequest constrains one caller, not the capability** — the archive labels were bounded only by `PublishLegacyRmeImportRequest` (title 150 / description 2000); `normalizeTitle()` self-capped but `normalizeDescription()` did not, so the CLI could write a description the browser rejects. Closed on both sides: the command refuses `INVALID_ARCHIVE_LABEL` at the same bounds AND `normalizeDescription()` now bounds itself (unreachable from HTTP, which validates first). (2) The `--json` PII claim was overstated; corrected to the precise boundary above rather than left as an unqualified "structure only".

**Forbidden operational recovery (durable):** `UPDATE`/`DELETE` on `stg_rme_legacy_imports`/`trx_rme_legacy_records`, Tinker status edits, hand-editing `uploaded_by`/`published_by`/`reviewed_by`/`cancelled_by`, hand-adjusting a quota bucket, `migrate:fresh`, `db:wipe`. The CLI exists specifically to remove the temptation.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeImportLifecycleCliTest.php` (37) + `LegacyRmeImportLifecycleParityTest.php` (25); `tests/Feature/LegacyRme` **662 passed / 5 skipped (Poppler) / 0 failed** (600 baseline + 62 new).

## LEGACY-RME-SOD-1 — Mandatory Separate Publisher Enforcement & Production Activation (2026-08-16)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-sod-1-mandatory-separate-publisher-enforcement-production-activation` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ OPS-CLI-1 GO `3cf57ea`; do NOT target main). Doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-sod-1-mandatory-separate-publisher-enforcement.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/102-legacy-rme-separation-of-duties.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **No migration, no permission, no role/seeder, no route, no schema change; KTP/NIK never rendered; ledger untouched; `ROLL-4-WAVE-3` remains SKIPPED / NOT REQUIRED and no wave is created/admitted/approved.**

**The gap.** OPS-CLI-1 shipped `legacy_rme_operations.require_separate_publisher` **OFF** with "recommended owner follow-up: switch it on". Three things were unfinished, and only the first is obvious. (a) The switch was off, so the one account the ROLE SPLIT cannot constrain — a Super Admin, whose `Gate::before` bypass makes every policy answer yes — could file a document and certify it alone. (b) The check ran as gate 5 of `LegacyRmeImportLifecycleService::perform()`, i.e. **outside** the transaction: read the import, run five gates, then call `LegacyRmePublishService`, which opens the transaction and takes the row lock. `publish()`/`review()` are public, so any future caller reaching the service directly — job, seeder, recovery command, refactor — would have escaped SOD silently with no test noticing. (c) Only **publish** was covered, while reviewing is equally the CHECKER's duty (Wave-1 withheld BOTH `review` and `publish` from the maker), leaving "a human other than the filer looked at the pages" bypassable.

**One guard, consulted twice.** New `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Support\SeparatePublisherGuard` is the ONLY expression of the comparison and of the enablement switch. `LegacyRmeImportLifecycleService` gate 5 asks it (early, transaction-free refusal, both surfaces); `LegacyRmePublishService` **re-asserts it against the LOCKED row** inside both `reviewWithinTransaction()` and `publishWithinTransaction()` — placed BEFORE the idempotency/no-op shortcuts so the two enforcement points can never disagree. Structural tests pin it: `require_separate_publisher` may appear in exactly one file under `app/`, and neither `LegacyRmeImportController` nor `LegacyRmeImportAdminCommand` may reference `uploaded_by` at all.

**Scope decision (option B, evidence-based).** `GUARDED_ACTIONS = [review, publish]`. `cancel`/`retry` deliberately excluded — they carry the **intake** permission because they re-run the maker's own housekeeping. **Not retroactive:** publishing does NOT additionally demand `reviewed_by != uploaded_by`, because rows reviewed by their own uploader before activation would be stranded and rewriting their attribution is the history-editing this module forbids (pinned by a test). Pre-attribution rows (`uploaded_by IS NULL`) stay exempt.

**Config default flipped ON, fail-closed.** `(bool) env(..., false)` → `SeparatePublisherGuard::resolveEnabledFromEnv(env(...))`. The failure mode that matters is a **typo**, not a deliberate `false`: `env()` returns null for a misspelled key and `''` for a valueless key, and PHP casts both to false, so `LEGACY_RME_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_PUBLISHR=true` would have silently disabled a production safety invariant. Now unset / empty / whitespace / misspelled / unparseable → **ENABLED**; only `false`/`FALSE`/`0`/`off`/`no` disable. Runtime reads `config()`, never `env()`. Production also sets the line explicitly. **Compatibility was measured, not assumed:** `LegacyRmeImportFactory` leaves `uploaded_by` NULL and the shared `superAdmin()` helper mints a fresh account per call, so the entire pre-existing LegacyRme suite is green with the rule ON by default.

**Account SOD ≠ human SOD — never blurred in evidence.** The server enforces `imported_by_user_id != acting_user_id` and proves two ACCOUNTS touched the document. It CANNOT prove two HUMANS hold them; no identity mechanism here establishes that. Two people sharing one login therefore cannot satisfy the rule (desirable — shared accounts destroy accountability); one person with two logins can, which is why controlled migration operations still require genuinely separate human operators as an operational governance control with a human attestation.

**Refusal contract.** Browser → `ValidationException` on field `actor`; CLI → classified `SEPARATION_OF_DUTIES` (non-zero exit) from that same field, dry run reports `eligible:false` + the blocker; direct service call → in-transaction `LegacyRmePublishRefusal` → ValidationException. New stable codes `LegacyRmePdfFailure::SEPARATE_PUBLISHER_REQUIRED` / `SEPARATE_REVIEWER_REQUIRED`. **No new audit event invented:** early authorization refusals stay unaudited exactly as `PERMISSION_DENIED`/`POLICY_DENIED` do; a refusal that reaches the canonical service is recorded once as `LEGACY_RME_PUBLISH_REJECTED` with the failure code and no PII. UI (`settings/rme/legacy-imports/show.blade.php`) shows a "Pemisahan tugas (maker/checker)" panel and hides the doomed buttons — flags computed in the controller, never in Blade; the POST is still refused twice.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeSeparationOfDutiesTest.php` (44) incl. the Super-Admin-same-actor negative, HTTP/CLI/direct-service parity, the fail-closed env matrix, a TOCTOU proof (stale in-memory copy says exempt, locked row says violated → refused), zero-side-effect and idempotency deltas, and the two structural tests.

## LEGACY-RME-MASTERDATA-1 — RM 27541 Patient Resolution Investigation & Master-Data Integrity (2026-08-16)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-masterdata-1-rm-27541-patient-resolution-integrity` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline SOD-1 GO `9c803fe`; do NOT target main). Doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-masterdata-1-rm-27541-patient-resolution-integrity.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/90-legacy-rme-pdf-archive.mdc` §53–64; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **`FINAL_CLASSIFICATION = DOCUMENT_NOT_ELIGIBLE` (owner determination).** ROLL-4-WAVE-2 stays CLOSED/IMMUTABLE; ROLL-4-WAVE-3 stays SKIPPED/NOT REQUIRED; no wave opened, no import created, no record published, no patient created/edited/merged. **No migration, no permission, no role/seeder change, no route, no schema change; capability OFF, admission EMPTY, active wave NONE; SOD `LEGACY_RME_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_PUBLISHER=true` preserved.**

**Investigation (read-only, `srv1730088` `pilot`).** Source re-verified before interpretation: `RM Landak 3.pdf`, recorded == recomputed SHA-256 `96c78cc0…`, 288754 bytes, still on `legacy_rme_private` → `SOURCE_HASH_MATCH=YES`; raw RM `27541` confirmed by the Wave-2 400 DPI re-render + the owner's independent reading (**not** re-rendered again — the digit was already settled by a human, and re-rendering only makes another copy of a real patient's page). **`27541` exists nowhere:** 0 exact / 0 suffix / 0 substring across all 43 `mst_patients` rows (21 live + 22 soft-deleted); 0 hits across the 31 `stg_legacy_patient_imports` rows scanned on `raw_payload`+`normalized_payload`+`manual_rm_number`+`generated_medical_record_number`+`legacy_patient_id`; 0 hits in 447 `sys_audit_logs` rows. **`ROOT_CAUSE` = the patient never existed; the resolver returned nothing because there is nothing — `CODE_DEFECT_IN_RESOLUTION = NONE`.** The one-digit-away `DG-LDK2-2026-22541` (patient 43) was never matched and is not matchable: no fuzzy/edit-distance path exists anywhere in the chain. `SOURCE_RM_ERROR_CONFIRMED` was NOT available (no register/amendment/ledger contradicts `27541`; a one-digit resemblance is not evidence). **`RM_27541_FUTURE_MIGRATION = NOT_ELIGIBLE`** — reopening needs new authoritative external evidence + a fresh migration approval, never Wave-2.

**Shipped (closes the ad-hoc-SQL gap for the identity question, the OPS-CLI-1 precedent).** `php artisan legacy-rme:patient-resolution-audit [--rm=] [--json] [--strict]` → new read-only `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Services\LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditService` + `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Support\LegacyRmePatientResolution` (stable codes `EXACT_UNIQUE`/`EXACT_AMBIGUOUS`/`SEGMENT_UNIQUE`/`SEGMENT_AMBIGUOUS`/`NOT_FOUND`/`TOO_SHORT`/`EMPTY_INPUT`; `BINDABLE` is narrower than `RESOLVED` — ambiguity is resolved but never bindable) + `LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditCommand`. **Identity is exact:** a match requires the stored Nomor RM to equal the input, or the input to equal a **whole manual segment** — matching the segment rather than a raw `LIKE '%…'` is what stops `2541` swallowing `22541`; rows rejected for that reason are still shown under `suffix_crossed_manual_segment` so the operator sees why. Near misses (same length, edit distance 1 on the manual segment) are computed deliberately but returned under `investigative_signal` stamped `bindable=false`/`identity_authority=false`, and never when the input already resolved. Existence uses `withTrashed()` (a soft-deleted patient still owns its Nomor RM). `--strict` fails ONLY on a real master-data defect (unparseable or duplicated Nomor RM) — `NOT_FOUND` is a truthful answer and never fails the gate. **Not wired into any deploy gate.** PII policy: patient id, canonical Nomor RM, branch code, counts, booleans, stable codes — never name/KTP/NIK/phone/address/birth date/clinical detail (test-pinned).

**Master-data findings.** (1) **Patient 23** `medical_record_number '77727222'`, `branch_id NULL`, active, carrying 4 completed visits (ids 1–4) + 4 medical records + 4 odontograms + 3 lab orders + 1 PAID Rp 8.000.000 invoice + 1 payment: the RM is not `DG-{CABANG}-{TAHUN}-{NOMOR}`, so `PatientMedicalRecordNumberService::parse()` returns null and `LegacyRmeBranchResolver` fails closed → **this patient can never receive a legacy archive** (`ARCHIVE_BRANCH_UNRESOLVABLE`). Owner has authorised deleting the record and accepts the loss, but the deletion is **deliberately NOT executed in this sprint** — MASTERDATA-1 may not touch native clinical/billing/lab rows and SQL/Tinker repair is not a supported path; it needs its own scoped operation with a fresh pre-change backup. (2) **Binding-integrity product gap:** `stg_rme_legacy_imports` has **no column for the Nomor RM printed on the document**, so the server cannot verify the operator's patient choice against the source — which is how the aborted first Wave-2 staged import 9 (`RM Landak 3.pdf`, the RM-27541 document) AND import 11 (`RM Landak 4.pdf`) both against patient 46 `DG-LDK2-2026-22676`; both CANCELLED, `W2-003` never published, Wave-2's recorded outcome unchanged. Closing it means capturing the declared RM at upload and asserting it — a schema+workflow change to the migration write path, its own sprint. The detectable symptom IS shipped: distinct documents bound to one patient within a wave (`published 0`, `withdrawn 1`). (3) **Wave-2 wording correction (new evidence, NOT a rewrite):** Wave-2 says the rejected hash "appears in zero live imports"; it appears on import 9, `CANCELLED` but not soft-deleted (`imports_trashed=0`). Substance unchanged — never published, never admitted, never substituted.

**Schema discovery.** `mst_patients.medical_record_number` carries a **non-partial UNIQUE index** (migration `2026_06_03_030003`; production `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX mst_patients_medical_record_number_unique`), soft-deleted rows included — so exact duplicates are structurally impossible and the `EXACT_AMBIGUOUS` arm + duplicate check are **defence in depth**, exactly like `LegacyRmeBranchResolver`'s ambiguous-branch arm. Tests say so honestly rather than forging a duplicate the DB rejects. Realistic ambiguity (`SEGMENT_AMBIGUOUS`, same manual number in two branches) IS exercised.

**Durable rules (mirror §53–64):** identity is exact · similarity is investigative signal only, never authority, no fuzzy fallback · normalization never changes/drops/pads/guesses a significant digit · unknown and ambiguous both fail closed, no first-row-wins · existence includes soft-deleted · branch stays derived from the Nomor RM, never overridden to force a match · source documents are immutable evidence · **master-data repair and legacy migration are separate operations — fixing/creating a patient never authorises importing a document** · a missing patient is created only through canonical human registration, never invented · no SQL/Tinker patient/RM repair · wave evidence is never rewritten after a later correction · `RM 27541 = DOCUMENT_NOT_ELIGIBLE` · ROLL-4-WAVE-3 stays SKIPPED.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditTest.php` (21) — incl. `27541` never resolving to `22541`, `2541` never swallowing `22541`, leading zeros significant, near-miss never bindable and never returned on an exact hit, soft-deleted still found, ambiguity non-bindable, audit changes nothing (table byte-identical), PII-free JSON, strict exits 0 on `NOT_FOUND` and 1 on a real defect. Green: new 21; `tests/Feature/LegacyRme` 727 passed / 5 skipped; `--filter='Patient|CrossBranch|MedicalRecordNumber|AccessControl'` 502 passed / 1 skipped. **Side-effect delta zero on every table** (patients 21 live/43 all, visits 27, MRs 27, odontograms 27, invoices 19, payments 27, lab 13, satusehat 1, legacy imports 15, legacy records 10, jobs 0, failed_jobs 0). Deploy note: **no migration/seed** — the command must simply exist on production to be runnable there.

## LEGACY-RME-SOURCE-RM-BINDING-1 — Source RM Capture, Patient-Binding Enforcement & Wrong-Patient Prevention (2026-08-17)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-source-rm-binding-1-source-rm-capture-patient-binding-enforcement` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline MASTERDATA-1 GO `431d5af`; do NOT target main). Doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-source-rm-binding-1-source-rm-capture-patient-binding-enforcement.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/90-legacy-rme-pdf-archive.mdc` §65–79 (§63's product gap is marked CLOSED); operator runbook `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-migration-operations-runbook.md` §14a; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **Closes the binding-integrity product gap MASTERDATA-1 recorded.** ROLL-4-WAVE-2 stays CLOSED/IMMUTABLE; **ROLL-4-WAVE-3 stays SKIPPED / NOT REQUIRED** (no wave created, no approval, no quota, no GO tag); RM 27541 stays `DOCUMENT_NOT_ELIGIBLE` and is used only as a negative regression; SOD `LEGACY_RME_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_PUBLISHER=true` preserved; production stays `CAPABILITY=OFF / ADMISSION=EMPTY / ACTIVE_WAVE=NONE`. **No permission, no role/seeder change, no route.**

**The failure being removed.** Wave-2 produced a real wrong-patient binding: an operator selected one patient and uploaded a document belonging to another, and the write path could not tell, because the Nomor RM printed on the paper was never captured — there was no independent assertion to compare the selection against. It surfaced later from a frozen source hash and manual evidence.

**Migration (additive only, `migrate`, never `migrate:fresh`/`db:wipe`):** `2026_08_17_100001_add_source_rm_to_legacy_rme_tables` adds nullable indexed `source_rm_raw` / `source_rm_normalized` / `source_rm_resolution` to `stg_rme_legacy_imports` and `source_rm_raw` / `source_rm_normalized` to `trx_rme_legacy_records` (carried onto the immutable record beside `source_pdf_sha256`, so an audit can answer "what patient did this document claim?" without joining the soft-deleted staging row). **Nullable is for HISTORY ONLY and nothing is backfilled** — copying the selected patient's RM onto old rows would convert a derived assumption into false source evidence.

**Canonical chain:** human-confirmed source RM → `LegacyRmeSourceRmNormalizer` → `LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditService::resolveIdentity()` → exactly one patient → compare with the SELECTED patient → asserted-branch-segment check. New `LegacyRmeSourcePatientBindingService` is **the** rule (writes nothing, takes **no actor** — whether a document is about a patient cannot depend on who is asking); `LegacyRmeSourceRmBinding` + `LegacyRmeSourceRmFailure` carry the immutable result and the stable codes `SOURCE_RM_REQUIRED` / `_INVALID` / `_NOT_FOUND` / `_AMBIGUOUS` / `_PATIENT_MISMATCH` / `_BRANCH_MISMATCH` / `_CAPTURE_MISSING`. **Identity reuse, not duplication:** `resolveIdentity()` was extracted from MASTERDATA-1's `resolve()` so both share one private `identify()` — same matching, minus the near-miss scan, which is both too expensive for a write path and something a component deciding identity must never be able to see. **Normalization repairs transcription and never moves a digit** (outer whitespace, whitespace hugging a separator, Unicode dash variants; a parseable value is rebuilt through `PatientMedicalRecordNumberService::compose()`, the documented inverse of `parse()`, so the manual sequence is carried verbatim and only the prefix/branch-code are re-cased).

**Enforcement.** The gate runs **FIRST** in `LegacyRmeImportService::createFromUpload()` — before dates, branch derivation, admission, capacity, the operations gate, hashing, file storage and the in-transaction quota reservation — so a refused wrong-patient binding leaves **no staging row, no stored file, no queued job and quota delta 0**. Also on **retry** (`assertRetryAdmitted`) and, under the row lock, in `LegacyRmePublishService::review()` (before the re-review no-op shortcut, since review CERTIFIES) and `publish()` (beside the existing date/branch revalidation — date, branch and identity are the three facts a permanent record freezes, and all three are re-derived rather than trusted from staging time). **CANCEL is never gated** — every "cancel and re-import" instruction is a lie if cancel is blocked by the same refusal that produced it. Because the rule lives in the SERVICE, HTTP, a direct service call and `legacy-rme:import-admin` are the same door (pinned by a direct-service test and a CLI publish test); `StoreLegacyRmeImportRequest` validates SHAPE only.

**Design correction found by running the suite, not by reading it.** The guard originally re-resolved the archive's owning branch, which duplicated FIX-ROLL2-1 and — because the guard runs first — swallowed `LegacyRmeBranchResolver`'s specific refusals ("inactive", "not RME-enabled", "out of scope") behind a generic identity error, telling an operator to re-read a document when the real fix was master data. It now compares only the branch segment the DOCUMENT asserts against the patient's own (pure parsing, no query). `LegacyRmeBranchResolver` remains the sole owner of the owning branch.

**Non-disclosure.** A refusal never names the patient a rejected Nomor RM actually belongs to — no id, no name, no RM, in the message or the audit payload. The upload form is reachable by any `create_legacy_rme_imports` holder, so a helpful refusal would be a patient-enumeration oracle. New audit action `LEGACY_RME_SOURCE_RM_BINDING_REJECTED` (its own action: a wrong-patient refusal is operationally distinct from a branch, rollout or throttle refusal) with allow-listed keys `source_rm_normalized` + `source_rm_resolution` only — the NORMALIZED value, never the raw operator transcription (free text has been excluded from this allow-list since 1D).

**Pre-enforcement rows.** Terminal rows (PUBLISHED/CANCELLED/VOID) untouched — Wave-2R history renders unchanged. Non-terminal rows are refused at review/publish/retry with `SOURCE_RM_CAPTURE_MISSING` telling the operator to cancel and re-import. Production's resting state has none, so it is a safety net, not a migration task.

**UI.** Required "Nomor RM pada Dokumen" + its own attestation ("Saya memastikan nomor RM yang dimasukkan sesuai dengan nomor RM yang tercetak pada dokumen RME Legacy."), **deliberately NOT prefilled** from the selected patient and with no "copy from patient" helper — a value that agrees with the selection by construction cannot catch a wrong selection. Review screen shows the asserted RM beside the patient's own.

**Production proof without manufacturing data.** New read-only `php artisan legacy-rme:source-rm-binding-check --source-rm= --patient= [--normalize-only] [--json]` calls the same guard object the write path calls, creates nothing, needs no wave/admitted branch/enabled capability, and exits 1 on refusal — so the wrong-patient probe is EXPECTED to fail and an exit 0 there is the alarm. `LIVE_MUTATION=NOT_EXERCISED` by design; no migration wave is opened for smoke evidence.

**Compatibility.** `createFromUpload()` gained a **required** `$sourceRm` at position 3 — required and early on purpose, so adding a mandatory identity assertion breaks every caller loudly instead of defaulting silently (47 test call sites updated). `LegacyRmeImportFactory` stamps a coherent binding by default (a fixture builds the row a correct confirmation WOULD produce — that is not the forbidden production backfill), with `->withoutSourceRm()` for a genuine pre-enforcement row and `->sourceRm()` for a deliberately wrong one.

**Durable rules (mirror §65–79):** every new import captures the printed Nomor RM as structured data · the source RM is independent of the selected patient and is never prefilled/auto-copied · filename is never source-RM authority (`source_pdf_sha256` identifies the DOCUMENT, the source RM identifies the asserted PATIENT) · OCR is assistive only and never becomes authority without human confirmation · normalization never changes a digit · identity is EXACT and fuzzy matching is unreachable from the write path · every failure code fails closed with no override · the gate runs before queue/review and before quota · the rule lives in the service so HTTP/CLI/direct-call are one door · the source RM does not decide the owning branch (FIX-ROLL2-1 still does) and request `branch_id` is never an authority · the source RM is immutable after acceptance (correction = cancel + re-import) · the binding is revalidated at review/publish/retry · cancel is never gated · pre-enforcement rows are never given a manufactured source RM · a refusal never discloses whose Nomor RM it is.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeSourceRmBindingTest.php` (41) — normalization incl. the 27541 pair, the full binding matrix, zero-footprint on refusal (`Bus::assertNothingDispatched`), HTTP + direct-service + CLI parity, audit shape and non-disclosure, review/publish/retry revalidation, pre-enforcement refusal, cancel availability, bounded query count, and the archive boundary (no visit/MR/invoice/payment/lab/SATUSEHAT state). Deploy note: `php artisan migrate --force` (1 additive migration); **no seed, no permission change**; keep the capability flag OFF.

## CICD-BASELINE-REVERIFY-1 — Full-Suite Baseline Revalidation & Stale Failure-Debt Closure (2026-08-17)

Branch `feature/cicd-baseline-reverify-1-full-suite-baseline-stale-failure-debt-closure` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, base SHA `3234265` resolved from the remote per DEVFLOW-FIX-BASE-REF-1; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/cicd-baseline-reverify-1-full-suite-baseline-stale-failure-debt-closure.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-full-suite-baseline-failure-debt.mdc`; runbook section in `docs/runbooks/ci-runtime-control-runbook.md`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (INFRA_RELEASE). **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no business-logic change.**

**Result: `LEGACY_9_FAILURE_BASELINE = RETIRED`, `EXPECTED_FULL_SUITE_FAILURE_BASELINE = 0`.**

**The historical baseline, reconstructed from the raw job log (not from the summary describing it):** 9 failures at `cbe97129fce79ab5c54cce0eed10ae341f6a719a` (CTRL-3D closure), run `31293873172`, `9 failed, 5642 warnings, 1 risky, 13 passed (26604 assertions)`, GitHub-hosted `ubuntu-latest` + PHP 8.3 + **postgres:16**, unfiltered `php artisan test`. Evidence quality: **authoritative** — real CI, canonical runner and database, strict exit already in force. (The earlier run `29592369812` recorded `success` while logging `1202 failed` is the swallowed-exit defect and is **never** cited as a passing baseline.)

**All nine closed by one workstream — CICD-FIX-6 (`fe36f06`, PR #269)** — proven by the very next Full Suite (`31335720157`) reporting **0 failed** and by that commit touching exactly the implicated files. #1–#7 were `'GO'` vs `'WATCH'` release-evidence assertions fixed by `ReleaseEvidenceService` capturing evidence before the check. **#8 `Nsf21SqliteMigrationCompatibilityTest`** asserted `getDriverName() === 'sqlite'` in a PostgreSQL job — a broken premise, not a product defect — replaced by 3 tests opening an **explicit sqlite connection** (coverage 1→3, executes, not skipped). **#9 `RmeSmokeTestRouteTest`** was fixed at the *setup* end: the seeder now starts the Perawat online context via the real `UserOnlineContextService` (RME-BRANCH-SUN4 made it mandatory) **plus a new negative test** pinning that the redirect still fires when the context is released. Neither was deleted, skipped or weakened.

**Suite did not shrink:** `cbe9712` 5665 tests/26604 assertions → `3234265` **6566 tests/29344 assertions** (+901 tests, +2740 assertions); **0 skipped in every run**; all nine historical test files confirmed present in the current log; the step is `php artisan test` with **no `--filter`**. **Full Suite topology:** weekly `schedule` (`0 2 * * 0`) · `workflow_dispatch run_full_suite=true` · `push` to base; **always `ubuntu-latest`, never self-hosted** (`self_hosted_heavy_jobs` names only `critical_test_gate_self_hosted`); `postgres:16`; no declared timeout (GitHub's 360-min default).

**No failure-allowance mechanism exists to remove** — the nine were only ever prose. A scan of the workflow, `scripts/ci/`, `config/ci_runner.php`, `config/ci_runtime_control.php`, `app/Support/Cicd/` and `app/Services/Foundation/` finds no allowance token, so there is no subtraction path. Fail-closed is already pinned by CICD-FIX-6's `NsfReleaseGateExitPropagationTest` (`Run full Pest suite` registered in `ci_runner.strict_pipeline_steps`, with a **mutation test** proving that removing its strict shell would restore a false green) — deliberately not duplicated. Proven empirically: run `31928614428` reddened the entire gate on **one** failing test.

**Real non-determinism found and closed (this sprint's only code change).** That single failure — `RME\LabCaseCandidateQueueTest › it index searches by patient name`, `To contain: Oswaldo O'Kon` — was **not** one of the nine: the test compared a **faker-generated** name against the **raw** response body, but Blade renders through `{{ }}` so `O'Kon` arrives as `O&#039;Kon`. It passed or failed on the random seed (provably a flake: no commit between `9c803fe` and `3234265` touches that test or module, yet both later runs are green). Two occurrences of the class, both fixed **deterministically, never by loosening**: `LabCaseCandidateQueueTest` now pins both patient names — one deliberately carrying an apostrophe so the escaping path runs *every* time — asserts via `assertSee()` (which escapes the expected value) and adds the `assertDontSee` its own comment had promised; `RmePrescriptionTest` (latent twin — `assertSee('value="'.$visit->doctor->name.'"', false)` with `DoctorFactory` = `'Dr. '.fake()->name()`) now wraps the dynamic half in `e()`. **`assertSee()` escapes by default**, so the many `assertSee($model->name)` sites were never at risk — only the explicit `, false` form and raw body comparisons.

**Guard:** new `tests/Feature/Cicd/FullSuiteBaselineContractTest.php` (4 tests) — lives in `tests/Feature/Cicd`, which `ci_runner.critical_gate_required_filters` puts in the **critical gate**, so it runs on every PR, not just the weekly full suite. It pins (a) no expected-failure allowance token in the CI/governance surface — searching for the *mechanism*, not the number, so it stays valid if a future baseline is legitimately non-zero; (b) no raw response-body assertion against a dynamic value anywhere in `tests/`; (c) every `assertSee(…, false)` interpolating a text-bearing property passes through `e()`; (d) a behavioural test proving an escapable name is absent verbatim but present escaped. **Gotcha:** the file must **exclude itself** from its own scan — it quotes the offending patterns verbatim to explain and match them and otherwise reports itself (the same self-scan trap that pushed the deployment scanners' literals into config).

**Durable rules (mirror rule 92):** expected-failure baselines are temporary evidence, never a standing regression allowance · a baseline must name exact tests + signatures + authoritative run/SHA (a bare count is not a baseline) · retire only after per-entry reconciliation *and* coverage verification, never off one green run · deleting a test is not fixing it · a failure becoming skipped is not a fix · green must not come from filter drift (compare test/assertion inventory) · at baseline zero the gate fails closed · comparisons need compatible runner/DB/command/scope · wrong checkout, runner outage, orphaned Pest workers and absent runtime capability are environment faults, not application debt · CI evidence is SHA-exact and invalidated by any later candidate commit · post-merge Full Suite must settle before GO · the current baseline is always explicitly documented · **a test whose outcome depends on random fixture data is a defect, fixed by making it deterministic**.

**Warning-count claim CORRECTED (stale since `9484dd9`).** CICD-CTRL-3 §10 attributed the huge warning count to an absent `public/build/manifest.json` downgrading every layout-rendering test. **That explanation is superseded and must not be repeated:** `tests/TestCase.php` calls Laravel's `withoutVite()` — which shipped **in `9484dd9` itself**, the very merge those 5 637 warnings were measured on — so `@vite` renders nothing; locally with no `public/build` the same tests report **`PASS`, not warning**; and no manifest path appears anywhere in the current run's log. What IS established: warnings are **not** skips (tests execute and assert; assertions rose 26 604 → 29 344) and warnings **never mask failures** (9 failures beside 5 642 warnings, and 1 beside 6 486 — both red). **The true cause is NOT identified** — Pest truncates the inline warning text, so of 6 321 warning-marked tests only 2 469 still show the `→ file_get_contents(…` fragment and none retains a full path. Handed forward as separate work; this sprint deliberately does **not** invent a replacement explanation, since doing so would repeat the exact failure mode it exists to correct.

**Not done (deliberately):** the warning downgrade itself was **not** fixed here. Historical records were **not** rewritten: CICD-CTRL-3 §10 stays true of the SHA it describes and now carries a forward pointer.

## LEGACY-RME-STEADY-STATE-OPS-1 — Operationalization, SOP, Monitoring & Multi-Branch Routine Migration (2026-08-18)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-steady-state-ops-1-operationalization-multi-branch-routine-migration` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `126ea7617407247738db1d1217fdcab83a1e30e4` = CICD-BASELINE-REVERIFY-1; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-steady-state-ops-1-operationalization-multi-branch-routine-migration.md`; SOP `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-steady-state-operations-runbook.md`; one-page checklist `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-routine-batch-operator-checklist.md`; PII-free batch evidence template `docs/evidence/legacy-rme/routine-batch-evidence-template.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/103-legacy-rme-steady-state-operations.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **Converts legacy RME migration from an engineering wave per batch into routine production operations. NO control changed** — capability, admission, capacity backpressure, quota, operator assignment, pause/drain, reconciliation, completion sign-off, source-RM binding, clinical date rules and SOD all behave exactly as on the base commit; **no migration, no permission, no route, no view, no seeder, no mutating code path added**.

**Gap analysis first (most of the ask already existed).** Already shipped: `legacy-rme:wave-admin` (full batch lifecycle, dry-run default), `legacy-rme:import-admin` (lifecycle recovery), `legacy-rme:migration-status`, `legacy-rme:wave-status`, `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` (18 checks), `legacy-rme:source-rm-binding-check`, `legacy-rme:patient-resolution-audit`, quota + reconciliation + completion invariants, capacity backpressure, `SeparatePublisherGuard`, and a 509-line operations runbook. **Only three genuine gaps were built:** (1) **backup freshness was never gated** — the 18-check gate never asked whether a restore point existed; (2) **resting state was prose, not an assertion** (runbook §0); (3) **no per-branch readiness view**. Plus the operating model itself.

**Added.** `config/legacy_rme_steady_state.php` (routine-batch required properties · naming scheme · retired codes · sizing envelope · resting-state assertion set · multi-branch classification · backup window · stop-the-line codes · severity model · routine-batch-≠-GO-tag) — read-only by construction; deleting it admits nothing new. `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Services\LegacyRmeSteadyStateOpsService` — read-only aggregator, 11 guarded checks, **composes** `LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessService` (decision inherited verbatim) + `SeparatePublisherGuard::enabled()` + `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionService` + `LegacyRmeWaveBindingService` + `LegacyRmeMigrationReconciliationService` + `LegacyRmeMigrationQuotaService` + `LegacyRmeIngestionCapacityService` + `FoundationMonitoringStatusService` (MON-1/ENT-12 `deploy_backup` signal) + `ClinicalClock` + `BranchService::listRmeEnabled()`. Command `legacy-rme:ops-readiness` (`--json`/`--strict`/`--fail-on-warning`/`--skip-monitoring`/`--branch=`); exit `1` on NO_GO always, on WATCH only under strict.

**Durable rules (mirror rule 103):** legacy RME migration is routine operations, not a sprint per batch · a routine batch IS the existing ROLL-4 wave record (never a new table/state machine — a second path to the same clinical writes would be weaker) · **`ROLL-4-WAVE-3` stays SKIPPED / NOT REQUIRED and is now also REFUSED as a declared batch code** · every batch needs a FRESH approval (Wave-1/Wave-2R approvals are immutable evidence, never reused) · resting state = capability OFF + admission EMPTY + active batch NONE · **a routine batch never earns a GO tag** · no verified backup <24h ⇒ no batch (refusal, not warning) · closure needs zero in-flight/unexplained/quota-drift/unresolved-failures + written sign-off, no `--force` · a cancelled import still consumes quota · archive-only permanently (any native clinical/billing/lab/SATUSEHAT side effect is stop-the-line) · never SQL/Tinker for lifecycle state · never a shared maker/checker account.

**`MULTI_BRANCH_MODE = CONCURRENT_BRANCH_ISOLATED_WITHIN_ONE_ACTIVE_BATCH`** — read from the code, not aspirational: **across batches SEQUENTIAL** (`LegacyRmeWaveBindingService` resolves the operative batch from ONE declared code; concurrent batches unsupported and deliberately NOT added), **within one batch CONCURRENT + branch-isolated** (`ops_rme_legacy_wave_branches` carries per-branch status/quota/operators/transitions).

**Sizing derived, not invented:** `default_daily=25` is the ingestion queue's own single-worker pending ceiling (`capacity.max_pending_jobs` — beyond it uploads are already refused by backpressure, so a bigger allowance cannot make documents flow faster); `max_daily=100` is 4× that, bounded by human review throughput; above 100 is not routine (elevated approval); absolute rail stays the server-side `max_declarable_daily=500`. **MEASURED THROUGHPUT IS NOT AVAILABLE** — production evidence is Wave-1 (1 doc) + Wave-2R (4 docs) = five documents, far too small to fit a rate to. Bounded defaults for recoverability; never quote as a benchmark.

**Design decisions worth keeping:** backup freshness **reuses** the MON-1 signal rather than re-probing (a second "is the backup fresh?" implementation would be free to disagree with the first) · monitoring signals are collected **BY DEFAULT** — the first cut gated them behind `--include-monitoring`, which made the *default* invocation permanently NO_GO; `--skip-monitoring` now opts out and degrades honestly · **"not established" ≠ "stale"**: both block, but only a genuinely missing/stale backup is stop-the-line (`BACKUP_NOT_ESTABLISHED` vs `BACKUP_MISSING_OR_STALE`) — reporting "I did not look" as a failed control trains operators to ignore the loudest signal · the batch **naming pattern is advisory and deliberately NOT asserted** (a check would fire permanently against the legitimate historical `WAVE-1`/`WAVE-2R`) while **`WAVE-3` IS asserted** (a retired identity has no approval record and no historical rows to false-positive against) · a deliberately closed branch is `NOT_READY` with **zero blockers**, not a fault · `UNKNOWN` blocks exactly like `FAIL` · **human SOD is attested, never asserted** — the report carries `human_separation_verifiable_by_application: false` rather than implying it verified two humans · `require_separate_approver` deliberately left OFF (one staffed governance account) and reported as WARNING, never flipped.

**Architectural guard tripped and honoured (worth remembering).** The first implementation read `config('legacy_rme_operations.require_separate_publisher')` directly and `LegacyRmeSeparationOfDutiesTest > it gives the enablement switch exactly one home in the application` failed — that test scans `app/` for the literal and asserts `SeparatePublisherGuard` is its only reader, because a second reader *decides for itself* whether the rule applies. Fixed by asking `SeparatePublisherGuard::enabled()` and renaming the report key to `separate_publisher_enforced`. **The guard was right and the new code was wrong.** Any future LegacyRme work must read SOD through the guard, never its config key.

**`LIVE_ROUTINE_BATCH = NOT_EXERCISED`** — no genuine owner-authorized candidate set existed during this sprint; no patient/document/hash/approval/batch was fabricated to manufacture a green line. Verification is automated tests + read-only production checks + safe drills.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeSteadyStateOpsTest.php` (30) — deliberately weighted toward **refusals**, because the risk here is not a missing gate but *a report that lies* now that an operator acts on one line instead of four; a stale backup, unapproved branch, disabled SOD invariant, lapsed window, retired batch code and unevaluated check must each stop a batch alone; plus read-only proof (migration state byte-identical before/after) and that the approval reference is never echoed. **Test gotcha:** a dev box legitimately has no Poppler and no worker unit, so `deployment_readiness` is FAIL locally and dominates the global decision — assert check-level status, not `$report['decision']`, for anything unrelated to deployment. Visual reference (batch lifecycle, multi-branch concurrency, branch matrix, severity vocabulary, maker/checker control, incident flow) published as a PII-free artifact outside the repo; explanatory only — command output is the authority. Deploy note: **no migration/seed/permission**; on the VPS run `legacy-rme:ops-readiness --json` + `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` + `legacy-rme:migration-status` and confirm `Resting state: AT_REST`.

## LEGACY-RME-DOCTOR-WORKSPACE-1 — Embedded Read-Only Legacy PDF in the Doctor's RME Workspace (2026-08-18)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-doctor-workspace-1-embedded-read-only-legacy-pdf` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `cca18e2`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-doctor-workspace-1-embedded-read-only-legacy-pdf.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/104-legacy-rme-doctor-workspace-embedded-pdf.mdc`. **Presentation / clinical read surface only — NO migration, NO new route, NO new permission, NO new dependency, NO CSP change.**

**The problem (measured, not assumed).** A published legacy archive was already readable, but only from the clinical-history card included at **line 935 of the 985-line** `rme/visits/medical-record/show.blade.php` — below the visit card, the notes area, the whole multi-page handwriting canvas, the full-screen overlay editor, the structured-diagnosis card and the relocated sheet nav. The doctor had to scroll past the surface they were writing on to discover the patient's old RME existed.

**The fix.** The same documents now render as a **document rail at the top of the workspace** (directly under the workflow nav, above "Informasi Kunjungan"), in both `show.blade.php` and the zero-sheet `empty.blade.php` — the migrated patient whose ONLY RME is the archive is exactly the case that needed it. New partial `resources/views/rme/visits/partials/rme-workspace-documents.blade.php`. The existing clinical-history card is untouched and still rendered; the rail is **additive**.

**Architecture.** New `App\Modules\MedicalRecord\Support\RmeWorkspaceDocument` — a `final` non-persistent value object with an **explicit** `type` (`native`|`legacy`); no enum (codebase convention is `final` Support classes), no polymorphic table, PII-free. New `App\Modules\MedicalRecord\Services\RmeWorkspaceDocumentPresenter` — the native side is the sheet collection the controller **already resolved** (passed in, never re-queried, and `sheetsForPatient()` already eager-loads `clinicVisit` so there is no N+1); the legacy side comes from the **canonical** `LegacyRmePatientHistoryService::publishedRecordsFor()`, so publication status, VOID filtering, branch scope, the feature flag and `DoctorPatientScopeService` are never reimplemented. Cost: exactly **one** extra bounded indexed query per workspace load, O(1) in document count. Graphify confirms the dependency direction is MedicalRecord → LegacyRme with no reverse edge.

**Viewer.** In-page **overlay** (Alpine, inline `@push('scripts')`, no asset rebuild): zoom in/out/fit-width, page prev/next for multi-page, full-screen expand, Escape to close, focus moved to the close button, `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal`. **Chosen as an overlay specifically so the doctor never navigates away — unsaved handwriting/notes can never be discarded by reading the archive.** Native sheet selection still reloads via the existing `?sheet=` URL (pre-existing behaviour, unchanged).

**Nothing loads until asked.** The viewer body lives inside `<template x-if="open">` and binds `src` only after opening, so initial render fetches **zero** archive bytes no matter how many documents the patient has (test asserts no `src="…legacy-records…"` / `data="…legacy-records…"` in the HTML).

**Graceful degradation.** An archive imported without a working rasterizer (page_count > 0 but no rendered pages, and the poppler-missing case) falls back to the inline source PDF — which already streams `Content-Disposition: inline` through the same policy-gated route — via `hasPages()` plus an `onerror` recovery. No broken image in a clinical viewer.

**Security posture (all reused verbatim, nothing widened).** Bytes stream only from `rme.legacy-records.source` / `.pages.show`, each re-authorizing independently via `viewFile` — a rendered link is never treated as authorization. Same branch alone still never grants a doctor access (treating relationship required); other-branch is 404 (existence not probeable); guest redirects to login; a VOIDed record never streams even for Super Admin (enforced in the controller because the single global `Gate::before` grants Super Admin everything). **The application defines no `Content-Security-Policy`/`frame-src`/`X-Frame-Options` middleware at all** — verified, and this sprint added none; the overlay is same-origin. A structural test asserts the ONLY mutating `rme.legacy-records.*` route in the whole route table is `rme.legacy-records.void`.

**Bug caught before shipping.** The first cut anchored native sheet links on each sheet's OWN visit. The workspace redirects any non-canonical visit to the canonical URL and that redirect **drops `sheet`** — so selecting any sheet outside the canonical visit would have silently lost the selection. Native links are now anchored on the canonical workspace visit (exactly as `rm-sheet-nav` does) and a test pins it.

**Discovery gotcha worth remembering.** The primary checkout `/home/fikri/Projects/new_lab_app` is parked on the stale `ci-evidence/cicd-ctrl-3-db-guard-matrix` branch, which predates ALL LegacyRme work — `routes/web.php` there has zero legacy routes and `app/Modules/LegacyRme` holds only the 1A foundation. Discovery must run against the fetched base SHA (or a fresh worktree), never the parked checkout. **Graphify IS on PATH** (`/home/fikri/.local/bin/graphify`) — the CICD-CTRL-3 note that it was unavailable no longer holds; re-check rather than inherit that claim.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeDoctorWorkspaceDocumentsTest.php` (22) — rail renders **above** the history card, history card preserved, zero-native-sheet case, read-only in text not colour, explicit typing, only-`void`-mutates, no eager bytes, treating-doctor allowed, same-branch-no-relationship denied for BOTH listing and bytes, other-branch 404, guest redirect, direct page-URL re-authorized, non-published never listed, void never streams, multi-archive newest-first/distinct, single-vs-multi-page, no-rendered-pages fallback, native update form intact, canonical-anchor pin, zero downstream rows, no public storage URL / no KTP. Deploy: **no migration/seed/permission** — `Nothing to migrate.` expected.

## LEGACY-RME-DOCTOR-WORKSPACE-1A — Inline Legacy PDF Pages in the Handwritten RME Swipe Canvas (2026-08-18)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-doctor-workspace-1a-inline-legacy-pdf-swipe` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, BASE_SHA `f47735ac`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-doctor-workspace-1a-inline-legacy-pdf-swipe.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-legacy-rme-inline-workspace-pages.mdc`; doctor guidance = §14 of `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-pdf-rollout-runbook.md`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **Corrective/product-completion sprint.** LEGACY-RME-DOCTOR-WORKSPACE-1 was technically correct but did NOT implement the owner's intended UX: a doctor still had to leave the `RME Tulisan Tangan Lengkap` page navigation, pick a document from the top rail and enter a second viewer with its own internal paging. **1A changes presentation/navigation ONLY — never legacy clinical persistence.**

**Product rule: ONE PAGE NAVIGATION EXPERIENCE, TWO DATA SOURCES** — native handwritten page = editable, legacy PDF page = read-only. Native pages and legacy archive pages now form ONE numbered sequence behind the same `?rm_page=` index, the same `← Sebelumnya`/`Berikutnya →` buttons, the same numbered buttons and the same swipe zone. A legacy PDF with N pages contributes **N** virtual workspace pages (2 native + A(3) + B(2) ⇒ `Halaman X dari 7`). **No migration, no new route, no new permission, no schema change** — navigation was already server-side (`?rm_page=`), so the change is a unified sequence plus a branch on the page's explicit kind.

**New:** `App\Modules\MedicalRecord\Support\RmeWorkspacePage` (immutable PII-free projection; `type` is an EXPLICIT field — never inferred from an id range/index/label/URL; `readonly` hard-coded true on the legacy factory), `App\Modules\MedicalRecord\Services\RmeWorkspacePageSequencer` (`sequenceFor`/`resolveActivePage` clamping a crafted `rm_page`/`nativeAnchorFor`), and 3 Blade partials under `resources/views/rme/visits/medical-record/partials/`: `legacy-archive-page` (read-only surface: zoom in/out/fit, fullscreen, `<img>` per page with PDF fallback), plus **extracted** `rm-page-navigator` and `rm-page-swipe-script` — extracted, NOT duplicated, so a second navigation system cannot grow; both the populated workspace and the zero-native-sheet empty state include the same two partials.

**Ordering (deliberate, not chronological):** native pages first in existing order, then the archive **newest document first**, pages ascending within each. Strict chronology would put every legacy page BEFORE every native page (the date rule guarantees legacy < earliest native), forcing a doctor to swipe through years of history to reach the page they must write on.

**Page-count authority:** the number of ACTUALLY RENDERED `trx_rme_legacy_record_pages` rows (one `loadCount` aggregate), NOT the declared `page_count` — a record can declare a count while nothing was rasterised. A record with 0 rendered pages contributes exactly ONE fallback page offering the inline source PDF; never dropped, never expanded into pages that could only 404.

**Clinical safety:** the editable figure is **not rendered at all** on an archive page (no inert-but-present canvas to aim a stylus at); the canvas + open editor keep `data-ignore-swipe`, and the archive viewer sets it **while zoomed** (a zoomed doctor is panning, not turning pages); new `beforeunload` guard + explicit confirm when closing the editor with live strokes, with a `submitting` flag so a save is never mistaken for data loss; `+ Tambah Halaman RM` always targets the canonical NATIVE handwriting endpoint, including while an archive page is active. Read-only is stated in TEXT ("Hanya Baca") and legacy page buttons carry an `L` prefix + spoken `aria-label` — never colour alone.

**Authorization unchanged and deliberately NOT re-derived:** the sequencer resolves nothing itself; the archive comes from `LegacyRmePatientHistoryService::publishedRecordsFor()` (read permissions + branch scope + doctor patient scope + published-only/VOID). A test asserts the sequencer source contains no `STATUS_PUBLISHED`/`branchIdsFor`/`doctorCanAccessPatient`/`LegacyRmeRecord::query`. Being listed is NOT authorization — bytes still stream through the policy-gated route which re-authorizes per request; **same branch alone still grants nothing**.

**Performance:** the patient's archive is now resolved **ONCE per request** and shared by the clinical history, the document rail and the page sequence (it was resolved TWICE before this sprint) — via a backward-compatible optional `?Collection $legacyRecords` parameter on both `LegacyRmePatientHistoryService::patientClinicalHistoryFor()` and `RmeWorkspaceDocumentPresenter::documentsFor()`. Rendered-page counts are one aggregate; only the ACTIVE page's image is fetched; no PDF is base64'd into the HTML.

**Gotcha:** `MedicalRecord::orderedHandwritingPages()` already has an `is_legacy` key meaning "legacy handwriting STORAGE path" (Sprint 60 read-through) — that is unrelated to the legacy PDF ARCHIVE. The new DTO uses a separate `type` field; never conflate the two. Every `MedicalRecord` contributes at least one native page (the page-1 read-through slot), so a patient with 0 native SHEETS is the zero-native case, not 0 pages.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeDoctorWorkspaceInlinePagesTest.php` (32). Unchanged: SOURCE-RM-BINDING, SOD, ClinicalClock, the clinical date rule, patient-history rendering, production resting state (`CAPABILITY=OFF`/`ADMISSION=EMPTY`/`ACTIVE_BATCH=NONE`), `ROLL-4-WAVE-3 = SKIPPED / NOT REQUIRED`, and `CURRENT_VALID_EXPECTED_FULL_SUITE_FAILURE_BASELINE = 0`. Deploy: **no migration/seed**; deploy runs INSIDE the VPS via `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start`.

## LEGACY-RME-PROGRAM-CLOSURE-1 — Final Program Audit, Foundation Revalidation & Production Steady-State Closure (2026-08-18)

Branch `feature/legacy-rme-program-closure-1-final-program-audit-production-steady-state` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, canonical remote `BASE_SHA=f246cb31e699cfb2901fe82b5d5f94ed2e2290da`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/legacy-rme-program-closure-1-final-program-audit-production-steady-state.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/105-legacy-rme-program-closure.mdc`. **The Legacy RME engineering programme is CLOSED.**

```
ENGINEERING_ROLLOUT_MODE = CLOSED
STEADY_STATE_OPERATIONS  = AUTHORITATIVE
FUTURE_ROUTINE_BATCHES   = OPERATIONAL
```

**Closure sprint, not a feature sprint** — no migration, no route, no permission, no runtime behaviour change, no clinical/business mutation, no new migration wave, no patient-data correction. Adds one program-level contract test plus the closure record, rule and manifest. **A synchronization deployment was performed at the owner's explicit request: `RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=NO`, `SYNC_DEPLOYMENT=YES`.**

**Authority.** Base resolved per DEVFLOW-FIX-BASE-REF-1 — canonical **remote** SHA wins; the local checkout was proven stale (`f47735a`, one commit behind) and discarded. Production was already at the exact base. Discovery ran from a fresh worktree, deliberately not the primary checkout (parked on `ci-evidence/cicd-ctrl-3-db-guard-matrix`).

**Foundation ledger.** All **27** programme GO tags verified: local peel == remote peel, every one an ancestor of the canonical base, **none moved or rewritten**. `ROLL-4-WAVE-3` has **no tag and requires none** — it stays `SKIPPED / NOT REQUIRED`.

**Invariants revalidated in code (A–K, all PASS):** archive boundary (publish writes only legacy + staging tables; no `Odontogram`/`Prescription`/`RmeInvoice`/`RmePayment`/`LabOrder`/`LabCaseCandidate`/`Satusehat` symbol anywhere in the module); source-RM binding (exact-unique, four explicit refusal codes, `levenshtein()` exists **only** in the read-only CLI diagnostic on rows stamped `bindable => false`); RM-derived branch authority (submitted `branch_id` only ever *compared*, never applied); strict date cutoff (both bounds are `! ->lessThan(...)`, so **equality fails**); SOD (pure account-id comparison inside the transaction under the row lock — the global Super Admin `Gate::before` **cannot reach it**); immutability (`update`/`delete` hard-wired `false`, repository exposes **no** update method, `markVoided` is the only state change); doctor scope (practice branches ∩ active RME-enabled, `[]` denies everything, listing path gated identically to the viewer — **same branch alone grants nothing**); private PDF (policy-gated stream, `serve => false`, zero `Storage::url`/`asset(` hits, no request-supplied path); inline workspace (`RmeWorkspacePage::legacy()` hard-codes `readonly: true` — not a parameter; the legacy partial has no `<canvas>` and no `<form>`); ops CLI (thin adapter over the same lifecycle service the HTTP path uses).

**Audit finding run down, not accepted.** The three date bounds in `config/legacy_rme.php` are plain booleans rather than fail-closed resolvers. Verified on production: all three `true`, plus `cutoff_invariants.require_medical_record = true`, **zero** environment overrides — and the config values carry **no `env()` call at all**, so there is no runtime surface on which to weaken the cutoff; only a reviewed code change could. That property (the *absence* of env plumbing) is now pinned by the closure contract test. Production is also **stricter than the repo default**: `require_separate_approver = true` as well as `require_separate_publisher`.

**Residuals.** `OPEN_BLOCKERS = 0`. Sweep of CLAUDE.md, 44 rules, sprint docs, runbooks, ADRs and the manifest: 0 blockers, 0 stale current-guidance text, 8 non-blocking known limitations, 4 separate future workstreams, ~55 historical-only items (past-tense evidence in immutable sprint records, each already superseded by a later GO — left untouched). The retired **nine-failure baseline** appears only twice, both explicitly historical; current guidance asserts **0** and `FullSuiteBaselineContractTest` pins the *mechanism*. The "Graphify unavailable" claim **does not exist in the repository** — it lived only in assistant session memory, and CLAUDE.md already carries the retraction; Graphify v0.8.35 was confirmed on PATH and used here.

**Gotcha (cost an investigation).** In a fresh worktree every DB-touching governance gate fails with a Postgres auth error, because `.env` seeded from the example file carries no local credentials — this is **not** pre-existing failure debt. Wire the worktree to the local dev DB first; all ten gates then return GO. Likewise `legacy-rme:ops-readiness` / `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` return NO_GO **locally** (dev laptop has no legacy tables, no seeded permissions, no approved pilot scope, no backups) while being GO on production — **they fail closed on an unprepared deployment by design**. Never read either as a code defect without checking the environment first.

**Production steady state (verified):** `APP_ENV=pilot`, debug OFF, maintenance OFF, clinical timezone `Asia/Makassar` canonical; `SOD=true` (publisher **and** approver); `CAPABILITY=OFF` / `ADMISSION=EMPTY` / `ACTIVE_BATCH=NONE`; `ops-readiness` **GO / AT_REST / READY_FOR_ROUTINE_BATCH=YES**; `rollout-readiness` GO; health 200×4; nginx + php8.3-fpm + queue worker active; 0 failed jobs. All four RME branches report `NOT_READY` because none is approved or admitted — that is the closed pre-wave resting state, **not** a defect. `READY_FOR_ROUTINE_BATCH=YES` means *safe to begin with fresh approval*, never *a migration is open*.

**Known non-blockers (recorded truthfully):** SOD proves **account** separation, not two humans (human maker/checker stays an attested operational control; `uploaded_by IS NULL` rows are exempt); `LegacyRmeMigrationOperationsController:202,206` hydrates models directly (guarded, no business logic, wave-operator assignment only — recorded, not refactored during closure); rule-file prefix collisions at `92-`/`97-` (cosmetic, renumbering would churn cross-references for no safety gain); the Full Suite warning-summary phenomenon classified `KNOWN_NON_BLOCKING_CI_OBSERVABILITY_DEBT` **with no invented cause** (void if evidence ever shows it masking real failures); Wave-2 refusal-on-overflow and pause/resume not exercised live; live browser swipe not exercised with real doctor credentials; `LIVE_ROUTINE_BATCH=NOT_EXERCISED` (no genuine owner-approved candidate existed and nothing was fabricated).

**Separate future workstreams (not held against closure):** Pest warning-downgrade root cause; patient master-data hygiene (RM 27541 stays `DOCUMENT_NOT_ELIGIBLE`, **not reopened**; patient 23 untouched); TKM1/ATG3/SUN4 routine migration (operational under STEADY-STATE-OPS-1, needs fresh approval — not engineering backlog).

**Standing facts preserved:** `CURRENT_VALID_EXPECTED_FULL_SUITE_FAILURE_BASELINE = 0` and `ROLL-4-WAVE-3 = SKIPPED / NOT REQUIRED`. Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeProgramClosureContractTest.php` (program-level invariants only — deliberately does **not** duplicate `FullSuiteBaselineContractTest` or the per-sprint suites). GO tag: `legacy-rme-program-closure-1-production-steady-state-final-go`. Deploy: **no migration/seed**; runs INSIDE the VPS via `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start`.

**Future bugs or features create their own workstreams. They do not retroactively reopen this programme unless a closure invariant is proven invalid.**

GO tag `legacy-rme-program-closure-1-production-steady-state-final-go` @ `24334c7` (the deployed runtime); this evidence commit is deliberately **NOT** deployed (recorded as `FINAL_EVIDENCE_SHA`, never redeployed to manufacture SHA equality).
