**The two "pre-existing" Monitoring failures were never monitor defects.** `PilotPerformanceSnapshotLogAnalyzerTest:162` and `PilotPerformanceSnapshotCommandTest:227` both failed `-'OK' +'WATCH'`. Instrumenting `collect()` showed `app=OK database=OK http=OK logs=OK` and **`resources=WATCH`** — the log analyser was innocent. `overall_status` is `Classifier::worst(app, database, resources, http, logs)`, the tests neutralised `database` and `http` via `skip_db`/`skip_http`, and **there was no equivalent for `resources`**, which reads live host disk (`classifyDiskFreeGb`: `<10 GB FIX`, `<20 GB WATCH`, `>=20 GB OK`). The dev host had **15.27 GB** free. **Causation proven by changing only the filesystem `storage_path()` points at** — `/home` 15.27 GB → WATCH → FAIL; `/` 55.52 GB → OK, exit 0 → PASS, same commit. The tests pass on any host ≥20 GB and fail below it, so **no CI gate could ever have caught this** (runners are spacious, and `PilotPerformanceSnapshot` was not in the critical filter at all).

**A third test was passing for the wrong reason.** `PilotPerformanceSnapshotCommandTest:248` asserts `exitCodeForStatus(overall) === 1`; on this host `resources=WATCH` supplied that 1 whatever the logs said, and below 10 GB it would have failed with exit 3 (FIX). Same root cause, in scope.

**The obvious fix is measurably wrong — always mutation-test a "make it deterministic" repair.** Recomputing the expectation from the observed sections (`worst(expectedLogStatus, app, database, resources, http)`) looks principled and is a tautology on exactly the hosts that hurt: dropping `$sections['logs']['status']` from the `worst()` call — no log verdict can reach the aggregate or the exit code — was **undetected (21 passed)** under that approach and **fails (1 failed)** after the real fix. When the environment floor is already WATCH it absorbs the missing term.

**Fix: substitute the input, never reinterpret it.** New `App\Services\Monitoring\PilotPerformanceSnapshotDiskProbe` (pass-through to `disk_free_space()`, `false`→`null`); `PilotPerformanceSnapshotService` gains one constructor argument and one changed line in `collectResourceHealth()`. `storage_path()`, `disk_path`, `readMemInfo()`, `sys_getloadavg()` and every threshold untouched; the command resolves from the container with no binding, so production is byte-identical. Tests pin the disk via `pilotSnapshotDiskProbe()` (tests/Pest.php) and keep `overall_status === 'OK'` / `exitCodeForStatus(...) === 1` verbatim. **A `collect()` option or `--no-resources` flag was rejected**: it would put a `STATUS_OK`-emitting branch inside the monitor while the disk is at FIX — a lie-generating path one line from an operator flag. `resources` stays an unconditional argument to `worst()`; unreadable disk stays WATCH + warning.

**Net-new coverage where there was none.** The disk monitor had **zero** tests because the syscall was unstubbable. `PilotPerformanceSnapshotResourceSectionTest` (6) now pins the escalation (5 GB→FIX→exit 3, 15 GB→WATCH→exit 1, 100 GB→OK→exit 0, unreadable→WATCH+warning) and the wiring (container resolves the real probe; **no file outside `app/Services/Monitoring/PilotPerformanceSnapshot*` may name the probe** — a source scan, because a binding registered behind `environment('pilot')` is invisible to a resolve-time assertion under `APP_ENV=testing`). `PilotPerformanceSnapshot` added to the NSF-R011 critical filter on **both** runner variants (they must stay identical).

**Governance.** `sprint:test-plan` fail-closes to FULL REQUIRED SUITE because no regression-matrix category matches `app/Services/Monitoring/**`; that escalation was deliberately **not** softened by adding a category — weakening a fail-closed control to dodge an escalation is the opposite of the point. Durable rules (112): a Monitoring failure is root-caused not normalised; **"pre-existing" requires reproducing the same signature on a pristine worktree at the exact base SHA** — earlier prose is not evidence; an assertion on `overall_status`/exit code must pin every section it is not testing; the disk alarm stays reachable and fail-closed; a contract update keeps every assertion the change does not invalidate, proven by mutation. Rule 112 §R6 tabulates three residual host couplings left unfixed (LegacyRme ingestion capacity 2 GiB — inert; `HealthCheckService` 18% vs warn 15 — **3 points**; self-hosted runner health 16 GB vs min 15 — **1 GB**) so they are not rediscovered as new.

**Verification.** Negative control on a pristine base worktree: `2 failed, 3 skipped, 107 passed`. After: Unit Monitoring+Console **115 passed / 597 assertions / 0 failed**; `--filter=Monitoring` **91 passed** (base 1 failed / 85 passed); `Cicd|Devflow|FullSuiteBaseline|FoundationMonitoring|MonitoringObservability|HealthCheck` 420 passed; `Cicd|Devflow|DedicatedSelfHostedRunner` 289 passed; **PostgreSQL 16.14** pinned container (production parity) Unit 36 + Feature 40 passed with 133 tables migrated — local PG is **18.6** and must not be used, CLAUDE.md already records PG 18 causing self-hosted-only `SQLSTATE[25P02]` failures. Security review CRITICAL 0 / HIGH 0, 3 MEDIUM resolved. `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=DEFERRED_BY_GLOBAL_TEMPORARY_POLICY`.

## MONITORING-LOGS-WATCH-ROOT-CAUSE-1 — the logs WATCH was correct; the logs section can no longer report OK without evidence (2026-08-22)

Branch `feature/monitoring-logs-watch-root-cause-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `test-baseline-monitoring-failures-1-go` @ `84ac695`). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/monitoring-logs-watch-root-cause-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/113-monitoring-logs-watch-truthfulness.mdc` (extends 112). **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no clinical-workflow change.**

**Root cause — the WATCH was CORRECT.** `logs=WATCH` because 5 real `ERROR` events sat inside the 24 h window (contract: 1–20 fresh → WATCH). Sanitised: (1) `SQLSTATE[08006]` Postgres refused `2026-08-21T06:16:25Z`; (2) flysystem `Unable to create a directory` under the private RME consent path `13:07:16Z` — **a genuine application error, already recovered** (a directory left mid-migration by the INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 `www-data`→`daengtisiams` move; a later deploy's ownership normalisation fixed it, and the same consent wrote successfully at `2026-08-22T01:35Z` with both signature PNGs); (3)(4) `tinker`/psysh errors from the previous sprint — event (4) at `2026-08-22 01:02:51 WITA` is exactly the "latest known error" carried in; (5) **a psysh error created by this investigation's own `tinker` probe**, not deleted, which pushed natural recovery from `2026-08-23 01:02:51` to `2026-08-23 14:06:51 WITA` (~13 h). **Natural ageing observed live on production:** fresh fell 5→4 between `06:07:35Z` and `06:18:11Z` as event (1) crossed its boundary at `06:16:25Z`, with no intervention.

**Contract (measured, not assumed):** `pilot:performance-snapshot`, default `--since=24h`, scans the single `storage/logs/laravel.log`, last 2 MiB; `/ERROR|CRITICAL|SQLSTATE|timeout|exception|emergency|fatal/i`; events grouped under a `[Y-m-d H:i:s]` header; fresh = `ts >= now - lookback` (boundary inclusive); 0→OK, 1–20→WATCH, 21–100→INVESTIGATE, >100→FIX; overall = `worst(app,database,resources,http,logs)` — all five verified present. Clock is `now()` in the app timezone (**UTC**), matching Monolog; Monitoring has **zero** `ClinicalClock` references and must not be moved to WITA (Graphify shows `ClinicalClock::now()` as a graph *neighbour* only — both declare `.now()`). **The snapshot is a real production job:** `daengtisiams-pilot-snapshot.timer`, `OnCalendar=Sat *-*-* 19:30:00` UTC, `Persistent=true`, as the `daengtisiams` identity — so "no repo script invokes it" is not evidence it is unscheduled.

**Defects fixed (all reproduced by executing the code, not by reading it):** (D1) an error event whose header timestamp failed `Carbon::parse` was counted in **no** bucket while `timestamp_parse_status` still said `ok` → classifier returned **OK**: a real ERROR produced a green monitor; it also leaked its continuation lines onto the next event. Now `undated_error_like_count` + parse `partial`/`failed` → **WATCH**, state cleared. (D2) `readTail()` returned `''` on a failed `fopen`, indistinguishable from "scanned it, found nothing" → **OK** plus a *fabricated* non-zero `tail_bytes_scanned`; now returns `null` → **WATCH** + warning + `0`. Writing the test exposed that the `fopen` warning is promoted to an `ErrorException` and **aborted the whole snapshot**, so `@filesize`/`@fopen` now let the explicit failure check govern — a monitor that dies on an unreadable log is worse than one that reports WATCH. (D3) an absent log file was **silently** OK; status stays OK deliberately (Laravel creates it on first write; escalating would fire on every fresh checkout = permanent false WATCH) but now always carries a warning + "not verified" reason. (D4) **masking**: 150 fresh events (contractually FIX) returned a flat **WATCH** when 21 orphan lines were present, discarding an already-correct count exactly when the log was noisiest — guard moved inside the `freshCount === 0` branch, so undetermined freshness escalates via `worst()` and never suppresses. (D5) **confirmed false green** — the 2 MiB cap is a **byte** budget, not a time window, so a full-window verdict was answered from a partial scan: reproduced by execution with filler volume the only variable, the same in-window `SQLSTATE[08006]` gives `logs=WATCH` at 13,096 B but **`logs=OK`** at 2,600,096 B with `fresh=0 orphan=0 parse=ok warnings=(none)` (the orphan guard cannot rescue it — 0/1/5/20 orphans all return OK, only ≥21 flips, under an unrelated reason). Fixed by a **window-coverage** check, not by alarming on truncation: new `oldest_scanned_event_at` + `window_fully_covered`; uncovered window with nothing countable → **WATCH**. Negative control pinned — a truncated scan whose tail still reaches past the cutoff stays **OK**, so no permanent false WATCH on busy hosts. **[SUPERSEDED 2026-08-23 by MONITORING-LOG-COVERAGE-ANCHOR-INJECTION-1 — the D5 fix was right that truncation alone must not alarm, and wrong about what may prove coverage.** "The tail reaches past the cutoff" is a fact about the log's *contents*, and it implies nothing about the skipped prefix unless the file is strictly chronological — which nothing enforces. That made the same false green reachable again through content: prepend an in-window ERROR to the negative-control fixture and the monitor answered OK while the error sat unread. Coverage is now decided from the read offset alone, and the permanent-WATCH concern is answered by a bounded, operator-tunable scan budget instead of by a guess. See the MONITORING-LOG-COVERAGE-ANCHOR-INJECTION-1 section below.]**

**Durable rules (113):** R1 a WATCH from a real event is never suppressed — no log truncation/deletion/level-rewrite, no message allowlist, no window shortening, no threshold raise, no section disable; recovery is by ageing or by fixing the runtime. R2 the logs section reports OK only when it actually read the log and actually determined the window is clear — absence of evidence is never evidence of absence. R3 a false WATCH is a defect too; every escalation must be shown not to fire on a healthy log. R4 unparseable noise escalates, never masks. R5 the scan budget is disclosed. R6 operator tooling shares the application log and monitoring counts it — the remedy is not running `tinker` on production, never a psysh filter. R7 monitoring is UTC, not `ClinicalClock`. R8 the snapshot is a scheduled production job.

**Not changed on purpose:** thresholds, window, error pattern, aggregation, clock. **Recorded not fixed:** `Carbon` rollover mis-bucketing (only moves a timestamp later, cannot make a false green) — **this reasoning was WRONG and is superseded by MONITORING-LOG-TIMESTAMP-ROLLOVER-1: month `00` rolls BACKWARD into the previous year (`2026-00-15` → `2025-12-15`), carrying an error out of the window into `historical` where it stops driving the verdict, which is a false green; it is now fixed**; `worst()` degrading an unknown status to OK (unreachable from current callers); the log path is hardcoded rather than resolved from the configured channel — production is `LOG_CHANNEL=stack`/`LOG_STACK=single` so it is correct today, but a switch to `daily` would go permanently green.

**Negative controls:** production 118/118 headers parse under one uniform format with zero invalid components (and 118 matches the snapshot's own `timestamped_lines`); file present, readable, 775 KB ≪ 2 MiB ⇒ **no new escalation can fire on production and its verdict was unchanged**. **Mutation-tested:** restoring D1 fails 4 tests, D4 fails 1, D2 fails 1, D5 fails 1; all reverted → 54 passed. Tests: new `PilotPerformanceSnapshotLogSourceTest` (18). Green: Monitoring **54 passed / 216 assertions**, dependency regression (`SlowQueryAudit|Monitoring|PilotPerformanceSnapshot`) **129 passed / 647 assertions**, 1 pre-existing pgsql-only skip; pint + `git diff --check` clean. `KNOWN_MONITORING_FAILURES = 0` preserved. GO tag on merge: `monitoring-logs-watch-root-cause-1-go`. Deploy note: **no migration/seed**; post-deploy verify with `pilot:performance-snapshot --json` (side-effect free) — a remaining WATCH is correct while a real in-window event has not yet aged out.

## MONITORING-LOG-SOURCE-RESILIENCE-1 — Monitoring reads where the application writes (2026-08-22)

Branch `feature/monitoring-log-source-resilience-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `monitoring-logs-watch-root-cause-1-go` @ `b88be64f58a6692a7c0d60655802d3d702510359`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/monitoring-log-source-resilience-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/114-monitoring-log-source-authority.mdc`; runbook section "Which log files does Monitoring read?" in `docs/runbooks/mon-1-foundation-monitoring-observability-runbook.md`. **Closes residual #3 of MONITORING-LOGS-WATCH-ROOT-CAUSE-1.** **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no clinical change; KTP/NIK not applicable; lookback window (24h), severity bands and the analyzer's parsing/ageing are unchanged.**

**Root cause.** Both verdict-producing monitors read a hardcoded `storage/logs/laravel.log`. That path was correct only by coincidence — production resolves `logging.default=stack → channels=[single] → .../storage/logs/laravel.log`. Flip `LOG_CHANNEL`/`LOG_STACK` to `daily`, or add a stack member, and the application writes errors elsewhere while the monitor keeps reading a file that has stopped moving → `logs=OK` on a failing system. **Reproduced first:** 3 negative controls (error in today's daily file / behind a `stack→[daily]` / in yesterday's file 30s after midnight) all returned `OK` against the old code and `WATCH` after.

**New `App\Services\Monitoring\MonitoringLogSourceResolver`** — sole authority for *which files carry this app's log events*; it does not parse lines or classify health. Reads `config('logging.*')`, **never `env()`** (production runs `config:cache`, where env is not the runtime authority). Expands `stack` recursively with cycle + depth guards; `single` → one file covering all history; `daily` → one file per calendar day intersecting the window, named by Monolog's `{filename}-{date}` applied to the **configured** path with day boundaries in `config('app.timezone')`. Unsupported drivers (syslog/stderr/Slack/custom monolog) are **reported, never dropped**. A `null` channel is exempt — detected by its `NullHandler` class, **not** by the channel being named `null` (the shipped `null` channel's driver is `monolog`). Rotated-file expansion is capped at 31 files (an unbounded `--since=365d` would otherwise open a year of files at 2 MiB each); hitting the cap marks coverage incomplete rather than answering from the part that fit.

**Both monitors share it.** `PilotPerformanceSnapshotService::collectLogSummary()` folds a source *set*: counts sum, statuses take the worse, timestamp extremes widen, coverage is the conjunction, and each source must be read back to its own required reach (window cutoff for `single`; later of cutoff and day-start for a day-file). `FoundationMonitoringStatusService::applicationLogSignal()` (MON-1) consumes the same resolver — it previously answered **GO** for a missing file. **The duplicate `config('foundation_monitoring.paths.laravel_log')` knob was retired**: a second declared path could disagree with the running logger and, being the one actually read, would have won silently and made this fix inert. Do not reintroduce it.

**Fail-closed contract.** Not OK when: no supported source configured; nothing readable actually read; an unsupported member sits in a monitored stack; a `single` file is missing; a present file cannot be opened (no fabricated byte count); any source is truncated short of its required reach; or the rotated-file cap bit. **The one tolerated absence:** a `daily` day-file may be missing when its directory was listable *and* the day is inside the channel's own `days` retention — nothing could have removed it, so "not there" is an observed empty day. **Deliberate supersession:** the previous sprint scored a missing file `OK` + warning; that cannot separate a quiet system from a relocated log, so absence is now **unverified (WATCH)**. Accepted cost: a fresh checkout that never logged reports WATCH — honest, since Monitoring GO has never meant Monitoring green. The metric schema stays stable across verdicts (every key emitted, `null` for unmeasured) so dashboards keep parsing. Counting across sources can only **over**state severity (a stack writing one record to two files counts it twice), never understate — do not replace the sum with a max, which would demote a burst split across files.

**Security posture:** monitored paths come only from trusted config — no route or CLI flag accepts one, and the `log_path` seam on `collect()` is internal/test-only (pinned by a test). New metrics expose channel names + `basename()` only, never full paths; the MON-1 masked error excerpt is unchanged.

**Tests:** new `tests/Unit/Services/Monitoring/MonitoringLogSourceResilienceTest.php` (24) — single/daily/stack/mixed-stack/midnight-rollover/unsupported-member/retention-horizon/unlistable-directory/150-event burst split across sources/partial-scan coverage/resolver units incl. nested + cyclic stacks/config-not-env/path-authority. `KNOWN_MONITORING_FAILURES = 0` preserved (rule 112). Full Suite: `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `DEFERRED_BY_GLOBAL_TEMPORARY_POLICY`. GO tag on merge: `monitoring-log-source-resilience-1-go`. Deploy note: **no migration/seed**; production resolves to one file today, so deployed behaviour is unchanged — the value is that the next `LOG_CHANNEL`/`LOG_STACK` change cannot silently blind the monitor. Verify post-deploy with `pilot:performance-snapshot --json` (side-effect free) and the read-only `php -r` config dump in the MON-1 runbook — **never `php artisan tinker`, which writes real ERROR records** (rule 113 R6).

## MONITORING-LOG-TIMESTAMP-ROLLOVER-1 — an event's age comes from the timestamp the log wrote, not one the parser invented (2026-08-22)

Branch `feature/monitoring-log-timestamp-rollover-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `monitoring-log-source-resilience-1-go` @ `a7622c7`). Doc `docs/sprints/monitoring-log-timestamp-rollover-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/115-monitoring-log-timestamp-authority.mdc`. **Closes the Carbon date-rollover residual — `CODE_CHANGE_REQUIRED=true`, both a false green and a false WATCH were reproduced.** **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no runtime driver change; one file in `app/` changed.**

**Why it survived two sprints:** MONITORING-LOGS-WATCH-ROOT-CAUSE-1 deferred it on the written grounds that rollover *"only ever moves a timestamp later, so it cannot manufacture a false green"*. **That reasoning is wrong**, and the backlog entry told every later reader it was safe. `Carbon` rolls month `00` **backward into the previous year** (`2026-00-15` → `2025-12-15`) and day `00` backward into the previous month (`2026-08-00` → `2026-07-31`). Both the sprint doc and the CLAUDE.md entry are now corrected in place rather than left to mislead again.

**The defect, measured end-to-end.** `Carbon::parse()` is inconsistently permissive: it **throws** on `2026-13-01` / `2026-08-32` / hour `25` / minute `61` (correctly unageable) but silently **normalises** `2026-02-30`→`2026-03-02`, `2026-02-29`(non-leap)→`2026-03-01`, `2026-08-00`→`2026-07-31`, `2026-00-15`→`2025-12-15`. **FALSE GREEN:** on 5 Jan an `ERROR` stamped `[2026-00-15]` reported `historical=1, undated=0, parse=ok` — byte-identical to a genuinely old event — so it contributed nothing and the section could report **OK** while holding an error whose real date is unknowable. **FALSE WATCH:** on 2 Mar an `ERROR` stamped `[2026-02-30]` reported `fresh=1` and published `latest_fresh_error_at=2026-03-02T10:00:00+00:00` — a WATCH on an instant that never occurred, unclearable by fixing anything real. The fabricated date also anchored `oldest_scanned_event_at`, widening a window-coverage claim the scan had not earned. The audit additionally found the header's trailing `[^\]]*` segment honoured arbitrary PHP relative modifiers (`[… 10:00:00 -1 year]` → 2025), an **unbounded** age shift that still reported `parse=ok`.

**The fix (one method).** `extractTimestamp()` captures the literal calendar digits alongside the parsed value and requires the parse to reproduce them exactly (`$timestamp->format('Y-m-d H:i:s') !== $literal → null`), compared in the value's **own** timezone so explicit offsets stay honoured. It is a **faithfulness test, not a format whitelist** — everything that round-trips is accepted unchanged (Laravel `Y-m-d H:i:s` = `LogManager::$dateFormat`, what production writes; Monolog ISO-8601+offset; fractional seconds; offsets; real leap days), and only a value the parser had to *change* is rejected, into the **existing** unageable path that already fails closed (`undated>0` ⇒ WATCH, OK provably unreachable — `PilotPerformanceSnapshotClassifier:192-207`). It closes the relative-modifier class as a by-product. **Never replace this with a list of permitted formats:** a whitelist that guessed wrong would turn every real error into `undated` and cause a permanent, un-clearable WATCH storm.

**Boundary pinned:** `event >= window_start` (**inclusive**) — `now-24:00:00` is INSIDE, `now-24:00:01` is OUTSIDE. **Not changed on purpose:** timezones (`config('app.timezone')='UTC'`; log lines carry no offset so writer and reader share PHP's default by construction, and daily rotation uses the same value — everything already agreed, nothing was "normalised to WITA"); `ClinicalClock` stays unwired from monitoring; explicit offsets/tz names stay honoured (they are faithful instants, not corruption); Carbon mutability (every `subSeconds`/`subDays`/`startOfDay`/`setTimezone` in the stack is already `->copy()`-guarded — no immutability rewrite warranted).

**Recorded, NOT fixed (honest backlog):** ~~`oldest_scanned_event_at` can still be anchored by an injected *valid* ancient benign line (e.g. `[2020-01-01 00:00:00] production.DEBUG:`), converting a truncation WATCH into OK — a log-injection/coverage concern in a different subsystem needing its own reproducer~~ — **CLOSED 2026-08-23 by MONITORING-LOG-COVERAGE-ANCHOR-INJECTION-1. The residual was real, was reproduced end to end, and was a false green, not merely a metadata wobble**; `undated_error_like_count` cannot escalate past WATCH (fail-closed, but severity under-reports); `RestoreDrillEvidenceService::ageHours()` uses `strtotime()` on `completed_at`, same normalisation family, different subsystem (ROLL-5-1A evidence).

**Tests:** new `tests/Unit/Services/Monitoring/PilotPerformanceSnapshotTimestampRolloverTest.php` (29 / 75 assertions) — day/month/year/leap-day rollover, both false-green and false-WATCH directions, the relative-modifier class, the 24h boundary triple, UTC↔WITA display-vs-classification, future timestamps, coverage-anchor integrity, and negative controls for every real format. **Every test freezes the clock (`Carbon::setTestNow`)** — none depends on the real date, midnight, a month end or a year end. **Negative control (load-bearing):** the suite run against the pre-fix analyzer → **12 failed / 17 passed**; post-fix → **29 passed**. The 17 passing in both are the invariants, proving the fix changes nothing it should not. Regression `tests/Unit/Services/Monitoring` + `tests/Unit/Console/PilotPerformanceSnapshotCommandTest` + `tests/Feature/Foundation` → **517 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed** (2629 assertions). `KNOWN_MONITORING_FAILURES = 0` preserved (rule 112). Full Suite: `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `DEFERRED_BY_GLOBAL_TEMPORARY_POLICY` (rule 107 ACTIVE). **CI mapping is explicit:** the suite is named `PilotPerformanceSnapshotTimestampRolloverTest` so it matches the `PilotPerformanceSnapshot` token already in **both** NSF-R011 critical-gate variants, rather than repeating the earlier "suite exists locally, matches no filter" defect. GO tag on merge: `monitoring-log-timestamp-rollover-1-go`. Deploy note: **no migration/seed**; production writes well-formed timestamps so deployed behaviour is unchanged — the value is that a corrupt one can no longer be aged with false confidence. Verify post-deploy with `pilot:performance-snapshot --json` (side-effect free) — **never `php artisan tinker`, which writes real ERROR records** (rule 113 R6).

## MONITORING-LOG-COVERAGE-ANCHOR-INJECTION-1 — log coverage is proven by the bytes actually read, never by a timestamp inside them (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/monitoring-log-coverage-anchor-injection-1`, base `01d2870d` (`monitoring-log-timestamp-rollover-1-go`). Type `RUNTIME_FIX`. **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no runtime driver change.** Closes the residual the previous sprint recorded as honest backlog — and that residual was a **false green**, not a metadata wobble.

**The defect (reproduced by executing the code, not by reading it).** A source larger than the 2 MiB budget is read tail-only, so its **prefix** is skipped. Coverage was then decided by `$sourceCovered = ! $truncated || (Carbon::parse($oldest_scanned_event_at) <= $source->requiredCoverageFrom($cutoff))` — i.e. by the oldest event timestamp *inside the scanned tail*, on the unstated assumption that the file is strictly chronological. That made log **content** the authority over bytes the monitor never opened. Frozen clock `2026-08-22 12:00:00`, 24h window, a real in-window `pilot.ERROR` planted in the unread prefix: an all-ancient-`INFO` tail → `window_fully_covered=true`, `fresh=0`, **`logs=OK`**, `overall=OK`, reason *"No fresh error events within lookback window."*; the same file with **fresh** filler → WATCH. And the decisive one — fresh filler plus **one** injected `[2019-01-01 00:00:00]` line → back to **OK**. **Two files differing by a single line gave WATCH and OK.** The classifier was never at fault: `PilotPerformanceSnapshotClassifier` already returns WATCH for `!$windowFullyCovered`, and that flag is read only on the zero-count branch — it was handed a `true` it should never have been given.

**Reachability (mechanism verified; scope stated honestly).** Any line merely *starting* `[Y-m-d H:i:s…]` is an event header. Laravel builds `new LineFormatter(null, $dateFormat, true, true, true)` (`Illuminate/Log/LogManager.php:488`) — the third argument is `$allowInlineLineBreaks` — so a newline inside a **message or a context array value** becomes a real physical line. Verified by formatting a record through that exact construction: a context value `"connection failed\n[2019-01-01 00:00:00] production.INFO: recovered"` produced two physical lines, both matching the header shape, and the analyzer returned `oldest_scanned_event_at = 2019-01-01`. `QueryException` messages embed SQL + bindings, and several services put an unbounded `$e->getMessage()` into a log context array. **Not claimed:** a proven end-to-end attacker-controlled path from an unauthenticated production request. It is not needed — Monitoring must stay correct against syntactically valid content it did not expect, whatever the origin (operator, import, vendor error string, multi-process interleaving, restored/concatenated log, or a line written with an explicit past timestamp).

**The fix.** `$sourceCovered = $coverage->isComplete()` — true only when the read began at byte 0. New value object `App\Services\Monitoring\MonitoringLogScanCoverage` is built from `fileBytes`/`scanStartOffset`/`bytesScanned` and **takes no timestamp, event or line**, so the comparison cannot be reintroduced by accident (a test asserts every public parameter is `int` and the file never mentions `Carbon`). `readTail()` now returns content **plus** the coverage, produced where the real offset is known — the caller's second `filesize()` is gone, because two stats of a file that changes underneath them is how coverage drifts from the read. `MonitoringLogSource::requiredCoverageFrom()` is **removed** (its only caller was the defect; a comment records what it was and why it went — `coversFrom` stays for the structural absent-day rule). `oldest_scanned_event_at` **stays**, documented as an observation that decides nothing — the goal was never to delete it. The scan budget becomes `foundation_monitoring.log_scan.max_source_bytes`, clamped to **[64 KiB, 64 MiB]**, default unchanged at 2 MiB: fail-closed needs a lever, and the "raise the scan budget" advice already in the warning had no knob behind it; it is clamped because buying coverage must not become an unbounded read. Warnings now name the skipped byte count and the config key. **Also fixed in passing** (found by reviewing the function being changed): `stream_get_contents($handle) ?: ''` collapsed a *failed read* — and the literal one-byte string `"0"` — into `''`, which for a file under the budget is indistinguishable from "read it all, found nothing" → coverage complete, zero events, **OK**, plus a fabricated non-zero `tail_bytes_scanned`. The read failure now returns `null` and fails closed.

**Negative controls (load-bearing).** Reintroducing the content-anchored expression makes **18 of the 35** new tests fail — the suite detects the defect rather than describing it; restored, 35 pass. An A/B differential pins that two files differing only in how old their tail events look must reach the same coverage verdict. Recoverability is pinned in both directions, because a monitor that can never go green is its own defect (rule 113 R3): fully read + clean → OK, +1 fresh error → WATCH, +150 → **FIX**, budget raised above file size → OK again. Exactly one existing test asserted that a truncated scan may report OK (`'still reports OK when a truncated scan did reach back past the cutoff'`) — it was the defect stated benignly, and it is **rewritten in place with its original reasoning quoted and answered**, not deleted; its stated motive (no permanent WATCH on busy hosts) is now met by the budget lever instead of by a guess.

**Not changed:** the 24h boundary (`event >= window_start`, inclusive), severity thresholds (0→OK, 1–20→WATCH, 21–100→INVESTIGATE, >100→FIX), timestamp faithfulness (rule 115), the absent-day tolerance, the resolver's stack/daily expansion and caps, Monitoring's UTC clock, and `MonitoringLogSourceResolver` path safety. **`FoundationMonitoringStatusService::applicationLogSignal()` is deliberately untouched** — it is a *separate* signal counting errors in the last 200 **lines**, computes no coverage, reads neither `window_fully_covered` nor `oldest_scanned_event_at`, and shares only the source resolver; it is therefore unaffected, and no second content-controlled coverage authority was left behind. A repo-wide audit confirmed there is exactly **one** coverage calculation in the codebase, and `source_coverage_complete` has **zero** readers in `app/` (output-only telemetry) — only `window_fully_covered` is load-bearing.

**Durable rules (116):** R1 coverage is a fact about the read, never about its contents — *an event cannot testify about bytes nobody read*. R2 event time and scan coverage are separate authorities and must not be conflated. R3 the coverage decision must be structurally unable to see content (do not add a `CarbonInterface` to `MonitoringLogScanCoverage`). R4 a partially read required source is never OK; do not substitute a monotonic-ordering argument for a read — Laravel logs are written by several processes and may be appended, restored or concatenated. R5 caps are safety limits, and reaching one costs coverage; the budget lever is clamped. R6 multi-source coverage is conjunctive — one source never certifies another. R7 valid-but-unexpected content must not manufacture a claim. R8 fix what is allowed to *decide*, never which payload is in the reproducer (ignoring `INFO` or trusting only `ERROR` would leave content in control and merely raise the exploit's price). R9 both directions are pinned.

**Residuals — recorded, not fixed:** log injection itself remains possible (`allowInlineLineBreaks: true` is Laravel's own formatter behaviour; changing it is a global logging change far outside a Monitoring fix) — a forged header can still perturb `oldest_scanned_event_at`, `timestamped_lines` and stack-grouping counts, but can no longer buy coverage, and the direction it can still push a verdict (adding a fake fresh error) fails safe toward WATCH; `undated_error_like_count` still cannot escalate past WATCH (MONITORING-UNDATED-SEVERITY-ESCALATION-1); `RestoreDrillEvidenceService::ageHours()` still uses `strtotime()` (RESTORE-DRILL-TIMESTAMP-FAITHFULNESS-1) — **superseded 2026-08-23: this residual was real and is now CLOSED.** The audit found it was not merely "permissive-looking": it was a decision defect that let 13 invalid literals reach `GO`, including a relative modifier on a genuinely 20-month-old drill and any future-dated timestamp (clamped to age 0.0). See the RESTORE-DRILL-TIMESTAMP-FAITHFULNESS-1 section below. Separately noted: `MonitoringLogSourceResilienceTest` matches no CI critical-gate token — a **pre-existing** gap, not introduced here, and the new suite covers the same multi-source properties under the matched `PilotPerformanceSnapshot` token. **Superseded 2026-08-23 by CI-MONITORING-CRITICAL-TOKEN-COVERAGE-1:** that reasoning was insufficient. Equivalent properties covered by an incidentally-matched sibling is additional defence, never the authority — and the sibling's own selection rested on the same filename coincidence, so a rename would have removed both. Measured at the base SHA, the critical filter selected 2252 tests across 130 files and ZERO from this suite. The gap is now CLOSED: `config/ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` declares the suites that must run and `SelfHostedRunnerScanner::criticalGateSuiteCoveragePosture()` fails the gate on any drift.

## RESTORE-DRILL-TIMESTAMP-FAITHFULNESS-1 — Restore Drill evidence age is computed only from a faithful timestamp (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/restore-drill-timestamp-faithfulness-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `a51e536`, which is also `monitoring-log-coverage-anchor-injection-1-go` and was the live VPS HEAD; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/restore-drill-timestamp-faithfulness-1.md`; runbook §7.1 `docs/runbooks/roll-5-backup-restore-drill-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/117-restore-drill-timestamp-authority.mdc`. **No migration, no route, no permission, no policy/middleware change; ledger/RME/clinical foundations untouched; no restore was performed anywhere.**

Closes the residual the previous sprint recorded but deliberately left open: `RestoreDrillEvidenceService::ageHours()` used `strtotime()`. **It was a real DECISION defect, reproduced end to end before being fixed** — 13 literals that are not valid evidence produced `GO`. Three defects compounded: (1) `max(0.0, …)` clamped a negative age to zero, so **any** future instant reported as the freshest value representable; (2) `strtotime()` normalises invalid calendar dates in **both** directions (Feb 30 → Mar 2 forward, month-zero → previous December backward — the same both-ways trap MONITORING-LOG-TIMESTAMP-ROLLOVER-1 found), rolls out-of-range fields over, ignores trailing junk, and **executes relative modifiers**, so `"2025-01-01T00:00:00Z +2 years"` — a genuinely 20-month-old drill — resolved into the future and via (1) became maximally fresh; (3) on parse failure the age fell back to `filemtime()`, so malformed evidence written today read as a drill run today. The chain is `completed_at → ageHours → stale → restore_drill_evidence signal → FiveBranchRolloutReadinessService::baseReadinessStatus/decideStage → ROLL-5 Stage-1/2/3 clearance`, so this decided rollout readiness, not just a display string.

**Canonical grammar (discovered, never guessed):** exactly one producer form — `scripts/rollout-restore-drill.sh:119` `date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ`, matching the runbook, the template doc, and all four existing test fixtures ⇒ `Y-m-d\TH:i:s\Z`, UTC, second precision. No offsets, fractional seconds, or epoch integers (no producer emits them). **Production held no restore-drill evidence at all** (both candidate paths ABSENT, verified read-only), so narrowing the parser invalidated nothing historical — and production restore-drill readiness is honestly `WATCH — evidence absent`, which was reported rather than manufactured.

**Fix.** New `App\Support\Foundation\RestoreDrillTimestampParser`: format-exact `createFromFormat('!Y-m-d\TH:i:s\Z', …, UTC)` plus an **exact byte-for-byte round-trip** — anything the parser had to normalise, roll over, or ignore to make legal is rejected. Because the canonical form declares UTC in-band, the round-trip compares like with like and can never mistake timezone normalisation for corruption. `ageHours()` → `evidenceAge()` returns `[trust_state, ?age]` with three unageable states — `missing` / `unparseable` / `future` — each yielding a `null` age **and** an explicit `evidence_timestamp_<state>` issue forcing `WATCH`. **That issue is load-bearing:** the staleness test is `age !== null && age > threshold`, so a `null` age is not "stale" and would otherwise have fallen straight through into `GO`. The `filemtime` fallback is deleted (mtime is an unrelated filesystem fact any copy/rsync/deploy resets). New config `rollout_readiness.thresholds.restore_drill_future_skew_minutes` (default 5, env `ROLLOUT_RESTORE_DRILL_FUTURE_SKEW_MINUTES`) bounds ordinary clock jitter only — it is not a licence to accept future-dated evidence. `timestamp_status` (a 4-value enum, no PII) is surfaced in the sanitized details and `--json` output.

**Boundary pinned inclusively:** `age_hours <= restore_drill_stale_hours` (720h/30d) is fresh; strictly greater is stale.

**Deliberately NOT merged with Monitoring.** Monitoring's log analyzer must accept several real logging formats (Laravel `Y-m-d H:i:s`, Monolog ISO-8601 with offsets, fractional seconds) and therefore correctly uses parse-then-verify-the-digits; Restore Drill has one producer and one format, so its correct contract is format-exact + round-trip. A `UniversalTimestampParser` would force one domain to accept input its own producer never emits. Two domains, two grammars, two parsers.

**Scope boundaries:** `started_at` feeds no decision and was left alone (smallest correct fix). ClinicalClock untouched — Restore Drill freshness is operational *instant* arithmetic, not clinical business-date semantics. Untrusted ⇒ `WATCH`, never `FAIL`; `FAIL`/`unsafe` stays reserved for production overwrite, production-like environment, leaked secret/PII, or a failed drill. **A parser sprint never authorises restoring over the live database**, and evidence is never edited to turn a verdict green.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/Foundation/RestoreDrillTimestampFaithfulnessTest.php` — **56 passed / 114 assertions**, every one with `Carbon::setTestNow()` frozen (no assertion depends on the wall clock or a nearby calendar rollover; day/month/year/leap-day rollovers each pinned to a continuous 2.0h age). **Mutation control: 28 of 56 fail when the fix is reverted** to `strtotime`+`filemtime`+clamp; zero mutation residue.

**CI coverage:** the new suite matched no `NSF-R011 Critical Test Gate` token, so its protection would never have run in CI. One token `RestoreDrill` was appended to **both** runner variants (GitHub-hosted + self-hosted, which must stay identical per CICD-CTRL-3), selecting 76 tests — the 56 new plus the existing `RestoreDrillEvidenceServiceTest` / `RolloutRestoreDrill*` / `RolloutStageOneClearanceTest` family. Required by this sprint, not opportunistic; same precedent as LEGACY-RME-PDF-1A. Green: new 56, `--filter=RestoreDrill` 76, `tests/Feature/Foundation` 466 passed/4 skipped/2387 assertions, `Monitoring|PilotPerformanceSnapshot` 215 passed. pint (whole repo) + `git diff --check` clean; manifest/devflow/shared-service/ci-runtime-control gates all GO.

**Residuals — recorded, not fixed:** `undated_error_like_count` still cannot escalate past WATCH (`MONITORING-UNDATED-SEVERITY-ESCALATION-1`, deliberately untouched — it needs its own severity/product contract); `MonitoringLogSourceResilienceTest` still matches no CI critical-gate token (**pre-existing**, not introduced here, and out of scope for a Restore Drill parser sprint). **Superseded 2026-08-23 by CI-MONITORING-CRITICAL-TOKEN-COVERAGE-1:** that reasoning was insufficient. Equivalent properties covered by an incidentally-matched sibling is additional defence, never the authority — and the sibling's own selection rested on the same filename coincidence, so a rename would have removed both. Measured at the base SHA, the critical filter selected 2252 tests across 130 files and ZERO from this suite. The gap is now CLOSED: `config/ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` declares the suites that must run and `SelfHostedRunnerScanner::criticalGateSuiteCoveragePosture()` fails the gate on any drift.

## MONITORING-UNDATED-SEVERITY-ESCALATION-1 — Unageable ERROR Evidence Carries Severity Weight (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/monitoring-undated-severity-escalation-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `restore-drill-timestamp-faithfulness-1-go` @ `09a02e3875654ea686a02d434f0b318229258958`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/monitoring-undated-severity-escalation-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/118-monitoring-undated-severity-escalation.mdc`; rule 115 R9 superseded **in place** (old reasoning retained, not deleted). **No migration, no route, no permission, no schema, no runtime driver change; one method changed.**

**Root cause: truncated severity ladder — a decision defect, not a display defect.** Rule 113 R2 and rule 115 R9 pinned that an unageable ERROR may never read as OK. Both describe a **floor**; nothing anywhere asserted a ceiling, and no rung above the first was ever extended to the undated bucket. Reproduced against the unmodified classifier at the base SHA: `undated=1` and `undated=1000` both returned WATCH — magnitude ignored entirely. Consequence: **150 error events with valid timestamps → FIX; the identical 150 with corrupt timestamps → WATCH** (two severity levels bought by corruption in a date field). Mixed evidence understated too: `fresh=15, undated=100` (up to 115 in-window events) → WATCH, and `fresh=20, undated=1` → WATCH while `fresh=21` alone is INVESTIGATE. `UNDER_SEVERITY_REPRODUCED=true`; `FALSE_GREEN_REPRODUCED=false` — OK was already unreachable, so the defect was false *calm*, never a false green.

**The measured premise (not assumed):** `undated_error_like_count` and `fresh_error_like_count` are produced by the **same event-grouping pass** and carry the **same unit** — one error-like log event, verified 1:1 at n=1/20/21/150 by running the real analyzer over synthetic tails, with a double-count probe confirming an undated event lands in that bucket and no other. The buckets differ in whether the monitor can say *when*, never in whether an error happened. **Two axes kept separate:** error-existence confidence is equal; timestamp confidence is absent in one. The defect was collapsing the second into the first.

**Contract:** since the monitor cannot rule out that an unageable event falls inside the window, the fail-closed reading is that it does, so the counts are **summed onto the canonical in-window ladder** — `errorEvidenceCount = fresh + undated`; `0`→OK, `1..20`→WATCH, `21..100`→INVESTIGATE, `>100`→FIX. **Not a new contract:** the ladder's first rung was already applied (`undated > 0 ⇒ WATCH` is numerically identical to `fresh >= 1 ⇒ WATCH`); only the higher rungs were missing. Rejected on evidence: *independent worst-of* (understates `15+15` = up to 30 in-window events as WATCH — rule 113 R4's failure mode relocated) and *separate higher undated thresholds* (would discount evidence the monitor has no basis to discount — the exact false confidence this sprint removes). **Deliberately not done:** `criticalFreshCount` stays fresh-only — the analyzer tests CRITICAL_PATTERN only on ageable events, so there is no measured undated critical count and inventing one would assert severity never observed.

**Every change in the decision matrix is an escalation; nothing is downgraded.** Escalated: `0/21`→INVESTIGATE, `0/101`→FIX, `0/150`→FIX, `15/100`→FIX, `coverage-incomplete + 150`→FIX. Unchanged: all fresh-only rungs, `0/0`→OK, `0/1`→WATCH, `0/20`→WATCH, `5/5`→WATCH, the R4 pin (`150 fresh/crit 20/orphan 21`→FIX), orphan-only WATCH, coverage-incomplete WATCH, and missing/unreadable/unsupported source (fails closed **before** classification, so undated can never downgrade it). Monotonicity proven **exhaustively over a 9×9 count grid on both axes**, not at hand-picked points.

**Security — no new alert-DoS primitive.** Reaching the undated bucket requires three gates: well-formed Laravel header shape, a **failed** faithfulness round-trip, and `ERROR_PATTERN`. Measured: corrupt-date + `INFO` → 0; plain text containing `ERROR` → orphan, not undated; malformed bracket → orphan. So undated demands **more** structure than fresh, and forging N well-formed ERROR headers is *easier* and **already** reached FIX before this sprint — pinned by a test asserting the undated verdict is never worse than the valid-dated one at equal volume. `CLASSIFIER PRIMITIVE CONFIRMED`; `ATTACKER-CONTROLLED PRODUCTION PATH NOT ESTABLISHED` (no such path demonstrated, none claimed). **Lifecycle:** undated evidence cannot age out (no usable age) but clears when the line leaves the physically scanned tail via rotation; nothing is persisted, so no un-clearable state is created. Named residual: a parser regression would now produce a FIX storm rather than a WATCH storm — which raises the cost of breaking rule 115 R2, and does not license relaxing it.

**Downstream:** `pilot:performance-snapshot` JSON/weekly evidence now reflects undated volume (intended); `resolveExitCode` returns SUCCESS unless `--fail-on-watch` is passed, and **no repo script, deploy step, or CI job invokes the command**, so escalation cannot block a deploy. **MON-1 `applicationLogSignal` deliberately unchanged** — it counts `.ERROR:` tail *lines* with no timestamp/ageing concept, so it has no undated blind spot, and the two signals answer different questions (one authority per question; do not "unify" them). `SlowQueryAudit*` untouched. **Not changed:** timestamp parser/faithfulness, `MonitoringLogScanCoverage` physical byte-offset authority, `MonitoringLogSourceResolver` (single/daily/stack, fail-closed), fresh thresholds, critical escalation, lookback window, retention, 2 MiB budget, `overall_status = worst(...)`, analyzer metric schema.

**Independent adversarial review:** a reviewer extracted the real pre-fix class at the base SHA and ran a differential harness — **216,000-case production-domain grid: 0 false greens, 0 downgrades, 0 reason drift; 3,294,912-case monotonicity sweep: 0 violations**; no CRITICAL, no HIGH. Over non-negative inputs `errorEvidenceCount === 0` is *strictly narrower* than the old `freshCount === 0` gate, so the OK path became harder to reach, not easier. Two follow-ups **fixed in-sprint**: (1) **reason accuracy** — a mix (`fresh=5, undated=1`) reported "freshness is unknown", hiding positively-confirmed in-window events behind a corruption story, and "some carry unparseable timestamps" under-stated the all-undated case; the reason now has **three static-literal branches** (fresh-only / all-undated / mixed) with the fresh-only wording byte-identical; (2) **input robustness** — summing two variables lost the accidental immunity the single-variable zero test had, so a cancelling pair (`fresh=1, undated=-1`) would have read as no-evidence → OK; each term is now floored at zero independently (unreachable from the analyzer, whose counters only increment from zero, but a latent footgun the old shape lacked). One finding **accepted and documented**: the **persistence asymmetry** — a fresh alert self-clears by ageing, an undated one clears only on rotation, so escalation converts a self-clearing WATCH into a FIX that persists until the lines leave the scanned tail; accepted deliberately, since the alternative is discounting error evidence the monitor cannot date.

Tests: new `tests/Unit/Services/Monitoring/PilotPerformanceSnapshotUndatedSeverityTest.php` (22) — named to match the existing `PilotPerformanceSnapshot` token so it is selected by the **Critical gate in both runner variants** with no CI token change. Evidence: pre-fix `6 failed/11 passed` (the 11 are genuine negative controls, not tautologies); post-fix **22 passed/1747 assertions**; **mutation control** restoring the pre-fix ceiling fails **10 of 22**, then removed and green re-verified; Monitoring regression `PilotPerformanceSnapshot|RestoreDrill|MonitoringLog` **243 passed, 0 failed** (`KNOWN_MONITORING_FAILURES=0` holds); dependency regression across the Graphify blast radius **362 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed**. Full Suite **not run** — Rule 107 ACTIVE, `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=DEFERRED_BY_GLOBAL_TEMPORARY_POLICY`. Deploy note: **no migration/seed**; production verification is read-only via `pilot:performance-snapshot --json` (never `tinker`, never synthetic log lines).

## CICD-CRITICAL-GATE-FILE-GET-CONTENTS-WARN-1 — Critical Gate warnings are attributable and judged (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/cicd-critical-gate-file-get-contents-warn-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `monitoring-undated-severity-escalation-1-go` @ `60b4ce3`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/cicd-critical-gate-file-get-contents-warn-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/119-critical-gate-warning-contract.mdc`; runbook section appended to `docs/runbooks/ci-runtime-control-runbook.md`. **No migration, no permission, no route, no product runtime change.** The objective was never "make CI output visually clean" — it was to make the gate's warnings attributable, actionable, deterministic and truthful.

**Reproduced from the authoritative log** (run `32616352306`, job `97137843814`): **128 WARN / 1 PASS across 129 files**, `Tests: 2222 warnings, 9 passed (9328 assertions)`, `critical_test_exit_status=0`.

**ROOT CAUSE (evidenced, not inferred).** Every warning is ONE PHP warning: `file_get_contents(<base_path>/…): Failed to open stream` raised at `vendor/vlucas/phpdotenv/src/Store/File/Reader.php:73`, reached through `Dotenv->safeLoad()` ← `Illuminate\Foundation\Bootstrap\LoadEnvironmentVariables::bootstrap()` ← `Application->bootstrapWith()`. The target is the **application environment file** at the repository root. It is **OPTIONAL by framework design** and deliberately never committed (INFRA-SEC-ENV-1), so all six app-booting CI jobs configure the application through their own `env:` block — but the framework still resolves the path and reads it on **every** application boot. **That is exactly 128 of 129:** every test that boots the application warned; the one file that never boots it — `Tests\Unit\Services\Monitoring\PilotPerformanceSnapshotClassifierTest`, which has **no `uses(TestCase::class)`** — passed, and its 9 tests are precisely the `9 passed`. The gate exited 0 because PHPUnit records a PHP warning as a *warning*, not a failure, and nothing ever judged the count. **Pest truncates the warning to terminal width**, which is why CI logs alone never yielded a path; recover it locally by booting the app under an error handler that prints message + backtrace. `phpdotenv` already writes `@\file_get_contents`, but PHPUnit's `ErrorHandler` honours suppression only for files on its own exclude list, so a third-party suppressed read still reaches the test result.

**The retracted Vite-manifest theory stays retracted and is now explained:** `tests/TestCase.php` calls `withoutVite()`, the gate never builds the frontend, no manifest path appears in any warning — and "the same tests pass locally" held only because a developer machine HAS an environment file. No `npm run build` was added.

**FIX AT THE CAUSAL BOUNDARY, NOT ON THE OUTPUT.** Every CI job that boots the application now writes an **empty** environment file (new step `Provision the CI environment file`, placed before every test invocation in all 6 jobs, truncating so a persistent self-hosted workspace cannot carry a stale one). That is the accurate representation of the state CI is already in — the read succeeds and yields **zero variables**. **Measured behaviour-neutral:** resolved-config hash identical with and without the file (`5397874e…`); faithful CI simulation (with the job's own `APP_KEY`) went `511 warnings, 9 passed` → `520 passed` with an **identical 4040 assertions**. The tracked example file is deliberately **NOT** copied — that would inject values CI never intended. Nothing is suppressed: no `@`, no stderr filter, no warning-text allowlist, no PHPUnit suppression toggle.

**SECOND DEFECT — the gate had no opinion about warnings.** A 2222-warning baseline made a genuinely new warning indistinguishable. New `config/ci_runner.php` → `critical_gate_warning_contract` (**`expected_warning_count = 0`**, deliberately **no** text allowlist), new plain unit-testable `App\Support\Cicd\CriticalGateWarningContract`, new `php artisan ci:assert-critical-gate-warning-contract` (`--log`/`--expected`/`--json`), wired into **both** critical variants. **Real failure detection keeps STRICT PRECEDENCE:** the step exits on a non-zero test status *before* the contract is consulted, so the contract can never turn a red gate green. The contract itself models five distinct resource states — missing / unreadable / read-failed / valid-empty / read — and **fails closed** on all non-readable ones, plus on a missing summary line and on zero tests reported. A read failure is never folded into an empty string.

**Audited, not rewritten:** three pre-existing app files use a suppressed read and are pinned as an **exact set** (a new adopter fails the guard). `PilotPerformanceSnapshotService::readMemInfo()` is the **reference shape** (`is_readable()` then explicit `=== false`); `RestoreDrillEvidenceService` casts a failed read to string so an unreadable file reports "invalid JSON" rather than "unreadable" but was **verified to fail closed** (left unchanged — RESTORE-DRILL-TIMESTAMP-FAITHFULNESS-1 is a preserved foundation); `ArchitectureUiGovernanceCheckCommand` runs 74 presence probes where absent and unreadable are deliberately the same outcome.

**Durable rules (mirror 119, R1–R8):** a recurring CI warning is never normalised as baseline noise until its emitter, target resource and exit-code effect are evidenced · required gates hold **zero unexplained warnings** and the baseline is never raised to absorb one · fix at the causal boundary — `@`/`error_reporting(0)`/`2>/dev/null`/`grep -v warning`/broad PHPUnit suppression toggles are all banned and test-enforced across every workflow, `scripts/ci/*.sh` and `phpunit.xml` · missing/unreadable/read-failed/empty/valid are **five** states, never collapsed, always fail-closed · diagnostics never obscure the authoritative exit code · the environment-file state is declared, never populated from the example file in CI · **the Critical Gate does not require frontend build artifacts** · any gate assertion must exist in **both** runner variants.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/Cicd/CriticalGateWarningContractTest.php` (21). **6 mutation controls all red** (provisioning removed · contract command removed · failure precedence removed · suppression marker added · baseline raised to 1 · read failure folded into empty), tree restores clean. Green: Cicd 220, Foundation consumers 43 (+2 skipped); `foundation:ci-runtime-control-check --strict`/`cicd-enterprise-gate-check`/`enterprise-documentation-check`/`security-compliance-check`/`roadmap-check --strict` all GO; release evidence capture+check (ci) exit 0; workflow YAML valid; `bash -n` clean; pint + `git diff --check` clean. **Explicitly left open:** `CI-MONITORING-CRITICAL-TOKEN-COVERAGE-1` is NOT absorbed — the cause was the application bootstrap's environment read, not the token classifier.

## CI-MONITORING-CRITICAL-TOKEN-COVERAGE-1 — mandatory critical suites are selected by an explicit registry, never a filename coincidence (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/ci-monitoring-critical-token-coverage-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `cicd-critical-gate-file-get-contents-warn-1-go` @ `5528ba97`, tag object `792d74ad`). Doc `docs/sprints/ci-monitoring-critical-token-coverage-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/120-critical-gate-mandatory-suite-coverage.mdc`. **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no runtime driver change; no product request path touched.**

**Root cause — `CRITICAL-SELECTION COVERAGE-BY-COINCIDENCE DEFECT`, reproduced before being fixed.** The NSF-R011 Critical Test Gate selects tests with a fixed `--filter` allowlist that is identical in both runner variants; the classifier decides only *whether* the gate runs, never *what* it runs. The only contract over that allowlist (`config/ci_runner.critical_gate_required_filters`, CICD-CTRL-3D) asks **"is this TOKEN present?"** — a question that cannot see the failure that matters, because a suite whose name no token matches is simply absent and absence looks exactly like green. Measured with the real runner at the base SHA: the critical filter selected **2252 tests across 130 files and ZERO from `MonitoringLogSourceResilienceTest`**. All eight suites in `tests/Unit/Services/Monitoring` ran **only because their filenames begin with `PilotPerformanceSnapshot`**; the one pinning that the monitor reads where the application actually writes (and fails closed on a missing/unreadable source) does not carry that prefix, so it ran only in the Full Suite — which is deferred under rule 107. `RENAME_GAP_BEFORE=true` (renaming any sibling out of the prefix would have removed its coverage as silently); `CRITICAL_SELECTION_FALSE_GREEN_BEFORE=true`.

**Why "add a token" was rejected.** Measured, not assumed: a bare `Monitoring` token selects 2346 tests / **146 files — 15 unrelated**, matched through the word appearing in Pest test DESCRIPTIONS (`--filter` matches case-insensitively against the full `Class::method`, and Pest builds that name from the file path, so the namespace carries the directory and the suffix carries the description). The narrow `MonitoringLogSource` token is bounded (+1 file) but still leaves coverage resting on what a file is called. Neither closes the defect *class*.

**Fix — extends the EXISTING authority, no second registry.** New `config/ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` declares the test FILES that must actually run (10 entries, each annotated with the contract it carries). New `SelfHostedRunnerScanner::criticalGateSuiteCoveragePosture()` derives the name PHPUnit filters against from each declared path and requires a token in **every** critical variant to match it; it FAILS on no-token-match, missing file (stale registry), single-variant coverage, an empty registry, or an unreadable workflow. Wired into `SelfHostedRunnerGovernanceService` as `CICDCTRL3-CRITICAL-SUITE-COVERAGE` (a break makes the decision FAIL), and enforced a second time by `tests/Feature/Cicd/CriticalGateSuiteCoverageTest.php`, which the `Cicd` token already selects — so the contract runs **inside the gate it protects**. The narrow token is added to both variants. Matching is a case-insensitive **literal** substring test: a literal hit guarantees a real PHPUnit selection, while a token that could only match through regex syntax is reported unselected — the check errs toward FAIL, never toward an unsubstantiated coverage claim. `RENAME_GAP_AFTER=false` — a rename either keeps the suite selected or fails the gate.

**Durable rules (120):** R1 a mandatory critical contract is selected by an explicit registry, never a filename/token coincidence. R2 it must be selected DIRECTLY — equivalent coverage in another incidentally-matched suite is defence, not authority. R3 a rename/move either preserves selection or FAILS; it never silently drops. R4 a stale registry entry fails closed. R5 an empty mandatory selection is not success. R6 coverage is verified per variant, never averaged. R7 critical scope stays bounded — do not promote a directory to avoid registry maintenance. R8 the Selective Module Gate and the (deferred) Full Suite are not substitutes. R9 adding a suite to `tests/Unit/Services/Monitoring` requires declaring it (directory completeness is asserted).

**Superseded:** the earlier "the new suite covers the same multi-source properties under the matched `PilotPerformanceSnapshot` token" reasoning (recorded in MONITORING-LOG-COVERAGE-ANCHOR-INJECTION-1 and RESTORE-DRILL-TIMESTAMP-FAITHFULNESS-1) is insufficient and is marked superseded in place — the sibling's own selection rested on the same coincidence.

**Selection cost:** 2252→**2290** tests (+38), 130→**132** files (+2), **0 removed**; both additions accounted for (the missing mandatory suite 24 + this sprint's contract suite 14). `OVER_SELECTION_DETECTED=false`. **Deliberately NOT promoted:** `tests/Feature/Foundation/FoundationMonitoring*` and the Sprint28/29/42/43 monitoring suites cover the read-only MON-1 console and historical planning docs, not the monitor's verdict — a recorded decision, not an omission.

**Preserved:** warning contract (expected warning count 0, no allowlist, no suppression, test exit status keeps strict precedence); `KNOWN_MONITORING_FAILURES=0` (rule 112); restore-drill timestamp authority (rule 117); all clinical foundations. `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `DEFERRED_BY_GLOBAL_TEMPORARY_POLICY`.

**Residual — recorded, not fixed:** `RestoreDrillEvidenceService` casts a failed read to string and reports it as invalid JSON; it fails closed, so it is not a correctness risk. Tracked as `RESTORE-DRILL-EVIDENCE-READ-STATE-1`, deliberately not absorbed.

## RESTORE-DRILL-EVIDENCE-READ-STATE-1 — Restore Drill Evidence Read-State Contract (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/restore-drill-evidence-read-state-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `ci-monitoring-critical-token-coverage-1-go` @ `5478d72`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/restore-drill-evidence-read-state-1.md`; runbook §7.2 `docs/runbooks/roll-5-backup-restore-drill-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-restore-drill-evidence-read-state.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (RUNTIME_FIX). **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no frontend change; ledger/RME/clinical untouched; production evidence never mutated and no live restore performed.**

**Root cause (reproduced on the base commit, not assumed).** `RestoreDrillEvidenceService` read evidence as `$raw = (string) @file_get_contents($found);`. `file_get_contents` returns `false` on failure, the cast turns that into `''`, and `''` is indistinguishable from a file that genuinely held nothing — so `json_decode` saw empty input and **every upstream fault was reported as "JSON tidak dapat diurai"**, including faults where not one byte was read. There was no `is_readable()` check anywhere in the path, and `locateEvidence()` skipped `filesize() === 0` candidates, so a zero-byte file was reported as *no file at all*. Twelve fixtures run against unmodified base code as uid 1000 (so `chmod 000` genuinely denies the read) produced four conflations: `EMPTY→ABSENT`, `UNREADABLE→INVALID_JSON`, `READ_FAILED→INVALID_JSON`, `VALID_JSON_NOT_OBJECT→INVALID_JSON`. **`FALSE_GO_REPRODUCED=false`** — every one of those states was already non-GO, so this is a state-integrity/observability defect, never a false-green one.

**Already known and accepted.** `tests/Feature/Cicd/CriticalGateWarningContractTest.php` carried a note that this service "casts a failed read to a string, so an unreadable evidence file reports 'invalid JSON' rather than 'unreadable'", judged acceptable because it still failed closed. The fail-closed half was correct and was re-measured; the half that does not hold up is that a vaguer reason is therefore harmless — it sends an operator to fix a document's format when the fault is a permission or an I/O failure. **That reasoning is superseded in place, in the file that carried it.**

**Fix.** New `App\Support\Foundation\RestoreDrillEvidenceReader` decides the read outcome once, before any content is interpreted: `absent | unreadable | read_failed | empty | ok`, with `contents` non-null only for `empty`/`ok` so a caller can never mistake *read nothing* for *read an empty document*. `false` is never coerced. Suppression (`@`) is warning-noise control only (the Critical Gate warning baseline is a declared `0`); the control flow is the explicit `=== false` check. The decode stage splits on `json_last_error()`: decoder rejection stays `invalid_json`; accepted-but-wrong-shape becomes `evidence_not_an_object` (both FAIL). New stable issue-code constants `ISSUE_ABSENT|EMPTY|UNREADABLE|READ_FAILED|INVALID_JSON|NOT_AN_OBJECT`, and every result now carries a machine-readable `details.read_state`.

**Deliberately NOT changed — the load-bearing decision.** `locateEvidence()` keeps its exact `is_file($abs) && filesize($abs) > 0` predicate. Letting a read fault fall through to the next candidate would have been strictly **more permissive** — a secondary path answering for a canonical file nobody could read. An existing-but-unreadable candidate therefore still blocks. The zero-byte case is instead detected by a separate `firstEmptyCandidate()` scan consulted only when no candidate was usable. **Measured differential across all 13 states: `READINESS_DELTAS = 0`** — every verdict preserved, only the reasons became true. Explicit asymmetric boundary: **zero bytes = no evidence recorded (WATCH); any bytes that are not valid evidence = corrupt evidence (FAIL)** — so whitespace-only stays FAIL exactly as at base.

**Durable rules:** (1) read state before parsing/validation; (2) a failed read is never empty content — no `(string) false`, no `?: ''`; (3) `invalid_json` means bytes were read and the decoder rejected them, never a filesystem fault and never a wrong-shape document; (4) successfully-read-empty ≠ read failure; (5) trust before freshness — an untrusted instant yields no age (RESTORE-DRILL-TIMESTAMP-FAITHFULNESS-1 preserved); (6) absent/empty/unreadable/read_failed/invalid_json/not_an_object/invalid-schema/unsafe/invalid-timestamp/future-timestamp/stale are all non-GO; (7) truthfulness must never buy permissiveness — changing a reason is allowed, moving a verdict is a separate explicitly-justified decision; (8) verify in production non-destructively — never mutate canonical evidence, never `chmod` it, never run a live restore to exercise a state (use `--path` with a throwaway file); (9) never guess Artisan flags on production.

**Verification.** Mutation control: reintroducing `(string) @file_get_contents($found)` fails **6** tests — the contract binds to the read boundary, not to message text. Real-filesystem `chmod 000` control skips (never silently passes) when the running user can read a `0000` file, with an injected-reader control covering the same state unconditionally. `READ_FAILED` is injected because a real I/O fault cannot be scheduled by a test — the state is real, only its timing is synthetic. Tests: new `tests/Feature/Foundation/RestoreDrillEvidenceReadStateTest.php` (24 passed / 110 assertions), declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` and **verified by execution** (`--list-tests`) to contribute 24 tests to the critical filter, so a rename can move its coverage but not delete it. `CriticalGateWarningContractTest` suppressed-read EXACT-SET inventory updated (service removed, reader added). **Gotcha:** that inventory is exact — moving or cleaning up a `@file_get_contents` without updating it fails CI by design, which is how this sprint's own change was caught.

## STORAGE-PUBLIC-CLINICAL-EVIDENCE-1 — Private Clinical Evidence Remediation (2026-08-23)

Branch `feature/storage-1-object-storage-readiness` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `restore-drill-evidence-read-state-1-go` @ `6c66762`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/storage-public-clinical-evidence-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/121-clinical-evidence-private-storage.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (SECURITY_FIX). **Active clinical-data confidentiality incident, contained then remediated.**

**STORAGE-1 was NOT re-run.** Its GO tag `storage-1-object-storage-readiness-go` @ `d8a9c73` (2026-07-05) is untouched and was not moved; the roadmap still marks STORAGE-1 `completed`. Its object-storage scaffold stays OFF and production stays on local storage — object-storage cutover remains a separate, separately-authorized sprint.

**What was found.** A storage inventory at the base commit found native clinical evidence written to Laravel's `public` disk, which is symlinked into the document root (`public/storage -> storage/app/public`) and therefore served by nginx with **no session, no policy, no branch check**. Proven on production, not inferred: a synthetic 32-byte non-PII probe written into `storage/app/public/handwritings/` was fetched over plain HTTPS with no credentials and returned **HTTP 200, byte-identical**; the probe was then deleted. No patient file was ever retrieved to prove the point. Exposed: **46** RME handwriting images, **8** prescription/doctor-signature canvases, **1** lab attachment (55 artifacts, 3.2 MB). Keys were only weakly unguessable — `handwritings/{branch}/{visit}/handwriting_p{N}_{YmdHis}.png`, whose only entropy is a one-second timestamp, against an unauthenticated endpoint with no rate limit. This violated the project's own shipped **STORAGE-R002** ("never a raw public disk URL"), which had been written for *object* storage and was never enforced for the *local* public disk — so every clinical module shipped after STORAGE-1 (Lab evidence 07-10, SATUSEHAT 07-17, LegacyRme 08-19) inherited the gap silently.

**Containment (first, before any code).** One reversible nginx rule — `location ^~ /storage/ { deny all; }` — on the canonical HTTPS server. Chosen over per-directory rules because all three top-level directories on that disk are clinical, so one rule is both smaller and gap-free. No file mutated. Backup `/etc/nginx/sites-available/asia-dental-lab.bak-storage-incident-20260823-151718`; reversal = delete the marked block + reload. Verified after the graceful reload settled: the path that previously returned 200 returns **403**, all three real artifact categories **403**, all 55 files intact, `/login` + `/health/live` + `/health/ready` **200**.

**Permanent fix.** New `clinical_evidence` disk (local, `visibility: private`, `serve: false`, `throw: true`, root `storage/app/clinical-evidence-private`, deliberately absent from `filesystems.links`). New `App\Support\Storage\ClinicalEvidenceStorage` is the **single storage authority** — call sites ask it for the disk and never name one, so a future writer cannot silently pick a served disk and governance has exactly one symbol to assert against. Write paths repointed: `MedicalRecordHandwritingController`, `RmePrescriptionService::storeCanvas`, `LabOrder\AttachmentService::upload`, `Delivery\PodService`. New authorized read routes `rme.handwritings.image`, `rme.handwriting-pages.image`, `rme.prescriptions.canvas`, `attachments.download`, `deliveries.receiver-signature` — thin controllers mirroring `LabWorkflowEvidenceController@show`: authorize, then stream; the filesystem key is never returned. `AttachmentDownloadController` maps `entity_type` → owning policy **explicitly and fails closed** on an unmapped type. Object keys are **preserved verbatim**, so the migration rewrites **no database column**.

**Migration.** `php artisan clinical-evidence:migrate-public` — `--apply` copies and verifies by SHA-256 with the **source untouched**; `--purge-source` re-verifies then deletes. Nothing is deleted before a byte-identical copy is proven. Each phase writes a manifest (key, size, both checksums, outcome) to `storage/app/clinical-evidence-migration/` as rollback evidence, and verifies every stored DB reference resolves privately (inline data-URI rows counted separately — they never touched the filesystem).

**Governance.** New **STORAGE-R006** — clinical evidence never on a publicly served disk (pinned rule-id assertion in `Storage1GovernanceIntegrationTest` updated R001–R006). New `tests/Feature/Storage/ClinicalEvidencePrivacyTest.php` (20) registered in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` **and** added as the `ClinicalEvidence` token to **both** runner variants of the critical gate — a control CI never selects is not a control. This deliberately broadens that registry beyond monitor-truthfulness; the precedent is earned.

**Gotchas.** (1) A Blade comment `{{-- --}}` inside an `@php` block is parsed as PHP and breaks the template — it cascaded to 39 test failures; always run `php artisan view:cache` after editing Blade. (2) `systemctl reload nginx` is graceful, so a request immediately after reload can be served by an old worker on the old config — the first containment verification produced a false 200 for exactly this reason; re-verify after workers cycle. (3) Grepping only `disk('public')` misses `storeAs($dir, $name, 'public')` — that form hid two writers. (4) `sys_attachments.uploaded_by` is a required FK.

**Print/PDF.** `orderedHandwritingPages(true)` embeds inline base64. `print-body` is shared by the browser print view **and** the dompdf export, and dompdf cannot present a session cookie, so a linked image would export blank — one code path removes that failure class entirely. `RmePdfPrintHardeningTest` now asserts the data URI is present **and** the route URL is absent.

## FINAL-STABILIZATION-RESIDUAL-AUDIT-1 — Residual Reconciliation Before the Consolidated Full Suite (2026-08-24)

Branch `feature/final-stabilization-residual-audit-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `storage-public-clinical-evidence-1-go` @ `dccfbe7`; do NOT target main). Ledger `docs/sprints/final-stabilization-residual-audit-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/121-final-stabilization-residual-audit.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (DOCS_ONLY). **Evidence-reconciliation sprint, not a fix sprint.** Every residual/WATCH/TODO/deferred item from the corrective series was reconciled against the CURRENT production authority and classified with evidence. **21 unique residuals: CLOSED 4, ACCEPTED_RISK 9, BLOCKED_EXTERNAL 3, DEFERRED 3, SUPERSEDED 1, NOT_APPLICABLE 1, REAL_DEFECT 0.** All eight blocker counts (real defect / critical+high security / required CI / deployment / privacy / clinical correctness / data loss) = **0**. `RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=false` — the only change under `app/` is a single docblock line (a comment; no executable statement); nothing in `config/`, `routes/` or migrations changed. **No migration, no permission, no route.**

**BIGGEST FIND (R-21) — four tests were ALREADY FAILING on the GO-tagged authority `dccfbe7` running in production.** STORAGE-PUBLIC-CLINICAL-EVIDENCE-1 added `STORAGE-R006` to `StorageGovernanceService` but updated only **1 of the 7** Architecture assertions that pin the storage rule list; **six were left stale in two different shapes** — `Lb1`/`Stateless1`/`Replica1`/`CacheRedis` still asserted the exact id list `R001–R005`, and `Obs1`/`Obs2` pinned `toHaveCount(5)`. **A grep for `'STORAGE-R005'` finds the four id lists and misses both count assertions, because a count never names what it counts** — the first sweep found four, running the whole Architecture suite found the other two. Verified pre-existing (R006 present in the service at the base commit, stale 5-element pin present in the tests at the base commit, this sprint's diff touches neither). **Nothing caught it because the Critical gate's 36-token filter does not select those suites and the Full Suite has been deferred since 2026-08-19 — a test can fail for days when the only gate that runs it is switched off.** Repinned all six + corrected the service docblock ("Publishes the STORAGE-R001..R005 rules" while publishing six). Verified: id-list suites 30 passed, count suites 11 passed, and the full local Architecture suite (2h28m) 408 passed / 2 failed — those 2 being exactly the pre-fix count assertions — i.e. an effective **410 passed / 0 failed**. **Durable rule: adding a governance rule means repinning EVERY pin for that family — both exact-list `toBe([...])` AND bare `toHaveCount(N)` — and sweeping keyed by SERVICE FILE, not id prefix** (a prefix-keyed sweep falsely flags OBS-1/OBS-2, which are deliberately separate services publishing `OBS-R001..R012` and `OBS-R013..R024`). `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0` (rule 107 still ACTIVE).

**Storage incident proven closed four independent ways** (source: writers 0, public-URL readers 0 across every binding form incl. `storeAs(…,'public')`/`storePublicly`; production filesystem: public real files **0**, private clinical **55** = exactly the 46 handwriting + 8 prescription + 1 lab attachment enumerated; verifier dry-run `decision=OK`, `source_objects_remaining=0`, `broken_by_migration=0`; nginx `location ^~ /storage/ { deny all; }` **effective — 403** on two non-PII probes over the canonical domain). No patient object was retrieved to prove privacy. **Gotcha:** the first nginx check used `grep -r` over `/etc/nginx/sites-enabled/` and found nothing — `grep -r` does NOT follow the symlinks every entry there is. The deny was present all along; a blocker was nearly filed against a working control.

**The 20 dangling references = ACCEPTED_RISK, classified not cleaned.** Re-measured at exactly 20; all are `sys_attachments` ids 1–20 (`trx_lab_deliveries` POD_SIGNATURE ×9 + POD_RECEIVER_PHOTO ×9, `trx_lab_orders` QC_PHOTO ×2), created 2026-06-04/05, 0 soft-deleted, objects absent from the SOURCE disk too. All 42 handwriting/prescription references resolve.
