Access **fails closed**: `AttachmentDownloadController` authorises first, then `abort_unless($disk->exists($path), 404)`; `ClinicalEvidenceStorage::dataUri()` returns `null` on absent (the clinical disk is `'throw' => true`, so every read path was verified guarded rather than assumed). No 500, no leak, no workflow block — Lab V2 uses `trx_lab_workflow_evidence`, a different table. **Reopen if** the count exceeds 20 or a dangling reference appears in any other table.

**`Odontogram::hasRecordedTeeth()` audited read-only and deliberately UNCHANGED.** Production: 32 rows, **31 predicate-true, all 31 carry a meaningful tooth status, ambiguous 0**, eligibility delta 0, history-visibility delta 0, `REAL_DEFECT_REPRODUCED=false`. **Measurement gotcha:** the first query tested `jsonb_typeof(teeth)='array'` and returned `predicate_true=0`, implying the predicate never matches production — wrong, because production stores `teeth` as a JSON **object** and PHP `is_array()` is true for a decoded object. Never model this predicate in SQL with `='array'`. Do not "fix" the predicate on theory; re-measure first and show a non-zero delta.

**The only source changes (3 files, all test/fixture/governance).** `database/factories/RmePrescriptionFactory.php` wrote prescription + doctor-signature canvases to the `'public'` disk while production writes via `ClinicalEvidenceStorage` — never an exposure (no production path invokes a factory; no seeder uses it) but it reproduced the removed storage layout inside the suite, and **caused a false green**: because the fixture wrote to a disk the read path does not consult, `prescriptionCanvasDataUri()` returned `null` (proven: `on_public=true, on_clinical=false, rxUri_null=true`) and `it('can print prescription with fields and canvas images')` did `assertSee(null)` — which casts to `''` and passes against any response, so the test could not fail when there was no canvas. Fixed: factory writes through `ClinicalEvidenceStorage`; the test asserts both data URIs start with `data:image/` before asserting the response contains them; and the anti-regression writer scan in `tests/Feature/Storage/ClinicalEvidencePrivacyTest.php` now covers **`database/` as well as `app/`** (that gap is how the factory survived the incident sprint — `Database\Factories` is a **production** autoload namespace, not dev-only). `tests/` stays excluded on purpose: that file writes to the public disk deliberately to prove the migration. **Both guards mutation-proven.** **Mutation gotcha:** the first mutation run PASSED because an earlier run had left `prescriptions/1/1/*.png` on the real clinical disk and the fixture reuses ids 1/1 — these tests write to the REAL local disk, not a fake, so local reruns can mask failures (recorded as residual R-14; CI is unaffected, fresh checkout). Clear `storage/app/clinical-evidence-private/prescriptions` before mutation-testing this area.

**Other classifications.** `laravel_log=WATCH` on production is CORRECT and was left alone — 7 window errors, **0 application errors**: a guessed `--skip-db` flag ×2 and one `psysh` write attempt, i.e. prior-session tooling debris that ages out (log never truncated). `queue_worker=UNKNOWN` is by MON-1 design; `systemctl` active+enabled, `queue:failed` empty. Restore-drill evidence absent → honest WATCH (`read_state=absent`, `unsafe=false`), DEFERRED — needs a disposable staging DB, never production. SATUSEHAT-2 GO tag verified **ABSENT** (correct) with enable/send/production flags all false; WhatsApp `WHATSAPP_ENABLED=false`/`driver=disabled` → BLOCKED_EXTERNAL (Meta credentials). Object-storage cutover DEFERRED (local private disk is the safe authority). **`TODO`/`FIXME`/`HACK`/`XXX` in `app/`+`config/`+`routes/`+`database/`+`resources/` = 0.** 36 `markTestSkipped` are all environment guards (pgsql/Poppler/root/absent-fixture), none hiding a failure. `.cursor/rules` prefix collisions (`92`×8, `100`×3, `97`×2 of 96 files) = index hygiene only — rules load by filename and no rule shadows another; renumbering would break every cross-reference, so left as-is. `FIX-LEGACY-RME-ROUTINE-OPS-1` (#315/#316) and `FIX-CLINIC-OPS-BRANCH-CONTEXT-WA-1` (#321/#322) are merged+deployed with no GO tag — governance record, not missing code; carried as `inherits_closure`/`inherits_hold`.

**Production audit discipline: `NEW_APPLICATION_ERRORS=0`, `NEW_TOOLING_ERRORS=0`** — `laravel.log` byte-identical (size 786268 / 124 ERROR) before and after the entire audit, achieved by reading every command signature from source first and using the migration command's read-only dry-run default instead of `tinker`.

**CI gate-selection verified empirically, not assumed (R-20).** `tests/Feature/Storage/ClinicalEvidencePrivacyTest.php` IS a registered `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` member and the Critical gate's 36-token filter selects **22** of its tests — so the extended `database/` writer scan runs in required CI; both runner variants' filters are byte-identical (no drift). `RmePrescriptionTest` is selected by **0** gates (not in the registry, and **no job consumes the classifier's `run_rme_tests` output** — a pre-existing documented gap), so the hardened canvas assertion is covered by the consolidated Full Suite, not by a required gate today; recorded as an honest residual rather than reflexively appended, per the registry's own "adding a suite is a governance decision, not a reflex" doctrine. **Verify gate selection with `pest --list-tests`, never by reading the filter** — `--filter` is a substring match, so "is my class name in the filter" is the wrong question. The classifier resolves this diff to `unknown_high_risk` (unrecognised `database/factories/` path → strongest gate).

**Verdict: `READY_FOR_AUTHORITATIVE_CONSOLIDATED_FULL_SUITE=true`.** Next stage `AUTHORITATIVE-CONSOLIDATED-FULL-SUITE-AND-CLOSURE-1` requires explicit user authorisation — this audit certifies readiness and must not run it.

## FIX-RECEIPT-PDF-TEXT-CONTIGUITY-1 — PDF field assertions read their own column (2026-08-25)

Branch `feature/fix-receipt-pdf-text-contiguity-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, base authority `e1675586` / `final-stabilization-residual-audit-1-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/fix-receipt-pdf-text-contiguity-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/122-pdf-text-contiguity.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml`. **Corrective sprint for the single failure that reddened the one authorised consolidated Full Suite (run 32700184849, job 97362090553): `tests/Feature/RME/RmeReceiptOnePageTest.php:215` → `expect($text)->toContain($visit->patient->name)` against `pdftotext -layout` output.** **No migration, no permission, no route, no runtime application file — four test files only; `RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=false`.**

**Root cause (reproduced on the clean base authority, byte-identical to the recorded signature).** `pdftotext -layout` serialises the **visual** layout, so a value overflowing its fixed-width cell wraps and the neighbouring column is interleaved into the same lines — `Nama Pasien  Miss Marcella O'Conner  No. Rekam Medis  MRN-…` / `             DVM`. **The receipt is correct**: the full name renders, nothing clipped, nothing mis-escaped. The assertion tested a *layout* property while claiming to test a *content* property, and failed on ~6% of faker names. **The apostrophe/escaping reading is DISPROVEN** — `Alexandria Catherine Santoso` (28, **no** apostrophe) fails identically while `O'Brien` (7, apostrophe) passes; it is length, and `e()` fixes nothing. **No character threshold is encoded anywhere** — the wrap point is a proportional-font pixel width (20 chars wrapped where 22 did not), so the fixture is pinned by demonstrated behaviour instead.

**Two further shapes found by measuring rather than assuming:** a long treatment description is split **around** its own qty/price row (so "join the next line" cannot rebuild it), and the RME visit PDF **stacks** labels above full-width values (wraps at ~145 chars; `mst_patients.name` allows 150).

**The corrected contract.** `tests/Pest.php` gains column-bounded, wrap-tolerant readers beside `pdfExtractText`: `pdfLayoutFieldValue` (value beside **or** stacked beneath the label), `pdfLayoutColumnText` (table column band between two headings), `pdfLayoutFullWidthText` (centred header/section lines), `pdfNormalizeText` (collapses whitespace runs only — removes no character), `pdfLayoutSegments` (splits a layout line into column cells; `-layout` separates columns with ≥2 spaces). Reading a value out of **its own column band** is *stricter* than the substring search it replaces — a neighbouring column is structurally outside the band — and the patient name is now compared for **equality**. **Explicitly rejected:** stripping all whitespace, flattening the page into one haystack (both fuse `Nama Pasien` with `No. Rekam Medis`), per-token containment (the tokens exist elsewhere), and any change to the receipt itself.

**Determinism.** `receiptVisit()` pins `receiptWrappingPatientName()` = `Alexandria Catherine Santoso` (deliberately apostrophe-free), so the wrap is exercised on **every** run, not 6% of them; and the coverage claim is itself asserted — if the fixture ever stops wrapping the test says so rather than silently lapsing. Matrix: short-plain / short-apostrophe / long-plain / long-apostrophe / wrapped-description all pass; wrong patient, lost wrapped tail, reordered tokens and wrong MRN all still fail; the one-page contract is preserved.

**Guard — the third shape.** `FullSuiteBaselineContractTest` pinned two shapes, both against a rendered **HTML body** (`content())->toContain($var)`, unescaped `assertSee`); neither matches PDF-extracted text, which is why this survived. Now pinned, **narrowly**: only files that extract PDF text, and only the free-text property list the `assertSee` guard already uses (`name|description|title|address|notes`) — `->toContain` is **not** banned wholesale; literals, identifiers, formatted money and `pdfNormalizeText(...)` stay legal. Two traps closed in its own implementation: arguments are read with a **paren counter** (so one safe assertion cannot launder an unsafe one on the same line), and comments are stripped with the **PHP tokenizer** (otherwise any file documenting the defect reports itself, and the cheapest way to silence the guard becomes deleting the explanation). Running it immediately found a third live instance — the centred clinic-name header — and a second affected file, `MedicalRecordPrintOdontogramSeparationTest`; both corrected.

**Mutation controls (residue 0):** restoring the old assertion fails both the receipt test and the guard (which names the offender); a helper that drops the wrapped tail produces 4 failures incl. the explicit partial-name control; the forbidden shape planted in a *different* file is caught and named; rendering a different patient name fails the identity assertions.

**Durable rules:** (1) `pdftotext -layout` output is a layout serialisation — never assume a wrap-capable dynamic field is one contiguous substring. (2) Tests covering wrapping must use a **deterministic** value known to wrap; never rely on faker probability to enter the branch. (3) If the PDF holds all required semantic content and only extraction contiguity differs, **correct the test contract** — never alter valid production escaping/layout to satisfy a brittle assertion; escalate instead if the document genuinely clips or loses characters. (4) PDF dynamic-field assertions must be field-aware and false-positive resistant; whole-page flattening is not a substitute. (5) The run-32700184849 failure is **immutable evidence**; a corrective sprint uses targeted runs only. (6) `e7b8dde460f3678f2a97b376e83aea66c168c173` is a **FAILED closure candidate — unmerged, undeployed, NO-GO**, never production authority.

**Full Suite:** `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=NOT_AUTHORIZED_AFTER_PREVIOUS_FAILURE`. This sprint carries its own GO tag; it does **NOT** close the programme — `STABILIZATION_PROGRAM_CLOSURE=NO_GO_PENDING_NEW_AUTHORIZED_FULL_SUITE`, and a new complete-suite execution requires a new explicit user authorisation.

## FIX-PDF-TEMPFILE-LEAK-1 — PDF temp-file ownership contract (2026-08-25)

Branch `feature/fix-pdf-tempfile-leak-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `fix-receipt-pdf-text-contiguity-1-go` @ `9576af9f3cb12357fea192fde48f7a83b19643ee`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/fix-pdf-tempfile-leak-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/123-test-temporary-file-ownership.mdc`. Closes a **pre-existing test-infrastructure resource-lifecycle defect** found after the receipt-contiguity corrective. **`RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=false` — zero `app/`, `resources/`, `routes/`, migration or permission changes.**

**Root cause.** `tests/Pest.php:1032` read `tempnam(sys_get_temp_dir(), 'dms-pdf-').'.pdf'`. `tempnam()` does not merely reserve a name — it **creates the file** — so the helper owned two artifacts while its `finally` cleaned only the derived `.pdf`. Every PDF assertion in the suite stranded one zero-byte orphan. Measured on the clean base authority: **55** `dms-pdf-*` orphans, **100% zero-byte, 0 carrying the `.pdf` suffix** — proof the derived file was always cleaned and the allocation never was.

**Fix.** The `tempnam()` allocation **is** the document path — the second artifact is removed rather than a second `unlink` added, so no path is left to forget. `PDF_EXTENSION_REQUIRED=false`, verified empirically: Poppler dispatches on the file header, and `pdftotext`/`pdfinfo` read the same bytes identically with and without a suffix. Dropping it also keeps tempnam's `0600` mode and closes the TOCTOU window on the never-atomically-reserved derived name.

**Verified.** Task temp delta **0** on all four paths — success, callback-throws (exception preserved), PDF unreadable, and 10 repeated invocations. Mutation controls all red as required: restore the old derivation → 5 failed; clean on success only → **exactly 1 failed** (the exception test alone, success stays green); remove cleanup entirely → 4 failed. Regression `73 passed / 400 assertions`, zero failures.

**Guard.** `tests/Feature/Cicd/PdfTempFileLifecycleContractTest.php`, declared in `config/ci_runner.php` → `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` so `CriticalGateSuiteCoverageTest` **enforces** its selection instead of leaving it to a filter token a future edit could drop; all 6 tests confirmed selected by running the critical gate's exact filter through `pest --list-tests` (that filter is byte-identical in both runner variants). The guard is **behavioural, not textual** — its primary assertions name the exact handed-out path and the exact sibling the old derivation stranded, so they are concurrency-immune; a global temp-directory count is *not* authoritative on a shared machine.

**Durable rules:** (1) `tempnam()` creates a filesystem artifact — code deriving a second path from it owns and must clean **both**, and the better fix is to stop deriving. (2) Every test-created temporary artifact has one explicit owner and deterministic cleanup on success **and** failure, preserving the original failure semantics. (3) Leak tests compare the **helper-owned set** before/after; global temp-directory counts are not authoritative. (4) Never glob-delete the temp directory to hide a leak or to manufacture a zero count — clean what you created, **measure** pre-existing artifacts rather than deleting them. (5) Never trade atomic allocation for `uniqid()`, a timestamp, or a fixed filename; never weaken permissions; never build temp names from patient/record data.

**Scope boundary (measured, not refactored).** The same `tempnam(...).SUFFIX` shape exists at four other call sites, plus a separate never-cleaned leak at `ctl3a-`. Census: `ffcache` 77, `ctl3a-` 87, `lrme-poppler-` 20, `leg`/`bad` 4. **Deliberately out of scope** — this sprint fixes the shared helper every PDF assertion routes through; they are recorded in the sprint doc as a follow-up candidate. This is also why the guard is not a repo-wide textual ban: such a ban would fail on those siblings, and allowlisting them would bless a known leak. The 55 pre-existing `dms-pdf-*` orphans were **measured, not deleted**.

**Discovered, NOT fixed here — a pre-existing SLA flake.** `LabOperationalAnalyticsMetricTest:129` asserts `median_lateness_days` `toBeGreaterThan(1.0)`. `LabOperationalAnalyticsService` computes `round($due->diffInMinutes($delivered) / 1440, 2)`, and the `late` fixture uses a `subDays(2)` **date** compared against end-of-due-day. Just after midnight the gap is ~1443 min → `1.002` → rounds to **exactly `1.00`** → the strict `>` fails. Failure window is roughly the **first 7 minutes of each day** (00:00–00:06 FAIL, 00:07+ pass) — about 0.5% of runs. CI run `32790351218` executed it at `00:02:59Z`, inside the window; the base-branch run passed at `21:59Z`, outside it. Not caused by this sprint (`git diff 9576af9f..HEAD` over `tests/Feature/LabWorkflow/` and `app/Modules/LabOrder/` is empty, and that suite never calls the PDF helper). Deliberately left unfixed — relaxing to `>=` would drop the property the assertion exists to assert, so the **fixture** needs to be made deterministic (wider `late` offset or a frozen clock), which belongs to a sprint owning the Lab analytics contract. **Worth doing before the next Full Suite authorisation is spent**, since a ~0.5% time-of-day failure can redden the single authorised run for an unrelated reason.

**Full Suite:** `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=NOT_AUTHORIZED_AFTER_PREVIOUS_FAILURE`. Run `32700184849` remains immutable failed evidence. This sprint carries its own GO tag; it does **NOT** close the programme — `STABILIZATION_PROGRAM_CLOSURE=NO_GO_PENDING_NEW_AUTHORIZED_FULL_SUITE`, and a new complete-suite execution requires a new explicit user authorisation.

## FIX-LAB-ANALYTICS-MEDIAN-LATENESS-DAY-BOUNDARY-1 — Lab analytics lateness stops depending on the hour CI starts (2026-08-25)

Branch `feature/fix-lab-analytics-median-lateness-day-boundary-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `fix-pdf-tempfile-leak-1-go` @ `2c267002b4af4e39c8e100608f88b2fb75d55118`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/fix-lab-analytics-median-lateness-day-boundary-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/124-time-dependent-test-determinism.mdc`. Closes the time-of-day flake FIX-PDF-TEMPFILE-LEAK-1 recorded as known-and-deferred. **No migration, no permission, no route, no runtime application change — `RUNTIME_APPLICATION_FILES_CHANGED=0`, `RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=false`.**

**Root cause — test fixture, not runtime.** `LabOperationalAnalyticsMetricTest` pinned the deadline to a calendar date (`due_date => now()->subDays(2)->toDateString()`) but the delivery to a wall-clock instant (`opLog($late, DELIVERED, now())`). `slaCompliance()` measures lateness as an **elapsed duration from the END of the due day** (`round($due->diffInMinutes($delivered)/1440, 2)`), so those records were `1.00` days late just after midnight and `2.00` just before it. Against `toBeGreaterThan(1.0)` the verdict was decided by the runner's start time. The metric itself is deterministic given its inputs — only the inputs were not.

**Failure window pinned to the second: `[00:00:00, 00:07:11]`** in the application timezone (7m12s/day, ~0.5% of runs), reproduced by binary search under a controlled clock — never by waiting for midnight and never by rerunning CI. `00:07:11` → `1.0` FAIL, `00:07:12` → `1.01` PASS. **This refines the earlier "00:07+ pass" estimate**, which was one minute optimistic: the edge is arithmetic (`raw ≥ 1.005` needs `≥ 432s` past midnight). Base-authority control: 1 failure at `00:02:59` (the reported CI instant), **2** at `2026-06-01 00:05:00`, 0 at midday — the midday green is what hid it.

**Time authority (`LAB_ANALYTICS_TIME_AUTHORITY = APPLICATION_TIMEZONE`, UTC), established from code not from CI's clock.** `resolvePeriod()` uses `today()`, `slaCompliance()` uses `Carbon::parse(...)->endOfDay()`, `config/app.php` hardcodes `'timezone' => 'UTC'`, and Graphify found no `ClinicalClock` edge in the `LabOrder` module. **`ClinicalClock` (Asia/Makassar) was deliberately NOT borrowed** — it is the *clinical*-date authority and nothing here is clinical. Pinned behaviourally by a case frozen at `16:00Z` (WITA midnight) asserting the window still ends on the UTC date.

**The false fix was rejected with evidence, not opinion.** Widening `>` to `>=` goes green without touching the defect: with the runtime mutated to return a **mean** instead of a median, the weakened assertion passes **12/12** while the new boundary suite fails **10/14**.

**Fix.** `tests/Pest.php` gains canonical `pinTestClock()` / `freeTestClock()` (application-timezone reference clock; small and shared — no system-wide temporal framework). The SLA case is pinned *at the instant it used to fail* (`2026-05-15 00:02:59`) **and** its delivery is anchored to its own due day (`$dueDay->addDays(3)->startOfDay()` → exactly `2.00` days late), so the expectation is exact (`toBe(2.0)`) and intrinsic to the fixture rather than to the chosen instant. New `LabOperationalAnalyticsDayBoundaryTest` (14) asserts **invariance** across the boundary (both window edges + the reported failure instant + WITA midnight), true median semantics (1/2/4 → `2.0`, not the `2.33` mean), the even-count convention (1/4 → `2.5`), "the due day itself is never late", the timezone authority, and no leaked clock.

**Same-class sibling found and fixed:** the QC first-pass test placed its first attempt `subMinutes(10)` before a live `now()` inside a **calendar-month** window, so during the first 10 minutes of every 1st the failed attempt fell out of period and the test silently became a 2/2 first-pass assertion (reproduced at `2026-06-01 00:05:00`). Siblings **audited and cleared** from the repository's actual filter columns: throughput, external turnaround (filters `returned_at`) and technician KPI (assignments never period-filtered) move both endpoints together and are duration-stable.

**Honest negative:** the "remove the `afterEach` clock release" mutation did **NOT** fail — Laravel's `InteractsWithTestCaseLifecycle::tearDownTheTestEnvironment()` already calls `Carbon::setTestNow()`, so the framework guarantees the reset and the leak guard cannot observe that removal. The explicit release is kept as documented pairing/redundancy, not claimed as what the guard proves.

**CI coverage enumerated, not inferred:** classifier `*/Lab*` → `run_lab_tests` → Selective Module Gate `--filter='Lab'`; `--list-tests` selects 626 tests including all 12 metric + all 14 boundary tests. **Not** added to `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` — that registry is scoped by its own doctrine to monitor-truthfulness, and Critical is not broadened because Selective caught it.

**Full Suite:** `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=NOT_AUTHORIZED_AFTER_PREVIOUS_FAILURE`. Run `32700184849` remains immutable failed evidence. This sprint carries its own GO tag; it does **NOT** close the programme — `STABILIZATION_PROGRAM_CLOSURE=NO_GO_PENDING_NEW_AUTHORIZED_FULL_SUITE`, and a new complete-suite execution requires a new explicit user authorisation. Next: **FIX-TEST-TEMPFILE-SIBLING-LEAKS-1** (`ffcache-`, `ctl3a-`, `lrme-poppler-`, `leg/bad`) — untouched here.

## FIX-TEST-TEMPFILE-SIBLING-LEAKS-1 — every temporary artifact has one owner that can reach it (2026-08-25)

Branch `feature/fix-test-tempfile-sibling-leaks-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `fix-lab-analytics-median-lateness-day-boundary-1-go` @ `baecf7c4808ba49d4f228e828fc5a435592f64df`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/fix-test-tempfile-sibling-leaks-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/123-test-temporary-file-ownership.mdc` (**extended, not duplicated**). Closes the backlog `FIX-PDF-TEMPFILE-LEAK-1` measured and deliberately left. **No migration, no permission, no route; `RUNTIME_APPLICATION_FILES_CHANGED=0`, `RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=false`.**

**The surface was ~2× the handover.** The brief listed 4 prefixes / ~188 orphans. The repository-wide same-class census found **392 orphans across 10 prefixes in 8 test files**, and **six call sites had no cleanup written on any path at all**: `rdrs1-` **124** (`RestoreDrillEvidenceReadStateTest`), `ffcache` 79, `ctl3a-home-` 51, `ctl3c-` 40 (`RuntimeEvidenceTruthfulnessTest`), `ctl3a-bin-` 36, `lrme-poppler-` 20, `ci-bare-host-` 16 (`DedicatedSelfHostedRunnerTest`), `leg` 12 (**both** artifacts), `ctl3d-bin-` 11, `infra-sec-env-1-` 3 (passing-path-only cleanup). The four marked † in the sprint doc were **not in the handover** — which is why the census was run instead of trusting the list. `bad` showed 0 files but is shape-identical to `leg`, so it was **reproduced** rather than dismissed (the base run created `bad…​.csv`; `/tmp` had simply been reaped). The "never cleans at all" site is confirmed as the `ctl3a-`/`ctl3d-` family. **SAFE, unchanged:** `lrme-render-`, `unit`, `cicd-ctrl-`, `tfs-*`, `pilot-log-`, `devflow-fix-base-ref-1-`, `dh1*`, `infra-sec-runtime-1-`, `stress-bench-`.

**Baseline reproduced, not inferred.** The standing `/tmp` census proves accumulation, not that today's code leaks. Five suites at the unmodified base authority stranded **131 fresh orphans in one pass while all 98 tests PASSED** — that is the measurement the fix is judged against.

**Two lifecycles, two owners** (measured before refactoring, per the brief). *Scoped* — created/used/destroyed in one call: a `finally` is the whole contract, so `ffcache` and `lrme-poppler-` only needed the single-artifact correction (drop the derived suffix, keep the allocation as the document, exactly as `pdfWithTempFile` does). The suffix was **proven** unnecessary, not assumed: `require` executes an extensionless file, and `pdfinfo`/`pdftoppm` plus this module's own inspector dispatch on the `%PDF-` header. *Deferred* — must outlive its creator (a stub PATH a child process still reads; an `UploadedFile` whose request has not run), so no `finally` can reach it: new `tempArtifactFile()` / `tempArtifactDir()` / `releaseTempArtifacts()` in `tests/Pest.php`, drained by a **global `afterEach`** so a call site cannot forget it and so it runs on the failing path — precisely where `SecretFilePermissionHardeningTest`'s existing explicit `removeFixtureDir()` calls were being skipped (those calls stay; the drain is an idempotent net). The drain removes **exactly the recorded paths, never a prefix glob**, so concurrent test processes and historical orphans are safe by construction. `ctl3a-wf-` fixtures live in the project tree, outside the `/tmp`-confined registry, so each got its own `finally`.

**Mutation found two defects in the guard itself.** (1) The symlink test linked to a **file** — `unlink()` never follows a symlink, so it passed with the guard removed; the catastrophic shape is a link to a **directory**, which `is_dir()` follows, making an unguarded remover `scandir` INTO the target (proven: `is_link=1 is_file=0 is_dir=1` → `would delete: precious.txt`). Fixtures really do contain such links (`ci-bare-host-` symlinks `/bin/bash` onto a stub PATH). (2) The confinement test **failed by deleting `composer.json`** — a guard whose failure mode is damage is not a guard; it now probes a fixture it created. Matrix M1–M7 all fail as designed after repair; `MUTATION_RESIDUE=0`.

**Guard.** New `tests/Feature/Cicd/TempFileSiblingLeakContractTest.php` (15 tests) for the *deferred* contract, alongside the existing `PdfTempFileLifecycleContractTest` for the *scoped* one; declared in `config/ci_runner.php` → `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` so `CriticalGateSuiteCoverageTest` **enforces** selection. All 15 confirmed selected by running each critical filter through `pest --list-tests`; the two filters are byte-identical, so **both** runner variants execute it. Its shape check is **tokenised, not grepped** — a textual scan for `tempnam(...).SUFFIX` matches the guard's own census table and failure message, so the guard reported itself (the same class of trap as the UI-governance Blade-comment scan). `tempnam()` is **not** banned; it is the correct atomic primitive, and deriving a second path from it is the defect.

**Evidence.** Modified suites together **216 passed / 837 assertions**; `tests/Unit` **246 passed / 7 skipped**; new guard **15 passed / 98 assertions**. `TASK_CREATED_TEMPFILES_AFTER_CLEANUP=0`; success/exception/child-process-failure/read-failure/10× repeat deltas all **0**. Historical baseline **447 before and after** — `HISTORICAL_ORPHANS_DELETED_BY_SPRINT=0`; the 131 artifacts the baseline reproduction itself created were removed **by exact path from the recorded diff**, never by `rm -f /tmp/<prefix>*`. `app/Console/Commands/StressBenchmarkRmePagesCommand.php` is the only `tempnam()` outside `tests/`, showed **0** orphans, and was classified and left alone.

**Full Suite:** `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=NOT_AUTHORIZED_AFTER_PREVIOUS_FAILURE`. This sprint carries its own GO tag; it does **NOT** close the programme — `STABILIZATION_PROGRAM_CLOSURE=NO_GO_PENDING_NEW_AUTHORIZED_FULL_SUITE`.

## FIX-CI-GATE-WORKDIR-TEMPFILE-LEAK-1 — R-22: ownership is the allocation API, not a prefix list (2026-08-26)

Branch `feature/fix-ci-gate-workdir-tempfile-leak-1` (base authority `fix-test-tempfile-sibling-leaks-1-go` @ `a75d65ac`, verified peeled and identical to production `VPS_HEAD` before implementation; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/fix-ci-gate-workdir-tempfile-leak-1.md`; rules folded into the existing `.cursor/rules/123-test-temporary-file-ownership.mdc` (**no parallel rule system**). Closes **R-22**, the blocker raised by `FINAL-PRE-FULL-SUITE-READINESS-CHECK-1` — whose branch (`2c32be7`/`8cc9251`) is **docs-only evidence, was never merged, and is not a corrective base**.

**RUNTIME_APPLICATION_FILES_CHANGED=0 · RUNTIME_BEHAVIOR_CHANGE=false · TEST_SEMANTICS_CHANGED=true.**

**R-22 reproduced before any edit.** `NsfReleaseGateExitPropagationTest` allocated two workdirs with a raw `mkdir` on a `sys_get_temp_dir()`-rooted path (lines 109 `ctl3b-`, 322 `fix6-fullsuite-`), so they were never registry allocations and no assertion about the registry's *behaviour* could see them: **42 tests passed and stranded 18 directory trees** (17 + 1). After the fix: 45 pass, **delta 0**, and **0 unattributed `/tmp` entries** across the whole 323-test prior-corrective regression.

**The defect was the AUTHORITY, not the two call sites.** The readiness audit proved a known-prefix census reported `447 / 447 / delta 0` while an unknown prefix leaked +18 elsewhere. A census can only report names it was told in advance, so it is structurally incapable of reporting an allocation nobody registered. Adding `ctl3b`/`fix6-fullsuite` to a list would have closed the symptom and left the defect. **Authoritative = the canonical allocation API + its registry owner; diagnostic = prefix counts.** A new prefix must never need central registration to earn cleanup — `tempArtifactDir('brand-new-thing-')` is protected the moment it is written.

**Guard.** New `tests/Feature/Cicd/CiGateWorkdirOwnershipContractTest.php` (8 tests) keys on the **shape** of an allocation, never its spelling: it flags any governed `tests/Feature/Cicd` file that roots a directory in the temp root outside the canonical allocator, via a variable or inline. Its detector needles are **assembled from string fragments** so the guard can police its own file — a guard excluded from the surface it guards guards nothing (same trap class as the UI-governance Blade-comment scan). Plus 3 call-site lifecycle pins beside `runNsfStepBody()` (success/failure/10× repeat). `tempnam()` files are deliberately **not** banned — correct atomic primitive, already governed by the sibling contract; banning it would reopen call sites measurement shows are closed. Declared in `config/ci_runner.php` → `critical_gate_mandatory_suites`; all 8 confirmed selected by `pest --list-tests` under the critical filter, which appears **twice** (both runner variants).

**Mutation matrix M1–M8 all behaved as designed.** M3 (**never-registered prefix, non-canonical**) → FAIL and M4 (**never-registered prefix, through the API**) → PASS with delta 0 are jointly the whole sprint. **M6 found a real defect in this sprint's own guard**: a ternary returning `tempArtifactDir()` on the passing path and a raw path on the failing one earned a free pass from a `str_contains($rhs, 'tempArtifactDir(')` skip — containing an owner is not having one, and the exempted path was the failing one. Fixed by stripping canonical calls from the expression *before* asking whether it still reaches the temp root, and pinned permanently. The detector also **fails closed** on a `preg` engine fault (M8) — an empty match set is indistinguishable from a clean file, the same fail-open shape the monitoring correctives kept finding; scope stated honestly, since the detector's own patterns are linear (a `pcre.backtrack_limit` of 20 does not fault them), so it is defence in depth for a future non-linear edit rather than a live path. `MUTATION_RESIDUE=0` (M7's own damage — two dangling `cgwl1-linkroot-` trees whose external dummies it destroyed, confirming the confinement test is load-bearing — removed by exact path).

**Measuring an unknown prefix safely.** `sys_get_temp_dir()` resolves once per process, so an allocation cannot be redirected mid-run; the census-vs-ownership control runs a **child process with `TMPDIR` pointed at a root the test allocated**, so it owns 100% of that root and no concurrent process can perturb the measurement. Shared `/tmp` is never scanned or swept.

**Historical orphans preserved.** 111 measured (104 `ctl3b`, 7 `fix6-fullsuite`) — not hardcoded from the report. `HISTORICAL_R22_ORPHANS_DELETED_BY_SPRINT=0`; the 153 trees the sprint's own reproduction and mutation runs created were removed **by exact path** against the pre-reproduction snapshot (`TASK_CREATED_R22_ORPHANS_AFTER=0`, survivors verified to be exactly the original 111). The previously closed families (`ctl3c-` 40, `ctl3a-bin-` 36, `ctl3a-home-` 51, `ctl3d-bin-` 11, `ci-bare-host-` 16, `rdrs1-` 124, `dms-pdf-` 55) were re-measured, did **not** grow, and were not reopened. `CiClassifierBaseAuthorityTest` allocates raw but owns its paths in a `$GLOBALS` registry drained in `afterEach` with 0 standing orphans → classified EXPLICIT_SAFE and allowlisted, kept honest by a test that fails if the exemption goes stale. `UNKNOWN_CICD_TEMP_ALLOCATORS=0`.

**Full Suite:** `FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`, `FULL_SUITE_STATUS=NOT_AUTHORIZED_AFTER_PREVIOUS_FAILURE` (the prior authorisation was consumed by run `32700184849`, which FAILED). This sprint carries its own GO tag; it does **NOT** close the programme — `STABILIZATION_PROGRAM_CLOSURE=NO_GO_PENDING_NEW_AUTHORIZED_FULL_SUITE`. Next step is to **repeat the Final Pre-Full-Suite Readiness Check**, not to run a Full Suite.

## FINAL-PRE-FULL-SUITE-READINESS-CHECK-1 — readiness GO (retry after R-22) (2026-08-26)

Branch `feature/final-pre-full-suite-readiness-check-1-r2` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`; do NOT target main). Base authority `fix-ci-gate-workdir-tempfile-leak-1-go` @ `64147d3e6db29bc8080ed22c97a1672170e3476a`, tree `8ccbea79f82e378165b4a47cc16c0103edb94104` — production HEAD/tree matched exactly **before** the audit began. **Readiness GO ≠ Full Suite authorisation.** It means only `READY_TO_REQUEST_NEW_AUTHORITATIVE_FULL_SUITE_AUTHORIZATION=true`. `STABILIZATION_PROGRAM_CLOSURE` stays `NO_GO_PENDING_NEW_AUTHORIZED_FULL_SUITE`; no programme-closure tag was created.

**R-22 re-verified CLOSED by a better instrument.** The previous audit false-greened because a known-prefix census can only report names it was told about in advance. This retry gave each suite a **private temporary root** and counted everything left in it — prefix-independent by construction. Ten suites, **4138 passed / 24 skipped / 0 failed / 18316 assertions**, every private root draining to **0**. Corroborating shared-`/tmp` census: **11 of 11 families unchanged** (ctl3b 104, fix6-fullsuite 7, dms-pdf 55, rdrs1 124, ffcache 79, ctl3a-home 51, ctl3c 40, ctl3a-bin 36, lrme-poppler 20, ci-bare-host 16, ctl3d-bin 11). `HISTORICAL_ORPHANS_DELETED_BY_READINESS=0`. Architecture **410 passed / 3496 assertions** — the prior healthy baseline exactly.

Both added (99 and 27 passed). All **17** mandatory-registry suites covered, 0 gaps. Coverage stays bounded on purpose: running every directory is a complete suite under another alias.

**Gotcha — the "ctl3 fixtures moved to 0700" claim is only half true.** Only the two R-22 workdirs take `tempArtifactDir()`'s `0700` default. `ctl3a-bin-`, `ctl3a-home-`, `ctl3c-`, `ctl3d-bin-` and `tsl1-proc-` still request `0o777` → **775** on disk at umask 0002. Group- not world-writable, canonically owned, 12 hex chars of entropy in the name. ACCEPTED_RISK, not closed.

**Full Suite non-execution is structural.** `full_suite_gate` needs `full_suite_authorized=='true'` **and** a schedule/dispatch/push event. On `pull_request` no event branch matches; on push-to-base the ACTIVE temporary policy forces the flag false. A PR and a merge therefore cannot reach it. `THIS_SPRINT_FULL_SUITE_EXECUTION_COUNT=0`.

**Residual ledger rebuilt and re-measured (26 items):** CLOSED 5 · ACCEPTED_RISK 13 · BLOCKED_EXTERNAL 3 · DEFERRED 4 · SUPERSEDED 1 · **REAL_DEFECT 0**. R-02 re-measured live (63 checked / 43 resolved / **20** unresolved / `broken_by_migration=0` / decision OK — reopen trigger not fired). R-17 TODO count **3**, all `TODO(branch-scope)` dated to `b53bbe3` 2026-06-04. All blocker counters **0**.

Production: `/login`+`/health/{live,ready,lb}` 200, `/storage/<synthetic>` **403**, public clinical objects **0**, clinical writers/readers **0/0**, legacy **at rest**, object-storage cutover **not executed**.

**Durable rules pinned (rule 125):** an audit measures and hands off, never repairs what it finds · readiness GO only permits *asking* for a Full Suite · a failed authorised run gets no automatic rerun · ownership is authoritative and a prefix census only diagnostic · guards fail closed and containing an owner is not having one · historical residue preserved, own artifacts removed by exact path · a truthful WATCH beats a manufactured GO · never run Tinker/PsySH on production · an audit must audit its own instrument.

## AUTHORITATIVE-CONSOLIDATED-FULL-SUITE-2 — Final Stabilization Programme Closure (2026-08-26)

Branch `feature/authoritative-consolidated-full-suite-2-final-closure` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`; do NOT target main). Baseline `final-pre-full-suite-readiness-check-1-go` @ `e9d172c`, tree `18972f0e` — at freeze the readiness tag, the base branch head and the production HEAD were all the same commit. Closure contract: `docs/sprints/authoritative-consolidated-full-suite-2-final-closure.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/126-authoritative-full-suite-and-final-closure.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (DOCS_ONLY, GOVERNANCE). **Governance text only — `RUNTIME_APPLICATION_FILES_CHANGED=0`, no migration, no route, no permission, no test-semantics or CI-semantics change.**

**Why the closure contract ships BEFORE the suite.** The authoritative Full Suite must run on the exact tree that later becomes the deployed closure tree. A closure that runs the suite first and then commits its evidence has not tested the tree it ships — the commit recording the PASS is by construction absent from the tree that passed. So this candidate is frozen before the trigger and never changes afterwards; `full_suite_status` stays `PENDING_AUTHORIZED_EXECUTION` in the manifest and the doc says PENDING. **The PASS is asserted only by the annotated GO tag and the CI run — never by a post-PASS commit.**

**The trap this sprint deliberately did not walk into.** `.github/ci-policy/full-suite-policy.json` names the consolidated closure as the occasion to flip the GLOBAL TEMPORARY FULL-SUITE POLICY to `RETIRED`. Doing that *inside the closure candidate* is a defect: with the policy RETIRED, `scripts/ci/resolve-full-suite-policy.sh` resolves a `push` to base as `POLICY_RETIRED_PUSH_TO_BASE` → `full_suite_authorized=true`, and merging the candidate **is** such a push — so the merge would immediately launch a second, unauthorised ~5h Full Suite. The policy is therefore left **ACTIVE** through closure (merge push → `TEMPORARY_FULL_SUITE_POLICY_ACTIVE` → gate skipped); retirement is a separate, separately-authorised act.

**Canonical execution mechanism (read from the workflow source, not assumed):** `workflow_dispatch` on `.github/workflows/foundation-evidence-gates.yml` with **both** `run_full_suite=true` **and** `full_suite_policy_override=true` — while the policy is ACTIVE the first alone resolves `TEMPORARY_FULL_SUITE_POLICY_ACTIVE_OVERRIDE_REQUIRED`. `full_suite_gate` also needs one critical-gate variant to have genuinely succeeded (a skipped sibling never satisfies it), and the suite step runs under `shell: bash`, so GitHub's `-eo pipefail` makes the `| tee` propagate the exit code rather than swallow it. **`concurrency: foundation-evidence-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress` means a second dispatch on the same ref cancels the first — never dispatch twice on one ref.** On `workflow_dispatch` there is no `github.event.before`, so the classifier fails closed to `unknown_high_risk` and the suite runs at full strength regardless of the docs-only diff.

**Authorisation model.** One explicit user authorisation = exactly one attempt, and it is **consumed at trigger, not at pass**. FAIL / TIMEOUT / CANCELLED / PARTIAL / runner loss / wrong SHA / misroute / platform outage are all terminal: no automatic rerun, no merge, no deploy, no tag, and a new explicit authorisation is required. Readiness GO is not authorisation — it only permits asking for one. Full Suite #1 (run `32700184849`, candidate `e7b8dde`, 1 failed / 7372 passed, a PDF line-wrap **test-contract** defect) consumed the previous authorisation; it is never re-run and its evidence is never altered.

**Residual ledger** carried and re-reconciled from the readiness audit, unchanged because this sprint changes no runtime behaviour: 26 items — CLOSED 5, ACCEPTED_RISK 13, BLOCKED_EXTERNAL 3, DEFERRED 4, SUPERSEDED 1, **REAL_DEFECT 0**. Monitoring `laravel_log` WATCH and the absent restore-drill evidence stay truthful operational states — never relabelled GO, never manufactured; SATUSEHAT/Meta external blocks stay BLOCKED_EXTERNAL rather than being promoted to defects by closure pressure.

**Durable foundations pinned (rule 126):** one attempt per authorisation consumed at trigger · no automatic rerun · tested tree immutable from trigger · candidate==tested==merged==deployed==GO-tag tree (squash may change the SHA, never the tree) · no post-PASS evidence commit · base movement preventing an identical-tree merge is a closure blocker · never retire the temporary policy in the closure candidate · prefix census is diagnostic while the allocation API is the ownership authority · unknown-prefix temp allocation fails closed · killing a wrapper does not prove children died · shared-evidence suites run serially · monitoring/restore WATCH stays truthful · no interactive REPL on production · clinical evidence never returns to public storage · **Full Suite PASS alone is not closure** (identical-tree merge + VPS deploy exit 0 + production verification + GO tag are all required) · the deploy runs on the VPS, never locally, and a started deploy is not a successful deploy.

## FIX-RME-PRINT-REMOVE-PATIENT-VISIT-DUPLICATE-1 — RME print drops the duplicated patient/visit card (2026-08-27)

Branch `feature/fix-rme-print-remove-patient-visit-duplicate-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`; do NOT target main). Baseline `authoritative-consolidated-full-suite-2-final-program-closure-go` @ `865d1f4`, tree `e43b4634` — verified byte-identical to the deployed production HEAD before any edit. Sprint doc `docs/sprints/fix-rme-print-remove-patient-visit-duplicate-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/127-rme-print-composition-no-duplicate-patient-card.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (UI_POLISH, RME). **First development sprint after `PRODUCTION_STEADY_STATE=true`; not a reopened stabilization sprint.**

**What changed.** "Cetak RME" no longer renders the **`Data Pasien & Kunjungan`** card — the twelve-cell block listing Nama Pasien, No. Rekam Medis, Ponsel, Nomor WA, No. Kunjungan, Tanggal Kunjungan, Cabang, Klinik, Antrian, Dokter, Tindakan Awal, Keluhan Utama. It restated as typed metadata what the clinician had already written by hand on the RM sheet the same document reproduces immediately below, so page one duplicated page two. The print now opens on the clinical content. **Presentation only: one `section-block` removed from one Blade partial. No migration, no route, no permission, no policy, no controller, no service, no query, no schema, no JS/CSS asset (so no `npm run build`).** Nothing was deleted from the data — every field is still stored and still shown on the visit detail screen (`rme.visits.show`).

**One partial, both canonical outputs.** `rme.visits.print` (browser) and `rme.visits.pdf` (dompdf) are separate wrappers that both `@include('rme.visits.partials.print-body')`. The card lived in that shared partial, so a single edit removes it from both and they cannot drift apart later.

**Duplicate card ≠ document identification.** Removing the card must never make a printed clinical sheet unattributable, so the wrappers' own header/footer are deliberately KEPT: each `<title>` carries the patient name and visit number, each footer the visit number and print timestamp, and the clinic logo stays. **The trap:** a naive `assertDontSee('Klinik')` fails on the logo's `alt` text ("Logo Klinik Gigi Daengtisia") and would demand the branding be deleted. Assert the card's **cell markup** (`<dt>Klinik</dt>`) instead, paired with an assertion that the card's *values* are absent — which also catches a card reinstated under a different caption.

**Tests.** New `tests/Feature/RME/RmePrintPatientVisitDuplicateRemovalTest.php` (9): caption gone (escaped **and** raw), all twelve `<dt>` cells gone, the *values* gone, handwriting preserved and still embedded inline as a `data:` URI (never linked), document identity preserved, no empty `section-block`/`info-grid` residue with the medical record now the opening section, the PDF drops the card too, and authorization + branch isolation unchanged on **both** routes. **Mutation-verified**: with the original template restored, the 5 removal tests fail and the 4 invariant tests still pass — the removal assertions are not vacuous and the invariants are genuinely invariant. Five stale tests across `MedicalRecordPrintOdontogramSeparationTest`, `RmePdfPrintHardeningTest` and `RmePatientFormatConsentTest` asserted the card *renders*; each was amended to keep its original purpose while re-anchoring on surviving content — the PDF extraction proof moved from the card's `Nama Pasien` cell to the pinned visit number in the footer (still document-specific, still wrap-proof per FIX-RECEIPT-PDF-TEXT-CONTIGUITY-1), and the "no KTP on print" privacy anchor moved from `Nomor WA` to `Rekam Medis` so it still cannot pass merely because the page failed to render. **None was weakened to a vacuous pass.**

**CI selection (the failure mode CI-MONITORING-CRITICAL-TOKEN-COVERAGE-1 exists for).** A new suite matching no filter token never runs in a required gate. `RmePrintPatientVisitDuplicate` was added to the NSF-R011 critical filter in **both** runner variants, the two filter strings verified byte-identical, and selection proven empirically with `pest --filter='<the exact CI filter>' --list-tests` — never inferred from reading the regex. Neither `critical_gate_required_filters` (scoped to CI's own tooling) nor `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` (scoped to monitor truthfulness, and explicit that "adding a suite is a governance decision, not a reflex") was touched.

**Full Suite: not run, not required.** `full_suite_gate` needs `classify.outputs.full_suite_authorized == 'true'`, and while the GLOBAL TEMPORARY FULL-SUITE POLICY is **ACTIVE** the resolver returns `false` for both automatic events (`schedule` and post-merge `push` to base) — so the merge cannot self-launch one. The only surviving path is a deliberate `workflow_dispatch` with both inputs. This sprint neither requested that dispatch nor altered the policy.

**Recorded finding, deliberately NOT actioned.** `ClinicVisitController::resolvePrintViewData()` still eager-loads `doctor`, `branch`, `clinic` and `initialTreatment`, which the composition no longer reads — four dead eager loads. Left in place: `Model::preventLazyLoading` is not enabled anywhere (verified), so they are a harmless four-row prefetch, and removing them would widen a print-only change into `ClinicVisit`. Handed off rather than folded into scope.

**Durable rules pinned (rule 127):** the RME print carries clinical content, never a restatement of what the clinician already wrote by hand · a duplicate information card is not the same thing as document identification, and the header/footer/logo stay · one shared partial feeds both print and PDF, so change it once and never fork it · "remove X from the RME print" is always about the COMPOSITION, never the record, schema, workflow or detail screen · handwriting stays embedded inline from the private disk, never linked · removing a section must leave no empty wrapper residue · a new print-regression suite must be proven selected by BOTH critical gate variants.

## FEATURE-LEGACY-IMPORT-HUB-1 — Legacy Import Hub: one home, one ceiling (2026-08-27)

Branch `feature/legacy-import-hub-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`; do NOT target main). Baseline `fix-rme-print-remove-patient-visit-duplicate-1-go` @ `5abd4ac`, tree `9496904` — verified exact-match against the deployed production HEAD before any edit. Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-legacy-import-hub-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/128-legacy-import-hub.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT, LegacyImport).

**What already existed, and was NOT rebuilt.** Three legacy importers, built at different times, each complete on its own terms: **Legacy Patient** (Sprint 62.3, `settings.patients.import.*`, CSV → staging → preview → commit, gated `manage patients`, no feature flag), **Legacy RME** (LEGACY-RME-PDF-1A..ROLL-4, `settings.rme.legacy-imports.*`, five named permissions, flag `rme.legacy_pdf_archive`) and **Legacy Odontogram** (FIX-04b, `settings.rme.legacy-odontograms.*`, its own five permissions, flag `rme.legacy_odontogram_archive`). Two lived under *Master Data RME*; Legacy Odontogram had **no sidebar entry at all**. Not one line of any importer's validation, branch resolution, review, publish, void or read path changed.

**What is new — exactly two things.** (1) `ops_legacy_import_daily_quotas` and `LegacyImportDailyQuotaService`: **100 accepted records, per branch, per clinical day, per import type**, enforced through one code path in all three importers. (2) `Import Data Legacy` — one sidebar group (Ringkasan + the three uploads) and the read-only landing page `settings.legacy-imports.index`. The two legacy entries were MOVED out of *Master Data RME* (and that group's `@canany` lost the two legacy-RME permissions, which would otherwise open an empty group for a legacy-only operator).

**The ceiling, word by word.** **ACCEPTED** — charged inside the transaction that writes the record, so a refused upload / failed validation / duplicate / error row / rolled-back transaction consumes nothing and needs no compensating write. **BRANCH** — always server-resolved (archives: the branch-code segment of the Nomor RM; Patient: the row's required-and-strict `Cabang` column); a request-supplied branch is never the authority. **CLINICAL DAY** — `ClinicalClock`, never `config('app.timezone')`, which resolves to UTC in production and would roll the quota over at 08:00 WITA. **IMPORT TYPE** — counted separately; a full RME quota does not touch Patient or Odontogram. `NULL` declines to limit, `0` admits nothing, and a value above `max_declarable_daily` (500) is **clamped** with the hub saying so.

**Where each importer is charged.** RME and Odontogram: one staged document, inside `createFromUpload`'s transaction. **Legacy Patient: one COMMITTED patient row, inside `commit`'s transaction, reserved AFTER the rows are known** — staging is not acceptance (a staged batch writes nothing to `mst_patients` and can be discarded), and reserving late means a row the RM/KTP re-check skips costs nothing. A multi-branch batch is **all-or-nothing**. A **retry** re-queues render work for an already-charged document and is never charged again; a **re-commit** of a committed batch is a no-op. **Rollback deliberately does NOT refund** — a compensating write can itself fail, and a ceiling's safe direction is to under-admit.

**Composition, never replacement.** The ROLL-4 wave/branch quota is UNCHANGED. It answers a different question — how much may this WAVE take today across every branch enrolled — that the hub ceiling cannot express. Both apply, the tighter wins, neither can widen the other. **Never delete the wave quota to "consolidate".**

**Lock ordering — do not invert.** Legacy RME takes two locks; `createFromUpload` is the single site that takes both and takes **the hub bucket first, always**. Both order by `branch_id`; `reserveMany` sorts branch ids before locking. A future caller needing both must keep this order or the two form a cycle.

**Concurrency, proven not asserted.** SQLite compiles `lockForUpdate()` to nothing, so the SQLite suite — correct as it is — cannot prove the property that matters. `LegacyImportHubConcurrencyTest` runs against **PostgreSQL 16.14** (production major) and **skips loudly** elsewhere. It also cannot use `RefreshDatabase`: that trait never commits, so a second session sees no row, and `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` on an invisible row raises nothing and would report "not locked" whether or not the lock existed — a false green on the one property the file exists to prove. It drives the real service over a connection `RefreshDatabase` does not manage, and asserts the probe CAN see the row when unheld so the lock assertion cannot pass for the wrong reason. 4 passed.

**Migration.** One additive table, `UNIQUE(import_type, branch_id, quota_date)` with every component NOT NULL (PostgreSQL treats NULLs as distinct in a unique index — a nullable component would silently permit two buckets for one day, each below the ceiling). No column altered or dropped, no backfill.

**Authorization.** The hub accepts the union of the three capabilities' view permissions and grants **nothing**: an actor holding only `manage patients` reaches the hub and still gets 403 on both archives. Branch scope = governance tier (any archive review/publish/void permission) sees the whole RME set, anyone else sees only their server-resolved branch, and only when RME-enabled; MAIN yields none. A forged `?branch_id=` changes nothing. **No permission created, widened, or granted to a new role.**

**Mutation testing — and the one that survived.** Ten mutants; nine killed immediately. **M6 (hub route's permission middleware deleted) SURVIVED**: the controller's own `abort_unless` re-check kept the *outcome* correct while a *layer* was removed, so every behavioural test stayed green. Gap closed with a test that pins the middleware list itself; M6 now killed. **Durable lesson: when a controller re-checks what middleware enforces, the middleware needs its own assertion — behavioural tests cannot see a deleted layer.**

**CI selection.** `LegacyImportHub` added to the NSF-R011 critical filter in **both** runner variants (they must stay identical), AND the quota/integration/surface suites declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` — a control that exists but is never selected is not a control, and the mutation run showed each importer's unwiring was caught ONLY by the integration suite. The concurrency suite is deliberately **not** declared mandatory: it can only ever skip on CI's SQLite, and a declared always-skipping suite reads as evidence it is not.

**Green.** `tests/Feature/LegacyImportHub` 53 passed / 4 skipped / 146 assertions (SQLite); concurrency 4 passed (PostgreSQL 16.14); `Cicd|CiMonitoring|CriticalToken` 286 passed. Legacy imports still create no clinic visit, medical record, odontogram, invoice, payment, prescription, lab order or SATUSEHAT resource.

**RBAC finding, and the owner decision that closed it.** FIX-04b created the five legacy ODONTOGRAM permissions and assigned them to **no role**, so a complete capability was reachable only by Super Admin — and because the *Master Data RME* sidebar group is wrapped in `@unless($user?->hasRole('Admin Klinik'))`, Admin Klinik could not even see the legacy **RME** entry for permissions it already held. The hub surfaced both gaps. Owner decision: **mirror the legacy RME archive exactly.** `Admin Klinik` += `view_`/`create_legacy_odontogram_imports` (intake only); `Supervisor RME` += `view_`/`review_`/`publish_legacy_odontogram_imports` (certify only). **The omissions are load-bearing**: Admin Klinik gets no review/publish so the operator who files a chart never certifies it into patient history; Supervisor RME gets no `create_` so it cannot review its own intake; `void_legacy_odontogram_records` stays Super Admin, because retracting published clinical evidence is heavier than publishing it. Doctor/Kasir/Owner/Perawat unchanged. **No permission was created** — only existing ones assigned. `LegacyOdontogramFoundationTest`'s "NO role holds these" assertion was re-anchored to the stricter **exact-holder-set per permission** (a grant to any other role, or of a withheld duty, now fails) rather than deleted; `SupervisorRmeRolePermissionTest`'s exact list repinned +3.

**Activation posture chosen for this deploy.** Both migration flags ON; **no ROLL-4 wave created**. Legacy Patient and Legacy Odontogram become fully usable immediately. Legacy RME's surface opens and its hub card states plainly that ROLL-3 branch admission and an ACTIVE wave are still required — creating one is a separate governed act with its own owner approval reference, and this sprint deliberately does not perform it.

**Deploy.** `php artisan migrate --force` (one additive table) + `db:seed --class=RoleSeeder --force` + `permission:cache-reset` (the odontogram grants). No new permission definition. **Capability activation is an environment change**: `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_PDF_ARCHIVE` and `FEATURE_RME_LEGACY_ODONTOGRAM_ARCHIVE`; Legacy Patient has no flag. Turning a flag on does not by itself make Legacy RME usable — ROLL-3 branch admission and a matching ACTIVE ROLL-4 wave are still required, and the hub card says so rather than implying a green flag is the whole story.

**SHIPPED + VPS PRODUCTION DEPLOYED (2026-08-27).** PR #349 squash-merged as `637b5a44427825b4eb08cca954eee114a55a5c23`; **merge tree `46982273d782fbb87fdd09bc800a687ef45c6741` is byte-identical to the CI-validated candidate tree** (`ef441a2`), so the squash preserved exactly what was tested. CI run `33091703594` = success — CICD-CTRL Classifier, NSF-R012 Quality, CICD-CTRL Selective Module, NSF-R011 Critical (one variant ran, the sibling skipped by design), NSF-9 Release Safety + Smoke, NSF-10 Release Evidence all green; **NSF-R011 Full Suite skipped** (GLOBAL TEMPORARY FULL-SUITE POLICY `ACTIVE`, untouched by this sprint). Annotated GO tag `feature-legacy-import-hub-1-go` @ `637b5a4`, exact-match at VPS HEAD. **Deployed** inside the VPS via `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start` (the SSH pipe did drop mid-deploy — the detached runner survived it exactly as designed): `exit=0`, `DEPLOY OK`, `DEPLOY_HEAD_TARGET_MATCH=YES`, migration `2026_08_27_100001` **Ran**, `VPS_TREE` == merge tree. Post-activation smoke **6/6 GO, 0 warnings, 0 errors**. Production HTTP on the canonical domain: `/login` `/health/live` `/health/ready` `/health/lb` all 200, `/storage/` **403**; all four legacy surfaces 302 (auth-gated) where `/settings/legacy-imports` was **404 before the deploy** — independent proof the route actually landed rather than being inferred from the deploy log. Live ceiling on production: **100 for all three types**, `max_declarable_daily` 500, hub enabled.

**DEPLOY GOTCHA WORTH REMEMBERING — seed PERMISSIONS before ROLES.** The first `db:seed --class=RoleSeeder` on production FAILED with `PermissionDoesNotExist: view_legacy_odontogram_imports`. The legacy ODONTOGRAM permissions had **never been seeded into production at all** — FIX-04b defined them in `PermissionSeeder` and assigned them to no role, and nothing had re-run that seeder since. The capability was not merely role-less, it was permission-less. Fixed by running `PermissionSeeder` **first**, then `RoleSeeder`, then `permission:cache-reset`. **The failed attempt wrote one `pilot.ERROR` line into `laravel.log` at 19:29:39 UTC**, which will hold the MON-1 logs section at WATCH for ~24h — the same self-inflicted class as the documented production-tinker case. It is a CLI seeder error, not a user-facing runtime error, it is fully corrected, and **zero errors were logged after 19:30**. Rule for any future sprint granting a permission that may never have been seeded: **`PermissionSeeder` → `RoleSeeder` → `permission:cache-reset`, in that order.** Production matrix verified afterwards with `php artisan permission:show`: Admin Klinik `view_`+`create_`; Supervisor RME `view_`+`review_`+`publish_`; `void_` Super Admin only — separation of duties intact in production.

**ACTIVATION, STATED HONESTLY.** Env flags flipped on the VPS (backup `/root/env-backup-legacy-import-hub-1-20260827-193102`, mode 0600; the env file itself stayed `640 root:daengtisiams`), config + route caches rebuilt **as the runtime user** `daengtisiams`. `foundation:feature-flags` confirms both flags `enabled=true … via=env captured=yes` — the override survives `config:cache`, so the ROLL-1 inert-override trap is avoided. `FLAG-RISKY-NOT-ENABLED` now warns naming both flags: that is the governance surface correctly REPORTING a deliberate activation, not a failure. **Legacy Patient = ACTIVE end-to-end. Legacy Odontogram = ACTIVE end-to-end. Legacy RME = CAPABILITY ON but NOT end-to-end usable**: per the owner's explicit decision this deploy creates no ROLL-4 wave and admits no branch, so the canonical post-switch proof `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness --expect=on` reports **12 GO + 1 WATCH on `branch_admission`** ("switched on but no branch is admitted, so no migration can start"). That WATCH is the chosen posture, not a defect; the hub card says so rather than implying a green flag is the whole story. Admitting a branch and activating a wave remains a separate governed act with its own owner approval reference.

## FEATURE-LEGACY-IMPORT-HUB-1A — Legacy RME Activation (2026-08-28)

Branch `feature/legacy-import-hub-1a-rme-activation` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `feature-legacy-import-hub-1-go` @ runtime `637b5a4`, branch tip `393f888` = docs-only evidence commit, intentionally undeployed; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-legacy-import-hub-1a-rme-activation.md`; runbook §8c in `docs/runbooks/legacy-rme-pdf-rollout-runbook.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/100-legacy-rme-activation-states.mdc`. **No migration, no route, no permission, no policy, no middleware change.**

**The gap closed.** HUB-1 left Legacy Patient + Legacy Odontogram end-to-end usable and Legacy RME with `rme.legacy_pdf_archive` **ON** but `LEGACY_RME_ADMITTED_BRANCH_CODES` / `..._APPROVED_BRANCH_CODES` / `..._APPROVAL_REFERENCE` / `LEGACY_RME_WAVE` all **empty** and every historical ROLL-4 wave terminal — so every upload was refused server-side for a full release while the hub reported **"Aktif"**. **Four states, never again conflated: `capability ON` ≠ `admission active` ≠ `wave active` ≠ `end-to-end usable`.**

**Code (report-only, one behavioural change).** New read-only `App\Modules\LegacyRme\Services\LegacyRmeActivationStateService` COMPOSES the existing authorities (`LegacyRmeFeatureGuard::migrationEnabled()`, `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionService` incl. `decideForBranchCode()` for the per-branch verdict, `LegacyRmeWaveBindingService`, `LegacyRmeOperationsGateService::enforced()`) and re-implements none — the report cannot drift from the gate that decides an upload (a test asserts this by direct comparison, not a hard-coded expectation). Stable blocker codes `CAPABILITY_OFF|NO_BRANCH_ADMITTED|APPROVAL_MISSING|APPROVAL_INCOMPLETE|WAVE_NOT_DECLARED|WAVE_NOT_REGISTERED|WAVE_NOT_ACTIVE|WAVE_BINDING_MISMATCH|WAVE_UNREADABLE`, ordered like the runtime chain; prose lives in the view. `LegacyImportHubService`: `has_additional_gates` now means "this TYPE has extra gates" (fixed), new `additional_gates` = "what they say now" (evaluated, `null` for types without), `status` gains **`belum_dibuka`**; state evaluated ONCE per page (no N+1). View drops the permanent footnote "Halaman ini tidak dapat memastikan keduanya". **Gotcha:** `LegacyImportHubService` is autowired — its constructor gained a 5th arg; nothing constructs it manually.

**Production activation (owner decisions, not inferred).** Approval `ROLL-4-WAVE-4-OWNER-APPROVAL-2026-08-28`, wave **`WAVE-4`** (ROLL-4 lineage: Wave-1 COMPLETED, Wave-2 CANCELLED, Wave-2R COMPLETED, Wave-3 formally SKIPPED), admitted **TKM1, LDK2, ATG3, SUN4**, per-branch daily **100**. Excluded: MAIN (not RME-enabled + permanently forbidden), SYN4A (inactive/soft-deleted synthetic). **Operator staffing truth:** production has exactly ONE branch-bound clinic operator — Yuni FO (user 7, Admin Klinik, LDK2); TKM1/ATG3/SUN4 have no Admin Klinik account, so per owner decision **Super Admin operates those three** (Supervisor RME publishes; separate-publisher/approver stay enforced — account separation is verifiable, human separation is not and is never claimed). **Standing follow-up:** give TKM1/ATG3/SUN4 their own Admin Klinik accounts and reassign. **Activation imports nothing** — no scan, no bulk ingest.

**Wave quota gotcha:** the ROLL-4 operations layer's own default per-branch daily quota is **25**; a wave must pass `--per-branch-daily-quota=100` explicitly or it silently narrows the approved 100 ceiling to a quarter. Two counters apply (hub `ops_legacy_import_daily_quotas` + the wave's own) and the tighter wins.

**Mutation-testing gotchas (both cost real time).** (1) A mutation harness must revert by **file copy, never `git checkout --`**: the baseline was uncommitted and one target untracked, so `git checkout` failed to restore the mutated file (mutations ACCUMULATED, making every verdict unreliable) while silently reverting real uncommitted work in a tracked file. The contaminated run's "12/12 killed" was discarded. (2) Re-run from a verified-green baseline surfaced 2 survivors: **M9 admission-allowlist bypass was a REAL gap** — every existing test kept the admitted and approved sets identical, letting the approval-coverage check mask a broken allowlist; the uncovered case is the staged rollout (approve the whole wave, admit branches one at a time) where **approved-but-not-admitted must still be refused**. Test added, M9 killed. **M10 MAIN-forbidden bypass is an equivalent mutant** — MAIN is guarded twice (`decide()` check + the filter inside `admittedBranchCodes()`); removing BOTH is killed, proving the protection is covered. Final: 16 attempted, 15 killed, 1 equivalent, **0 real survivors**.

**Two fail-open defects were found by SELF-REVIEW, not by the tests, both in this sprint's own new code:** (a) the hub disclosed gate state (wave code/status/admitted branches) to an actor who may not view the capability — the closed gate paragraph was permission-guarded but the open one was not; fixed in the PAYLOAD (`$gates = $hasAdditionalGates && $mayView ? ... : null`) not the template, so page and data cannot drift again; (b) **a failure to evaluate the gates was reported as an ABSENCE of gates** — the hub `catch` returned `null`, `null` means "type has no extra gates", so `status()` fell through to **`aktif`**, restoring the exact lie the sprint removes via an exception. Fixed with `LegacyRmeActivationStateService::unavailable()` (`open=false`, blocker `STATE_UNAVAILABLE`, same key shape as a real evaluation). Reverting that one line to `null` fails the new test — verified by mutation.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/LegacyImportHub/LegacyImportHubOperationalStateTest.php` (17) + `tests/Feature/LegacyRme/LegacyRmeActivationContractTest.php` (8, the four-branch production-shaped rehearsal — proven here rather than by publishing fake evidence into real patient histories); 2 existing HUB-1 surface assertions updated (they encoded the disclaimer). Green: LegacyImportHub + LegacyRme **996 passed / 9 skipped / 0 failed**; `sprint:manifest-check`/`sprint:scope-audit`/`foundation:devflow-check`/`foundation:shared-service-audit` GO; pint + `git diff --check` clean. **Test-fixture gotcha:** `legacyRmeArchivablePatient()` calls `legacyRmeBranch()`, which ADMITS and ENROLS its branch — build the patient BEFORE pinning a narrower allowlist, or a denial test passes for the opposite reason.

Rollback = clear the four env keys (NORMAL DRAIN: new intake stops, staged evidence finishes, published archives stay readable); capability OFF is the EMERGENCY STOP. Neither deletes a document.

**FEATURE-LEGACY-IMPORT-HUB-1A SHIPPED + ACTIVATED IN PRODUCTION (2026-08-28 WITA):** PR #351 squash-merged as `76bd7c0` (tree `08d03e5d` — identical to candidate `1173613`, squash preserved the tree exactly). CI run `33130384816` success: CICD-CTRL Classifier / NSF-R012 Quality / NSF-R011 Critical / CICD-CTRL Selective Module / NSF-9 Release Safety+Smoke / NSF-10 Release Evidence all pass; NSF-R011 Full Suite **skipped** per the standing CI-TEMP-FULL-SUITE-SCHEDULE-GATE deferral (not authorized by this sprint, not run). Deployed INSIDE the VPS via `bash scripts/deploy-vps-runner.sh start` — `exit=0`, `DEPLOY OK`, `DEPLOY_HEAD_TARGET_MATCH=YES`; VPS HEAD/tree `76bd7c0`/`08d03e5d`. GO tag `feature-legacy-import-hub-1a-rme-activation-go` @ `76bd7c0`. **Production activation applied:** env `LEGACY_RME_ADMITTED_BRANCH_CODES`/`..._APPROVED_BRANCH_CODES`=`TKM1,LDK2,ATG3,SUN4`, `..._APPROVAL_REFERENCE`=`ROLL-4-WAVE-4-OWNER-APPROVAL-2026-08-28`, `LEGACY_RME_WAVE`=`WAVE-4`; wave WAVE-4 registered(actor 1)→approved(actor 11 Supervisor RME, separate approver)→ACTIVE, 4 branches ACTIVE, per-branch daily 100, window 2026-08-28..2026-09-30; operators LDK2→7, TKM1/ATG3/SUN4→1. **Verified:** `legacy-rme:rollout-readiness` **GO** (branch_admission WATCH cleared), `legacy-rme:migration-status` "Accepting new documents: **yes**" + binding matches, **0 documents accepted by activation** (every branch 0/0/0/0, storage 0 docs, reconciliation balanced — activation imports nothing), canonical-domain `/login`+`/health/*` 200 and `/storage/*` 403, **0 new Laravel errors** (log byte-size unchanged at 791487, zero entries dated 2026-08-28).

**Two production gotchas (both cost real time):** (1) **Order is load-bearing** — `createWave()` mirrors the DECLARED approval + approved branch set at creation time and rejects branches outside it, so env must be set and config cache rebuilt (as the runtime user) BEFORE registering the wave, else registration fails or records a null approval that then reads as a binding mismatch. (2) **`sed -i` on the environment file breaks its ownership** — it is `0640 root:daengtisiams` (INFRA-SEC-ENV-1) and `sed -i` recreates the inode as `root:root`, silently cutting the runtime's read access; re-assert `chown root:daengtisiams` + `chmod 640` and verify `runuser -u daengtisiams -- test -r .env` immediately after any edit. Also: the NSF-9 smoke's `http://127.0.0.1/login` 404 is the KNOWN co-tenant nginx loopback shadow, not a regression — verify over `https://daengtisia.online`.

**ACCEPTED WATCH — batch sizing (owner decision, do not "fix" silently).** `legacy-rme:ops-readiness` reports `batch_size_policy` WATCH + `ready_for_routine_batch=false` because WAVE-4 declares no WAVE-level daily quota. The check passes ONLY at a wave-level quota **≤100/day across all four branches combined** (above 100 → "exceeds the routine envelope"), so **no wave-level value both satisfies the routine envelope and preserves the owner-approved 100 PER BRANCH** — the envelope is derived from single-worker queue throughput bounded by human review capacity, and a 4-branch wave is not a routine batch by that definition. Owner chose to **accept the WATCH unchanged**: every branch is still hard-capped at 100/day by TWO independent counters (hub ceiling + wave per-branch quota), and raising `LEGACY_RME_ROUTINE_MAX_DAILY` to 400 would only move the line the check measures against while a **single** Supervisor RME account reviews/publishes for all four branches. WATCH is non-blocking (`blockers=[]`, `stop_the_line=[]`, FAIL=0). **There is NO post-registration setter for the wave-level quota** (settable only at `register`), so revisiting it means cancel + re-register under a new code — cheapest while few documents exist. **Standing follow-up:** give TKM1/ATG3/SUN4 their own Admin Klinik operator accounts and reassign, so clinic archives are migrated by clinic staff rather than the governance account.

## FEATURE-DAILY-BRANCH-CONTEXT-LOCK-1 — Daily Working-Branch Lock for Kasir & Admin Klinik (2026-08-29)

Branch `feature/daily-branch-context-lock-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, BASE_SHA `b9d05a8`; production runtime at start `76bd7c0` / tag `feature-legacy-import-hub-1a-rme-activation-go` — the base tip was one docs-only commit ahead, deliberately undeployed). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-daily-branch-context-lock-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-daily-branch-context-lock.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml`.

**The defect closed.** A **Kasir** or **Admin Klinik** could change working branch as often as they liked by simply re-opening the online-context selector (`POST /rme/online-context/kasir|admin-clinic`), which rewrote the single `trx_user_online_contexts` row. That row is priority #1 in `BranchContext::forUser()` and `RmeWorkingBranchScope` resolves the whole clinic-operations **and cashier financial** workspace from it, so a free re-selection silently moved which invoices, receivables and payments the cashier could act on. Proven on the baseline with a probe (select A → `BranchContext`=A; select B → `BranchContext`=B, both passing); that probe is now inverted in the shipped suite.

**Contract.** First branch of a clinical day = free; a second *different* branch = refused until a Super Admin approves; the *same* branch again = idempotent; a new clinical day = one new free selection. **Doctor and Perawat are deliberately NOT locked.**

**The authority is a table, never the session.** New `trx_daily_branch_contexts`, `UNIQUE(user_id, clinical_date)`, `current_branch_id` = the authority. `UserOnlineContextService::activeContextBranchId()` lets it **override** the session-scoped online-context row, so a tampered/stale `branch_id` does not move the scope and an approved switch takes effect in every existing session on the next request. The override only ever *replaces* a branch, never resurrects a `null` (an offline operator still has no working context).

**Key key.** Uniqueness is `(user_id, clinical_date)` and deliberately **NOT** `role_context` — a user holding both `Kasir` and `Admin Klinik` would otherwise open one role context at one branch and the other at a second branch on the same day. One human, one clinical day, one branch.

**Guard placement.** `DailyBranchContextService::assertSelectable()` is called inside `startAdminClinicSession()`/`startKasirSession()` **after** the branch-eligibility assert — the single chokepoint covering HTTP, console and the test helpers at once, and ordered so an approved switch can never become a route to a branch the user was not entitled to work in.

**An approval is an event, not a credential.** The switch is applied INSIDE the approval transaction, so there is no replayable token. Every binding is re-asserted under the row lock: user (the requester's context is locked, not the approver's), **source** (the live context must still sit on `source_branch_id`, else stale → refused), **destination** (re-validated for eligibility at decision time — an approval is never a grant of access to a branch the user could not otherwise work in), **clinical day** (a previous-day request is refused **without relying on a cron**).

**Two gotchas found by testing, not review, both in the day-boundary guard.** (a) An opportunistic `EXPIRED` stamp inside the decision transaction is rolled back by the very exception that refuses the decision. (b) Moving that stamp to a pre-transaction step then made the staleness guard **unreachable** — the refusal a test observed actually came from the `isPending()` check a few lines later, so deleting the guard left every test green (caught by mutation M13). Final design: staleness is checked FIRST, inside the locked decision, and is the single observable boundary; the `EXPIRED` bookkeeping lives only in the queue listing (`BranchChangeRequestRepository::expireStale`, called by `index()`). The test asserts the row is still `PENDING` at the moment of the attempt, so the refusal can only be the guard.

**Self-approval is NOT enforced by the policy.** `Gate::before` grants Super Admin every ability before any policy runs, so `BranchChangeRequestPolicy::decide()`'s self-check never executes for the one actor who could be both. The enforced comparison lives in `BranchChangeApprovalService::lockPendingRequest()`. The policy clause is defence-in-depth for non-Super-Admins.

**No new permission.** Approval authority is the canonical Super Admin role, published once as the `branch-change-request.approve` gate and consumed by BOTH the route group and the sidebar (same shape as `satusehat.access`). Inventing `approve_branch_switch` would let approval authority be granted to someone the lock constrains.

**Mass assignment gotcha.** `status`/`decided_by_user_id`/`decided_at`/`decision_note`/`applied_at` are absent from `BranchChangeRequest::$fillable` (a forged `status=approved` has nowhere to land) — which means `update()` **silently discards** exactly the fields a decision must write. The repository therefore uses `forceFill`, and `create()` states the initial `pending` status explicitly (a mass-assigned create returns a model with NO `status` attribute, so `isPending()` answers false on a genuinely pending row).
