**Migrations (additive only — `migrate`, never `migrate:fresh`/`db:wipe`):** `2026_08_29_100001_create_trx_daily_branch_contexts_table`, `2026_08_29_100002_create_trx_branch_change_requests_table` (partial unique index `WHERE status='pending'` = at most one open request per user per clinical day; branch FKs `restrictOnDelete`). **No seeder, no permission, no role change.** **First-day note:** on deployment day every locked operator's next selection is their free first selection, because no daily context exists yet — intended, not a gap.

**Clinical day** comes from `ClinicalClock` (Asia/Makassar), never `Carbon::today()`. The interval is a **calendar day**, not a rolling 24 hours from the first selection; both properties are pinned with a frozen clock (23:59:59 WITA still locked, 00:00:00 WITA free again, and a 09:00 selection does not stay locked until 09:00 tomorrow).

**PostgreSQL transaction-abort trap (found by CI, invisible to SQLite).** PostgreSQL aborts the WHOLE transaction on any failed statement (`25P02` thereafter until rollback), so catching a unique violation and continuing — green on SQLite — leaves a poisoned connection. Hit BOTH violation handlers; the serious one was `assertSelectable()`'s first-selection race re-read, which runs inside `DB::transaction` and was therefore **broken on production PostgreSQL**. The PG concurrency suite missed it because its cases are refused by the lock check before reaching the INSERT. Fix: wrap each INSERT in a nested `DB::transaction` (Laravel = `SAVEPOINT`). Verified by reproducing on `postgres:16.14` then re-running: `tests/Feature/AccessControl` = **108 passed on PostgreSQL where nothing skips** (SQLite reports 9 skipped). **Standing rule: code that catches a DB error and continues must be exercised against PostgreSQL, never trusted from a SQLite run.**

**Concurrency** is database-level: `UNIQUE(user_id, clinical_date)` (the first-selection loser re-reads the committed winner and is judged against it — never a second context), the partial unique index, and `lockForUpdate()` on both the context and the request row. Row locks are unobservable on SQLite, so `DailyBranchContextConcurrencyTest` runs on **PostgreSQL 16** and **skips out loud** elsewhere (pattern lifted from `LegacyImportHubConcurrencyTest`).

**Surfaces.** `rme.branch-change-requests.{create,store,cancel}` for the operator; `.{index,approve,reject}` behind the Super Admin gate. All six are added to `EnsureRmeOnlineContext::EXEMPT_ROUTE_NAMES` — without it a locked-but-offline cashier is bounced to the selector where the only branch offered is the one they are escaping. It widens nothing: `BranchChangeRequestPolicy::create` still requires a daily context that only a completed selection creates. The online-context selector now renders the locked branch (single option + "Ajukan Pindah Cabang") instead of a free dropdown that only fails on submit; the hidden option is a courtesy, the server guard is the boundary.

**Blast radius:** exactly one existing test changed — `FixClinicOpsWorkingBranchContextTest > "follows the cashier to a new branch when the working context changes"`. Its intent (cashier scope follows the working branch) is unchanged and still asserted; it became two tests — refused without an approval, applied with one.

Tests: `tests/Feature/AccessControl/{DailyBranchContextLockTest,BranchChangeApprovalTest,DailyBranchContextBypassTest,DailyBranchContextConcurrencyTest}.php` + `tests/Feature/AccessControl/helpers.php`. CI: `DailyBranchContext|BranchChange` added to BOTH critical-gate filter variants and the four suites declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` (a security control that the gate never selects is not a control).

## REVISION-RME-REPORTS-TODAY-DEFAULT-1 — RME reports default to the clinical today (2026-08-29)

Branch `revision/rme-reports-today-default-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `1f80286`, production runtime, GO tag `feature-daily-branch-context-lock-1-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-rme-reports-today-default-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/129-rme-report-default-clinical-today.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml`.

**Laporan Pasien RME** (`rme.reports.patients*`) and **Laporan Pembayaran RME** (`rme.reports.payments*`) now show **only the current clinical day** by default. **No migration, no route, no permission, no policy, no schema change**; no registration/queue/room/consent/RME/odontogram/visit-completion/invoice/payment write behaviour touched.

**The defect.** Both reports applied their date predicate only when the operator supplied one (`->when($request->filled('date_from'), ...)`), so a bare report URL returned **every visit and every payment the branch had ever recorded** — and the CSV export and print view share those query builders, so one click on *Export Excel* from a freshly opened report produced a full historical extract.

**Contract:** no explicit date filter → `from = to = clinical today`; an explicit valid filter → that period; **reset → today, never all history**. Explicit outranks default; the period is **always bounded**.

**Canonical report date = `trx_clinic_visits.visit_date` for BOTH reports** (audited, not assumed). The payment report already reached it through `whereHas('clinicVisit')`, so its date **semantics are unchanged** — only the default changed. Deliberately NOT switched to `trx_rme_payments.paid_at`, and visit/payment/invoice dates were not collapsed together.

**New:** `App\Modules\RmeInvoice\Support\RmeReportDateScope` (the single normalization authority) + `RmeReportDateRange` (immutable period + Indonesian label). All **six** surfaces — patients/payments × index/export/print — read **one** range per request, which is what makes screen, totals, CSV, print and filter summary provably agree; the screen can no longer say "today" while the export returns the archive. The print filter summary now **always** states `Periode: …` (a printout that omitted its own range could not be audited).

**Gotcha — memoize on the REQUEST, never on the controller.** The first implementation used a `private ?RmeReportDateRange` property on `RmeReportController`. Laravel caches the controller instance on the `Route` object and the `Router` is a singleton, so that property survives between requests wherever the app is not torn down per request (every multi-request test, and any long-lived worker) — it pinned the report to the first request's day and served a stale "today" past the clinical midnight. The memo now lives in `$request->attributes`. **Found by running the clinical-midnight test, not by reading the code.** This is STATELESS-1 applied to a controller property.

**"Today" is `App\Support\Clinical\ClinicalClock`** (Asia/Makassar) — never `now()`/`Carbon::today()`/`date()`. `config/app.php` is UTC by design; between 16:00 and 24:00 UTC it is already tomorrow in the clinic.

**Fail closed.** A malformed/partial/empty bound (`not-a-date`, `29/08/2026`, `2026-13-45`, `''`) is treated as absent, and no usable bound means today — **no input turns an unfiltered report into an all-history report**. Bounds parse **strictly** via `!Y-m-d` with a round-trip check, because `Carbon::parse('2026-13-45')` silently rolls to 2027-02-14 (same rollover class as MONITORING-LOG-TIMESTAMP-ROLLOVER-1).

**Branch authority untouched.** A date filter widens the **period**, never the **branch scope**: every report query ANDs `authorised branch scope AND date period AND search`. `RmeWorkingBranchScope` stays the only branch authority (FIX-04/FIX-09), a request `branch_id` is still only narrowing, and the daily branch lock still decides a context-bound Kasir's/Admin Klinik's branch. Proven by *"never lets a historical date filter widen the Admin Klinik branch scope"* (historical range **+** crafted foreign `branch_id` → own branch only).

**Test-fixture flake closed.** `RmeReportFilterTest`/`RmeReportExportTest` stamped "today" fixtures with `now()->toDateString()` (**UTC**), overriding `ClinicVisitFactory`'s own `ClinicalClock` default (FIX-06). Harmless while reports were unbounded; the moment the default became today, those fixtures were *clinical yesterday* for the eight hours between 16:00–24:00 UTC and both suites would have failed for a third of every day. Both now use the existing `clinicalToday()` helper. Same class as the lab SLA midnight flake.

**Surfaces that do not exist (reported, not claimed):** no pagination (both lists use `->limit(100)`, no `page` param), no user-facing sorting (order fixed at `latest('visit_date')`/`latest('paid_at')`), no server-side PDF (*Cetak / PDF* is a Blade print view + browser print, sharing the report query builder). Adding any later must preserve the normalized period.

**Durable rules:** (1) both RME reports default to the clinical today; (2) history requires an explicit date filter; (3) reset returns to today; (4) search — and any future pagination/sorting — stays inside the selected period; (5) export and print use the same normalized period as the screen; (6) a date filter never widens branch authorization; (7) `RmeWorkingBranchScope` is the branch authority and request `branch_id` is never authority; (8) `ClinicalClock` is the only "today"; (9) an invalid filter fails closed to today; (10) the period is memoized per request, never on a controller; (11) canonical report date is `trx_clinic_visits.visit_date`.

Tests: new `tests/Feature/RME/RmeReportTodayDefaultTest.php` (26 — default/explicit/reset/search/malformed/export/print/branch matrix for Admin Klinik + Kasir/clinical-midnight rollover). **Mutation validation:** 12 attempted, 10 killed, 0 real survivors, 2 N/A (M5 no pagination surface; M9 identical code path to M3). Two lessons: M4's first "survivor" was the harness failing to apply its regex — a mutation that does not apply is an INVALID run, never coverage; and M8 (drop the controller `allows()` guard) survived initially because `RmeWorkingBranchScope::narrow()` re-checks membership independently, so row data is protected twice — but `resolveBranchId()` also feeds the print filter summary, the one path with no downstream re-check, where a crafted `branch_id` would print a foreign branch NAME above correctly-scoped rows; now pinned by *"never echoes a foreign branch name into the printed filter summary"*. CI: `RmeReportTodayDefault` added to both critical-gate filter variants and to `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` (a control the gate never selects is not a control).

## REVISION-NEW-VISIT-PATIENT-SEARCH-COMBOBOX-1 — Kunjungan Baru patient selection (2026-08-29)

Branch `revision/new-visit-patient-search-combobox-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `revision-rme-reports-today-default-1-go` @ `756bc7d`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-new-visit-patient-search-combobox-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/100-new-visit-patient-search-combobox.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **No migration, no permission, no policy, no route rename.**

**What it replaced.** "Kunjungan Baru" had TWO stacked controls: a `<input type="search">` that only set `option.hidden`, plus a native `<select name="patient_id">` **preloaded with every patient the controller returned** — and each `<option>` label ended with that patient's **phone number**. The controller query was `Patient::with('branch')->orderBy('name')` with `DoctorPatientScopeService::applyPatientScopeForUser`, which returns the query **unchanged for every non-Doctor role** — so Admin Klinik / Kasir / Perawat / Owner / Supervisor RME / Super Admin got every patient in every branch (RME-enabled or not, active or not) serialised into the page. Collapsing to one control was the visible half; removing the estate-wide preload and scoping the search was the substantive half.

**Now.** ONE `<x-patient-combobox>` (hidden `patient_id` is the only submitted value) backed by `GET rme/visits/patient-search` (`rme.visits.patient-search` → `ClinicVisitController::patientSearch`, gated by the ClinicVisit `create` ability). New `App\Modules\Patient\Services\PatientSelectorSearchService` (`MIN_QUERY_LENGTH=2`, `RESULT_LIMIT=15`) → new `PatientRepositoryInterface::searchSelectable()`/`findSelectable()` (the service owns authorization, the repository owns the query; doctor scope passed down as a `Closure`). **Branch scope is delegated to `RmeWorkingBranchScope::branchIdsFor()`** — the same canonical authority `ClinicVisitPolicy` uses: a context-bound role (Admin Klinik/Perawat/Kasir) searches ONLY its active working branch and **fails closed to empty** with no context; governance roles get the active RME-enabled set; Doctors are additionally narrowed by `DoctorPatientScopeService`; legacy `branch_id IS NULL` patients stay selectable; non-RME-branch patients never are. **The endpoint accepts no `branch_id` and no `limit`** — neither can be widened/raised from the query string. LIKE metacharacters escaped with an explicit `ESCAPE '\'` clause (portable PG + SQLite).

**Least disclosure:** the response carries exactly `id`, `name`, `medical_record_number`, `branch_label` — pinned by an **exact key-set assertion**, so phone/WhatsApp/KTP/address/DOB/email cannot reappear silently. (`branch_label` is included on the `CrossBranchPatientLookupService` precedent that classifies it non-sensitive; for an RM-bearing patient it only restates the branch code already inside the Nomor RM.)

**Submit-time boundary (closed a real pre-existing IDOR):** `StoreClinicVisitRequest` validated `patient_id` with `Rule::exists` **and nothing else**, so any authorized user could POST any patient id from any branch. It now re-asserts via `PatientSelectorSearchService::isSelectable()` — deliberately the SAME scope the search uses, so selectable and submittable cannot drift apart. `prepareForValidation` also nulls `patient_id` when `patient_mode === 'new'` (a hidden panel still submits its inputs).

**Browser-only invariants get their own test layer.** The state machine lives in `resources/js/patient-combobox.js` (a plain factory; `app.js` only does `Alpine.data('patientCombobox', ...)`) so it is unit-testable: `tests/js/patient-combobox.test.mjs` via **Node's built-in runner, no new dependency** (`npm run test:js`, 18 tests). It pins (a) typed text is NEVER a selection and any keystroke after a selection clears `patient_id`; (b) a slower earlier response can never overwrite a newer query — monotonic request sequence + AbortController, re-checked after **every** await **including after `response.json()`** (the second suspension point, the one usually forgotten). **Do not move this back inline into `app.js`.**

**Gotchas found:** (1) `_form.blade.php` had a second `<x-patient-search-select>` in an `@else` branch for `$visit !== null` — unreachable dead code (`_form` is only included from `create.blade.php` with `visit => null`; `edit.blade.php` does not include it) referencing a now-absent `$patients`. Removed — it was exactly the "hidden duplicate select" this revision must not leave behind. (2) A test that switched an Admin Klinik's branch mid-day via `rmeMakeAdminClinicActive` correctly **threw** — the DAILY-BRANCH-CONTEXT-LOCK-1 lock owns mid-day moves; the test now proves both halves (unapproved switch refused, then `BranchChangeApprovalService::request()`+`approve()` and the search follows). (3) `PatientFactory` sets no `branch_id`, so existing fixtures are legacy-null and stay selectable — that is why the new submit-time boundary did not break the RME suite.

**Deliberately unchanged:** inactive patients stay selectable ("inactive" ≠ "unauthorized"); `CrossBranchPatientLookupService` untouched and must never become a selection source (it returns no patient id); the legacy `x-patient-search-select` component still serves `resources/views/lab-orders/_form.blade.php` (dental Lab Order — different page, out of scope, carries the same preload+phone shape and deserves its own sprint). Dusk hooks `@patient-search` / `@patient-select` preserved so `tests/Browser/RmeCreateSmokeTest` still asserts the real control.

Tests: `tests/Feature/RME/NewVisitPatientSearchComboboxTest.php` (32) + `tests/js/patient-combobox.test.mjs` (19).

## BUGFIX-NEW-VISIT-PATIENT-SEARCH-RUNTIME-1 — New Visit patient search survives PostgreSQL (2026-08-29)

Branch `bugfix/new-visit-patient-search-runtime-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `revision-new-visit-patient-search-combobox-1-go` @ `eba713b`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/bugfix-new-visit-patient-search-runtime-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/130-raw-sql-driver-parity-and-gate-selection.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (RUNTIME_FIX). **Production regression fix. NO migration, no schema, no permission, no policy, no route, no route rename, no frontend change.**

**The outage.** Every patient search on "Kunjungan Baru" returned HTTP 500 in production; the operator only ever saw the frontend's generic transport message `Gagal mencari pasien. Silakan coba lagi.`, so a total breakdown of patient selection looked like an unhelpful UI. Measured from production logs, not guessed: one `pilot.ERROR` per keystroke on route `rme.visits.patient-search`, `SQLSTATE[HY093]: Invalid parameter number: parameter was not defined`. The request reached the controller, passed authorization and validation, and was correctly branch-scoped (`branch_id in (2)`) — it died in the driver.

**Root cause.** `PatientRepository::searchSelectable()` escaped LIKE metacharacters with a backslash and declared it in SQL: `->whereRaw("LOWER(name) LIKE LOWER(?) ESCAPE '\\'", [$like])`, rendering `ESCAPE '\'`. That is valid SQL and both engines execute it. The fault is one layer up: PDO must rewrite `?` into `$n` before pdo_pgsql can send a statement, and **through PHP 8.3** its parser treats a backslash inside a single-quoted literal as escaping the closing quote — the literal runs on, swallows the placeholders behind it, and PDO binds fewer parameters than the three supplied. Isolated at the PDO layer: **pgsql + PHP 8.3.33 = FAIL (HY093); pgsql + PHP 8.5.4 = OK; sqlite (either) = OK.**

**Why every test was green — three blind spots at once.** (1) **Driver**: pdo_sqlite takes positional `?` natively, so PDO never runs the rewriting parser and the fault is *genuinely absent* there, not merely untested; `phpunit.xml` sets `DB_CONNECTION=sqlite`. (2) **Selection**: `NewVisitPatientSearchComboboxTest` already covered branch scope, IDOR, privacy, bounds and wildcards — and matched **no token** in either NSF-R011 critical filter, so the one failing combination (PostgreSQL 16 on PHP 8.3, which is both the gate and production) was never pointed at it. (3) **Runtime identity**: the VPS default CLI is PHP **8.5.8** — that is the co-tenant `aish-pos`; DaengtisiaMS is served by the `php8.3-fpm` pool `daengtisiams` (`/etc/php/8.3/fpm/pool.d/daengtisiams.conf`), so reproducing from the VPS command line would also have passed.

**Fix, in three parts.** (a) The LIKE escape character is now the private constant `PatientRepository::LIKE_ESCAPE = '!'`, read by both the SQL clause and `escapeLike()` so they cannot drift; the explicit `ESCAPE` clause stays (it is what keeps `%`/`_` literal identically on both engines) and the character escapes itself first. (b) Both patient-search suites are declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` **and** selected by a `NewVisitPatientSearch` token in **both** critical-gate variants — declaring without a token fails `CriticalGateSuiteCoverageTest`, a token without a declaration can be dropped silently. (c) New `tests/Feature/RME/NewVisitPatientSearchRuntimeTest.php` replays PDO's own tokenising rule in PHP and asserts placeholder count equals binding count, so the class fails on SQLite and on PHP 8.5 too and can never again hide behind whichever database the suite points at.

**Preserved, explicitly.** No authorization was relaxed to make search work: `RmeWorkingBranchScope` still owns branch scope, the endpoint still accepts no `branch_id`, the daily branch context still owns mid-day moves, `patient_id` is still re-authorized at submit, ceiling 15 / min query 2 / debounce 300 ms unchanged, response still exactly `id`/`name`/`medical_record_number`/`branch_label`. The frontend is untouched — it was correct, and rendering the error state for a real 500 is its job.

**Recorded, not fixed (out of scope):** `patientVisitOptions` lacks a branch check; the dental Lab Order form still exposes phone via the retired `x-patient-search-select`; `CrossBranchPatientLookupService` and `LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditService` escape with a backslash but emit no `ESCAPE` clause (cannot hit this fault; their escaping is a no-op on SQLite); `ReportingSummaryGovernanceService` has `ESCAPE '\'` in four statements that carry **zero bindings**, so there are no placeholders to miscount.

**Durable rules (mirror 130):** a SQL string literal must never close on a backslash · SQLite green is not PostgreSQL green · production's PHP is the FPM pool's version, not the CLI default · a suite no filter token matches is not a control · a successful empty result is not an error (`SUCCESS_WITH_RESULTS` / `SUCCESS_EMPTY_RESULTS` / `SEARCH_RUNTIME_ERROR` stay distinct).

## REVISION-NEW-VISIT-GLOBAL-PATIENT-LOOKUP-1 — New Visit patient lookup is global patient identity (2026-08-30)

Branch `revision/new-visit-global-patient-lookup-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `bugfix-new-visit-patient-search-runtime-1-go` @ `31a88b8c`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-new-visit-global-patient-lookup-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/101-new-visit-global-patient-identity-lookup.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (MODULE_SPRINT). **Deliberate business-rule revision, not a bug fix. NO migration, no schema, no column, no index, no route, no route rename, no permission, no policy, no role change.**

**THE CANONICAL RULE THIS SPRINT ESTABLISHES — GLOBAL PATIENT IDENTITY FOUNDATION.** Authorized New Visit registration may search the global patient registry by patient **name** or **Nomor RM**. Patient identity lookup is global for registration, but **clinical history authorization remains independently scoped**. Search may disclose only minimum registration identity. Selecting a patient from another branch **never** changes the operator's `DailyBranchContext`. The new `ClinicVisit` branch comes from the operator's authorized work context — **not** from the patient's RM origin branch and **not** from a request `branch_id`. A cross-branch patient selection **reuses the existing patient master row**: it must not mutate the patient's RM and must not create a duplicate patient.

```
PATIENT IDENTITY  !=  VISIT BRANCH AUTHORITY
```

**And the domain distinction that must never be conflated:** for **LEGACY RME**, the RM branch code may determine the Legacy evidence branch; for a **NATIVE NEW VISIT**, `DailyBranchContext` determines the visit branch. Different domains, different rules.

**What changed, precisely.** One method: `PatientSelectorSearchService::authorizedBranchIds()` now returns `BranchService::rmeEnabledIds()` (plus the repository's existing legacy `branch_id IS NULL` admission) instead of `RmeWorkingBranchScope::branchIdsFor()`. That service is already registration-specific — the search endpoint, the submit-time `patient_id` re-authorization (`StoreClinicVisitRequest::validateExistingPatientSelectable`) and the create-page prefill all resolve through it — so **search selectability == submit selectability** structurally, not by convention. No `Patient::all()`, no preload, no new god-service.

**Three deliberate design calls, each defensible and each tested.** (1) **"The registry" = active + RME-enabled branches, not every `mst_patients` row.** That is exactly what a governance role (Owner / Supervisor RME / Super Admin) already reads; going wider would newly expose MAIN, disabled and non-RME branches that *no* role sees and where no visit may be registered. (2) **The fail-closed gate survived.** A context-bound operator (Admin Klinik / Perawat / Kasir) with **no valid working context** still reads nothing — global means "any branch's patient", not "no authority required"; someone not working anywhere cannot register anywhere. This also stops the enumeration surface widening to unattached accounts, at zero cost to the requirement. (3) **The doctor clinical scope was left alone.** A Doctor holds `manage_clinic_visits` and is a registration actor, but `DoctorPatientScopeService` still narrows them to their own RM scope — that is a CLINICAL scope, not a branch scope, and widening it would be exactly the identity-vs-clinical confusion this sprint exists to prevent.

**`RmeWorkingBranchScope` was NOT globally relaxed.** It remains the canonical working-branch authority for the visit list, patient queue, RME reports, cashier and receivables. Only this one registration identity lookup is global. Do not relax the scope service itself to satisfy a lookup requirement.

**Visit branch authority — unchanged, now pinned by tests.** For a context-bound operator `StoreClinicVisitRequest::prepareForValidation()` already overwrites `branch_id` with the daily context before validation runs, and `ClinicVisitService::resolveBranchId()` re-checks it against the active RME set. This sprint adds no behaviour there — it adds the tests that make the behaviour impossible to lose, because a widened lookup is precisely the change that would make losing it plausible. Governance roles are not context-bound by design and still pick the branch on the form; that selector is their authorized work context.

**Preserved verbatim:** response keys exactly `id`/`name`/`medical_record_number`/`branch_label` (never phone, WhatsApp, KTP/NIK, address, DOB, email, occupation, or anything clinical — the wider scope makes this ceiling *more* important); `MIN_QUERY_LENGTH` 2; `RESULT_LIMIT` 15; debounce 300 ms; monotonic-sequence + AbortController stale-response safety; endpoint accepts no `branch_id` and no `limit`; `LIKE_ESCAPE = '!'` (never a backslash — see BUGFIX-NEW-VISIT-PATIENT-SEARCH-RUNTIME-1) and the placeholder-parity guard suite. Frontend churn is one line of helper copy: the combobox already rendered `branch_label`, which is **disambiguation only and never authority**.

**CI gate.** `NewVisitGlobalPatientLookupTest` matched **no** token in either critical filter — the same blind spot that let the predecessor outage ship. It is now declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` and selected by a `NewVisitGlobalPatientLookup` token in **both** gate variants.

**Rules synchronised, no contradiction left standing:** `.cursor/rules/100-new-visit-patient-search-combobox.mdc` section 3 (branch-scoped lookup) is explicitly marked **SUPERSEDED** and re-pointed at the new rule 101; section 8 re-worded. Rule 100's other nine sections stay in force.

**Evidence.** TDD observed — the new suite failed **16 of 29** before any production code changed. `NewVisitGlobalPatientLookupTest` 29 passed; combobox+runtime suites 42 passed; **combined 71 passed / 305 assertions on PostgreSQL 16.14 + PHP 8.3.33** (the pinned CI runtime image, production's FPM major.minor, driver asserted as `pgsql` rather than assumed — SQLite green is not PostgreSQL green); regression 639 passed / 9 skipped; `tests/js/patient-combobox.test.mjs` 19 passed; Dusk `NewVisitGlobalPatientLookupBrowserTest` (new, a genuinely context-bound Admin Klinik — a Super Admin would prove nothing, it already had estate-wide scope) 2 passed + `NewVisitPatientSearchBrowserTest` 4 passed. **Mutation: 15 attempted, 15 killed, 0 real survivors** (revert by file copy, never `git checkout --`, because the sprint adds untracked test files).

**Recorded, NOT fixed here (and this sprint is explicitly not their justification):** `ClinicVisitController::patientVisitOptions` lacks a branch check — a test in the new suite asserts cross-branch **visit detail** is still refused; the dental Lab Order form still exposes phone via the retired `x-patient-search-select` (a different component that does not use this service); `CrossBranchPatientLookupService` and `LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditService` escape LIKE with a backslash but emit no `ESCAPE` clause.

## BUGFIX-LEGACY-ODONTOGRAM-PATIENT-LOOKUP-1 — the operator can find the patient, and is told when they cannot (2026-08-30)

Branch `bugfix/legacy-odontogram-patient-lookup-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, base tip `d9579db`, production runtime at start `d1ce4d3` / tag `revision-new-visit-global-patient-lookup-1-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/bugfix-legacy-odontogram-patient-lookup-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/131-legacy-odontogram-patient-lookup.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (RUNTIME_FIX).

**The defect.** On **Import Data Legacy → Unggah Arsip Odontogram Lama** (`GET /settings/rme/legacy-odontograms/create`) the operator entered a patient identifier and nothing appeared. The page asked for `mst_patients.id` — a surrogate key DaengtisiaMS displays on **no** screen, report, print-out or export; the string `ID Pasien` occurred in exactly one Blade file in the repo, this one. Every other patient-selection surface (the sibling Legacy RME importer, the New Visit combobox) uses the canonical **Nomor RM**. The controller read it with `$request->integer('patient_id')`, i.e. `intval()`, which never fails — it guesses: the Nomor RM became `0`; `'1abc'` and `patient_id[]=x` both became **`1`** and silently displayed patient #1. The view then had ONE `@else` for every outcome, so FOUND / NOT_FOUND / ERROR all rendered the same panel shown before any input. HTTP 200, nothing logged — confirmed on the pilot, whose 8,718-line `laravel.log` holds **zero** legacy-odontogram entries. **Why tests missed it:** the suite GETs that route in exactly two places, both permission-negative; no test ever loaded it *with* an identifier. The happy path was only ever exercised through `POST .../store`, which already had a FormRequest and was always correct.

**The fix (no migration, no new route, no new permission, no JavaScript).** New `LookupLegacyOdontogramPatientRequest` (canonical identifier boundary; `FILTER_VALIDATE_INT`, never `intval()`), `LegacyOdontogramPatientLookupService` (decides the state, catches + logs failure), `LegacyOdontogramPatientLookup` (IDLE/FOUND/AMBIGUOUS/NOT_FOUND/TOO_SHORT/TOO_MANY/ERROR), least-disclosure `LegacyOdontogramPatientIdentity` DTO, and `LegacyOdontogramPatientRepositoryInterface`+`Repository` — the module's only door to `mst_patients`, column-projected. Step 1 now asks for the **Nomor RM**; `patient_id` still accepted so links/bookmarks keep working. Same single GET form, same full-page reload.

**Durable rules (mirror rule 131):** (1) the lookup accepts the identifier the operator holds — an internal database id is never the sole means of finding a patient; (2) a lookup reports a STATE, never a nullable patient, and `IDLE` is the only blank state; (3) a failing lookup is `ERROR`, never `NOT_FOUND` and never a 500; (4) **`intval()`/`Request::integer()` is forbidden for a patient identifier in this module** — it guesses, and on a permanent-clinical-evidence screen a guess files one patient's chart into another's history; (5) **FOUND is not authorization** — `patient_id` is re-resolved and re-authorized server-side on submit; (6) patient identity is global, the archive's branch is not (still derived from the Nomor RM, request `branch_id` never read); (7) least disclosure enforced by the DTO + the repository's projected column list, not by template discipline.

**Gotcha — the projection nearly deleted a clinical guard.** `LegacyOdontogramDateRuleService` reads `$patient->date_of_birth`; Eloquent returns `null` for an *unselected* attribute (strict attribute access is not enabled on this codebase) and that rule SKIPS a null birth date by design. So column-projecting the Patient silently removed the "archive dated before the patient was born" refusal — failing **open**, with no error. `date_of_birth` is in `IDENTITY_COLUMNS` for that reason and is never rendered. **Second gotcha:** `QueryException::getMessage()` interpolates the query bindings into the SQL it reports, so logging it raw writes the operator's searched Nomor RM into `laravel.log` on exactly the faults the ERROR state handles — the message is cut at ` (Connection:` and masked. Both found by adversarial review of this sprint's own diff, both pinned by tests (mutations M16/M17).

**Evidence.** TDD observed — **13 of 32 failed** before any production code changed. `LegacyOdontogramPatientLookupTest` 40 passed/128 assertions; `tests/Feature/LegacyOdontogram` 139 passed; regression (LegacyOdontogram+LegacyRme+LegacyImportHub+Patient+AccessControl) **1250 passed / 18 skipped / 0 failed**; **PostgreSQL 16.14 + PHP 8.3.33** (pinned CI runtime image — production's real FPM runtime is `php8.3-fpm-daengtisiams.sock`, NOT the 8.5 CLI on the same host, which belongs to the co-tenant) 139 passed with the driver **asserted** as `pgsql` rather than assumed; Dusk browser (real Chrome, real route, real Blade, PG16) 4 passed/46 assertions, with the runtime-error state provoked by genuinely renaming `mst_patients` away — a real driver error, reversed immediately, no stub. **Mutation: 18 attempted, 17 killed, 1 equivalent, 0 real survivors.**

**Mutation testing changed the work.** Three survivors were real gaps and were closed: a **form-field rename** survived because every HTTP test passed `rm` in the query string and none exercised the rendered form (the exact defect class being fixed); a **URL path rename** survived because the sibling assertion uses `toContain` and `.../create-broken` contains `.../create` (now `toEndWith`); deleting the controller's **`authorize()`** survived because the route middleware checks the same permission (the middleware is now lifted in a test to prove the second gate and the capability-404-before-403 ordering are real). A fourth — **stale patient identity via the session** — is unreachable at the feature layer (Laravel's test client does not carry the session cookie between requests) *and* at the browser layer, so it is pinned where it IS observable: the FormRequest must derive the identifier from the request alone.

**Two mutation-harness defects found and fixed (durable, see memory).** (1) A mutation whose anchor no longer matched was applied with plain `str.replace()`, which no-ops silently, then reported SURVIVED — a test-suite hole that did not exist; substitutions must assert the file changed. (2) Restoring a mutated `.blade.php` with `shutil.copy2` **preserves mtime**, and Blade recompiles only when *source mtime >= compiled mtime* — so the restored template looked older than the artifact compiled while mutated, Blade kept serving the mutation, and **nine subsequent mutations were reported KILLED by a poisoned view cache rather than by the mutation**. Restore now stamps the file new and runs `view:clear`. Any mutation run touching Blade is untrustworthy without this.

**Incidental, in the touched view:** `<x-ui.card title="3. Dokumen &amp; Tanggal">` passes `&amp;` as a PROP which `{{ }}` escapes again, rendering a literal `&amp;` on screen (visible in the browser screenshot) — one character, fixed. **Recorded, NOT fixed:** a richer patient picker (name search / type-ahead / combobox) is a separate UX sprint; this bugfix adds no client-side machinery. Also unpinned by any test: `LegacyOdontogramFoundationTest`'s route assertions use `toContain`, so a path rename that appends a suffix still passes there.

## REVISION-LEGACY-ODONTOGRAM-NATIVE-OPTIONAL-1 — a paper chart does not need permission from the present (2026-08-31)

Branch `revision/legacy-odontogram-native-optional-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, base tip + production runtime at start `d319881f` / tree `1b012a3a` / tag `bugfix-legacy-odontogram-patient-lookup-1-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-legacy-odontogram-native-optional-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/132-legacy-odontogram-native-optional.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml` (RUNTIME_FIX).

**The decision.** `LEGACY_ODONTOGRAM_REQUIRES_NATIVE = false`. A **Legacy Odontogram** is historical clinical evidence read off a paper chart; a **Native Odontogram** is an examination *this system* performed. They are different domain records, and the archive does not need the second to accept the first — the patients whose charts most need archiving are exactly the ones this clinic has never examined. `LegacyOdontogramDateRuleService::evaluate()` refused them outright (`PATIENT_HAS_NO_NATIVE_ODONTOGRAM`, line 127), reached from three call sites that all funnel through this one service: `LegacyOdontogramImportService:93` (`assert()` at upload), `LegacyOdontogramPublishService:164` (`evaluate()` re-check at publish) and `LegacyOdontogramImportController:122` (`snapshotCutoff()`, UI guidance only); the upload screen additionally said *"arsip lama belum dapat diarsipkan"*. TDD observed: **15 of 23 failed** before any production line changed.

**The change — one rule, one service, no migration/route/permission/schema.** The constant `CODE_PATIENT_HAS_NO_NATIVE_ODONTOGRAM`, its `CODES` entry, the refusal branch and `requireNativeReference()` were **deleted**, and so was `config('legacy_odontogram.dates.require_native_odontogram_reference')`. Deleted, **not defaulted off**: a surviving switch would leave the retired rule one settings edit from returning and two contradictory rules simultaneously active.

**The distinction that is the whole sprint: `NATIVE OPTIONAL != NATIVE CUTOFF REMOVED`.** `require_strictly_before_native` stays on. When a meaningful native odontogram exists it still bounds the archive — at the **EARLIEST** one (never the latest), **STRICTLY** earlier, with `SAME_DATE_POLICY = REJECT` (equal is the overlap case), and a contentless placeholder still not counting (`Odontogram::hasRecordedTeeth()`). **Retiring the gate makes that predicate MORE load-bearing, not less:** it used to act in two *opposite* directions, so a mistake was partly self-limiting; with only the bound left, a wrongly-counted placeholder has exactly one effect — inventing a bound on a date where nothing was charted, and refusing a genuinely historical document. Rule 111 §2 now says so explicitly.

**The risk this revision creates: `NO NATIVE FOUND != THE LOOKUP FAILED`.** Before, both outcomes refused, so conflating them was untidy but harmless. Now absence **allows**, so folding a database fault into `null` would turn a broken query into permission to file a chart with **no chronological bound at all**. `PatientEarliestNativeOdontogramDateResolver` → `LegacyOdontogramNativeReferenceRepositoryInterface` has **no try/catch** on that path — already true, now load-bearing, documented at both layers and pinned by a test that binds a throwing repository and asserts the exception propagates rather than becoming a pass. No cutoff is ever fabricated either: no epoch, no `9999-12-31`, no `now()`.

**Retiring a rule that ran FIRST is not a bypass for the rest.** `LEGACY_DATE_IN_FUTURE`, `LEGACY_DATE_BEFORE_PATIENT_BIRTH` and `LEGACY_DATE_INVALID` are each pinned for the no-native case — today and the future still refused, before-birth still refused, equal-to-birth still accepted, null birth date still skipped rather than invented.

**Preserved.** No native side effects (no `ClinicVisit`, native `Odontogram`, `MedicalRecord`, invoice, payment, prescription, `LabOrder`, SATUSEHAT candidate) — **in particular the archive never satisfies a cutoff by creating an empty native odontogram**, the tempting "fix" for the old gate and expressly forbidden. Patient master still required and never created/duplicated/edited; the Nomor RM (the archive's branch authority) never rewritten. A native odontogram may arrive later: the archive is not converted, deleted or mutated, the two coexist, the new native bounds the *next* archive, and a staged archive is re-evaluated at publish so a document that stopped being historical in staging is refused then. Branch from Nomor RM (submitted `branch_id` ignored — asserted, not assumed), private disk, `READY_FOR_REVIEW → REVIEWED → PUBLISHED`, VOID + fresh import, daily hub quota, and the rule-131 lookup all unchanged.

**Mutation testing found one real hole — in a claim the codebase already made.** 18 mutations, 15 killed first pass, **0 real survivors**. (a) **M13 — REAL GAP, closed:** rule 131 §5 says "FOUND is not authorization — `patient_id` is re-resolved and re-authorized server-side on submit", but replacing `$this->resolvePatient(...)` with a bare `Patient::find()` passed all 163 tests. `LegacyOdontogramPatientRepository::baseQuery()` is where `DoctorPatientScopeService` is applied, so the bare lookup silently drops the actor's patient scope; now pinned by a test that binds a refusing repository stub and asserts a 404. (b) **M15 — mis-placed mutant:** it injected the status change *after* the `canTransitionTo` guard it claimed to bypass, so it changed nothing; re-placed before the guard it is KILLED. (c) **M16 — EQUIVALENT, proven not asserted:** nulling the intake's quota `preview()` survived, and so did removing the ceiling from the authoritative `reserve()` — *individually*, because the two are deliberately redundant (`preview` refuses early so a 20 MiB PDF is never streamed and hashed; `reserve` decides under a row lock; same `legacy_import_quota` message). Removing **BOTH** is KILLED. **Gotcha:** the hub's quota suite exercises the quota SERVICE directly and never through an importer's intake, so nothing pinned that the odontogram intake honours the ceiling at all — that is now covered too. **Harness note:** revert by CONTENT (a `git checkout --` cannot restore the untracked new suite and would destroy it), and bump mtime + clear compiled views on both apply and revert.

**Rules synchronised (`SUPERSEDED_RULES`).** New `.cursor/rules/132-legacy-odontogram-native-optional.mdc`. Rule 111 §2 eligibility half → retired, bound documented as the surviving direction; rule 109 §5 legacy-date clause qualified with "when the patient has one". Both edited **in place**.

## BUGFIX-LEGACY-ODONTOGRAM-QUEUE-CONSUMER-1 — Dedicated queue producer ↔ consumer contract (2026-08-31)

Branch `bugfix/legacy-odontogram-queue-consumer-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `revision-legacy-odontogram-native-optional-1-go` @ `7364a2f`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/bugfix-legacy-odontogram-queue-consumer-1.md`; durable contract `docs/architecture/queue-producer-consumer-contract.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/133-dedicated-queue-producer-consumer-contract.mdc`. **No migration, no permission, no schema, no route, no frontend change.**

**The defect.** A legacy odontogram PDF uploaded to the production pilot dispatched `ProcessLegacyOdontogramPdfImport` to `legacy-odontogram-documents`; the production worker consumed `default,reports,notifications,maintenance,legacy-rme-documents` and **not** that queue. The job was therefore never reserved — `jobs.id=17`, `attempts=0`, `reserved_at=NULL`, `failed_jobs` empty, no exception, no log line — and `stg_odontogram_legacy_imports.id=1` sat at `QUEUED`, which is indistinguishable from work still in progress. `PRODUCER_QUEUE != WORKER_CONSUMED_QUEUE`.

**Why the one-line fix was not the deliverable.** This was the SECOND occurrence: LEGACY-RME-PDF-ROLL-2 hit the identical shape on `legacy-rme-documents` and answered with a `queue_contract` check **scoped to one module** (`LegacyRmeRolloutReadinessService`), which Legacy Odontogram inherited none of. Adding one queue name to one worker would have set up the third. The durable deliverable is **ENT5-Q009**, a module-agnostic contract enforcing `PRODUCED ⊆ ALLOWED`, `PRODUCED(active) ⊆ WORKER_CONSUMED`, `WORKER_CONSUMED ⊆ ALLOWED`, that **fails** CI (not reports).

**Functional fix.** Worker unit `--queue` gains `legacy-odontogram-documents` (**appended** — order is priority; the two render queues stay at the tail so a multi-minute rasterization never delays `default`); `allowed_queue_names` gains `legacy-odontogram-documents` + `satusehat`; `queue_worker.approved_queues` corrected. **The producer was deliberately NOT repointed at `default`** — the dedicated queue is an intentional isolation boundary.

**Two properties make the guard hard to fool.** (1) **Runtime resolution, not grep** — every dedicated queue is env-overridable (`LEGACY_ODONTOGRAM_QUEUE`/`LEGACY_RME_QUEUE`/`SATUSEHAT_QUEUE`), so the registry names the *config key* and the check resolves it as the runtime does. (2) **Registry completeness** — any `app/` file routing work to a named queue must be covered by a registry entry (scan patterns live in config so the scanner never matches itself), so a future module either registers and is parity-checked, or fails outright. Unit parsing is **`ExecStart`-anchored**: comments dropped, continuations joined, names compared **exactly** (`x-archive` never satisfies `x`) — a queue named only in a comment is not consumed.

**Three findings the guard surfaced unprompted.** (a) **`satusehat` was produced but never declared** in any allowlist — harmless only because SATUSEHAT is fail-closed/disabled; now declared, with its *consumer* requirement suspended **dynamically** via `consumer_required_when: satusehat.enabled` (a live flag read, NOT a static exemption — enabling SATUSEHAT restores the requirement by itself). (b) **`enterprise_foundation_runtime_hardening.queue_worker.approved_queues` had become decoration** — four names against a unit consuming five since ROLL-2, because it was only ever compared *upward* to the ENT-5 allowlist and never *sideways* to the unit; now held to `ExecStart` exactly. (c) **`StartLimitIntervalSec=0` was silently ignored in production** — declared under `[Service]`, which systemd does not honour (`systemctl show` reported `StartLimitIntervalUSec=10s` against a file asking for `0`); moved to `[Unit]` and pinned by **ENT5-Q010**.

**Installed-unit drift is an OPERATIONAL OBSERVATION, never a check — and this was learned the hard way.** It shipped as a `warn()` first, and the very next production deploy proved that wrong: ENT-5 went `WATCH`, ENT-8/ENT-9/ENT-10/ENT-11 each re-verify ENT-5's decision and inherited it, and `scripts/deploy-vps.sh` asserts **ENT-11 is `GO`** — so the deploy aborted at the gate phase, and the unit could not be installed until the deploy finished. A permanent deadlock for every future unit-changing sprint. (The earlier reasoning checked only that the *commands* exit 0 without `--strict`; it missed the script's explicit `GO` assertion.) Drift is now reported in the payload and printed by the command under `Installed worker unit (operational note — does not affect the decision)`, and the ENT-5 decision stays about the repository. The hard half is untouched: ENT5-Q009 still FAILS when the **tracked** unit is missing a produced queue. **Durable rule: CI can only ever verify the tracked unit; the installed unit is the operator's step, verified by the activation runbook — never a deploy gate.**

**A job stuck at QUEUED is not a failure.** If domain status is `QUEUED` with the backend job pending, `attempts=0` and `reserved_at` NULL, the job was never reserved and the investigation target is the **consumer path**. Never `UPDATE` the domain status, never retry/redispatch/clone/re-upload: restore the consumer and let the existing job drain naturally — an unattempted job is fully recoverable, and retrying destroys the evidence of which side was broken. This sprint recovered the production document exactly that way.

**CI selection is enforced, not incidental.** `tests/Feature/Queue/QueueProducerConsumerParityTest.php` lives under a path no existing filter token selected, so it is declared in `config/ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` **and** a `QueueProducerConsumerParity` token was added to **both** critical gate variants; `SelfHostedRunnerScanner::criticalGateSuiteCoveragePosture()` verifies every declared suite is matched by every variant. A control that exists but no gate selects is not a control.

**TDD evidence.** Guard written first against the unfixed tree → `Decision: FAIL` (2 errors, 4 distinct violations). After the fix → `13/13 passed, Decision: GO`, `--strict` exit 0.

## REVISION-TELKOMAS-BRANCH-CODE-TKM1-TO-TLK1-1 — Telkomas Branch Code Foundation (2026-08-31)

Branch `revision/telkomas-branch-code-tkm1-to-tlk1-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `2fb8c4c` / tree `80e5404b` / GO tag `bugfix-legacy-odontogram-queue-consumer-1-go`, confirmed as live production HEAD before work began; do NOT target main). Doc `docs/sprints/revision-telkomas-branch-code-tkm1-to-tlk1-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-telkomas-branch-code-canonical.mdc` (SUPERSEDES the "TKM1 = Cabang Telkomas" registry line in rule 73 and in the RME-BRANCH-SUN4 block above).

**TELKOMAS BRANCH CODE FOUNDATION.** Cabang Telkomas canonical branch code is **`TLK1`**. `TKM1` is deprecated and must never be generated for new application records, new patient medical-record numbers, active branch mappings, new imports, reports, exports, fixtures, or other canonical application output. `TKM1` is accepted ONLY as a historical compatibility alias where pre-existing identifiers or immutable historical evidence require it. `TKM1` and `TLK1` resolve to the SAME Telkomas branch identity (same `mst_branches` row, same id, same patients), but the canonical code the application emits is `TLK1`. Historical RM values using `TKM1` resolve to their canonical `TLK1` form. Production RM migration is collision-safe, transactional, auditable and fails closed; it never creates duplicate patients and never changes the Telkomas branch primary identity. Immutable published clinical evidence and historical audit records are NOT rewritten merely to replace `TKM1` text. Unknown, ambiguous, inactive or invalid branch codes **fail closed** — there is no `unknown → Telkomas` fallback and no fuzzy matching.

**What the inventory found (this was not a cosmetic rename).** Production `mst_branches` id 1 had ALREADY been renamed to `TLK1` by hand while the repository still said `TKM1`, producing three defects — one live, two latent: (1) `RmeBranchSeeder` looked Telkomas up by `TKM1` alone, so the next routine `db:seed --class=RmeBranchSeeder --force` would have **created a duplicate "Cabang Telkomas"**, splitting one clinic's patients across two branch ids (branch id is the isolation boundary); (2) legacy-archive branch derivation resolved `Branch::where('code', 'TKM1')` → not found, so patient 36 (`DG-TKM1-2024-9985`) had an **unreachable** legacy RME/odontogram archive; (3) the WAVE-4 allowlist admitted the literal token `TKM1` from the environment while derivation reported `TLK1`, which would have **locked Telkomas out of the wave it is approved for**.

**Design.** One-way alias policy in `App\Modules\Branch\Support\BranchCodeAlias` (`canonicalize`/`isHistoricalAlias`/`historicalAliasesFor`/`equivalentCodes`); **never** `$code === 'TKM1' || $code === 'TLK1'` at a call site. `PatientMedicalRecordNumberService::branchCodeFrom()` now reports the CANONICAL code (so `LegacyRmeBranchResolver` and `LegacyOdontogramBranchBindingService` needed **no change** — one fix serves both), plus new `literalBranchCodeFrom()` (the historical fact), `canonicalizeBranchCode()` (parser-driven, **branch segment only**, preserves prefix/year/sequence verbatim, null for non-canonical input, leaves `DG-LDK2-2024-TKM1` untouched) and `equivalentNumbers()`. Old-card compatibility: exact identity resolution widened to every spelling in `CrossBranchPatientLookupService` + `LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditService` — **never** a second patient row. `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionService` + `config/legacy_rme_rollout.php` canonicalize BOTH sides (allowlist, approved set, forbidden set, pilot scope, and the resolved code), so a stale environment cannot lock an approved branch out. `RmeBranchSeeder` matches every equivalent code before deciding a branch is missing, so it can never duplicate Telkomas (renaming stays the migration's job).

**Deliberately preserved (do NOT "tidy" these).** `LegacyRmeSourceRmNormalizer` transcribes the source Nomor RM **faithfully** — it recases but never canonicalizes, because the paper document really does say `TKM1` and published evidence must not claim otherwise; reachability comes from alias-aware matching downstream. `trx_clinic_visits.visit_number` (4 rows, `VIS-TKM1-…`) are **issued transactional identifiers** on completed visits, printed on paper already given to patients; nothing derives a branch from them and the generator already emits `TLK1`. `sys_audit_logs` (5 rows), `stg_legacy_patient_imports` (2 rows incl. raw/normalized payloads), published legacy evidence + its stored source RM/bytes/checksums, and terminal (COMPLETED/CANCELLED) waves — all untouched. Historical wave/pilot/incident records keep their `TKM1` text (`ROUTINE-20260819-TKM1-01` remains immutable audit evidence).

**Migration** `2026_08_31_100001_revise_telkomas_branch_code_tkm1_to_tlk1.php`: single `DB::transaction`, every target enumerated, **no column scan and no `replace(col,'TKM1','TLK1')` anywhere**. Preflight asserts no branch-code collision and no RM collision (checked across ALL patients incl. soft-deleted, before the FIRST write) → else `RuntimeException` and nothing is written; then renames the branch code in place (primary key untouched), migrates patient Nomor RM via parse/compose, and canonicalizes **non-terminal** rollout waves only. **Irreversible by design** — after this runs the app legitimately issues `TLK1` and nothing distinguishes migrated from newly-created rows, so `down()` is a documented no-op and rollback is the pre-migrate backup. **Idempotent**, which is what made it safe against a production already renamed by hand. Production preflight: `TLK1_BRANCH_COLLISION=0`, `RM_COLLISION_COUNT=0`.

**Test-infra:** 530 `TKM1` fixture occurrences across `tests/` renamed to `TLK1` (classified, scoped to `tests/`), and the `tests/Pest.php` legacy helpers (`legacyRmeBranch`/`legacyRmeAssignOperator`/`legacyRmeOperator`/`legacyRmeArchivablePatient`) now default to `TLK1`. Dedicated alias coverage in `tests/Feature/Branch/TelkomasBranchCodeAliasTest.php` + `TelkomasBranchCodeMigrationTest.php`. **Two real implementation gaps were caught by tests, not by reading:** `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionService::decide()` and its defence-in-depth allowlist re-normalizer only upper-cased instead of canonicalizing.

## FEATURE-DOCTOR-ACCOUNT-PERFORMANCE-INCOME-LINKAGE-1 — Doctor Account Identity Link (2026-08-31)

Branch `feature/doctor-account-performance-income-linkage-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `revision-telkomas-branch-code-tkm1-to-tlk1-1-go` @ `8b3468e`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-doctor-account-performance-income-linkage-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/92-doctor-account-identity-link.mdc`. **Audit-then-build sprint. NO migration** — `mst_doctors.user_id` (nullable, **unique**, FK→`users`, `nullOnDelete`) has existed since Sprint 66.0 (`2026_06_29_120002`); no role/seeder grant, no income formula, no historical row touched.

**Audit verdict (CASE B — relation existed, UI did not).** Kinerja and Pendapatan are ONE feature: `rme.reports.doctor-performance` → sidebar "Kinerja & Pendapatan Dokter"; `DoctorPerformanceReportService::resolveAccess()` already resolved identity IDOR-safely from `mst_doctors.user_id`. But `user_id` was **unsettable through the application**: neither `StoreDoctorRequest` nor `UpdateDoctorRequest` validates it (so `validated()` strips it), `settings/doctors/_form.blade.php` has no account field, and unlike technicians (`lab:technician-link-user`) there was no CLI linker — while `rme:doctor-performance-access-audit` tells operators to *"Link the affected doctor accounts via master data"*, a control that did not exist. Only direct production SQL could link a doctor. **Second finding:** `DoctorUserResolver::resolveForUser()` fell back to case-insensitive **email matching**, and that resolver feeds `DoctorPatientScopeService` (which patients/visits/RM an account may open) and `UserOnlineContextService` visit doctor attribution — i.e. email was runtime clinical authority. Production at sprint time: 5 doctors / 2 linked / 3 unlinked, **0 users with the `Doctor` role**, 2 doctors whose email matched a user account (latent, unexploited, safely closable).

**Built.** (1) Canonical `App\Modules\Doctor\Services\DoctorIdentityResolver` — identity strictly from `mst_doctors.user_id`, **identity only** (`is_active`/branch/permission stay their callers' decisions, so no consumer policy changed). `DoctorUserResolver` now delegates to it with its `is_active` filter intact and **the email fallback removed** (unlinked accounts fail closed on the existing "belum terhubung ke data master dokter" message); `DoctorPerformanceReportService` resolves through the same service so kinerja/pendapatan can never drift onto a second notion of identity. (2) **Master Data RME → Relasi Akun Dokter**: routes `settings.doctors.account-links.{index,store,destroy}` declared **before** the `doctors` resource (static segment never shadowed — legacy-patient-import precedent), thin `DoctorAccountLinkController`, `LinkDoctorAccountRequest` (shape only), `DoctorAccountLinkService` (all eligibility + audited write), `DoctorPolicy::manageAccountLink`, view `settings/doctors/account-links/index.blade.php`, sidebar entry. (3) New permission **`manage_doctor_account_links`**, classified in `PermissionGroupingService` (`master_data` group — **mandatory**: three tests assert every `PermissionSeeder::PERMISSIONS` entry is grouped), granted to **no** role; Super Admin reaches it via `Gate::before`. Deliberately narrower than `manage doctors` so a future grant of doctor master-data maintenance to a clinic admin cannot silently also hand over the power to redirect who reads another doctor's income.

**Durable rules (mirror `.cursor/rules/92`):** auth identity ≠ clinical identity, joined only by `mst_doctors.user_id`; `DoctorIdentityResolver` is the single resolver (no ad-hoc `where('user_id')` in controllers/policies/views/tests); identity is NEVER inferred from name/email/phone; one account ↔ one doctor (locked service check **and** DB unique index); doctor self-access derives the doctor server-side and ignores a request `doctor_id`; an unlinked doctor account fails closed (never the first doctor, never a fake empty report); linking never rewrites visits/RM/invoices/payments/earnings; linking never grants a role; relink is explicit (`confirm_relink`), never a silent overwrite; every link/relink/unlink audits to `sys_audit_logs` (`DOCTOR_ACCOUNT_LINK`/`_RELINK`/`_UNLINK`) with technical ids only; **linkage chooses WHO the doctor is, it does not redefine HOW kinerja/pendapatan is calculated**; sidebar is UX only, route+policy authorization is the boundary.

**Usage:** Super Admin → Master Data RME → Relasi Akun Dokter → pick doctor → pick account (only active + `Doctor` role + unlinked are offered) → Hubungkan; tick **Ganti akun** to relink; **Putuskan** to unlink (doctor, account and all history preserved, self-access withdrawn immediately). Tests: `tests/Feature/MasterData/DoctorAccountLinkTest.php` (25). Deploy: **no migration**; run `db:seed --class=PermissionSeeder --force` + `permission:cache-reset`, then `rme:doctor-performance-access-audit`. Production doctors stay unlinked until an administrator links them deliberately.

**FEATURE-DOCTOR-ACCOUNT-PERFORMANCE-INCOME-LINKAGE-1 follow-up — CLI linker + a migration-order lesson (2026-09-01):** New `php artisan rme:doctor-link-user --doctor=<id|code> --user=<id|email> [--apply] [--confirm-relink] [--json]` (dry-run default) — thin wrapper over `DoctorAccountLinkService`, so the CLI enforces the identical guards and writes the identical `sys_audit_logs` row as the UI. It exists because on the pilot `tinker` is barred (psysh writes ERROR lines into the app log and pins the monitor to WATCH for 24h) and raw SQL would bypass both the eligibility checks and the audit trail. **Migration-order lesson:** removing the email fallback and adding the linking UI shipped together, which revoked doctor-workspace access for 2 live accounts (user 14→doctor 16, user 15→doctor 20) that had only ever resolved via email. Root cause was a measurement error — the pre-change count of Doctor-role users used an over-escaped `model_type = 'App\\Models\\User'` and returned 0 when the true count was 4. **Rule: when narrowing an identity resolver, measure who depends on the loose path first, and sanity-check that the measurement returns non-zero somewhere it should — a query matching nothing looks exactly like a clean estate.** Repair was owner-approved and persisted the mapping the system was already using; no historical row rewritten.

## REVISION-RME-CONSENT-ODONTOGRAM-PRECONSENT-EDIT-1 — the odontogram may be charted before the signature; the record and the finish may not (2026-09-01)

Branch `revision/rme-consent-odontogram-preconsent-edit-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `feature-doctor-account-performance-income-linkage-1-go` @ `e9e6450`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-rme-consent-odontogram-preconsent-edit-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/134-odontogram-pre-consent-authoring.mdc`. **No migration, no route, no permission, no policy, no schema change.**

**The rule.** After the Doctor clicks "Mulai Pemeriksaan" (`in_progress`), the authorized Doctor may author and save the active visit's Native Odontogram **even with the same-visit consent unsigned**. Consent stays mandatory before authoring the active **RME**, and before **"Selesai Pemeriksaan"** (`in_progress → cashier_pending`) — including when the chart is already complete.

**Why.** FIX-RME-EXAM-CONSENT-ODONTOGRAM-HISTORY-3 / CORRECTIVE-03 gated the active odontogram on consent, reasoning it is a clinical finding "exactly like the note beside it". It is not: charting is **observation**, and the `PERSETUJUAN TINDAKAN MEDIS` names the treatment *derived from* that observation. Requiring the signature first made the workflow **circular** — the form could not be completed until the finding existed, and the finding could not be recorded until the form was signed.

**The change is one condition in one method.** `RmeVisitConsentService::assertOdontogramAuthoringAllowed()` went from four checks to three: (1) the patient has a single current `in_progress` encounter (ambiguity fails closed), (2) the actor may work on it (branch + `DoctorPatientScopeService`; unresolvable actor fails closed), (3) the chart IS that encounter's own chart. The `hasValidConsent()` check is removed, and its absence is **stated in the method** so it never reads as an oversight. Small because CORRECTIVE-03 had centralised the rule: all three write paths (`saveForVisit`/`updatePlaceholder`/`finalize`) already funnelled through that assertion and the page already derived its controls from `canAuthorOdontogramFor()`, which delegates to it — there was no second check anywhere to hunt down. **Do not add one.**

**Odontogram `finalize()` is also permitted pre-consent** — it is a chart-level lifecycle act (Sprint 59: a finalized chart stays revisable) and leaves the visit `in_progress`. It shares the one assertion; splitting it out would create a second divergent consent rule. "Selesai Pemeriksaan" is the *workflow* transition and stays gated in `ClinicVisitService::transitionStatus()`.

**Unchanged and re-pinned:** RME blocked pre-consent (also after charting — charting is never an implicit consent); finish blocked pre-consent even with a complete chart; charting before "Mulai Pemeriksaan" refused (`registered`/`waiting`) and after it (`cashier_pending`/`completed`/`cancelled`); non-Doctor and other-Doctor refused; historical native charts and published Legacy Odontograms permanently immutable; another visit's consent never unlocks the live RME; a save creates/signs no consent and advances no status; pre-consent charting survives the later signature (same row, same teeth, no duplicate).

**UI.** The odontogram page's warning ("...belum dapat diubah. Pasien harus menandatangani form consent sebelum dokter dapat mencatat atau mengubah odontogram") was **false** after this change and is replaced by a reminder of what is still true: charting is available; the RME and Selesai Pemeriksaan remain locked until the signature. Controller `$odontogramConsentRequired` → `$consentPendingForRme`. Operationally load-bearing — a doctor can now finish the whole chart without ever meeting the gate and would otherwise first learn the visit is stuck when trying to finish it.

**Superseded:** the CORRECTIVE-03 clause "consent gates the active odontogram", corrected in place in `.cursor/rules/109-rme-exam-consent-odontogram-history.mdc` (§0 diagram, §2 bullet, §2c coverage, §4 wording); rule 134 is authoritative where the two disagree. Everything else in rule 109 stands.

**Tests:** new `tests/Feature/RME/RmeConsentOdontogramPreConsentEditTest.php` (31) — written first; **17 failed / 14 passed** against unmodified source, the 14 being every guard-preservation case. Four cases plus the read-only-notice case in `RmeExamConsentCorrective3Test.php` were **revised, not deleted**, each carrying a `REVISED by` note naming what it used to assert. Declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites`: it is selected today only by the bare `Odontogram` filter token — a substring of its own filename, exactly the "coverage rested on a filename coincidence" failure that registry exists to prevent.

## BUGFIX-RME-PRECONSENT-FIRST-PAGE-UI-GATE-1 — the empty state stopped offering a create the server refuses (2026-09-02)

Branch `bugfix/rme-preconsent-first-page-ui-gate-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `revision-rme-consent-odontogram-preconsent-edit-1-go` @ `2c9ec15`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/bugfix-rme-preconsent-first-page-ui-gate-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/135-rme-authoring-ui-capability-mirror.mdc`. **No migration, no route, no permission, no policy, no schema change. The backend gate was NOT touched — it was already correct.**

**The bug.** Doctor + `in_progress` + consent UNSIGNED + patient with **no RM sheet** was offered an actionable **"Buat Halaman RM Pertama"**, and the server refused the create when they pressed it: `UI = ALLOW`, `BACKEND = DENY`. Reproduced before any change — the new suite's red baseline was 11 failed / 11 passed, and *every* failure was a UI-capability assertion while *every* backend-denial and foundation-preservation assertion already passed. That split is the diagnosis.

**Root cause — a permission check standing in for a capability check.** `MedicalRecordController::show()` renders two workspaces from one method, and they consulted two different authorities for one act. The POPULATED workspace resolved `canAuthorRme`/`addSheetConsentRequired` from `RmeVisitConsentService::canAuthorRmeForPatient()`. The EMPTY workspace resolved `canEdit` from `can('create', [MedicalRecord::class, $workspaceVisit])` — and `MedicalRecordPolicy::create()` checks only `manage_clinic_visits` + RME-enabled branch + `DoctorPatientScopeService::authorizeVisitAccess()`. It carries **no consent condition, correctly**: consent is not a permission, it is a property of the encounter. Meanwhile `MedicalRecordService::createDraft()` asserts `assertRmeAuthoringAllowedForPatient()` (single current encounter · `in_progress` · actor may work on it · valid consent for THAT encounter).

**The fix — the empty state joins the rule that already existed.** No new predicate, and the same key the sheet-add control already uses: the controller now also passes `addSheetConsentRequired` = `! canAuthorRmeForPatient($patientId, $user)` and `activeEncounter`; `resources/views/rme/visits/medical-record/empty.blade.php` renders from them, mirroring the populated workspace — a `warning` alert naming the real reason (`Belum ada pemeriksaan yang berjalan` / `Persetujuan Tindakan Medis belum ditandatangani` + "Pilih Form Consent" link) and the CTA as a **disabled button**, never a live `<form>` at the store route. Disabled rather than hidden follows `rm-sheet-nav.blade.php`'s convention for the identical act: the doctor stays oriented instead of discovering the lock by pressing.

**Durable rule (mirror `.cursor/rules/135`, cross-linked both ways with rule 134):** the active RME authoring UI must mirror the server RME authoring capability; **a permission check is not a capability check** — any control whose act is gated by encounter state, consent or workflow status must consult the capability, not only the permission; UI visibility is never the security boundary; **never re-derive the consent rule in Blade** (a template writing its own `$visit->consent && …` creates a second rule that drifts — exactly this bug); and when UI and backend disagree the resolution is always **UI DENY + BACKEND DENY**, never a relaxed server.

**Explicitly unchanged and re-pinned by this sprint's own suite:** the odontogram stays **editable** pre-consent (REVISION-…-PRECONSENT-EDIT-1 — blocking the record must never drag the chart down with it), "Selesai Pemeriksaan" stays refused pre-consent, before-start refused, other-Doctor and non-Doctor refused, terminal visits closed (a `cancelled` visit has no workspace at all — it is not an eligible RM anchor, so the page 404s before any capability question), another visit's / another patient's consent never unlocks the control, and a forged `source_visit_id` never becomes an authority.

**Superseded:** `tests/Browser/OdontogramPreConsentEditBrowserTest.php` step 4 used to *press* the button and watch the server refuse, with a note recording that it "is gated on the manage permission only, not on consent". That note described this bug; the step now asserts the disabled control and the absent form, keeping its original claim intact.

**CI wiring is double, deliberately:** token `RmePreConsentFirstPageUiGate` in **both** critical-gate filter variants, **and** the file declared in `config/ci_runner.php` `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` (STORAGE-PUBLIC-CLINICAL-EVIDENCE-1 precedent — the mismatch was live in production, nothing in CI asserted the two authorities agreed, and the likely regression produces no error, no exception and no log line, only a control that fails when pressed). The declaration is what makes the token unremovable: `SelfHostedRunnerScanner` fails the gate if a declared file is selected by no token in every variant.

**Tests:** new `tests/Feature/RME/RmePreConsentFirstPageUiGateTest.php` (22) — green on SQLite **and** PostgreSQL 16 (the production driver); new `tests/Browser/RmePreConsentFirstPageBrowserTest.php` (2 Chrome journeys against PG16) proving "not actionable" by asking Selenium whether the element is enabled rather than grepping a label — a disabled button still reads "Buat Halaman RM Pertama", so the label is not the signal, the submittable form is.

## REVISION-SUNU-BRANCH-CODE-SUN4-TO-SPN4-1 — Cabang Sunu canonical code becomes SPN4 (2026-09-02)

Branch `revision/sunu-branch-code-sun4-to-spn4-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`; do NOT target main). Doc `docs/sprints/revision-sunu-branch-code-sun4-to-spn4-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/136-sunu-branch-code-canonical.mdc`; shared mechanics `.cursor/rules/92-telkomas-branch-code-canonical.mdc` (now registry-wide). **Canonical branch code registry: TLK1=Cabang Telkomas, LDK2=Cabang Landak, ATG3=Cabang Antang, `SPN4`=Cabang Sunu.** `SUN4` is DEPRECATED — accepted as a historical alias, never generated again. The sprint NAME `RME-BRANCH-SUN4` is a historical identifier and is NOT renamed.

**This was a DRIFT REPAIR, not a clean rename.** Production `mst_branches` id 5 already read `SPN4` (renamed by hand) while the repository still declared `SUN4` — the same half-applied state Telkomas was found in one revision earlier. Two live defects followed: (1) patient id 39 still held `DG-SUN4-2026-564`, so the branch code encoded in her Nomor RM named NO branch and RM-derived resolution failed closed, making her legacy archive unreachable; (2) the ACTIVE wave 5 still listed `SUN4` in `approved_branch_codes` plus a `SUN4` wave-branch and wave-operator row while the branch master answered `SPN4`, locking an approved branch out of its own wave. **Never assume repository and production agree on a branch code; check both, and treat a rename as possible drift repair.**

**Change surface.** `BranchCodeAlias` gains `SUNU_CANONICAL='SPN4'` / `SUNU_HISTORICAL='SUN4'` and ONE row in `HISTORICAL_ALIASES` — that is the whole behavioural change, because the class is already the single chokepoint (`PatientMedicalRecordNumberService`, `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionService`, `config/legacy_rme_rollout.php`, `RmeBranchSeeder` all inherited it with no new conditional). `RmeBranchSeeder` registers Sunu under `BranchCodeAlias::SUNU_CANONICAL` and — **live defect fixed** — its restore/re-enable guard now compares `$code !== BranchCodeAlias::SUNU_CANONICAL` instead of the literal `'SUN4'`, which the registry-key change would otherwise have turned into dead code, leaving a soft-deleted Cabang Sunu permanently unselectable. Migration `2026_09_02_100001_revise_sunu_branch_code_sun4_to_spn4.php` is collision-checked, transactional, fail-closed, idempotent and deliberately irreversible (rollback = restore from the pre-deploy backup); targets are enumerated (`mst_branches.code`, `mst_patients.medical_record_number`, live rollout rows only) and Nomor RM goes through the parser/composer so only the branch-code SEGMENT can change — `DG-LDK2-2026-SUN4` is left alone. Collisions: `SPN4_BRANCH_COLLISION_COUNT=0`, `RM_TRANSFORM_COLLISION_COUNT=0`.

**Preserved on purpose** (each is a record, not a live identifier): `sys_audit_logs`, `stg_legacy_patient_imports`, published legacy RME/odontogram evidence and its transcribed source RM, issued `trx_clinic_visits.visit_number`, and TERMINAL (COMPLETED/CANCELLED) rollout waves — production's cancelled wave 4 still names Telkomas' deprecated code, correctly. No clinical file was opened or rewritten to remove a literal `SUN4`. No `jobs.payload` was hand-edited.

**Old-card compatibility** is inherited, not re-implemented: all 5 RM lookup surfaces (`LegacyOdontogramPatientRepository`, `LegacyRmePatientResolutionAuditService`, `PatientRepository::paginate`, `PatientRepository::searchSelectable`, `CrossBranchPatientLookupService`) reach `equivalentNumbers()` → `BranchCodeAlias::equivalentCodes()`, and all 5 are pinned.

**CI gotcha (§81 shape, caught by a failing test).** Declaring the suites in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` was NOT enough — `CriticalGateSuiteCoverageTest` went red because no workflow filter token matched them. A `SunuBranchCode` token was added to **both** critical-gate filter variants (GitHub-hosted and self-hosted). **A test the gate never selects is not a control.**

Tests: `tests/Feature/Branch/SunuBranchCodeAliasTest.php` + `SunuBranchCodeMigrationTest.php`. Sibling fixtures repinned SUN4→SPN4 across 9 files (`RmeBranchSeederTest`, `LegacyRmeActivationContractTest`, `LegacyRmeBranchAdmissionTest`, `LegacyRmeMigrationEligibilityBranchTest`, `LegacyOdontogramIntakeTest`, `PerawatOnlineContextTest`, `NewVisitGlobalPatientLookupTest`, `RmeVisitConsentGateTest`, `RmeExamConsentCorrectiveTest`, `RmeExamConsentOdontogramHistoryTest`, plus the Telkomas stale-env expectation); `SUN4` now survives in tests ONLY where it is the deprecated code under test. Deploy: `php artisan migrate --force` (1 data migration) + `db:seed --class=RmeBranchSeeder --force` (idempotent); no permission/schema-column change.

## REVISION-SUNU-ADD-ROOM-A-B-1 — Cabang Sunu gets Ruangan A + Ruangan B (2026-09-02)

Branch `revision/sunu-add-room-a-b-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `73c27a3`, baseline GO tag `revision-sunu-branch-code-sun4-to-spn4-1-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-sunu-add-room-a-b-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/137-branch-room-provisioning.mdc`. **Master data only — NO migration, NO route, NO permission, NO policy, NO controller/service/view change.**

**Decision:** Cabang Sunu — canonical code **SPN4**, pilot branch id 5 — has two ACTIVE rooms **Ruangan A** (`SPN-A`) and **Ruangan B** (`SPN-B`), both on the EXISTING Sunu branch identity. `ROOT_CLASSIFICATION = MISSING_MASTER_DATA`: production carried rooms for TLK1/LDK2/ATG3/MAIN and **zero for SPN4 in any state** (no inactive, no soft-deleted, no duplicate — verified read-only before any write). Codes were **derived, not invented**, from the convention the existing rows already establish (`<3-letter branch prefix>-<letter>`: `TKM-A`, `LDK-A`, `ATG-A`), read off the CANONICAL `SPN4`. Telkomas' rooms stay `TKM-*` because they were issued before the `TKM1 → TLK1` rename — issued identifiers are deliberately preserved.

**The schema fact that drove the design:** `mst_clinic_rooms` carries `UNIQUE(branch_id, code)` **and** `UNIQUE(branch_id, name)`, and **neither index is conditioned on `deleted_at`** — a soft-deleted room still occupies both slots, so a blind insert raises a constraint violation and aborts the deploy rather than creating a duplicate. Every lookup in provisioning is `withTrashed()`. This is why the existing `ClinicRoomSeeder` could not be reused: it is `updateOrCreate` on a non-trashed query, scoped to whatever branch `BranchContext` resolves to (in practice MAIN). It is untouched and stays — it answers a different question.

**New `database/seeders/RmeBranchRoomSeeder.php`** (canonical per-branch room registry, `RmeBranchSeeder` precedent, registered in `DatabaseSeeder` after `ClinicRoomSeeder`): branch resolved by **code** via `BranchCodeAlias::equivalentCodes()` — never by display name (Master Data owns that label) and never by canonical code alone (a deployment still on `SUN4` must be recognised, not duplicated); **name is the identity**, `code` is only assigned on creation, so a room already named "Ruangan A" under an operator-chosen code is ADOPTED and its code left alone; convergence restores a trashed room and activates an inactive one but never rewrites code/type/description; **fails closed** when the declared code is held by a differently-named room or when code and name resolve to two different rows; never creates a branch (`RmeBranchSeeder` owns that, and `DatabaseSeeder` runs it first); never deletes; never touches another branch. Registry declares **Cabang Sunu only** — re-declaring sibling branches could only rename or duplicate their operator-created rooms.
