**Consumers:** there are **no hard-coded room lists anywhere** in the repo; every consumer reads master data through `ClinicVisitService::activeRoomsForBranch()` (filters `branch_id` + `status = active`), so the Sunu rooms appear in the doctor online-context and patient-queue room selectors with no code change. **Assignability has a second pre-existing gate (Sprint 66.1.4):** the treating doctor is resolved FROM the room, so a room is only assignable while exactly one doctor is online in it — creating rooms does not put a doctor in them, which is exactly the no-auto-assignment property required. Branch boundary was already server-side in `ClinicVisitService::assignRoom()` (`room.branch_id === visit.branch_id`, from the visit, never a request field) and is now pinned for Sunu in both directions.

**Testing gotcha worth keeping:** asserting only `toThrow(ValidationException::class)` on room assignment is **NOT sufficient** — mutation testing proved that deleting the branch check still throws, from doctor resolution further down, so a type-only assertion passed against code that had lost the branch boundary entirely. Assert the refusal REASON. Also: the test helper `sunuBranch()` already exists in `SunuBranchCodeAliasTest.php`, so Pest global helpers in a sibling `tests/Feature/Branch/` file must be uniquely named (fatal redeclare otherwise).

**Evidence:** `tests/Feature/Branch/SunuClinicRoomProvisioningTest.php` (28); mutation matrix 13 mutants **13 killed / 0 survivors** (restore by copy — the seeder is untracked, so `git checkout --` cannot restore it); PostgreSQL **16.14** (production's version) 28/28; PG16 regression branch/room/visit **320 passed**, CI/DEVFLOW **359 passed**; deploy-command smoke ran `db:seed --class=RmeBranchRoomSeeder --force` three times → exactly two active rooms, branch count unchanged, no second Cabang Sunu. CI critical-gate token `SunuClinicRoom` added to **both** critical variants; deliberately NOT added to `critical_gate_mandatory_suites` (that registry documents its own bar — controls whose failure was live in production — and this sprint does not meet it).

**Deploy note:** no migration, no seed of permissions; run `php artisan db:seed --class=RmeBranchRoomSeeder --force` post-deploy (idempotent). **Rollback** is to set the two rooms `status = inactive` via Master Data Ruangan — never to delete them, since a room may already be referenced by a visit.

## FEATURE-DOCTOR-TRUSTED-ANDROID-DEVICE-LOCK-1 — Phase 1: Doctor room-scoped access + RME/Odontogram print denial (2026-09-03)

Branch `feature/doctor-trusted-device-lock-1-phase-1-room-scope-print-deny` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `931ad32e` = production/`revision-sunu-add-room-a-b-1-go`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/138-doctor-room-scoped-access-and-print-deny.mdc`; manifest `.sprint/current.yml`.

**PHASE 1 OF 4 — the device-INDEPENDENT half.** The full feature stacks `TRUSTED ANDROID DEVICE + DAILY BRANCH LOCK + ROOM CONTEXT + RBAC`. Phase 1 ships only what needs no hardware. **The full-feature GO tag `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` is NOT created and must NOT be created until device enforcement is genuinely active.** Evidence-backed blockers recorded at readiness: `adb devices` empty and no Android vendor on `lsusb` (no clinic tablet exists — owner confirmed none procured), no `.jks`/`.keystore` and no signing governance anywhere in the repo, and zero registered devices — so switching on "Doctor login requires a trusted device" would lock every doctor out of all four RME branches. Phases 2 (device registry + `Master Data → Device Dokter`, shipped OFF), 3 (Android Kotlin kiosk app — **blocked on hardware + signing governance**) and 4 (pilot → enforcement → feature GO) remain.

**No migration, no new permission, no new route, no schema change, no Android code.**

**Canonical authority:** new `App\Modules\RME\Services\DoctorRoomScopeService` — the ONLY place implementing room scope + clinical print denial. "Is this a scoped Doctor" delegates to `DoctorPatientScopeService::shouldApplyDoctorScope()`, so Owner / Super Admin / Supervisor RME stay exempt by the one existing definition, and Perawat / Admin Klinik / Kasir are never room-scoped.

**§16 room isolation.** The doctor's room is read server-side from the live online context (`trx_user_online_contexts`, `role_context=doctor`, `status=online`, Sprint 66.0 `startDoctorSession`) — **never** from the request. A scoped Doctor's `clinic_room_id`/`branch_id` list filters are dropped in the controller and the room selector is not rendered, so there is no "Semua Ruangan". Enforced as **both** a list scope (`ClinicVisitService::doctorVisitScope()`) and a per-visit policy guard (`ClinicVisitPolicy::view|update|transition|print`, `OdontogramPolicy`), so hiding a row and denying a direct URL are the same rule. Fails closed: no resolvable room ⇒ no ACTIVE visit.

**§17 history boundary (the trap).** The restriction covers ONLY `ClinicVisit::PRE_EXAM_STATUSES` (registered/waiting/in_progress). **Gotcha found during implementation:** the first cut put a bare `where('clinic_room_id', …)` in `doctorVisitScope()` — but that closure ALSO feeds `ClinicVisitService::paginate()` (Daftar Kunjungan), which lists historical visits, so it would have hidden a doctor's own completed records. `applyRoomScope()` now narrows only ACTIVE rows and must stay the mirror image of `deniesActiveVisitOutsideRoom()`. `cashier_pending`/`completed`/`cancelled` are never room-restricted.

**§18/§19 print denial.** `rme.visits.print`, `rme.visits.pdf` (`ClinicVisitPolicy::print`) and `rme.odontograms.print` (`OdontogramPolicy::print`) are denied for a scoped Doctor. Both Blade actions were **already** wrapped in `@can('print', …)`, so the policy denial hides the buttons with no UI-only check — do not add one. **Prescription printing (`rme.prescriptions.print`) is out of scope and still works** (asserted alongside, which proves the denial is targeted, not blanket). Doctor read/edit lifecycle (consent gate, room gate, finalisation, historical immutability) is unchanged.

**§23 audit.** `DOCTOR_ROOM_ACCESS_REJECTED`, `DOCTOR_PRINT_RME_REJECTED`, `DOCTOR_PRINT_ODONTOGRAM_REJECTED` via the shared `AuditLogService`/`sys_audit_logs`. Written from **controllers, never policies** — policies are also evaluated for `@can` UI gating, so auditing there would log a "rejection" every time a button is merely hidden. Payload is identifiers + status only; no PII/KTP/NIK.

**`completeExamination` is deliberately NOT room-guarded** — the HTTP path authorises `transition` (room-guarded) first and the ability is re-checked inside `ClinicVisitService::transitionStatus()`; guarding it too would strand a doctor whose online context lapsed mid-encounter (CI caught this via 8 `tests/Feature/Pilot/RmeLabCandidateE2EValidationTest` failures). `MedicalRecordPolicy` deliberately untouched: `MedicalRecordController@show` authorises `view` on the **opened** visit before the Sprint 64.0 canonical redirect, so the RME surface is covered without disturbing the patient-centric workspace resolver.

**Test-fixture contract changed (2 files, superseded assertions).** `RoomAssignmentWorklistTest` now pins the doctor's online room (`$this->doctorRoom`) and puts the doctor-facing visits in it — a doctor is only ever scoped to the room they are working. `DoctorPatientScopeTest::scopeVisit()` defaults `clinic_room_id` to `test()->room` (the room `scopeDoctorOnline` uses) instead of inventing a throwaway room per visit, otherwise patient-scope tests silently become room-scope tests. Two Sprint 66.2 assertions that a Doctor may **print** another doctor's odontogram now assert `assertForbidden()` (§19), and the shared-patient test asserts sharing survives via **history** while the other room's live encounter stays forbidden (§16/§17).

**Known finding left untouched (§22):** `GET rme/online-context/rooms` may disclose room metadata cross-branch for an arbitrary `branch_id`. Mapped and NOT on this sprint's critical path — Phase 1 never reads a room from the request. Recorded, not half-fixed, not bundled.

## FEATURE-DOCTOR-TRUSTED-ANDROID-DEVICE-LOCK-1 — Phase 2: Doctor device registry + Master Data → Device Dokter (2026-09-03)

Branch `feature/doctor-trusted-device-lock-1-phase-2-device-registry` (base = Phase 1 GO `be12862424fb74fa3a87d98800f2996dca0a8958`, tree `3d1e88d4…`, which was also production; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-2.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/139-doctor-device-registry.mdc` (Phase 1 rule 138 stays in force).

**PHASE 2 OF 4 — CAPABILITY ONLY.** `DEVICE_REGISTRY_AVAILABLE=true`, `DEVICE_MANAGEMENT_AVAILABLE=true`, **`DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`**, **`DOCTOR_BROWSER_LOGIN_DENIED=false`**. The full feature stays NO-GO and the full-feature tag `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` is **still NOT created** — there is no Android app, no enrolment API, no session binding. Phase 3 (Android kiosk app) remains blocked on clinic hardware + a signing-key decision; Phase 4 is pilot → enforcement → feature GO.

**Enforcement is OFF structurally, not merely unswitched.** Nothing in authentication/session/middleware references `DoctorDevice`; `DoctorDeviceAccessTest` asserts it by scanning the auth surfaces for the class name. **Mutant M7 proves the assertion bites** — adding one `DoctorDevice` mention to `PostAuthenticationRedirectService` fails the suite. A "doctor can still log in" test alone would NOT prove this (a coupling that hasn't fired yet still passes). **No feature flag was added** — deliberate minimum scope; create one only in the phase that enforces.

**Domain.** Additive migration `2026_09_03_100001_create_mst_doctor_devices_table` → `mst_doctor_devices`; module `app/Modules/DoctorDevice/` mirroring the ClinicRoom master-data template (Model/Interface/Repository/Service/Policy/3 Requests/Controller + `settings/doctor-devices/*` views). Two axes kept **orthogonal on purpose**: `status` (`active`/`disabled`/`revoked` — the administrative decision) vs `identity_state` (`unverified`/`cryptographically_verified` — whether the hardware ever PROVED itself). An admin-typed row is a registered database record; `cryptographically_verified` is **unreachable in Phase 2 by construction**, so nothing hand-entered can masquerade as trusted hardware. Trust anchor is `public_key_fingerprint` (public fingerprint only) — **no MAC, no IMEI, no private key, no reusable enrolment secret**.

**Lifecycle:** `ACTIVE ⇄ DISABLED`, `ACTIVE→REVOKED`, `DISABLED→REVOKED`, **`REVOKED` terminal** (reactivate refuses; reusing the hardware means a fresh cryptographic identity later). Disable + revoke both require a written reason; revoke is high-friction in the UI. **There is NO delete path at all** — no route, no soft delete, no service method; a device that was ever trusted keeps its security history.

**Status is never a payload.** `status`, `identity_state`, `public_key_fingerprint`, `uuid` and every lifecycle stamp are outside `$fillable`, AND the service writes only explicit allow-listed arrays. The repository's `create()` uses `forceFill` on purpose — `$fillable` guards against REQUEST mass assignment, while the service payload is the real allow-list (it must write `uuid`/registration stamps).

**Branch binding** is server-authoritative: an active RME-enabled branch (`BranchService::rmeEnabledIds()`), re-validated on create AND update. Canonical codes only (`TLK1`, `SPN4`).

**Authorization:** new `view_doctor_devices` + `manage_doctor_devices` seeded but granted to **NO role** → Super Admin only via the global `Gate::before` (the ENT-7 `view_developer_console` precedent). They land in the PermissionGroupingService `other` catch-all, same as `view_developer_console`, so the "every permission in a bucket" contract still passes. Sidebar is never the boundary.

**Two mutation findings worth remembering.** (1) **M1 first survived** because the test aimed at it used an Admin Klinik actor, who never reaches the policy — the route middleware rejects them first, so the test could not distinguish policy from middleware. Closed with a user holding *only* `view_doctor_devices`, who passes the middleware and must still be refused every write. (2) **M2 is inert as a single-line mutant BY DESIGN**: `updateMetadata()` both `array_intersect_key`-filters the payload and writes an explicitly constructed `$changes` array — two independent barriers, so removing either alone changes nothing. M2 is therefore a **compound** mutant (both barriers removed) and is killed in that form.

**Gotcha:** module controllers must `use AuthorizesRequests` — Laravel 12's base `Controller` has no `authorize()`, so `$this->authorize(...)` fatals with a 500 without the trait.

**CI:** both suites registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` + a `DoctorDevice` token added to BOTH critical variants (they must stay identical) — the Phase 1 lesson that a suite no token selects is a control nothing enforces.

Evidence: Phase 2 suites 39 passed; RME+Pilot regression 1554 passed (Phase 1 intact); AccessControl 99 passed/9 skipped; permission+sidebar 441 passed; **PostgreSQL 16.14 parity 147 passed** with all 4 FKs / 3 unique constraints / 3 indexes verified in `pg_constraint`+`pg_indexes`, and migrate→rollback→migrate clean; **mutation matrix 9 mutants / 9 killed / 0 survivors**.

## FEATURE-DOCTOR-TRUSTED-ANDROID-DEVICE-LOCK-1 — Phase 3: Android Clinic App + cryptographic device enrolment (2026-09-03)

Branch `feature/doctor-trusted-device-lock-1-phase-3-android-enrollment` (base = Phase 2 GO `ca12e4a6…`, tree `0f50c53e…`, which was also production; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-3.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/140-android-clinic-device-identity.mdc` (rules 138 Phase 1 + 139 Phase 2 still in force); runbook `docs/runbooks/android-clinic-device-provisioning.md`.

**PHASE 3 OF 5 — CAPABILITY ONLY.** `DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`, `DOCTOR_BROWSER_LOGIN_DENIED=false`. **Real-device validation NOT PERFORMED** (no clinic tablet exists) and **production signing governance UNRESOLVED** — release builds declare no `signingConfig`; the only artifact is a **debug** APK for emulator/CI, never a release. Full feature stays **NO-GO**; `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` still NOT created.

**Android app** `android/daengtisia-clinic` (`com.daengtisia.clinic`, v0.3.0-phase3, Kotlin, AGP 9.0.1 + Gradle 9.1.0, minSdk 26/target 35). Dependency-light on purpose: platform `HttpsURLConnection` + `org.json`, **no OkHttp/Retrofit/serialization lib**. `DeviceIdentityManager` generates an **EC P-256 keypair inside the Android Keystore** — private key non-exportable, never transmitted/stored in prefs/passed to the WebView/logged; there is deliberately **no `exportPrivateKey`**. StrongBox requested **opportunistically** with TEE fallback (a hard requirement would refuse legitimate tablets). `SecureWebViewClient`/`UrlAllowlist`: https-only, clinic host only, cleartext off (manifest **and** network-security-config), `onReceivedSslError` **always cancels — never `proceed()`, not even in debug**, no `addJavascriptInterface`, file/content/universal access off, external links **blocked rather than handed to Chrome**. `KioskController` = **Device Owner + Lock Task, NOT screen pinning**; not-provisioned is a supported state reported honestly; no hard-coded exit PIN.

**Server protocol.** Additive migrations `2026_09_03_100002..4`: enrolment columns on `mst_doctor_devices` + `trx_doctor_device_enrollments` + `trx_doctor_device_challenges`. Stateless device channel `/device-api/v1/*` wired via `withRouting(api: routes/device_api.php, apiPrefix: 'device-api')` — **no session cookie, no CSRF**, rate limited. Flow: request → one-time pairing code → Super Admin approves on the **existing** Device Dokter screen → key bound → challenge → signature → verified. Pairing code stored **only as a keyed hash**; public key **frozen at request time** (cannot be swapped before approval); **approval binds but does NOT verify** — the hardware must still sign. Every denial returns the **same opaque error** (reason only in audit) so the endpoint cannot enumerate the estate.

**Gotcha — a real vulnerability caught by a test:** the first cut burned the challenge nonce *inside* the verification transaction. A denial throws → transaction rolls back → the burn is undone → an attacker can retry signatures against a fixed nonce forever. `DoctorDeviceProofService::verifyProof()` now claims the nonce in its **own committed transaction** BEFORE verifying. Mutant M15 reinstates the bug. **Do not merge those phases back together.**

**Gotcha — M6 was inert as written:** removing the `isRevoked()` branch changed nothing because a revoked device also fails `isActive()`. That branch's real job is the distinct **audit reason** (`device_revoked` vs `device_disabled`) an incident responder needs; a test now pins that.

**Gotcha — AGP 9 ships built-in Kotlin support:** declaring `org.jetbrains.kotlin.android` alongside AGP 9.0.1 is a hard build error, not redundancy. Also: `avdmanager` writes AVDs to `~/.config/.android/avd` while the emulator searches `$HOME/.android/avd` — export `ANDROID_AVD_HOME`.

**Enforcement OFF structurally:** nothing in auth/session/web middleware references `DoctorDevice` and **no web route carries a device middleware** — pinned by a source scan AND a sweep of every registered route's middleware (mutant M9). Device-API middleware, if ever added, must live in `app/Modules/DoctorDevice/Middleware/`, never `app/Http/Middleware/`, so the Phase 2 assertion stays true.

**Emulator validation** on a clean **`google_apis` (non-Play)** image — Play-Store images refuse `dpm set-device-owner`: install, **Device Owner provisioned**, `mLockTaskModeState=LOCKED`, app is `topResumedActivity`, 0 BROWSABLE deep links; 7 instrumentation tests against the **real AndroidKeyStore** (key generation, idempotent identity, fingerprint parity with the server formula, signature verification, wrong-nonce rejection, identity destruction, second install ⇒ different identity so a copied APK inherits no trust).

Evidence: server device suites 77; **RME+Pilot+AccessControl+DoctorDevice 1729 passed / 0 failed** (Phase 1+2 intact); **PostgreSQL 16.14 parity 76 passed** with FKs/unique nonce/unique pairing-code hash/7 new columns verified; Android unit 28; Android instrumentation 7; **mutation matrix 15 mutants / 15 killed / 0 survivors**. New CI job `android_clinic_app_gate` (unit + lint + assembleDebug, and asserts the four security suites actually produced results); both Phase 3 PHP suites registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`.

## FEATURE-DOCTOR-TRUSTED-ANDROID-DEVICE-LOCK-1 — Phase 3.5: Android production readiness & governance (2026-09-03)

Branch `feature/doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-3-5-readiness` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline Phase 3 GO `bb9901c7`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-3-5.md`; decision `docs/adr/0009-android-production-signing-distribution-and-device-management.md`; governance `docs/governance/android-production-signing-governance.md`; runbooks `docs/runbooks/android-{release-distribution-and-rollback,signing-key-backup-and-recovery,device-loss-replacement-and-decommission,clinic-device-provisioning}.md`; procurement `docs/operations/clinic-tablet-procurement-specification.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/141-android-production-release-governance.mdc`.

**READINESS AND GOVERNANCE ONLY.** `DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`, `DOCTOR_BROWSER_LOGIN_DENIED=false`, `ANDROID_REAL_DEVICE_VALIDATION=false`, **`PRODUCTION_SIGNING_KEY_PROVISIONED=false`**. Full feature stays **NO-GO**; `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` is **still NOT created**. **No migration, no route, no permission, no frontend, no schema.** Phase 1 room scope + RME/odontogram print denial, Phase 2 registry lifecycle, and Phase 3 enrolment/replay/WebView contracts all unchanged.

**What changed is the Phase 3 posture line "production signing governance is UNRESOLVED" — now RESOLVED (ADR 0009).** Rule 11 of `140-android-clinic-device-identity.mdc` is superseded by rule 141; 140's other rules stand.

**The four decisions.** (1) **Signing = Play App Signing**: Google KMS holds the app signing key, the clinic holds only an **upload** key. The deciding property is narrow — an upload key is *resettable*, an app signing key is not — and in this system losing the app signing key destroys **every device enrolment**, because clearing app data destroys the Keystore identity and the forced reinstall generates a key no administrator approved. Rejected: self-managed keystore (every failure terminal), keystore in CI secrets (PR CI runs untrusted code ⇒ every fork a release authority), MDM-only signing (vendor without removing the problem). (2) **Distribution = Managed Google Play private app** (AAB; package name **permanent from first publish**). (3) **Device management** = self-owned Device Owner (pilot) → EMM dedicated device (fleet, ≥5); under EMM **our app is NOT the Device Owner**, the DPC is. (4) **Rollback = halt rollout, then forward-fix under a HIGHER versionCode** — Play refuses a non-greater versionCode and Android blocks downgrade installs, so "reinstall the previous APK" does not exist; halting does not recall already-updated devices; the **first release on a track cannot be halted at all**.

**Pilot exception, bounded not discovered:** a self-owned Device Owner tablet has **no Google account** (a precondition of `dpm set-device-owner`), so it cannot install from Managed Google Play. The single pilot device side-loads the **Play-signed** artifact over ADB — identical signing identity, different transport. Side-loading a *locally* signed build is forbidden: it validates an identity production never uses.

**Deliberately NOT done: no production key was created.** Creating an irreplaceable signing identity on an ephemeral context with no recorded custodian, to make a sprint look green, is the exact failure this governance exists to prevent. The procedure was instead rehearsed end to end with a **disposable** key (generated → signed v1 → encrypted backup → primary shredded → restored → signed v2), and the decisive evidence is that **v1 and v2 carry an identical signer certificate** — which is what an Android update actually requires. Comparing the PKCS#7 blocks instead reports a false failure (they are signatures over each artifact's own content). Honest pair: `SIGNING_RECOVERY_RUNBOOK_TESTED_WITH_DISPOSABLE_KEY=true` / `PRODUCTION_KEY_RECOVERY_VERIFIED=false`.

**Built:** `config/android_release.php` (all forbidden literals live here, never in the scanner); `app/Support/Android/KotlinSourceScanner.php` (comment stripping, literal-**aware**, nesting block comments); `app/Support/Android/AndroidReleaseGovernanceScanner.php` (17 read-only checks; signs nothing, needs no secret, no network — safe on a fork PR); `php artisan android:release-readiness [--json] [--strict] [--manifest=]`. Suite `tests/Feature/DoctorDevice/DoctorDeviceAndroidReleaseGovernanceTest.php` (31) registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`.

**Gotcha — do not over-apply the Phase 3 comment-stripping lesson.** Rule 13 of 140 says strip comments so a guard stops matching its own prose. Applying that as "blank the strings too" **breaks this guard**: the forbidden token is `signingConfigs.getByName("debug")`, which *contains a string literal*, so blanking strings makes it unmatchable and a genuinely debug-signed release passes. Literal awareness means "a `//` inside a string does not open a comment", never "delete the string". Mutant M8 pins exactly this.

**Mutation finding — M2 survived the first cut.** Emptying the key-material pattern lists left both `no_committed_key_material` and `keystore_ignored` reporting PASS while enforcing nothing: an absence assertion over an empty list passes on nothing at all. Fixed by a `key_material_guard_armed` check that fails closed on an empty or disarmed list. Final matrix: **18 mutants / 18 killed / 0 survivors**, baseline green before and after, every revert verified by sha256 (copy-based revert — `git checkout --` cannot restore untracked files and would eat uncommitted work).

**Security review found the sharpest defect, and it followed from the literal-preservation rule above.** Because string literals are kept, a **file-wide** search for `release {` is hijackable by any earlier string containing that text (`val releaseNotes = "release { see the wiki }"`). Run through the real scanner it printed **PASS — "does not fall back to the debug signing identity"** about a build that did exactly that, because the non-vacuity check asserted its markers against the *whole file* while the token check read the *extracted block*. A decoy inside `mapOf(...)` produced an **empty** extraction that also passed. Fixed by anchoring extraction to `android > buildTypes > release`, asserting markers against the extracted block, and failing on an empty block (M13/M14). Three sibling disarm paths were fixed with it: the armed set covered 2 of 6 declared patterns (M15); `keystore_ignored` substring-matched raw ignore text, so a comment satisfied it and so did `!android/**/*.jks`, a negation that *un-ignores* keystores while the check reported they were ignored (M16); and the coupling scan skipped missing directories silently while watching `app/Http/Middleware`, which is **not** where this codebase registers route middleware — a Phase 4 hook at `app/Modules/DoctorDevice/Middleware` would have been invisible (M17). Required surfaces now fail closed when absent; future enforcement paths are *watched*, since they legitimately do not exist yet. **The architecture was sound; the detection was not** — every defect was a guard reporting success while enforcing nothing. `SECURITY_CRITICAL=0`, `SECURITY_HIGH=0` after fixes.

**Gotcha — a source-scanning contract caught this sprint's own comment.** Bounding the scanner's reads introduced a suppressed read, adding this file to the exact-set inventory `CriticalGateWarningContractTest` maintains. Correct fix = the guarded shape that contract calls exemplary (`is_readable()` then explicit `=== false`), NOT growing the exception list. The suite then stayed red because the **comment explaining why the suppression operator is avoided named the operator**, and that contract matches raw source. Rule 141 §14 demonstrated on its own author. Never write a scanned literal in the prose that explains avoiding it.

**Device spec is anchored to Android Enterprise Recommended**, and no model list is frozen into config — devices are archived from the AER directory when an OEM stops shipping updates, so a config model list becomes a procurement trap. Criteria in config; a **dated** candidate shortlist in the procurement doc, each row requiring per-SKU verification at purchase. Indonesian retail availability and pricing could **not** be verified from primary sources and are marked as such rather than invented.

**Phase 4 entry needs two human actions with lead times:** procure one AER-listed pilot tablet, and create the Play Developer **role account** + enrol Play App Signing. Everything else is written down and gated.

## REVISION-DOCTOR-ANDROID-DIRECT-APK-SIGNING-DISTRIBUTION-1 — self-managed signing + direct APK (2026-09-03)

Branch `revision/doctor-android-direct-apk-signing-distribution-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline Phase 3.5 GO `7554a17e`; do NOT target main). Decision `docs/adr/0010-android-direct-apk-signing-and-distribution.md`; revision doc `docs/sprints/revision-doctor-android-direct-apk-signing-distribution-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/142-android-direct-apk-signing-distribution.mdc`.

**ANDROID DISTRIBUTION IS NOW: DIRECT ADMIN-MANAGED SIGNED APK.** `GOOGLE PLAY = NOT USED`. `MANAGED GOOGLE PLAY = NOT USED`. `PLAY APP SIGNING = NOT USED`. `PLAY DEVELOPER ACCOUNT = NOT REQUIRED`. **Production signing key = SELF-MANAGED BY DAENGTISIAMS.** Release artifact = **APK** (never AAB — a bundle is a Play upload format nothing else converts). Installer = **authorized DaengtisiaMS Admin/IT**. Device trust = **cryptographic Keystore enrolment, never possession of the APK**. Device Owner + Lock Task unchanged. `DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`, `DOCTOR_BROWSER_LOGIN_DENIED=false`, `ANDROID_REAL_DEVICE_VALIDATION=false`, `PRODUCTION_SIGNING_KEY_PROVISIONED=false`. Full feature stays **NO-GO**; `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` still **NOT created**. **Do not reintroduce Google Play without a new ADR.**

**Supersession, not rewriting.** Phase 3.5's ADR 0009 (Play App Signing + Managed Google Play) and its sprint doc are **preserved unedited** with `SUPERSEDED` banners; rule 141 §2 and §7 carry supersession notes. The Phase 3.5 readiness GO is **not withdrawn** — release governance, versionCode discipline, Device Owner concepts, procurement requirements, pilot acceptance gates and the device-loss runbook all survive. Only Google-Play-specific signing/distribution assumptions were replaced. **No migration, no route, no permission, no schema change.**

**The risk moved onto the clinic, and it got worse — say so.** ADR 0009's argument (an upload key is resettable, an app signing key is not) was **NOT refuted**; it was accepted as a cost. Follow the platform chain: Android requires an update to be signed by the **same certificate** → a mismatched APK installs only after an **uninstall** → uninstall **erases app data** → app data holds the **Keystore device identity** → that identity IS the DoctorDevice enrolment. So losing this key costs **the entire fleet's enrolment**, not just updates. Compensation is procedural and deliberate: **3 custodians / 3 encrypted copies (1 offsite, 1 sealed cold) / 90-day restore drill** — stricter than the Play era's 2 and 180.

**Gotcha — Play was silently enforcing half our versionCode rule.** The **platform** rule is `versionCode >= installed`; **Play** additionally refused anything not **strictly >**. Removing Play removed that enforcement, so two different builds could now share a versionCode and Android would install one over the other in silence. Config splits `platform_version_code_rule` from `governance_version_code_rule` so nobody mistakes ours for the platform's. **There is no production downgrade** — it needs an uninstall; `adb install -d` bypasses the block only for **debuggable** builds.

**Gotcha — a term scan cannot judge a supersession (third occurrence of this trap).** The prose explaining why Play was dropped necessarily NAMES Play, so scanning active docs for "Google Play" goes red on a repo that already obeys the rule (same class as the Phase 3 TLS guard and the Phase 3.5 read guard). The load-bearing assertion is the **machine-readable negatives** (`google_play_required => false` + siblings), which prose cannot satisfy; the term scan only requires that a file naming a Play term also carries a negation/supersession marker — which a quiet reintroduction would lack.

**Deleted, not widened:** the Phase-3.5 "pilot exception" (side-load a Play-signed artifact) is **gone** — it existed only because a Device Owner tablet has no Google account and so could not install from Managed Google Play; with direct install canonical, pilot and fleet use one path. **EMM is advisory, not required** at any fleet size (the "EMM required at 5 devices" rule was a Play dependency wearing a device costume); advisory past ~10 devices, owner's decision.

**Built:** `config/android_release.php` rewritten (signing/distribution/device-management/versioning + new `installation` and `update_contract` sections + Play-remnant scan contract); `AndroidReleaseGovernanceScanner` +4 checks (`apk_is_canonical_release_artifact`, `google_play_not_required`, `signing_authority_is_self_managed`, `active_authority_acknowledges_supersession`/`historical_authority_marked_superseded`); new `AndroidReleaseArtifactVerifier` + `SignerFingerprintResolver` seam + `ApksignerFingerprintResolver`; new **`php artisan android:verify-release <apk> <manifest>`** (non-zero exit = DO NOT INSTALL; reads only public material so anyone who may install may verify). New runbook `docs/runbooks/android-direct-apk-installation.md`. Suites `DoctorDeviceAndroidReleaseGovernanceTest` (32) + new `DoctorDeviceAndroidDirectApkReleaseTest` (15), both in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`.

**CRITICAL caught by security review — the replacement for Play was a checksum, not a signature check.** The verifier first read the expected certificate fingerprint **from the release manifest shipping beside the APK**: self-consistency, not authenticity. An attacker replacing the artifact replaces the manifest with it (digest = their build, fingerprint = their key), and every other check is a public constant from this repo — the command exited **0** and the runbook said install. Fixed by a **trust anchor**: `config/android_release.signing.production_certificate_sha256` is the authority and the APK is compared against it; the manifest's copy is only a cross-check. It is `null` today so the verifier **FAILS CLOSED** — correct, since no authority exists yet — and **pinning it is a Phase-4 entry step** beside provisioning the key. Related HIGH: the runbook claimed `versionName`/`versionCode`/`package_name` were validated against the APK; only SHA-256 and the signer fingerprint are APK-derived, and the text now says so. Also fixed: supersession regex anchored to the Status VALUE (a doc saying "current authority… NOT SUPERSEDED" previously passed), apksigner parse anchored + multi-signer ambiguity rejected, `--` argv terminator, bounded manifest read, boolean `version_code` rejected.

**Drill finding worth keeping:** an attacker-signed artifact **verifies as a validly signed package**. Validity is not authority — only comparing the signer certificate fingerprint against the release authority catches a substitution. That is why the verifier compares fingerprints rather than trusting `apksigner verify` alone, and why an unreadable fingerprint fails closed instead of being skipped.

## REVISION-DOCTOR-AUTO-DEVICE-APPROVAL-APP-ONLY-LOGIN-1 — doctor↔device approval + app-only gate (2026-09-03)

Branch `revision/doctor-auto-device-approval-app-only-login-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report`, baseline `revision-doctor-android-direct-apk-signing-distribution-1-go` @ `bf4c464`, tree `3e1baae` — identical on origin and VPS; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-doctor-auto-device-approval-app-only-login-1.md`; ADR `docs/adr/0011-doctor-device-authorization-and-app-only-login.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/143-doctor-device-authorization-and-app-only-login.mdc`; runbook `docs/runbooks/doctor-device-approval-operations.md`.

**Ships the COMPLETE trusted-device capability with enforcement OFF.** `DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`, `DOCTOR_BROWSER_LOGIN_DENIED=false`, `ANDROID_REAL_DEVICE_VALIDATION=false`, Phase 4 NOT started, full feature still **NO-GO** (`feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` deliberately absent).

**Domain.** New `DoctorDeviceAuthorization` ("this doctor may use this device") separate from the physical `DoctorDevice`: one tablet, one device row, one authorization per doctor. **3 additive migrations** — `mst_doctor_device_authorizations` (UNIQUE(doctor_id,doctor_device_id) = the idempotency guarantee), `trx_doctor_device_login_tickets`, `trx_doctor_device_login_challenges`. The login challenge table is SEPARATE from the Phase 3 one on purpose: `trx_doctor_device_challenges.doctor_device_id` is NOT NULL and its comment says why, and a first login happens before any device row exists — reusing it would have meant relaxing a live Phase 3 invariant.

**Flow (`POST device-api/v1/doctor/login`).** Sign a server nonce → burn it in its OWN committed transaction → verify against the enrolled key → validate credentials with `Hash::check` (never `Auth::attempt`, which would start a session on a stateless channel) → resolve a linked active Doctor → resolve/provision the device → resolve/request the authorization → mint a ticket ONLY if enforcement is on AND everything is ACTIVE. **Nothing is written before the credentials pass**, so a wrong password, a forged signature or a non-doctor account creates nothing. One opaque 422 for every failure.

**`DoctorDevice::STATUS_PENDING_APPROVAL`** — auto-provisioned, key-proven, trusted by nothing. Strictly LESS privileged than every prior status (`isActive()` false ⇒ proof endpoint, `isTrustworthy()` and ticket minting all still refuse it). Approving the authorization promotes it in the same transaction = the single operator decision. **`reactivate` tightened to accept only `disabled`** — otherwise it was a side door that admitted hardware nobody approved.

**Lifecycle.** PENDING→ACTIVE→REVOKED(terminal); PENDING→REJECTED; REJECTED reopens only via privileged `allowReRequest`, which records WHICH rejection it forgives (`re_request_allowed_for_rejected_at`). **Gotcha:** the first implementation compared "allowance newer than rejection" — same-second timestamps made a strict `>` refuse the legitimate path. Identity of the forgiven rejection is exact; clock ordering is not.

**Gate.** `App\Modules\DoctorDevice\Services\DoctorAppLoginGate` is the ONLY reader of `doctor.trusted_device_enforcement` (default false, risk critical) — pinned by a test that greps `app/`, `routes/`, `bootstrap/`. Browser denial comes from the ABSENCE of a server-verified device session, never a User-Agent/header. `EnsureDoctorDeviceSession` appended to the `web` group returns on its first line while the flag is off. Ticket redemption `GET device-login/{ticket}` re-asserts everything and refuses outright when enforcement is off.

**Approval surface.** New top-level sidebar group **Approval → Approval Device Dokter** (pending badge), routes `doctor-device-authorizations.{index,show,approve,reject,revoke,allow-re-request}`, deliberately NOT under `settings.` and separate from Master Data → Device Dokter. New permissions `view_doctor_device_authorizations` + `manage_doctor_device_authorizations` → **Supervisor RME** (and Super Admin via `Gate::before`); Supervisor RME gains nothing else, notably NOT `view_/manage_doctor_devices`. `SupervisorRmeRolePermissionTest` exact-list pin repinned (+2). Named rate limiters `doctor-app-login` (10/min) + `doctor-app-login-challenge` (30/min) in `AppServiceProvider` — an anonymous `throttle:n,1` signature is domain+IP, not URI, so an inline limit would have tightened the whole Phase 3 device channel to the same budget.

**Two prior structural contracts narrowed, with their own review** (both said "Phase 4 is where that changes"): auth surfaces may now reference exactly `DoctorAppLoginGate`/`DoctorDeviceSessionService`/`EnsureDoctorDeviceSession` and nothing else; exactly one device middleware may appear on the web stack. `config/android_release.enforcement.flag_exists` → true + `flag_key` + `flag_default_off`, and `android:release-readiness` now verifies OFF against the REAL feature-flag registry (check renamed `authentication_coupled_only_through_the_gate`).

**Blade trap (cost real debugging time, twice).** `sidebar.blade.php` line ~759 uses the INLINE php directive, which has no closing token. Blade extracts raw blocks non-greedily from an opening php directive to the next closing one BEFORE compiling or stripping comments, so adding a closing token further down — **including inside a comment** — hands that lone opener a partner and turns ~170 lines into an uncompiled raw block (undefined variable, then a parse error, far from the cause). The badge count is a single inline expression and the explaining comment avoids writing those tokens with their at-sign.

**Android.** New `DoctorLoginStateMachine` (+`DoctorLoginScreen`/`DoctorLoginResult`), `EnrollmentApi::requestLoginChallenge/doctorLogin/doctorAuthorizationStatus`, `DeviceProofMessage.PURPOSE_DOCTOR_LOGIN` (distinct purpose ⇒ no cross-replay), native credential form + waiting/rejected/revoked screens with manual "Periksa Status" (no timer polling). **APPROVED_ENFORCEMENT_OFF is a first-class state**: approved with no ticket means enforcement is off and the ordinary web login opens — treating it as a failure would strand a doctor on a waiting screen. The password is never persisted, so approval invites a fresh login rather than an auto-login. `./gradlew test` 37 passed (8 new), `lint` + `assembleDebug` green. **Kotlin gotcha:** a local `val text` shadows the implicit receiver's `text` inside `apply {}`.

**Tests (67 new).** `DoctorDeviceAuthorizationLifecycleTest` (17), `DoctorDeviceAppLoginRequestTest` (17), `DoctorDeviceEnforcementGateTest` (18 — the enforcement-OFF half is the deployment safety contract and is written first), `DoctorDeviceApprovalAccessTest` (12), plus 3 updated structural contracts. All four registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites` (the `DoctorDevice` filter token already selects them; declaring them is what stops a rename silently unselecting them). **Test gotcha:** flip the flag by rewriting the whole `feature_flags.flags` array — the key contains dots, so `config()->set('feature_flags.flags.doctor.trusted_device_enforcement.default')` builds a structure nothing reads and the test passes while enforcement is still off.

**Preserved:** Phase 1 room isolation + RME/odontogram print denial, Phase 2 device lifecycle and terminal revocation, Phase 3 challenge/response and the opaque device channel, Daily Branch Lock, `BranchContext` as the only branch authority (a provisional device's branch is server-resolved from the doctor's own RME branches, never requested), direct signed-APK distribution, Google Play unused.

**Deploy:** `php artisan migrate --force` (3 additive tables) + `db:seed --class=PermissionSeeder --force` + `db:seed --class=RoleSeeder --force` + `permission:cache-reset`. Leave `FEATURE_DOCTOR_TRUSTED_DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT` unset/false.

## REVISION-ANDROID-RELEASE-READINESS-PHASE4A-PILOT-AUTHORITY-1 (2026-09-04)

Branch `revision/android-release-readiness-phase4a-pilot-authority-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `e99bc212`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-android-release-readiness-phase4a-pilot-authority-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/143-android-release-readiness-runtime-identity.mdc` (rule 141 §17 updated, nothing withdrawn). **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no clinical mutation.**

**Fix — the readiness gate was green for root and red for the identity that runs production.** `android:release-readiness` returned `GO 22/22` as root and `FAIL 20/22` as `daengtisiams`. INFRA-SEC-RUNTIME-1 pairs a **root-owned deployed tree** with an unprivileged runtime, so git answered both scanner call sites with `fatal: detected dubious ownership`. `no_committed_key_material` failed closed correctly, but `gradle_wrapper_executable` reported *"android/daengtisia-clinic/gradlew is not tracked by git"* — the wrapper was tracked all along; git had merely declined to look. **A gate that is only green for root is a gate operators learn to run as root.**

**Fix shape (constrained as hard as its effect):** every git invocation now goes through one private `git()` helper that prepends an **invocation-scoped** `-c safe.directory=<the repository the scanner was constructed to audit>` and nothing else. **Explicitly rejected:** `safe.directory=*` (trusts every repo on the host), any persistent `--global`/`--system` entry (outlives process/deploy/memory), chowning the production repo (root ownership IS the boundary), running the audit as root, and treating a git error as "clean". `safe.directory` is honoured only from *protected* configuration — the **command scope is protected**, which is why `-c` works where a repo-local entry would not. **Verified empirically on both git versions in play (production 2.43.0, dev 2.53.0), not assumed:** scoped trust → index read; the same option naming a **different** path → still refused; a parent entry does **not** trust a **nested** repository (so a repo planted under the app dir gains nothing). Git is invoked with an **argument array**, so `-c`, the `safe.directory=<path>` value and the subcommand are separate argv entries and no path ever reaches a shell; `basePath` comes from the container binding `base_path()` and is never request input. `errorOutput()` is **matched against a config marker, never echoed**.

**Error classification (new `reason` key on checks):** `index_access_error` (git could not read the index — *no* conclusion about contents) · `forbidden_key_material_found` · `not_tracked` · `clean` / `tracked`. The wrapper check's `! successful()` and `output === ''` branches were **one branch with one message**; they are now separate, because they send an operator to two completely different places. **Fail-closed is unchanged** — an unreadable index is still FAIL.

**Owner Phase 4A sign-off — recorded, NOT exercised.** New `enforcement.owner_signoff`: `phase_4a_pilot_authorized => true`, `authorized_scope => 'pilot_branch_or_device'` (never global), `pilot_doctor => 'drg Karmila'`, `pilot_branch => 'Cabang Sunu'`, `rollback_owner => 'Custodian 1 / Raushan Fikri Ridha / IT'`, `pilot_activated => false`. Human authority is stored **as written text, never as production row ids** — resolving names to records belongs to the phase that runs the pilot; an id copied between environments is how the wrong doctor gets enrolled.

**`may_be_enabled_in_phase` decision:** one number could not carry two answers ("nothing before 5" became wrong; "Phase 4 is fine" is dangerously broad — global is one rung down the same ladder). Split into `pilot_may_be_enabled_in_phase => '4A'` and `global_may_be_enabled_in_phase => 5`. `may_be_enabled_in_phase` is **retained at 5** because rule 141 / ADR 0009 / ADR 0011 / both Phase-4 closure records cite it by name, and now carries `may_be_enabled_in_phase_scope => 'global_doctor_enforcement'`; the scanner FAILs if the two ever drift. `global_enforcement_stage` names the global rung rather than deriving it from the last array element, so appending a rung cannot silently move what "global" means. The shipped five-rung ladder was reused — rule 141 §17 forbids building a parallel OFF/PILOT/GLOBAL model.

**Authorization ≠ activation.** After this revision: `DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`, `DOCTOR_BROWSER_LOGIN_DENIED=false`, `current_stage=off`, flag defaults false, **no** pilot target row, **no** `DoctorDeviceAuthorization` created, drg Karmila and every other doctor log in exactly as before. **Signing custody stays PARTIAL** (Custodian 1 established; Custodian 2 / offsite / Custodian 3 / sealed-cold **deferred by owner decision**); `production_certificate_sha256` stays `null`, no key provisioned, no certificate pinned.

**Why GO with no production key (do not mis-read the count):** the now-24 checks audit **repository release governance** only. Signed-artifact readiness is a *different* gate — `android:verify-release` and this command's `--manifest` path — and both still fail closed while the pin is null. That separation was preserved, not softened; every run prints `PRODUCTION_SIGNING_KEY_PROVISIONED=false` and `ANDROID_REAL_DEVICE_VALIDATION=false` so the inference cannot be made. Two new checks: `pilot_authority_recorded`, `global_enforcement_deferred` (22 → **24/24 GO**).

**Test gotcha (cost a silent skip):** forcing git's ownership refusal needs `GIT_TEST_ASSUME_DIFFERENT_OWNER=1` set in **both `putenv()` and `$_SERVER`** — Symfony builds a child environment from `getenv()` **INTERSECTED with `$_SERVER`**, so a putenv-only variable is filtered straight back out and the test skips instead of proving anything. The harness also pins `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL`/`GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM` to `/dev/null` so a developer's existing global `safe.directory` cannot mask the defect. `unshare --user --map-root-user` does **not** reproduce it (git skips the ownership check under euid 0).

**History preserved:** Phase 4 and the first Phase 4A attempt remain **BLOCKED / NO-GO** (forward reference added, nothing rewritten). Neither `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-4a-app-only-real-device-pilot-go` nor `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` was created. Tests: new `tests/Feature/DoctorDevice/DoctorDeviceAndroidRuntimeIdentityReadinessTest.php` (15), registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`; the pre-existing `shells out only to read-only git subcommands` control was **strengthened** (it now resolves the subcommand past option pairs and constrains the injected config to exactly one key/value, so `-c core.hooksPath=…` is a failure too). **Deploy:** no migration, no seed — deploy and re-run `sudo -u daengtisiams php artisan android:release-readiness --strict`.

## EVIDENCE-PHASE4A-REAL-DEVICE-KEYINFO-PREFLIGHT-1 (2026-09-05)

Branch `evidence/phase4a-real-device-keyinfo-preflight-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `520daccf`; do NOT target main). Evidence doc `docs/sprints/evidence-phase4a-real-device-keyinfo-preflight-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/144-android-real-device-keyinfo-preflight.mdc`; runbook procedure `docs/runbooks/android-clinic-device-provisioning.md` §2A. **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no clinical mutation. The tablet was NOT touched by this task** (`TABLET_TOUCHED_BY_TASK=false`, `ADB_STARTED_BY_TASK=false`, `REAL_DEVICE_TEST_RERUN=false`) — the measurements were already collected and this revision makes them durable.

**The claim that had never been measured.** Phases 1–3.5 *assumed* the device identity key lives in secure hardware. `DeviceIdentityManager` asks `AndroidKeyStore` and requests StrongBox opportunistically, and the Phase 3 instrumentation suite proves the enrolment **protocol** — but **every one of its 7 assertions would also pass against a software-backed key**. Nothing asked the keystore where the key it issued actually went.

**The trap this closes: a DEVICE CAPABILITY FLAG IS NOT PROOF.** `android.hardware.hardware_keystore` describes the **platform**, not the key. A keystore can issue a *particular* key in software — an algorithm it does not back, a spec it refuses, StrongBox asked of hardware that has none — and the flag reads **identically**. Authoritative proof is a property of the generated key: **`KeyInfo.getSecurityLevel()`** (API 31+). `KeyInfo.isInsideSecureHardware()` is the pre-31 compatibility path, is deprecated, and must never be the preferred proof. **Accepted: `TRUSTED_ENVIRONMENT`, `STRONGBOX`. Rejected: `SOFTWARE`, `UNKNOWN_SECURE`** (an unattested claim is not a measurement).

**Measured on real hardware — `PHASE4A_PILOT_TABLET_01`** (samsung SM-X236B, gta11pxx, Android 16 / API 36, patch 2026-07-05): `verifiedbootstate=green`, `flash.locked=1`, `vbmeta.device_state=locked`, `ro.kernel.qemu=0` (physical, not emulator). Capabilities: `hardware_keystore=300`, `app_attest_key` — **recorded as context, never as the verdict**; **StrongBox absent, which is NOT a failure** (requested via `runCatching` → `recoverCatching` fallback to TEE; `strongbox_mandatory => false`). Existing suite `DeviceIdentityInstrumentedTest` run targeted: **7 run / 0 failed / PASS**. Temporary `KeyInfo` probe: **1 run / 0 failed / PASS** → **`securityLevel = TRUSTED_ENVIRONMENT`, `privateKeyExportable = false`**. Non-exportability is load-bearing on its own: a key that can leave the device is a credential copyable to a second tablet, so `key_security_level_accepted` requires **both** halves.

**Probe hygiene.** The probe was **NOT committed** (restored via git; inspection worktree `git status --short` clean). It reused the test alias `daengtisiams_instrumented_test_key`, whose fixture destroys it in **both** `@Before` and `@After` — **not** the production alias `daengtisiams_device_identity_v1`, which was never generated. No debug/production package, no instrumentation residue on the device. Only the **test logcat buffer** was cleared — **no claim is made that system logs were destroyed**. The `isInsideSecureHardware is deprecated` warning came from the probe's own compatibility branch and is **not a finding**: the shipped `DeviceIdentityManager` calls neither API (verified — no `KeyInfo`/`securityLevel`/`isInsideSecureHardware` in committed Android source).

**Machine enforcement (not just prose).** New `config/android_release.real_device_preflight` carries the rule, the recorded evidence (logical label only) and explicit `unlocks` **negatives**. `AndroidReleaseGovernanceScanner::realDevicePreflightChecks()` adds 3 gate checks — `real_device_preflight_baseline` (refuses emulator / non-green boot / unlocked bootloader or vbmeta / missing label), `key_security_level_accepted` (refuses `SOFTWARE`/`UNKNOWN_SECURE`, an exportable key, a capability flag offered as proof, or an absent measurement), `preflight_unlocks_nothing` (refuses a pass read as unlocking signing/pinning/enrolment/enforcement) — taking the command **24 → 27/27 GO**. `required_documents` now includes this evidence doc **and** the Phase 4A pilot-authority doc (proven load-bearing: the gate went FAIL until the doc existed).

**Two adjacent summary lines that must never be conflated:** `ANDROID_REAL_DEVICE_VALIDATION=false` — the Phase 4 pilot (signed release installed, device enrolled, pilot run) has **not** happened; `ANDROID_REAL_DEVICE_HARDWARE_PREFLIGHT=PASS` — the key measurement has. `real_device_validation` was deliberately **left false**; the new summary key is derived from the checks themselves (`preflightPassed()`), never asserted independently, so a summary line cannot disagree with its own checks.

**Device serials stay out of git.** Governance records the **class** of device, never the instance. Model/product/API/patch are fine; ADB/USB serial, IMEI, MEID, ICCID, Android ID are not. Enforced two ways in `DoctorDeviceAndroidRealDevicePreflightTest`: no evidence **key** may name a hardware instance identifier, and no evidence **value** may contain a serial-shaped token (tokenise on non-alphanumerics, then flag any 8–20 char token carrying **both** a digit and an uppercase letter — the naive single-regex version false-positived on the revision id itself because unanchored lookaheads scanned past the token).

**What a preflight PASS does NOT unlock (all still false):** signing custody completion (**PARTIAL** — Custodian 1 = Raushan Fikri Ridha / IT; Custodian 2, offsite backup, Custodian 3, sealed cold backup **DEFERRED by owner**), production signing key provisioning, certificate pinning (`production_certificate_sha256` still `null`), production APK build or installation, production enrolment, pilot enforcement activation, global enforcement activation, Phase 4A completion, feature completion. **`TRUSTED_ENVIRONMENT` ≠ tamper-proof, ≠ secure element, ≠ StrongBox** — it means the Keystore reports TEE protection.

**Unchanged:** pilot = drg Karmila / Cabang Sunu, rollback owner Custodian 1 / Raushan Fikri Ridha / IT, `pilot_may_be_enabled_in_phase='4A'`, `global_may_be_enabled_in_phase=5`, `pilot_activated=false`, `current_stage=off`, `doctor_browser_login_denied=false`, flag defaults false. Device Owner / kiosk remain Phase 4B; Google Play unused; direct admin-managed signed APK canonical. **Phase 4 and the first Phase 4A attempt remain BLOCKED / NO-GO and are NOT rewritten** — a later gate passing does not retroactively turn an earlier blocked attempt into a GO. Neither `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-4a-app-only-real-device-pilot-go` nor `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` was created. Tests: new `tests/Feature/DoctorDevice/DoctorDeviceAndroidRealDevicePreflightTest.php` (24), registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`; **13 of them are adversarial** — each mutates the recorded evidence into a way it could be wrong and requires the scanner to go red, because a gate that only ever sees the correct value has never been shown to reject anything. **Adversarial review of the gate found 3 vacuity holes, all fixed with regression tests proven to fail on the pre-fix logic:** (a) the boot-state clause was the only one comparing **config to config**, so deleting both keys made `null === null` PASS with no boot state recorded — deletion must fail exactly like a wrong value; (b) `($evidence['keyinfo_tests_run'] ?? 0) >= 1` is non-strict and PHP 8 makes `'many' >= 1` and `true >= 1` both true, so the one clause separating a **measurement** from an **assertion** was satisfiable by an arbitrary string (`is_int()` first now); (c) `in_array(true, …, true)` is strict, so `1`/`1.0`/`'true'`/`'yes'` left `preflight_unlocks_nothing` green — every entry must now be exactly `false` and the failure names the offending key. (c) was **not** an activation bypass (`unlocks` is read only by this scanner and its own test; the same check re-asserts the four real config values) but was fixed anyway, because a governance record that can contradict itself is exactly what this file exists to prevent. **The gate validates a RECORDED value — nothing re-reads the tablet in CI.** It proves the record is internally consistent and not silently downgraded; it cannot prove the tablet measured TRUSTED_ENVIRONMENT, which is what the real-hardware run is for. **Deploy:** no migration, no seed — deploy and re-run `sudo -u daengtisiams php artisan android:release-readiness`. **Pre-existing, unrelated:** `.cursor/rules` has two rules numbered 143 (`143-android-release-readiness-runtime-identity` and `143-doctor-device-authorization-and-app-only-login`); left as-is, this evidence uses 144.

## PRODUCTION-ANDROID-SIGNING-CUSTODY-READINESS-1 (2026-09-05)

Branch `security/production-android-signing-custody-readiness-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `3ddbea0d`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/production-android-signing-custody-readiness-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/145-android-production-signing-custody-readiness.mdc`; governance §2.1/§2.2 in `docs/governance/android-production-signing-governance.md`; runbook §0 stage gates in `docs/runbooks/android-signing-key-backup-and-recovery.md`. **No migration, no permission, no route, no schema, no clinical mutation. The tablet was NOT touched** (`TABLET_TOUCHED_BY_TASK=false`, `ADB_STARTED_BY_TASK=false`). **NO PRODUCTION SIGNING KEY WAS GENERATED** — no keytool, no openssl signing identity, no placeholder, no "temporary production key", and no backup artifact written to any medium.

**What was missing.** The signing *policy* (three copies, encrypted, one offsite, one sealed cold, never in git/CI/VPS) has existed since Phase 3.5. What did not exist was **anyone named to hold them**. EVIDENCE-PHASE4A recorded custody as `PARTIAL` with Custodian 2, Custodian 3, offsite and sealed-cold all **DEFERRED by owner** — true when written, and **superseded forward, never rewritten**.

**The owner's designation (three DESTINATIONS).** Custodian 1 = **primary signing authority**, primary IT workstation, **Ubuntu**, Cabang Pusat, **Raushan Fikri Ridha / IT**, disk encryption + login password + screen lock; **the ONLY host on which the first production key may be generated**. Custodian 2 = **encrypted backup destination**, Admin Klinik workstation, **Windows**, Klinik Daengtisia, access **IT + Admin Klinik**, same three endpoint controls. Custodian 3 = **encrypted backup + sealed-cold + offsite**, **USB**, Kantor Management Klinik, **IT**; encrypted container only, **unrelated existing data is NOT wiped**, passphrase never on the same medium, offline except during an approved operation, no general daily use afterwards.

**READY IS NOT CREATED — the whole point.** `*_destination_ready` = a place prepared to receive a copy; `*_backup_created` = a copy exists. Every ready flag is paired with a created flag that is **false**, in the same config block. Still false and asserted false by the scanner: `production_signing_key_provisioned`, `backup_1_key_copy_created`, `backup_2_key_copy_created`, `sealed_cold_backup_created`, `offsite_backup_created`, `recovery_verified`, `production_certificate_sha256` (**null**), APK built, enrolment, pilot/global enforcement. `custody_state_machine_consistent` **FAILS** if a backup is claimed before a key, a recovery before a backup, or a provisioned key while no certificate is pinned; `custody_readiness_does_not_claim_provisioning` **FAILS** if the status is still `ready_for_provisioning` while any downstream fact is true. The config cannot drift into announcing backups that do not exist.

**Machine enforcement.** New `config/android_release.signing.custody` (lifecycle states, designation, negative facts, media rules, permitted/forbidden generation media, shared-access declaration). New `AndroidReleaseGovernanceScanner::signingCustodyChecks()` — **13 checks**, fail-closed on a missing block — plus `custodySecretLeaks()` and `custodyReadyForProvisioning()`. Command goes **27 → 40/40 GO**. **A deliberate behaviour change:** the summary line `production_signing_key_provisioned` was a hardcoded `false`; it now reads the recorded fact. A literal would keep printing "no key" forever *including after a key exists* — the same lie pointing the other way — so it is read from config and cross-checked by the consistency gate instead. Key-generation authority is **not trusted from a boolean**: the medium is cross-checked against permitted/forbidden lists, so a backup destination cannot promote itself by flipping one flag.

**TWO EXCEPTIONS RECORDED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED OVER** (the governance doc's own instruction is to record an exception, not pretend it is not one). **(a) Three media are NOT three people.** IT is responsible for Custodian 1 and 3 and holds access to Custodian 2, so **one party can reach every copy** — `single_party_can_reach_all_copies=true` with 5 compensating controls; "three custodians" in the governance table meant three *independent holders*, and a future reader must not upgrade three media into three people. **A second independent human custodian is an open item before the `operational` state.** **(b) Sealed-cold and offsite sit on ONE medium** (the table expected them on separate holders): three copies across three sites still satisfy the policy and any single-site loss leaves two, but losing Kantor Management Klinik leaves **no sealed copy** and the two survivors are both workstation disks — exactly the population a sealed offline copy exists to survive. Both are owner-accepted residual risks with the 90-day drill and re-sealing as compensating controls.

**Secret hygiene.** The committed custody block records **roles, media classes and organisation-level locations only** — no serial, filesystem identifier, private address or passphrase. `custody_records_no_secret_material` matches on the **exact leaf key** so policy vocabulary (`login_password`, `password_stored_with_key_permitted`) is not mistaken for a stored password, plus a long-hex value guard. **A test defect was found and fixed rather than worked around:** the first secret scan swept for the substring `passphrase` and failed on the compensating control legitimately named `passphrase_held_separately_from_every_medium` — renaming the control to satisfy the test would have been evasion, so the test was rewritten to match leaf keys and value shapes as the scanner does.

**Independent security review — CRITICAL 0, HIGH 0 — found 3 MEDIUM + 3 LOW defects, all fixed before merge.** Every one pointed the *safe* direction (a check verifying a self-declared value against another self-declared value, then printing a PASS message asserting a fact it never checked) — there was never a path to a green scan claiming a key exists. **M1 (most consequential — where the key may be born):** key generation read the permitted AND forbidden media from the same config block being edited, so `custodian_1.media='production_vps'` + `permitted=['production_vps']` in one edit gave PASS while printing "VPS ... excluded" — now anchored to `GENERATION_MEDIA_ALLOWLIST` / `GENERATION_MEDIA_MUST_FORBID` **constants the scanner owns** (config may forbid more, never less, and may not widen past the allowlist). **M2:** the concentration caveat was self-declared — `single_party_can_reach_all_copies=false` cost nothing while `authorized_access` three lines away showed IT on every destination; now **DERIVED** from the intersection of every custodian's `authorized_access` (denying a proven concentration FAILs; a destination with no access list FAILs). **M3:** the secret detector was a leaf-key denylist + one anchored hex regex — a USB serial, filesystem UUID, private street address, phone number and colon-separated fingerprint all passed under a message claiming none could; custodian entries now use a **field ALLOWLIST** (`CUSTODIAN_PERMITTED_FIELDS`) plus value-shape detection (hex runs, separator-bearing hex, UUID, phone, email, base64, street-level address vocabulary). **LOW:** a second primary authority hidden in plural `roles[]` (check 4 read only singular `role`); `count()` counting entries so `'custodian_4'=>[]` met the minimum; check 12 printing "those remain false" for any status past readiness. **Accepted limitation:** the scanner never observes a filesystem, so a key on disk with the flag left false is undetectable — this gate proves the RECORD is consistent, not the absence of a key.

**Adversarial.** 22 config mutations (authority removed; disk encryption / screen lock / login password off; offsite and sealed-cold roles dropped; plaintext permitted; password with key; key claimed without certificate; backup before key; recovery before backup; unknown state; VPS / Custodian 2 / USB each promoted to generation authority; too few destinations; shared access undeclared and declared without controls; planted serial and passphrase). **8 real code mutants applied to the scanner** (consistency forced PASS → 4 tests failed; secret detector stubbed → 2; generation restriction short-circuited → 3; readiness-honesty forced PASS → 1), file restored **by copy** and verified byte-identical by checksum after each. **One mutant initially SURVIVED**: disabling the custodian field allowlist broke nothing, because the fields the test probed (`device_id`, `unlock_hint`) are *also* on the key denylist — the test proved nothing about the allowlist. A test using names on neither list (`cabinet_number`, `notes`) closes it. **Final survivors: 0.**

**Stage gates (runbook §0) — only A is closed.** A custody readiness **CLOSED/GO** · B key provisioning · C backup creation · D recovery verification · E certificate pinning · F APK signing · G device installation — all **open**. **Stage B without C and D is worse than not starting**: it creates something irreplaceable and leaves it in one place. This GO authorises beginning `PRODUCTION-ANDROID-SIGNING-KEY-PROVISIONING-1` on Custodian 1 **and nothing else** — no separate key-generation consent is implied.

**Unchanged:** pilot = drg Karmila / Cabang Sunu, `pilot_activated=false`, `current_stage=off`, `device_enforcement_active=false`, `doctor_browser_login_denied=false`; global prohibited before Phase 5; Device Owner / kiosk remain Phase 4B; Google Play / Managed Google Play / Play App Signing unused (ADR 0010); the real-device KeyInfo preflight remains **PASS** authority and was **not re-run**. **Phase 4 and the first Phase 4A attempt remain BLOCKED / NO-GO and are NOT rewritten.** Neither `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-phase-4a-app-only-real-device-pilot-go` nor `feature-doctor-trusted-android-device-lock-1-go` was created. Tests: new `tests/Feature/DoctorDevice/DoctorDeviceAndroidSigningCustodyReadinessTest.php` (**57**), **declared in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`** rather than left to the `DoctorDevice` filter token (a token match is an accident of naming and nothing would announce the day it stopped selecting). **Deploy:** no migration, no seed — deploy and re-run `sudo -u daengtisiams php artisan android:release-readiness`. **Pre-existing, unrelated:** the two rules numbered 143 remain (this record uses 145); `DoctorDeviceApiAndNoEnforcementTest > it gates web routes behind device trust…` is **risky at baseline** (confirmed with these changes stashed).

## REVISION-PRODUCTION-SIGNING-CUSTODIAN1-ENCRYPTED-VAULT-1 (2026-09-05)

Branch `security/revision-production-signing-custodian1-encrypted-vault-1` (base `feature/sprint-26-phase-26-8-stabilization-closure-go-watch-no-go-report` @ `95a01036`; do NOT target main). Sprint doc `docs/sprints/revision-production-signing-custodian1-encrypted-vault-1.md`; rule mirror `.cursor/rules/146-android-signing-custodian1-encrypted-vault.mdc`; runbook `docs/runbooks/android-signing-key-backup-and-recovery.md` §0/§2b. **No production signing key generated, no backup created, no certificate pinned, no APK built, no tablet touched, no migration, no permission, no route.**

**The finding.** PRODUCTION-ANDROID-SIGNING-CUSTODY-READINESS-1 recorded `custodian_1.disk_encryption => true`; `custody_endpoint_controls_recorded` read that boolean and printed PASS, and the gate reported GO 40/40. The check name was accurate — the control was *recorded* — but nothing had ever *measured* it. PRODUCTION-ANDROID-SIGNING-KEY-PROVISIONING-1 measured it, because it was about to write an unrecoverable key to that disk, and the claim did not survive: `/` and `/home` mount from **raw ext4 partitions** (`/dev/sda1`, `/dev/sda3`), `/dev/mapper/` holds only `control`, no LUKS signature, no eCryptfs, no fscrypt, `lsattr -d` shows lowercase `e` (extent format) not `E`, and `cryptsetup` was not installed. A LUKS root would mount from `/dev/mapper/*`; it did not.

**The correction is a SPLIT, not a flip.** Flipping to `false` alone would have been truthful and useless (it would say the only authorised signing host is unfit to hold the key); leaving `true` was the defect. So: **`host_full_disk_encryption => false`** (the true host fact) **plus** `primary_secret_storage => [type=dedicated_encrypted_vault, encryption=luks2, verified=true, default_state=closed, auto_unlock=false, plaintext_keyfile=false, outside_repository=true]` (the control that covers the key). Neither half is readable as the other. **Never set `host_full_disk_encryption` true unless the disk genuinely is.**

**The vault.** 256 MiB **LUKS2** container on Custodian 1, created **non-destructively** beside the existing filesystem — no reinstall, no wipe, no repartition; `mkfs.ext4` ran only on the new `/dev/mapper` device, never a physical partition. `cryptsetup 2:2.8.4-1ubuntu4` from the official Ubuntu archive. Passphrase typed **interactively by the operator**, never in argv/env/file/script/chat, and distinct from the future keystore password and both backup passphrases. Verified as a **lifecycle, not a boolean**: opened → mounted → written → unmounted → closed → **proven to hide its contents while closed** → reopened → **proven to return them** → closed. Independent verification did not trust the script's own output — the LUKS2 magic was read straight from the header: **`4c554b53babe0002`** (`LUKS` `0xBABE` v2). Container `0600` 268435456 bytes not a symlink; parent + mountpoint `0700`; `namei` shows no symlink in the path; outside every repo/worktree; `/etc/crypttab` has **zero** entries and no systemd unit (no auto-unlock); no keyfile; resting state mapper **closed**, unmounted, mountpoint empty.

**Scanner.** `custody_endpoint_controls_recorded` no longer accepts a bare encryption boolean — a workstation satisfies encryption-at-rest **either** by host FDE **or** by a vault surviving *every* clause of `primaryVaultEncrypted()` (strict identity, so `"false"`, `1` or a missing key all fail). New **`custody_primary_secret_storage_encrypted`** targets the one machine that will hold the live keystore and states the host/vault distinction in words, so the report cannot blur "the backup machine is fine" into "the signing machine is fine". **`disk_encryption` is REMOVED from `CUSTODIAN_PERMITTED_FIELDS`, not renamed** — a config still carrying it is an unrecognised field and fails `custody_records_no_secret_material`, so the original ambiguous record cannot quietly satisfy the gate a second time. `custodianUnknownFields()` now **descends into `primary_secret_storage`**: it previously walked only a custodian's top level, so the first nested array would have been an unpoliced surface underneath the very check that exists to keep `unlock_hint` out of committed files. Gate: **GO, 41/41 PASS, 0 WATCH, 0 FAIL** (was 40/40; the new check is the 41st).

**Storage readiness is a place to put a key, not a key.** Still false: `production_signing_key_provisioned`, `backup_1/2_key_copy_created`, `sealed_cold_backup_created`, `offsite_backup_created`, `recovery_verified`, `production_certificate_sha256` (null, `pin_required_before_install=true`), production APK, pilot, global. `DEVICE_ENFORCEMENT_ACTIVE=false`, `current_stage=off`; drg Karmila at Cabang Sunu unaffected. Custodian 1 remains the **only** permitted key-generation host.

**Residual exposure, recorded not smoothed over.** The vault protects **at rest**; it does not make the workstation encrypted. Swap is `/swap.img`, **4 GB, file-backed on the unencrypted root filesystem**, so material in memory while the vault is open can page out in the clear — the operator took swap down for provisioning and restored it, and the runbook now requires the same for every signing operation (`/tmp` is tmpfs, which can itself swap). **Custodian 2's `host_full_disk_encryption` remains an operator DECLARATION about a remote Windows machine no scanner has measured** — not load-bearing, because custodian 2's copy must arrive inside its own encrypted container regardless (Admin Klinik can log in to that machine).

**Key provisioning stays BLOCKED — storage gap closed, physical custody gap not.** Backup 1 needs an operator at the Admin Klinik workstation; Backup 2 needs the Custodian 3 USB attached. PRODUCTION-ANDROID-SIGNING-KEY-PROVISIONING-1 is preserved as **BLOCKED BEFORE KEY GENERATION** in `docs/sprints/production-android-signing-key-provisioning-1-blocked.md` and must **not** be rewritten as a failed implementation — it stopped exactly where its own governance required. Tests: new `tests/Feature/DoctorDevice/DoctorDeviceCustodian1EncryptedVaultTest.php` (**19**), **registered in `ci_runner.critical_gate_mandatory_suites`** (a filter token is an accident of naming). **TDD evidence:** the new suite was run against the unfixed code first and produced **13 failures**, then green after the fix, with the implementation reverted and restored **by copy** and verified byte-identical by checksum. **Deploy:** no migration, no seed — deploy and re-run `sudo -u daengtisiams php artisan android:release-readiness`.
